(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{k})\leq(\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{k})\ \text{if and only if}\ \alpha_{i}\leq\beta_{i}\ \forall i=1,\ldots,k

is a partial order on
{\mathbb{A}}_{1}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{A}}_{k}
.

We often consider posets with a well-defined “depth” function. We introduce the following notion of homogeneous posets. In fact all moduli spaces considered in this paper are stratified by the following kind of posets.

Definition 3.1
.

A poset
{\mathbb{A}}
is called
homogeneous
if for each
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}
, the length of a maximal sequence of elements
\alpha=\alpha_{0}<\alpha_{1}<\cdots<\alpha_{k}
such that
\alpha_{k}\in{\mathbb{A}}^{\max}
is finite and only depends on
\alpha
. This length is called the
depth
or
codimension
of
\alpha
, denoted by
{\rm depth}(\alpha)
. In particular,

\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}^{\max}\Longleftrightarrow{\rm depth}(\alpha)=0.

A poset map between homogeneous posets is a called a
homogeneous
poset map if it preserves the depth.

Definition 3.2
.

Given a homogeneous poset
{\mathbb{A}}
, the
set of adjacent faces
of
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}
is defined to be

{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}:=\{\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}|\alpha\leq\beta\text{ and }{\rm depth}(\beta)=1\}.

It is straightforward to check that homogeneous posets also admit finite products with depth function being

{\rm depth}(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{k})={\rm depth}(\alpha_{1})+\cdots+{\rm depth}(\alpha_{k}).

We introudce the following “boundary stratum” notation. Given a poset
{\mathbb{A}}
and an element
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}
, denote

\partial^{\alpha}{\mathbb{A}}:=\{\alpha^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{A}}\ |\ \alpha^{\prime}\leq\alpha\}.

More generally, if
{\mathbb{B}}\subset{\mathbb{A}}
is a subset, denote

\partial^{\mathbb{B}}{\mathbb{A}}:=\bigcup_{\beta\in{\mathbb{B}}}\partial^{\beta}{\mathbb{A}}.

It has the induced partial order. If
{\mathbb{A}}
is a homogeneous poset, then
\partial^{\alpha}{\mathbb{A}}
is also homogeneous with depth function being shifted by
{\rm depth}(\alpha)
. Moreover, for each nonnegative integer
k
, denote

\partial^{[k]}{\mathbb{A}}:=\partial^{{\rm depth}^{-1}(k)}{\mathbb{A}}

which is still homogeneous with the depth function shifted by
k
.

3.1.2.
Stratified spaces

We introduce our notion of stratified topological spaces. We emphasize here that stratified spaces always refer to a poset. Moreover, the notion of stratified spaces should not be confused with the notion of prestratified spaces discussed in Appendix
A
.

Definition 3.3
.

Let
{\mathbb{A}}
be a (countable) poset. An
{\mathbb{A}}
-stratified space
(
{\mathbb{A}}
-space for short) is a locally compact, Hausdorff and second countable topological space
X
endowed with a continuous map

s:X\to{\mathbb{A}}

with respect to the Alexandrov topology on
{\mathbb{A}}
such that the range of
s
is finite. In particular, we can write

X=\bigsqcup_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}}X_{\alpha},\ {\rm where}\ X_{\alpha}:=s^{-1}(\alpha)

satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

Each
X_{\alpha}
(called a
stratum
) is locally closed (which can be empty
4
4

4

For example, in a moduli space of stable Floer cylinders, the subset of smooth Floer cylinders, which should be the top stratum, could be empty.
).

(2)

All but finitely many strata are empty.

(3)

For each
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}
, the subset

\partial^{\alpha}X:=\bigsqcup_{\beta\leq\alpha}X_{\beta}

is a closed set (which may contain the closure of
X_{\alpha}
properly). Note that this condition follows from the continuity of
s
.

We introduce the following notions for stratified spaces.

Definition 3.4
.

(1)

A map from an
{\mathbb{A}}_{1}
-space
X_{1}
to an
{\mathbb{A}}_{2}
-space
X_{2}
is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{X_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{s_{1}}
\textstyle{X_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s_{2}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{2}}

where
i:{\mathbb{A}}_{1}\to{\mathbb{A}}_{2}
is a poset map and
f
is a continuous map. If
{\mathbb{A}}_{1}
and
{\mathbb{A}}_{2}
are both homogeneous (see Definition
3.1
), then we require that
i
is a homogeneous map. We usually call such a map a
stratified map
to emphasize that it respects the stratifications.

(2)

A stratified map
f:X_{1}\to X_{2}
as above is called an
embedding
if
f
is a homeomorphism onto its image and
i:{\mathbb{A}}_{1}\to{\mathbb{A}}_{2}
is an injection. An open embedding is an embedding with an open image.

(3)

A stratified map
f:X_{1}\to X_{2}
is called a homeomorphism if
f
is a homeomorphism of topological spaces and the underlying poset map is an isomorphism.

(4)

The product of
{\mathbb{A}}_{i}
-spaces
X_{i}
(
i=1,\ldots,k
) is the product topological space
X_{1}\times\cdots\times X_{k}
stratified by the product poset
{\mathbb{A}}_{1}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{A}}_{k}
. It is easy to see

\partial^{(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{k})}(X_{1}\times\cdots\times X_{k})=\partial^{\alpha_{1}}X_{1}\times\cdots\times\partial^{\alpha_{k}}X_{k}.

(5)

Let
G
be a topological group. A
G
-action on an
{\mathbb{A}}
-space
X
is a continuous
G
-action on
X
which preserves each stratum. In this case, the
\partial^{\alpha}{\mathbb{A}}
-space
\partial^{\alpha}X
has an induced
G
-action.

3.2.
Topological flow categories and bimodules

The concept of flow categories was introduced by Cohen–Jones–Segal
[
CJS95
]
. We need a variant of the original construction similar to
[
Par16
, Section 7]
and
[
Abo22
, Section 7,8]
.

Setup 3.5
.

Let
N
be a nonnegative integer,
\Pi
be an infinite cyclic group, and
\omega:\Pi\to{\mathbb{Z}}
be a group injection.

Let
{\mathcal{P}}
be a countable poset equipped with the following extra data: a free
\Pi
-action and two functions (called the
action
and the
index
)

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}:{\mathcal{P}}\to{\mathbb{R}},\

\displaystyle\ {\rm ind}^{\mathcal{P}}:{\mathcal{P}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}/2N.

Assume the following conditions.

(1)

The
\Pi
-action is order-preserving. Namely, for all
p,q\in{\mathcal{P}}
and
a\in\Pi

p\leq q\Longleftrightarrow a\cdot p\leq a\cdot q.

(2)

For all
p\in{\mathcal{P}}
and
a\in\Pi
,

{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}(a\cdot p)={\mathcal{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}(p)+\omega(a)

(3.1)

and

{\rm ind}^{\mathcal{P}}(a\cdot p)=\rm{ind}^{\mathcal{P}}(p).

(3.2)

(3)

For all
p,q\in{\mathcal{P}}
,

p<q\Longrightarrow{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}(p)<{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}(q).

(4)

The quotient set
\underline{\smash{\mathcal{P}}}:={\mathcal{P}}/\Pi
is finite.

The conditions that
\omega:\Pi\to{\mathbb{Z}}
is injective and that
\underline{\smash{\mathcal{P}}}
is finite imply that
{\mathcal{P}}
is “locally finite-dimensional,” namely, for any pair of elements
p<q
of
{\mathcal{P}}
, there are at most finitely many elements lying between them.

Notation 3.6
.

Given a pair of elements
p<q
in
{\mathcal{P}}
, we define a poset

{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}:=\big\{\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\ |\ p<r_{1}<\cdots<r_{l}<q,\ r_{1},\ldots,r_{l}\in{\mathcal{P}}\big\}

whose partial order is induced by inclusion

ps_{1}\cdots s_{m}q\leq pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\Longleftrightarrow\{r_{1}\cdots r_{l}\}\subseteq\{s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}\}.

The poset
{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}
has a unique maximal element
pq
and is homogeneous whose depth function is

{\rm depth}(pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q)=l.

The following is a formal characterization of the feature that boundary strata of moduli spaces consist of broken trajectories. Namely, given a triple
prq\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}
, it is easy to see that there is an isomorphism of homogeneous posets

{\mathbb{A}}_{pr}^{\mathcal{P}}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{rq}^{\mathcal{P}}\cong\partial^{prq}{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}

(3.3)

under the concatenation of strings.

Now we introduce the notion of flow categories under the setting of Setup
3.5
.

Definition 3.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{P}}
be as in Setup
3.5
. A
flow category

T^{\mathcal{P}}
over
{\mathcal{P}}
is a topologically enriched category
5
5

5

Namely, the set of morphisms are topological spaces and composition maps are continuous.
with the set of objects given by
{\mathcal{P}}
, with morphism spaces
T_{pq}
satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

T_{pq}\neq\emptyset
only if
p\leq q
in
{\mathcal{P}}
.
6
6

6

In the Morse or Floer case, it is indeed true that
T_{pq}\neq\emptyset
if and only if
p\leq q
.

(2)

T_{pp}
is the singleton.

(3)

T_{pq}
is a compact
{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}
-space.

(4)

Given a triple
p<r<q
in
{\mathcal{P}}
, the composition map factors through a stratified homeomorphism

\textstyle{T_{pr}\times T_{rq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial^{prq}T_{pq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{pr}^{\mathcal{P}}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{rq}^{\mathcal{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial^{prq}{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}}

where the underlying poset isomorphism is the map (
3.3
). We require that whenever
p<r<s<q
, the following associativity diagram holds:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{pr}\times T_{rs}\times T_{sq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{ps}\times T_{sq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{pr}\times T_{rq}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{pq}.}

(5)

\Pi
defines a strict action on
T^{\mathcal{P}}
: for any
a\in\Pi
and
p,q\in{\mathcal{P}}
, there is a stratified homeomorphism

\textstyle{T_{pq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{a}}
\textstyle{T_{a\cdot p\ a\cdot q}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{a\cdot p\ a\cdot q}^{\mathcal{P}}}

where the underlying poset map is the natural isomorphism. Moreover, when
a_{1},a_{2}\in\Pi
, we require that the equation
\phi_{a_{1}\cdot a_{2}}=\phi_{a_{1}}\circ\phi_{a_{2}}
holds and
\phi_{0}
is the identity map for
a=0\in\Pi
.

Lemma 3.8
.

Given
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in\mathbb{A}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}
, the space
\partial^{\alpha}T_{pq}
is homeomorphic to
T_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times T_{r_{l}q}
as
\mathbb{A}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{A}^{\mathcal{P}}_{r_{l}q}
-spaces.

Proof.

We prove the statement by induction on
{\rm depth}(\alpha)
. For
{\rm depth}(\alpha)=0
, this is tautology, and for
{\rm depth}(\alpha)=1
, the assertion follows from Definition
3.7
. Suppose the lemma holds for all
{\rm depth}(\alpha)\leq l-1
. Now suppose
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
. Consider the homeomorphism between
\mathbb{A}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pr_{1}}\times\mathbb{A}^{\mathcal{P}}_{r_{1}q}
spaces
T_{pr_{1}}\times T_{r_{1}q}\to\partial^{pr_{1}q}T_{pq}
. Restricting the homeomorphism along the closed stratum
\mathbb{A}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pr_{1}}\times\partial^{r_{1}\cdots r_{l}q}\mathbb{A}^{\mathcal{P}}_{r_{1}q}
and using the induction hypothesis, we obtain a homeomorphism of
\mathbb{A}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{A}^{\mathcal{P}}_{r_{l}q}
-spaces

T_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times T_{r_{l}q}\to\partial^{\alpha}T_{pq}.

By associativity, if we construct such a homeomorphism by decomposing
\alpha
as
pr_{1}\cdots r_{k}
and
r_{k}\cdots r_{l}q
for some
1\leq k\leq l
, the resulting homeomorphism between the stratified spaces is the same.
∎

Definition
3.7
does not impose any regularity conditions or tangential structures on the morphism spaces. The purpose of such a definition is to single out the stratification structures and we will introduce several enhancements by putting various structures on the morphism spaces in the sequel.

3.2.1.
Flow bimodules

We first introduce the posets indexing strata in flow bimodules.

Notation 3.9
.

Suppose
{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
are two posets as in Setup
3.5
equipped with own action and index functions

\displaystyle\ ({\mathcal{A}}^{\mathcal{P}},{\rm ind}^{\mathcal{P}}):{\mathcal{P}}\to{\mathbb{R}}\times({\mathbb{Z}}/2N),\

\displaystyle\ ({\mathcal{A}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}},{\rm ind}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}):{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{R}}\times({\mathbb{Z}}/2N).

For
p\in{\mathcal{P}}
and
p^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
, define a poset

{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}:=\big\{\alpha=pq_{1}\cdots q_{k}q^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\cdots q^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}\ |\ p<q_{1}<\cdots<q_{k},\ q^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}<\cdots<q^{\prime}_{1}<p^{\prime}\big\}

The partial order is again induced by inclusion:

pq_{1}\cdots q_{k}q^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\cdots q^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}\leq p\tilde{q}_{1}\cdots\tilde{q}_{\tilde{k}}\tilde{q}^{\prime}_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\cdots\tilde{q}^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}\\
\Longleftrightarrow\{\tilde{q}_{1},\dots,\tilde{q}_{\tilde{k}}\}\subseteq\{q_{1},\dots,q_{k}\}\ {\rm and}\ \{\tilde{q}^{\prime}_{1}\cdots\tilde{q}^{\prime}_{\tilde{k}^{\prime}}\}\subseteq\{q^{\prime}_{1}\cdots q^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\}.

{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}
is a homogeneous poset with a unique maximal element
pp^{\prime}
and depth function

{\rm depth}(pq_{1}\cdots q_{k}q_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}\cdots q_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime})=k+k^{\prime}.

There are similar characterizations of “broken configurations.” If
p<q
are elements in
{\mathcal{P}}
, it is easy to see that there is a natural isomorphism of homogeneous posets

{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{qp^{\prime}}\cong\partial^{pqp^{\prime}}{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}

by concatenation of words. Such an isomorphism makes the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{pq_{1}}^{\mathcal{P}}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{q_{1}q_{2}}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{q_{2}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{pq_{2}}^{\mathcal{P}}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{q_{2}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq_{1}}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{q_{1}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}}

Similarly, if
q^{\prime}<p^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
, one has

{\mathbb{A}}_{pq^{\prime}}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}\cong\partial^{pq^{\prime}p^{\prime}}{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}},

which satisfies a similar commutative relation as above, and in this case the poset
{\mathbb{A}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
acts on the right. Moreover, these two types of isomorphisms are compatible in the following sense. Namely, the following diagram is commutative for which the arrows are induced by the obvious concatenation of words.

\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{qq^{\prime}}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{qp^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{pq^{\prime}}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}}

Definition 3.10
.

Let
T^{\mathcal{P}}
and
T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
be flow categories over
{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
respectively. A
flow bimodule

M
from
T^{\mathcal{P}}
to
T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
consists of the following data.

(1)

A compact
{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}
-space
M_{pp^{\prime}}
(which could be empty) for all
p\in{\mathcal{P}}
and
p^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
.

(2)

For
p<q
, a homeomorphism of stratified spaces

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 26.90326pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-26.90326pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}\times M_{qp^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern These data should be subject to the following conditions.

(1)

There is a constant
C>0
such that for all
p\in{\mathcal{P}}
,
p^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
,

M_{pp^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset\Longrightarrow{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}(p)<{\mathcal{A}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}(p^{\prime})+C.

(3.4)

(2)

For
p<q_{1}<q_{2}
in
{\mathcal{P}}
and
p^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
, the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq_{1}}\times T^{\mathcal{P}}_{q_{1}q_{2}}\times M_{q_{2}p^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq_{1}}\times M_{q_{1}p^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq_{2}}\times M_{q_{2}p^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{pp^{\prime}}}

where
T^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq_{1}}\times M_{q_{1}p^{\prime}}\rightarrow M_{pp^{\prime}}
is induced by the composition of the homeomorphism
T^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq_{1}}\times M_{q_{1}p^{\prime}}\rightarrow\partial^{pq_{1}p^{\prime}}M_{pp^{\prime}}
and the inclusion
\partial^{pq_{1}p^{\prime}}M_{pp^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow M_{pp^{\prime}}
and so forth.

(3)

Similarly, for
p\in{\mathcal{P}}
and
q_{2}^{\prime}<q_{1}^{\prime}<p^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{pq_{2}^{\prime}}\times T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q_{2}^{\prime}q_{1}^{\prime}}\times T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{pq_{1}^{\prime}}\times T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q_{1}^{\prime}p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{pq_{2}^{\prime}}\times T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q_{2}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{pp^{\prime}}.}

(4)

For
p<q
in
{\mathcal{P}}
and
q^{\prime}<p^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}\times M_{qq^{\prime}}\times T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{pq^{\prime}}\times T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}\times M_{qp^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{pp^{\prime}}.}

(5)

Strict
\Pi
-action: for any
a\in\Pi
, there is a stratified homeomorphism

\textstyle{M_{pp^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M_{a\cdot p\ a\cdot p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{a\cdot p\ a\cdot p^{\prime}}}

such that for
a_{1},a_{2}\in\Pi
the equation
\phi^{M}_{a_{1}\cdot a_{2}}=\phi^{M}_{a_{1}}\circ\phi^{M}_{a_{2}}
holds, and such that
\phi^{M}_{id}
is the identity map. Moreover, we require that the actions

T^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq_{1}}\times M_{q_{1}p^{\prime}}\rightarrow M_{pp^{\prime}},M_{pq_{1}^{\prime}}\times T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}\to M_{pp^{\prime}}

are
\Pi
-equivariant.

The following statement is the analog of Lemma
3.8
for flow bimodules. The associativity conditions from Definition
3.10
guarantees that the maps between the stratified spaces are well-defined.

Lemma 3.11
.

Suppose
M_{pp^{\prime}}
is nonempty. Given an element
\alpha=pq_{1}\cdots q_{k}q^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\cdots q^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}
, we have a stratified homeomorphism

94.68071pt\raise-33.86896pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}.

As spelled out in
[
Abo22
, Section 8]
, one can define “higher” homotopies between flow bimodules parametrized cubes
[0,1]^{n}
for any
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
and construct a cubically enriched category of flow bimodules. Such notions are useful for proving invariance of Floer homology/homotopy groups, but establishing such invariance is beyond the scope of this paper.

3.3.
Outer-collaring

In Floer theory one often needs to inductively construct structures (such as perturbations) on infinitely many moduli spaces. Each moduli space is an orbifold with corners in a suitable sense. One wishes to construct the structures such that near the boundary or corner they are of the product type. This requires various compatible collar structures near the boundary and corners. The existence of a compatible system of collar structures is difficult to construct directly. Instead, following
[
FOOO20
, Chapter 17]
, we take a short-cut by constructing the collars “outside.”

We will first consider the outer-collaring construction for individual stratified spaces, flow categories, and bimodules. They are operated in the topological category. This discussion is not immediately used in this section. Later we will also discuss outer-collaring of stratified objects in the smooth category.

3.3.1.
Outer-collaring of stratified spaces

We first discuss the outer-collaring of a single moduli space which may appear in either a flow category or a bimodule. We consider the first case (flow category) and the second case is completely similar. Abbreviate
{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}
by
{\mathbb{A}}
where
{\mathcal{P}}
satisfies conditions of Setup
3.5
and
p,q\in{\mathcal{P}}
. Let
X
be an
{\mathbb{A}}
-space whose strata are indexed by words
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
. Choose
r\geq 0
and we will define a new
{\mathbb{A}}
-space denoted by
X^{\boxplus r}
. As a set,

X^{\boxplus r}=\left(\bigsqcup_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}}\partial^{\alpha}X\times[-r,0]^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}\right)/\sim,

where
{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\subset{\mathbb{A}}
is the set of adjacent faces and where the equivalence relation
\sim
is generated by the following relation: if
\alpha\leq\beta
(which implies the inclusion of the sets of adjacent faces
{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}\subset{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}
), we identify

(x,(t_{i})_{i\in{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}})\in\partial^{\beta}X\times[-r,0]^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}

with

(y,(s_{j})_{j\in{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}})\in\partial^{\alpha}X\times[-r,0]^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}

if
x=y\in\partial^{\alpha}X
,
s_{j}=0
when
j\notin{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}
and
t_{j}=s_{j}
when
j\in{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}
. We call
X^{\boxplus r}
the
outer collaring
of
X
of width
r
(see Figure
1
).

X
\partial^{\beta_{2}}X
\partial^{\beta_{1}}X
\partial^{\alpha}X\times[-r,0]^{2}
\partial^{\beta_{1}}X\times[-r,0]
\partial^{\beta_{2}}X\times[-r,0]

Figure 1.
The local picture of an outer collaring of a prestratified space for
\alpha<\beta_{1},\beta_{2}
.

One has a natural identification
X=X^{\boxplus 0}
and the natural inclusion
X^{\boxplus r}\hookrightarrow X^{\boxplus r^{\prime}}
when
r\leq r^{\prime}
. Moreover,
X^{\boxplus r}
has a structure of an
{\mathbb{A}}
-space as follows. For each
\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}
, define the boundary stratum

\partial^{\beta}X^{\boxplus r}=\bigcup_{\alpha\leq\beta}\Big(\partial^{\alpha}X\times\{(t_{j})_{j\in{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}\ |\ t_{j}=-r\ \forall j\in{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}\}\Big).

Then the corresponding stratum for
\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}
is given by

X_{\beta}^{\boxplus r}=\partial^{\beta}X^{\boxplus r}\setminus\bigcup_{\alpha<\beta}\partial^{\alpha}X^{\boxplus r}.

It is easy to see that the induced map
X^{\boxplus r}\to{\mathbb{A}}
is a stratification.

The following lemma shows that the outer-collaring construction respects products.

Lemma 3.12
.

(1)

For any
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}
, we have
(\partial^{\alpha}X)^{{\boxplus r}}=\partial^{\alpha}X^{\boxplus r}
as
\partial^{\alpha}{\mathbb{A}}
-spaces.

(2)

If
X_{i}
are
{\mathbb{A}}_{i}
-spaces for
i=1,2
, then
X_{1}^{\boxplus r}\times X_{2}^{\boxplus r}=(X_{1}\times X_{2})^{{\boxplus r}}
as
{\mathbb{A}}_{1}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{2}
-spaces.

Proof.

These statements are true by inspecting the definition.
∎

Lemma 3.13
.

Given a stratified map

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 9.77777pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.77777pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 16.43573pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 33.77777pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 34.56247pt\raise-31.27777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 34.56247pt\raise-31.27777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{2}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

there is a canonical extension

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 12.87384pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.87384pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 14.80836pt\raise 7.23817pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.87708pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f^{\boxplus r}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.87384pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.74768pt\raise-25.00197pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-8.99307pt\raise-32.27974pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 20.39409pt\raise-36.78668pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\iota}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.75461pt\raise-32.27974pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 40.75461pt\raise-32.27974pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{2}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Proof.

Using the presentation

X_{i}^{\boxplus r}=\left(\bigsqcup_{\alpha_{i}\in{\mathbb{A}}_{i}}\partial^{\alpha}X_{i}\times[-r,0]^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha_{i}}}\right)/\sim\text{ for }i=1,2,

we define the restriction of
f^{{\boxplus r}}
over
\partial^{\alpha_{1}}X_{1}\times[-r,0]^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha_{1}}}\to\partial^{\iota(\alpha_{1})}X_{2}\times[-r,0]^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\iota(\alpha_{1})}}
to be
f\times\text{id}
. It is easy to see that equivalent points are mapped to equivalent points, therefore we can define
f^{{\boxplus r}}
to be the induced map.
∎

The outer collar construction is compatible with group actions. Let
G
be a topological group acting on
X
via stratum-preserving homeomorphisms. For each
r>0
, define the
G
-action on
X^{\boxplus r}
as follows. For each
g\in G
and
(x,(t_{i})_{i\in{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}})\in\partial^{\alpha}X\times[-r,0]^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}
, define

g\cdot(x,(t_{i})_{i\in{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}})=(gx,(t_{i})_{i\in{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}})\in\partial^{\alpha}X\times[-r,0]^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}\subset X^{\boxplus r}.

It is easy to check that the action is well-defined, continuous, and preserves strata.

3.3.2.
Outer-collaring of flow categories and bimodules

The outer-collaring construction described above can be naturally carried over to a system of stratified spaces included in a flow category or bimodule. Let
{\mathcal{P}}
satisfy Setup
3.5
and let
T^{\mathcal{P}}
be a topological flow category over
{\mathcal{P}}
(the
\Pi
-action in this discussion is trivial hence we omit the reference to it). Fix
r\geq 0
. All outer-collaring in this discussion will be of a fixed width
r
and will be labelled by
+
instead of
{\boxplus r}
. Then we can apply the outer-collaring construction to each individual space
T_{pq}
for all
p<q
, obtaining
{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}
-spaces
T_{pq}^{+}
. Define “composition maps”

T_{pr}^{+}\times T_{rq}^{+}\cong\partial^{prq}T_{pq}^{+}\hookrightarrow T_{pq}^{+}

as the composition

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 23.68022pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.68022pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T_{pr}^{+}\times T_{rq}^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.68022pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 47.68022pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(T_{pr}\times T_{rq})^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 133.45738pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 133.45738pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\partial^{prq}T_{pq})^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 211.4214pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 211.4214pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\partial^{prq}(T_{pq}^{+})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Here the first map comes from item (2) of Lemma
3.12
, the second map is the canonical outer-collaring of the original composition map
T_{pr}\times T_{rq}\to\partial^{prq}T_{pq}
, and the third map comes from item (1) of Lemma
3.12
. It is easy to check that the composition maps are still associative. Hence we obtained a new topological flow category over
{\mathcal{P}}
, which we call the
outer-collaring
of
T^{\mathcal{P}}
, denoted by

(T^{\mathcal{P}})^{+}

whose morphisms spaces are
T_{pq}^{+}
.

Similar happens when we apply outer-collaring to flow bimodules. Let
T^{\mathcal{P}}
and
T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
be flow categories over
{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
respectively and let
M_{{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
be a flow bimodule from
T^{{\mathcal{P}}}
to
T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
. By doing outer-collaring to each individual space
M_{pp^{\prime}}
for
p\in{\mathcal{P}}
and
p^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
, obtaining new spaces
M_{pp^{\prime}}^{+}
, one obtains a flow bimodule
M_{{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}^{+}
from
T^{{\mathcal{P}}+}
to
T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}+}
. This new flow bimodule is called the
outer-collaring
of
M_{{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
.

3.4.
Derived orbifold charts with stratifications

As mentioned before, the morphism spaces of flow categories and flow bimodules are
a priori
only topological spaces. We would like to define the notion of
derived orbifold lift
for (topological) flow categories and flow bimodules.

3.4.1.
Stratified topological manifolds corners

Before discussing smooth structures, we introduce a refined structure on stratified spaces in a special setting. It is well-known that “manifolds with corners” are identical to manifolds with boundary in the topological category. One way to define topological
{\mathbb{A}}
-manifolds is given by
[
Abo22
, Definition 2.4]
. We specialize
loc. cit.
which suffices for our purpose.

First we introduce some notations. The standard model for a manifold with corners is the space
[0,+\infty)^{k}
for
k\geq 0
. It is stratified by subspaces where a subset of coordinates are equal to
0
.

Notation 3.14
.

For any finite set
{\mathbb{F}}
, let
{\mathbb{A}}^{({\mathbb{F}})}
be the set of all subsets of
{\mathbb{F}}
where the partial order is induced by inclusion:

\alpha\leq\beta\Longleftrightarrow\beta\subseteq\alpha.

{\mathbb{A}}^{({\mathbb{F}})}
is homogeneous with a unique maximal element
\emptyset
and its depth function being

{\rm depth}(\alpha)=\#\alpha.

We abbeviate
{\mathbb{A}}^{(k)}:={\mathbb{A}}^{(\{1,\ldots,k\})}
. Then
[0,+\infty)^{k}
is an
{\mathbb{A}}^{(k)}
-space.

Definition 3.15
.

Let
{\mathbb{A}}
be a (countable) homogeneous poset. An
{\mathbb{A}}
-space is called a (topological)
{\mathbb{A}}
-manifold
if the following is true.

(1)

For any
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}
, the space
\partial^{\alpha}X
is a topological manifold (possibly with boundary) whose interior is
X_{\alpha}
.

(2)

Each
x\in X_{\alpha}\subset X
has a
corner chart
, i.e., a stratified open embedding

\textstyle{U_{x,\alpha}\times[0,1)^{{\rm depth}(\alpha)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{\alpha\}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{({\rm depth}(\alpha))}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{A}}

where
U_{x,\alpha}
is an open subset of
x
in
X_{\alpha}
(with the trivial stratification indexed by the singleton
\{\alpha\}
).

(3)

For each corner chart
\varphi:U_{x}\times[0,1)^{{\rm depth}(\alpha)}\to X
near
x\in X_{\alpha}
, one has

\alpha\leq\beta\Longrightarrow{\rm Im}\varphi\cap X_{\beta}\neq\emptyset.

In other words, corner charts touch all strata above
\alpha
.

Remark 3.16
.

Conditions of Definition
3.15
impose more restrictions on the homogeneous poset
{\mathbb{A}}
. For example, for each
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}
, the set
\{\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}\ |\ \alpha\leq\beta\}
is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{A}}^{({\rm depth}(\alpha))}
and hence contains a unique maximal element. Moreover, each
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}
has a definite set of elements
\alpha\leq\beta
with
{\rm depth}(\beta)=1
whose cardinality is equal to its depth. The posets
{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}
appeared in the disucssions of flow categories and flow bimodules all satisfy these additional restrictions.

Definition 3.17
.

Let
G
be a topological group acting on an
{\mathbb{A}}
-manifold
X
viewing
X
as an
{\mathbb{A}}
-space. We say that
X
is an
{\mathbb{A}}
-stratified
G
-manifold with corners if each
x\in X_{\alpha}
has a
G
-equivariant corner chart, i.e., a corner chart
U_{x,\alpha}\times[0,1)^{{\rm depth}(\alpha)}\to X
which is a
G
-equivariant map. Here
U_{x,\alpha}\subset U_{\alpha}
is a
G
-invariant open subset and
G
acts trivially on the factor
[0,1)^{{\rm depth}(\alpha)}
.

3.4.2.
Stratified smooth manifolds with corners

Next we recall some notions related to smooth manifolds with corners. We use the following notion of smoothness for functions defined on arbitrary subsets of Euclidean spaces. Let
A\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
be a subset. A function
f:A\to{\mathbb{R}}
is called smooth if for each
a\in A
there is an open neighborhood
U_{a}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
such that
f|_{A\cap U_{a}}
can be extended to a smooth function
f_{a}:U_{a}\to{\mathbb{R}}
. Then one can define the notion of smooth maps
f:A\to B
for subsets
A\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
B\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
. Then compositions of smooth maps are still smooth. A smooth map
f:A\to B
is a diffeomorphism if it admits a smooth inverse.

The concept of smooth manifolds with corners is defined as follows. Let
M
be a topological space. A chart (of an
n
-dimensional smooth manifold with corners) consists of an open subset
U\subset M
and a homeomorphism
\varphi:U\to[0,+\infty)^{n}
onto an open subset. Two charts
\varphi_{i}:U_{i}\to[0,+\infty)^{n}
,
i=1,2
, are compatible if
\varphi_{1}\circ\varphi_{2}^{-1}:\varphi_{2}(U_{1}\cap U_{2})\to\varphi_{1}(U_{1}\cap U_{2})
is a diffeomorphism. A structure of smooth
n
-dimensional manifold with corners is a maximal compatible atlas on
M
. If
M
is equipped with such a structure, we say that
M
is a smooth
n
-dimensional manifold with corners. Over smooth manifolds with corners, one can define the notions of smooth vector bundles, smooth connections, and smooth Riemannian metrics, etc.

The concept of manifold with faces is one which can exclude certain pathological scenarios. Let
M
be a smooth
n
-dimensional manifold with corners. For each point
x\in M
and each chart
\varphi:U\to[0,+\infty)^{n}
defined over an open neighborhood of
x
, the number
{\rm depth}(x)
of coordinates of
\varphi(x)
which are zero is independent of the choice of charts. Given
k\leq n
, the subset

\partial^{k}M:=\{x\in M\ |\ {\rm depth}(x)=k\}\subset M

is naturally an
n-k
-dimensional smooth manifold. A
connected face
of
M
is a connected component of the set
\{x\in M\ |\ {\rm depth}(x)=1\}
.
M
is called a
smooth manifold with faces
if every
x\in M
belongs to the closure of exactly
{\rm depth}(x)
different connected faces.

As we are treating various manifolds with faces which have more refined structure of stratifications, we would like to introduce the following concepts.

Definition 3.18
.

Let
{\mathbb{A}}
be a countable homogeneous poset.

(1)

A
smooth
{\mathbb{A}}
-manifold
is a smooth manifold with faces
M
which is also a topological
{\mathbb{A}}
-manifold such that for each
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}
,
M_{\alpha}
is an open and closed subset of
\partial^{{\rm depth}(\alpha)}M

and such that each
x\in M
has a smooth corner chart.

(2)

Let
G
be a compact Lie group. A smooth
G
-action on a smooth
{\mathbb{A}}
-manifold
M
is a
G
-action on the underlying topological
{\mathbb{A}}
-manifold of
M
which acts on
M
by smooth maps.

Note that any smooth
{\mathbb{A}}
-manifold
M
has a natural structure of a topological
{\mathbb{A}}
-manifold by forgetting the smooth structure.

Definition 3.19
.

A smooth map
f
from a smooth
{\mathbb{A}}
-manifold
M
to a smooth manifold without boundary
N
is called
stratified submersive
if for any (open) stratum
M_{\alpha}
, the restriction

f|_{M_{\alpha}}:M_{\alpha}\rightarrow N

is a smooth submersion.

Lemma 3.20
.

Suppose
f:M\to N
is stratified submersive. Then for any
y\in N
, the space
f^{-1}(y)
is a smooth
{\mathbb{A}}
-manifold. Moreover, if
G
is a compact Lie group acting on
M
and
f
is
G
-invariant, then
f^{-1}(y)
has a smooth
G
-action.

Proof.

By Definition
3.19
, the subsets
f^{-1}(y)\cap M_{\alpha}
are smooth manifolds. Using the collars near each stratum, we see that
f^{-1}(y)
is a smooth manifold with corners. We now verify that
f^{-1}(y)
is a manifold with faces, namely, each
x\in f^{-1}(y)
is contained in exactly
{\rm depth}(x)
connected faces. Indeed, viewing
x
as a point in
M
, by definition,
x
is contained in exactly
{\rm depth}(x)
connected faces of
M
. The implicit function theorem implies that
x
is contained locally in exactly
{\rm depth}(x)
faces and these faces are still disconnected globally in
f^{-1}(y)
. Hence
f^{-1}(y)
is a smooth manifold with faces.

Next, we see that the decomposition

f^{-1}(y)=\bigsqcup_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}}f^{-1}(y)\cap M_{\alpha}

induces an
{\mathbb{A}}
-space structure on
f^{-1}(y)
. Conditions of Definition
3.15
can then be verified using the implicit function theorem. Transversality implies that
f^{-1}(y)
has smooth corner charts. Hence
f^{-1}(y)
is a smooth
{\mathbb{A}}
-manifold. The equivariant case follows immediately from the definition.
∎

3.4.3.
Stratified smooth orbifolds with corners and derived orbifold charts

Smooth orbifolds with corners can be defined as in Section
2.1
, with the modification that for an
n
-dimensional orbifold with corners
{\mathcal{U}}
, each local chart
C=(U,{\mathit{\Gamma}},\psi)
has the following property: consider
{\mathbb{R}}^{n-k}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}^{k}
where
{\mathit{\Gamma}}
acts linearly on the first factor and trivially on the second factor. Then
U\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n-k}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}^{k}
is a
{\mathit{\Gamma}}
-invariant open subset and
\psi:U/{\mathit{\Gamma}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
is a homeomorphism onto an open subset of
{\mathcal{U}}
. Just as the manifold case, one can define the notion of smooth orbifold with faces and the notion of smooth
{\mathbb{A}}
-orbifolds for any homogeneous poset
{\mathbb{A}}
. We omit the discussions of bundles, sections, and embeddings in the stratified sense. Note that if
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}
is an
{\mathbb{A}}
-orbifold, the
\partial^{\alpha}{\mathbb{A}}
-space
\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}
is naturally a
\partial^{\alpha}{\mathbb{A}}
-orbifold.

Now we can generalize the definition of derived orbifold charts (see Definition
2.1
) to the case when the domains have corners or faces.

Definition 3.21

(Stratified derived orbifold charts)
.

(1)

An
{\mathbb{A}}
-stratified derived orbifold chart is a triple
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
, where
{\mathcal{U}}
is a smooth
{\mathbb{A}}
-orbifold,
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
is an orbifold vector bundle, and
{\mathcal{S}}:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
is a continuous section. We say that
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
is compact if
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
is compact.

(2)

Given
{\mathbb{A}}_{i}
-stratified derived orbifold charts
C_{i}=({\mathcal{U}}_{i},{\mathcal{E}}_{i},{\mathcal{S}}_{i})
,
i=1,2
, a chart embedding
{\bm{\iota}}_{21}:C_{1}\hookrightarrow C_{2}
consists of a smooth stratified embedding

\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{21}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{2}}

and a bundle embedding
\widehat{\iota}_{21}:{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
covering
\iota_{21}
which intertwines with the sections
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
.

(3)

Given an
{\mathbb{A}}
-space
X
, a
derived orbifold presentation
(or called a
D-chart presentation
) of
X
is a quadruple
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}},\psi)
consisting of an
{\mathbb{A}}
-stratified derived orbifold chart
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
with a homeomorphism of
{\mathbb{A}}
-spaces
\psi:{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)\to X
.

The following set of notations are frequently used. If
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}},\psi)
is a derived orbifold presentation of the
{\mathbb{A}}
-space
X
, for any stratum
\alpha
, the datum
(\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}},{\mathcal{S}}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}},\psi|_{{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)\cap\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}})
is a derived orbifold presentation of
\partial^{\alpha}X
. In practice, we might have a derived orbifold presentation of the boundary stratum
\partial^{\alpha}X
different from the restriction of a derived orbifold presentation of
X
. To carry out our construction of perturbations, we would like to relate the possibly different derived orbifold presentations of the boundary strata. This motivates the following definition, which should be thought of as a special case of the
Kuranishi diagram
from
[
Abo22
, Definition 6.6]
. Some notions appear in the following were defined in Definition
2.3
.

Definition 3.22
.

A
system of D-chart presentation
of an
{\mathbb{A}}
-space
X
consists of the following objects.

(1)

A collection of derived orbifold presentations

\big\{C_{\alpha}=({\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha},{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha},{\mathcal{S}}_{\alpha},\psi_{\alpha})\big\}_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}}

of the collection of boundary strata
\{\partial^{\alpha}X\}_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}}
as stratified spaces.

(2)

A collection of chart embeddings

\big\{{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}:C_{\alpha}\to\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}\big\}_{\alpha\leq\beta}.

These objects need to satisfy the following conditions.

(A)

The collection of chart embeddings satisfy the cocycle condition. More precisely, for any triple of strata
\alpha\leq\beta\leq\gamma
, there holds

\iota_{\gamma\beta}\circ\iota_{\beta\alpha}=\iota_{\gamma\alpha}.

(B)

Adjacent strata differ by a stabilization. More precisely, for any pair of strata
\alpha\leq\beta
, there exist an orbifold vector bundle
{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}
and a germ equivalence

{\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}}(C_{\alpha})\simeq\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}

In certain special cases the chart embeddings between different strata are open. For example in the case of Morse flow category and the pearly bimodule. We introduce the following notion.

Definition 3.23
.

A system of derived orbifold presentations of an
{\mathbb{A}}
-space
X
is called
single-layered
if all chart embeddings
{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}:C_{\alpha}\to\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}
are open embeddings, or equivalently, one can take
{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}=0
for all
\alpha\leq\beta
.

3.4.4.
Derived orbifold lifts of flow categories and bimodules

Now we can go back to the setup in Section
3.2
. The following definition imposes certain regular structures on morphism spaces of flow categories and flow bimodules.

Definition 3.24
.

Let
T^{\mathcal{P}}
be a flow category over the poset
{\mathcal{P}}
. A
derived orbifold lift
of
T^{\mathcal{P}}
, denoted by
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
, consists of the following objects.

(1)

A collection

\big\{C_{pq}=({\mathcal{U}}_{pq},{\mathcal{E}}_{pq},{\mathcal{S}}_{pq},\psi_{pq})\big\}_{p\leq q}

of derived orbifold presentations of the
{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}
-space
T_{pq}
such that for each connected component
{\mathcal{U}}_{pq,j}\subset{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
, one has

{\rm dim}_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq,j}-{\rm rank}_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{pq,j}}\equiv{\rm ind}^{\mathcal{P}}(p)-{\rm ind}^{\mathcal{P}}(q)-1\ {\rm mod}\ 2N.

(3.5)

(2)

A collection of chart embeddings

\big\{{\bm{\iota}}_{prq}:C_{pr}\times C_{rq}\hookrightarrow\partial^{prq}C_{pq}\big\}_{p\leq r\leq q}

(with the underlying poset identification
{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pr}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{rq}\cong\partial^{prq}{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}
). In particular, if
\iota_{prq}:{\mathcal{U}}_{pr}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{rq}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
is the associated domain embedding and
\widehat{\iota}_{prq}:{\mathcal{E}}_{pr}\boxplus{\mathcal{E}}_{rq}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}
is the associated bundle embedding, then the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{pr}\boxplus{\mathcal{E}}_{rq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\iota}_{prq}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}_{pr}\times{\mathcal{U}}_{rq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{prq}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{S}}_{pr}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{rq}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}}

(3.6)

These objects need to satisfy the following conditions.

(A)

For
p=q
, the space
{\mathcal{U}}_{pp}
is a singleton with trivial isotropy and
{\mathcal{E}}_{pp}=\{0\}
is the trivial bundle.

(B)

The chart embeddings satisfy the associativity. More precisely, whenever
p<r<s<q
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{C_{pr}\times C_{rs}\times C_{rq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\times{\bm{\iota}}_{rsq}}
\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}_{prs}\times{\rm id}}
\textstyle{\partial^{prs}C_{ps}\times C_{rq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}_{psq}}
\textstyle{C_{pr}\times\partial^{rsq}C_{rq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}_{prq}}
\textstyle{\partial^{prsq}C_{pq}}

(C)

For each
pq
and all
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}
, define

C_{\alpha}:=C_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times C_{r_{l}q}.

Then condition (B) implies that for each pair of elements
\alpha\leq\beta
in
{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}
, there is a well-defined chart embedding

{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}:C_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}.

Then we require that the collection
\{C_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}}
of derived orbifold presentations and the collection
\{{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}\}_{\alpha\leq\beta}
of chart embeddings constitute a system derived orbifold presentations of
T_{pq}
.

(D)

The strict
\Pi
-equivariance condition: for any
a\in\Pi
and
p<q
, there is an isomorphism between derived orbifold charts (in the obvious sense)
\tilde{\phi}_{a}:C_{pq}\to C_{a\cdot p\ a\cdot q}
satisfying
\tilde{\phi}_{a_{1}\cdot a_{2}}=\tilde{\phi}_{a_{1}}\circ\tilde{\phi}_{a_{2}}
, and
\tilde{\phi}_{a}
restricts to
\phi_{a}
along the zero locus
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{-1}(0)
to the map
\phi_{a}
from Definition
3.7
. Furthermore,
\tilde{\phi}_{0}={\rm Id}
for
a=0\in\Pi
should be the identity map.

Now consider derived orbifold lifts of bimodules.

Definition 3.25
.

Let
M
be a flow bimodule from a flow category
T^{\mathcal{P}}
to
T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
as in Definition
3.10
. Suppose
T^{\mathcal{P}}
resp.
T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
is endowed with a derived orbifold lift

{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}=\Big(\big\{C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}=({\mathcal{U}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq},{\mathcal{S}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq},\psi^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq})\big\}_{p<q},\big\{{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}^{\mathcal{P}}\big\}_{\alpha\leq\beta}\Big)\text{ resp. }

{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}=\Big(\big\{C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}=({\mathcal{U}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}},{\mathcal{E}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}},{\mathcal{S}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}},\psi^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}})\big\}_{p^{\prime}<q^{\prime}},\big\{{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}\big\}_{\alpha^{\prime}\leq\beta^{\prime}}\Big).

A
derived orbifold lift
of
M

compatible with (or extending)

{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
consists of the following objects.

(1)

A collection

\big\{C^{M}_{pp^{\prime}}=({\mathcal{U}}^{M}_{pp^{\prime}},{\mathcal{E}}^{M}_{pp^{\prime}},{\mathcal{S}}^{M}_{pp^{\prime}},\psi^{M}_{pp^{\prime}})\big\}_{p\in{\mathcal{P}},p^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}

of derived orbifold presentations of
M_{pp^{\prime}}
for
M_{pp^{\prime}}
as an
{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}
-space such that for each connected component
{\mathcal{U}}_{pp^{\prime},j}^{M}\subset{\mathcal{U}}_{pp^{\prime}}^{M}
, one has

{\rm dim}_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathcal{U}}_{pp^{\prime},j}^{M}-{\rm rank}_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathcal{E}}_{pp^{\prime}}^{M}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{pp^{\prime},j}^{M}}\equiv{\rm ind}^{\mathcal{P}}(p)-{\rm ind}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}(p^{\prime})\ {\rm mod}\ 2N.

(3.7)

(2)

Given
p\in{\mathcal{P}}
and
p^{\prime}\in{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
for
M_{pp^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset
, for any
p\leq q
, a chart embedding

{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pqp^{\prime}}:C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}\times C^{M}_{qp^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\partial^{pqp^{\prime}}C^{M}_{pp^{\prime}},

and for any
q^{\prime}\leq p^{\prime}
, a chart embedding

{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pq^{\prime}p^{\prime}}:C^{M}_{pq^{\prime}}\times C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\partial^{pq^{\prime}p^{\prime}}C^{M}_{pp^{\prime}}.

The precise meaning can be spelled out as in Equation (
3.6
).

These objects are required to satisfy the following conditions.

(A)

For
p=q
, the chart embedding
{\bm{\iota}}_{pqp^{\prime}}^{M}
is the identity map after identifying
C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}
with the trivial chart for the singleton. Similarly, for
q^{\prime}=p^{\prime}
, the chart embedding
{\bm{\iota}}_{pq^{\prime}p^{\prime}}^{M}
is also the identity map.

(B)

The chart embeddings satisfy the associativity. Namely, given
M_{pp^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset
, the following three diagrams commute if the relevant topological spaces are nonempty.

For
p<q_{1}<q_{2}
, we have

\textstyle{C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq_{1}}\times C^{\mathcal{P}}_{q_{1}q_{2}}\times C^{M}_{q_{2}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\times{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{q_{1}q_{2}p^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq_{1}q_{2}}\times{\rm id}}
\textstyle{\partial^{pq_{1}q_{2}}C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq_{2}}\times C^{M}_{q_{2}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pq_{2}p^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq_{1}}\times\partial^{q_{1}q_{2}p^{\prime}}C^{M}_{q_{1}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pq_{1}p^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\partial^{pq_{1}q_{2}p^{\prime}}C^{M}_{pp^{\prime}}.}

For
q_{2}^{\prime}<q_{1}^{\prime}<p^{\prime}
, we have

\textstyle{C^{M}_{pq_{2}^{\prime}}\times C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q_{2}^{\prime}q_{1}^{\prime}}\times C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\times{\bm{\iota}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q_{1}^{\prime}q_{2}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pq_{2}^{\prime}q_{1}^{\prime}}\times{\rm id}}
\textstyle{\partial^{pq_{2}^{\prime}q_{1}^{\prime}}C^{M}_{pq_{1}^{\prime}}\times C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pq_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{C^{M}_{pq_{2}^{\prime}}\times\partial^{q_{2}^{\prime}q_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q_{2}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pq_{2}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\partial^{pq_{2}^{\prime}q_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}C^{M}_{pp^{\prime}}.}

Finally, for
p<q
and
q^{\prime}<p^{\prime}
, we have

\textstyle{C^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{pq}\times C^{M}_{qq^{\prime}}\times C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm id}\times{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{qq^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pqq^{\prime}}\times{\rm id}}
\textstyle{\partial^{pqq^{\prime}}C^{M}_{pq^{\prime}}\times C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pq^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{C^{{\mathcal{P}}}_{pq}\times\partial^{qq^{\prime}p^{\prime}}C^{M}_{qp^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pqp^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\partial^{pqq^{\prime}p^{\prime}}C^{M}_{pp^{\prime}}.}

(C)

For each
p,p^{\prime}
and all
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}r_{l}^{\prime}\cdots r_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime}
, define

C_{\alpha}^{M}=C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times C^{M}_{r_{l}r_{l}^{\prime}}\times\cdots\times C^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{r_{1}^{\prime}p^{\prime}}.

As in Definition
3.24
, for each pair
\alpha\leq\beta
in
{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}
, there is a well-defined chart embedding

{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}^{M}:C_{\alpha}^{M}\hookrightarrow C_{\beta}^{M}.

It is required that the derived orbifold presentations
\{C_{\alpha}^{M}\}_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}}
and the collection of chart embeddings
\{{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}\}_{\alpha\leq\beta}
constitute a system of derived orbifold presentations of
M_{pp^{\prime}}
.

(D)

The strict
\Pi
-equivariance condition: for any
a\in\Pi
and
M_{pp^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset
, there is an isomorphism between derived orbifold charts
\tilde{\phi}^{M}_{a}:C^{M}_{pp^{\prime}}\to C^{M}_{a\cdot p\ a\cdot p^{\prime}}
satisfying
\tilde{\phi}^{M}_{a_{1}\cdot a_{2}}=\tilde{\phi}^{M}_{a_{1}}\circ\tilde{\phi}^{M}_{a_{2}}
, and
\tilde{\phi}^{M}_{a}
restricts to the map
\phi^{M}_{a}
from Definition
3.10
along the zero locus
({\mathcal{S}}^{M}_{pp^{\prime}})^{-1}(0)
. Moreover,
\tilde{\phi}^{M}_{id}
for
a=id\in\Pi
is the identity map. Moreover, the left and right actions of the charts from
T^{\mathcal{P}}
and
T^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}
on the charts of
M
should be
\Pi
-equivariant.

3.5.
Additional structures

In order to construct a coherent system of FOP perturbations, we need more structures on the derived orbifold lifts. They are the
collar structure
,
scaffolding
, and
straightening
.

3.5.1.
Collar structure

Definition 3.26
.

(1)

A
collar structure
on an
{\mathbb{A}}
-stratified derived orbifold chart
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
is a collection of open chart embeddings

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 90.45706pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}\ \ \forall\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}

(where
{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\subset{\mathbb{A}}
is the set of adjacent faces to
\alpha
, see Definition
3.2
and Notation
3.14
) covering the poset map

\partial^{\alpha}{\mathbb{A}}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{(|{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}|)}\to{\mathbb{A}}

satisfying the following conditions.

(a)

For a pair of stratum
\alpha\leq\beta
,
v\in{\mathcal{E}}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}}
,
t_{\beta}\in[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}
and
t_{\beta\alpha}\in[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}
(so that
(t_{\beta},t_{\beta\alpha})
is identified with an element of
[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}
), one has

\widehat{\theta}_{\alpha}^{\rm collar}(v,t_{\beta},t_{\beta\alpha})=\widehat{\theta}_{\beta}^{\rm collar}(\widehat{\theta}_{\alpha}^{\rm collar}(v,0,t_{\beta\alpha}),t_{\beta}).

(b)

For each
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}
,
x\in\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}
and
t_{\alpha}\in[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}
, one has

\widehat{\theta}_{\alpha}^{\rm collar}({\mathcal{S}}(x),t_{\alpha})={\mathcal{S}}(\theta_{\alpha}^{\rm collar}(x,t_{\alpha})).

(2)

A collar structure on a system of D-chart presentations
((C_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}},({\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha})_{\alpha\leq\beta})
of an
{\mathbb{A}}
-stratified space
X
(see Definition
3.22
) consists of collar structures on all derived orbifold charts
C_{\alpha}=({\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha},{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha},{\mathcal{S}}_{\alpha})
for all
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}
. Namely, for each
\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}
, a collection of open chart embedding

\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\theta}_{\beta\alpha}^{\rm collar}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{\beta\alpha}^{\rm collar}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}}

for all
\alpha<\beta
which satisfy conditions in item (1) above. We require the following condition is satisfied. For each triple
\alpha<\beta<\gamma

\widehat{\iota}_{\gamma\beta}\Big(\widehat{\theta}_{\beta\alpha}^{\rm collar}(v_{\beta},t_{\beta\alpha})\Big)=\widehat{\theta}_{\gamma\alpha}^{\rm collar}\Big(\widehat{\iota}_{\gamma\beta}(v_{\beta}),t_{\beta\alpha},0_{\gamma\beta}\Big)

for all
v_{\beta}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}}
and
t_{\beta\alpha}\in[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}
. Here
0_{\gamma\beta}=(0,\ldots,0)\in[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}}
and hence
(t_{\beta\alpha},0_{\gamma\beta})
is regarded as a point of
[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}}
.

(3)

A collar structure on a derived orbifold lift of a flow category (using the same notations as in Definition
3.24
), is a collection of collar structures on the chart
C_{pq}
such that the following is satisfied.

(a)

For each
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}
, the collar structures on
C_{pr_{1}},\ldots,C_{r_{l}q}
define a product collar structure on the product chart
C_{\alpha}
. Then the collection of collar structures on
C_{\alpha}
is a collar structure on the derived orbifold presentation of
T_{pq}
as defined in above item (2).

(b)

The collar structure is strictly
\Pi
-equivariant.

(4)

Suppose
{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
is a derived orbifold lift of a flow bimodule
M
from
T^{\mathcal{P}}
to
T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
which extends
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
. Suppose
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
are equipped with collar structures. Then a collar structure on
{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
which extends the collar structure on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
is a collection of collar structures on all the derived orbifold charts
({\mathcal{U}}_{pp^{\prime}}^{M},{\mathcal{E}}_{pp^{\prime}}^{M},{\mathcal{S}}_{pp^{\prime}}^{M},\psi_{pp^{\prime}}^{M})
of the form

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 47.13562pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-47.13562pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise collar}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 101.48285pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise \alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}

which satisfy similar compatibility conditions as the case of flow categories using the factorizations of boundary strata. We omit the details.

Remark 3.27
.

Although the description of collar structures seems to be very complicated, they are constructed using the outer-collaring method which implies the required conditions almost automatically.

3.5.2.
Scaffolding

Definition 3.28
.

A
scaffolding
of a system of D-chart presentations
((C_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}},({\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha})_{\alpha\leq\beta})
of an
{\mathbb{A}}
-space
X
is a collection of data

\big({\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha},{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha}\big)_{\alpha\leq\beta}

where for each pair
\alpha\leq\beta

(1)

the
difference bundle

{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}
is an orbifold vector bundle
{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}
. In notation, when
{\mathbb{A}}={\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}
resp.
{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}
and
\beta
is the maximal element
pq
resp.
pp^{\prime}
, denote
{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}
by
{\mathcal{F}}_{pq,\alpha}
resp.
{\mathcal{F}}_{pp^{\prime},\alpha}
.

(2)

the
stabilization map

{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha}
is a germ equivalence

{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha}=(\theta_{\beta\alpha},\widehat{\theta}_{\beta\alpha}):{\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}}(C_{\alpha})\simeq\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}

(3.8)

which extends the chart embedding
{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}:C_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}
. This germ equivalence induces a projection map

\pi_{\beta\alpha}:\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}\to C_{\alpha}

as well as a bundle splitting

{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}}={\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}\oplus{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}

(3.9)

where
{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}}={\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}
and a bundle isomorphism

\vartheta_{\beta\alpha}:\pi_{\beta\alpha}^{*}({\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}})\cong{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}}.

(3.10)

These objects need to satisfy the following conditions.

(A)

For any triple of stratum
\alpha\leq\beta\leq\gamma
, as subbundles of
{\mathcal{E}}_{\gamma}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}}
there holds

{\mathcal{F}}_{\gamma\alpha}={\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}\oplus{\mathcal{F}}_{\gamma\beta}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}}.

(3.11)

(B)

The stabilization map preserves stratum. More precisely, the following diagram commutes

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 31.00931pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-31.00931pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}}(C_{\alpha})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 33.59492pt\raise 6.4472pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.4139pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.00931pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 55.00931pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern It follows that

\pi_{\gamma\alpha}\circ(\iota_{\gamma\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}U_{\beta}})=\pi_{\beta\alpha}.
