(3.13)

Here the “
=
” arrow is induced from the identity
{\mathcal{F}}_{\gamma\alpha}={\mathcal{F}}_{\gamma\beta}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}}\oplus{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}
.

Now consider a derived orbifold lift of a flow category.

Definition 3.29
.

A
scaffolding
of a derived orbifold lift of a flow category
T^{\mathcal{P}}
consists of a collection of scaffoldings for the induced system of derived orbifold chart presentations of
T_{pq}

\Big(\big({\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha},{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha}\big)_{\alpha\leq\beta}\Big)_{p<q}

satisfying

(1)

Suppose
pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q=\alpha\leq\beta=ps_{1}\cdots s_{m}q
. Denote
\alpha_{0}=pr_{1}\cdots s_{1}
,
\ldots
,
\alpha_{m}=s_{m}\cdots r_{l}q
. Then as subbundles of
{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}}
, in view of the identification
{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}={\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha_{0}}\times\cdots\times{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha_{m}}
, one has

{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}={\mathcal{F}}_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus{\mathcal{F}}_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}.

(2)

With respect to the last identity, one has (as germs of maps)

{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha}={\bm{\theta}}_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}\times\cdots\times{\bm{\theta}}_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}.

Definition 3.30
.

Given two flow categories
T^{\mathcal{P}}
and
T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
endowed with derived orbifold lifts
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
respectively, let
M
be a flow bimodule from
T^{\mathcal{P}}
to
T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
endowed with a compatible derived orbifold lift
{\mathfrak{D}}^{M}
. Suppose
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
come with scaffoldings

\big(\big\{{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\beta\alpha},{\bm{\theta}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\beta\alpha}\big\}_{\alpha\leq\beta}\big)_{p<q},\text{ }\big(\big\{{\mathcal{F}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}},{\bm{\theta}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}\big\}_{\alpha^{\prime}\leq\beta^{\prime}}\big)_{p^{\prime}<q^{\prime}}.

A
scaffolding
of such a derived orbifold lift
compatible with
the given scaffoldings is given by a collection of scaffoldings for the induced system of derived orbifold chart presentations

\big(\big\{{\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{\beta}\tilde{\alpha}},{\bm{\theta}}_{\tilde{\beta}\tilde{\alpha}}\big\}_{\tilde{\alpha}\leq\tilde{\beta}}\big)_{\tilde{\alpha},\tilde{\beta}\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}^{M}}

satisfying similar conditions as in Definition
3.29
using the factorization of the boundary strata (see Definition
3.25
(C)).

3.5.3.
Straightening

As we will perform FOP perturbations over stratified charts, we need to consider straightenings in the stratified situation.

Definition 3.31

(Straightenings)
.

(1)

Let
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
be an
{\mathbb{A}}
-stratified derived orbifold chart. A
straightening
consists of a Riemannian metric on
{\mathcal{U}}
and a connection on
{\mathcal{E}}

such that for each
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}
, the restriction of the metric and the connection onto
{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}
(which is itself a smooth orbifold) is a straightening of
({\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha},{\mathcal{E}}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}},{\mathcal{S}}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}})
(see Definition
2.1
).

(2)

Consider a system of derived orbifold presentations on an
{\mathbb{A}}
-stratified space
X
. A
straightening
consists of a collection of straightenings of
C_{\alpha}
for all
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}
such that for each pair
\alpha\leq\beta
(with associated chart embedding
{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}=(\iota_{\beta\alpha},\widehat{\iota}_{\beta\alpha}):C_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}
), the domain embedding
\iota_{\beta\alpha}
is isometric and the bundle embedding preserves the connection, i.e., the image of the bundle embedding is preserved by the connection
\nabla^{{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}}
and the restriction of
\nabla^{{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}}
to the image of the bundle embedding coincides with
\nabla^{{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}}
.

(3)

Consider a derived orbifold lift
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
of a topological flow category
T^{\mathcal{P}}
over
{\mathcal{P}}
. A
straightening
on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
consists of a straightening on all charts
C_{pq}
satisfying the following condition. Fix
p<q
. For each
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
, there is a product straightening on the associated chart
C_{\alpha}=C_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times C_{r_{l}q}
. Then we require that the collection of these straightenings for all
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}
is a straightening on the system of derived orbifold presentations of
T_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}
.

(4)

It is straightforward to define the notion of straightenings on a derived orbifold lift of a flow bimodule
M
compatible with existing straightenings on the lifts of the two flow categories
T^{\mathcal{P}}
and
T^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}
with derived orbifold lifts
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}
. Namely, it consists of a straightening on all charts
C^{M}_{pp^{\prime}}
such that: for
\alpha=pq_{1}\cdots q_{k}q^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}\cdots q^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}
with
M_{\alpha}\neq\emptyset
, there is a product straightening on the product chart
C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq_{1}}\times\cdots\times C^{M}_{q_{k}q^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}}\times\cdots\times C^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}_{q^{\prime}_{1}p^{\prime}}
; we require that the collection of these straightenings for all
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}
is a straightening on the system of derived orbifold presentations of
M_{pp^{\prime}}
.

3.5.4.
Compatibility

We need the three kinds of additional structures (collar structure, scaffolding, and straightening) to be compatible in a certain sense. These compatibility conditions are necessary for the inductive construction of FOP perturbations. For example, once a perturbation is chosen on all boundary strata, it can be canonically extended to a neighborhood of the boundary using the collar structure; meanwhile, there is another automatic extension using the scaffolding. Hence a compatibility requirement between these two structures is necessary. We also need the automatic extensions remain in the class of FOP perturbations, which further requires that the straightening is compatible with the collar structure and the scaffolding.

Remark 3.32
.

In practice the three kinds of structures are constructed in different ways. The collar structure is constructed by the outer-collaring construction, which is very easy and straightforward. Then (see Subsection
5.8
) we construct scaffolding in the topological category. The scaffolding will play a role in the smoothing process. After smoothing, the topological charts become smooth. Then finally we construct the compatible straightenings.

Remark 3.33
.

In the following, we just define the notions of compatibility of a derived orbifold presentation. The relevant compatibility requirements for flow categories and flow bimodules are then reduced to the corresponding requirements for the induced derived orbifold presentations of all relevant topological spaces.

Definition 3.34
.

Consider a system of derived orbifold presentations
\{C_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}}
of an
{\mathbb{A}}
-stratified space
X
. Suppose it is equipped with a collar structure and a scaffolding. We say that they are compatible if the following conditions are satisfied.

(1)

Given a triple
\alpha<\beta<\gamma
, we require that the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{\gamma}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\gamma}}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}\times\{0\}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\theta}_{\gamma\alpha}^{\rm collar}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{\gamma}|_{\partial^{\beta}{\mathcal{U}}_{\gamma}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{\gamma}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}\times\{0\}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\theta}_{\gamma\alpha}^{\rm collar}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{\gamma}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{F}}_{\gamma\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}\times\{0\}^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\gamma}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\theta}_{\gamma\alpha}^{\rm collar}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{F}}_{\gamma\beta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Here the commutativity of the upper square is a requirement of the collar structure. Moreover, the bottom horizontal arrow uses the splitting (
3.9
) and
\widehat{\theta}_{\gamma\alpha}^{\rm collar}
is the restriction to the
{\mathcal{F}}_{\gamma\beta}
component.

(2)

The stablization maps also respect the collars. More precisely, for
\alpha<\beta<\gamma
, we require that

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 80.88812pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-80.88812pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Big({\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{\gamma\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}}}(\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta})\Big)\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 134.88812pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-25.83275pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 104.88812pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 134.88812pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{\gamma\beta}}(C_{\beta})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 165.14922pt\raise-25.75499pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-43.16508pt\raise-34.74501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\partial^{\alpha}C_{\gamma}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 83.47733pt\raise-27.51389pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.25891pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma\alpha}^{\rm collar}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 150.696pt\raise-34.74501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 104.88812pt\raise-34.74501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 150.696pt\raise-34.74501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\partial^{\beta}C_{\gamma}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Here the horizontal arrows are induced from the collar structure and the vertical arrows are restrictions of the open chart embeddings associated to the stabilization and scaffolding.

Finally we spell out the meaning of compatible straightenings.

Definition 3.35
.

Consider a system of D-chart presentations equipped with a collar structure and a scaffolding which are compatible. Then a straightening (see Definition
3.31
) is compatible with the collar structure and the scaffolding if the following conditions are satisfied.

(1)

For each pair
\alpha<\beta
, near
\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}
, using the corner coordinates induced from the map
\theta_{\beta\alpha}^{\rm collar}
, near
\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}
, one has

g_{\beta}=g_{\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}}+\sum_{i\in{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}dt_{i}^{2}.

(3.14)

Moreover, with respect to the bundle isomorphism
\widehat{\theta}_{\beta\alpha}^{\rm collar}
, near
\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}
one has

\nabla^{{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}}=\left(\widehat{\pi}_{\beta\alpha}^{\rm collar}\right)^{*}\nabla^{{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}}.

(2)

For each pair
\alpha\leq\beta
, the domain embedding

\iota_{\beta\alpha}:{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}

is isometric and totally geodesic. The normal bundle of this embedding has an induced metric and metric connection. Via the stabilization map
{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha}
which embeds
{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}
into
{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}
, the bundle
{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}
then carries a metric and metric connection.

(3)

The bundle embedding
{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}
preserves the connection.

(4)

The stabilization map on the domain is isometric.

(5)

Via the stabilization map (on the bundle), the connection on
{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}}
near the embedding image is the pullback connection, i.e., the direct sum of the pullback connections on
{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}
.

3.6.
Normal complex structure and orientations on flow categories

In order to carry out the FOP perturbations and define counts over
{\mathbb{Z}}
, we need to introduce normal complex structures on the derived orbifold lifts and certain orientation structures.

Definition 3.36
.

Let
T^{\mathcal{P}}
be a flow category over
{\mathcal{P}}
equipped with a derived orbifold lift
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
, with the collection of derived orbifold presentations

\big\{C_{pq}=({\mathcal{U}}_{pq},{\mathcal{E}}_{pq},{\mathcal{S}}_{pq},\psi_{pq})\big\}_{p\leq q}.

Moreover, assume that
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
has a scaffolding

{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}}=\Big(\big({\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha},{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha}\big)_{\alpha\leq\beta}\Big)_{p<q}.

Then a
normal complex structure
(or normally complex lift) on the pair
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}}
consists of the following data.

(1)

A normal complex structure on each of the derived orbifold chart
({\mathcal{U}}_{pq},{\mathcal{E}}_{pq},{\mathcal{S}}_{pq})
.

(2)

A complex structure on each of the orbifold vector bundles
{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}
.

(3)

For any pair
p,q\in{\mathcal{P}}
with
T^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}\neq\emptyset
and any pair of strata
\alpha\leq\beta
in
{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}
, the underlying map of the germ equivalence (
3.8
)

\theta_{\beta\alpha}:{\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}}({\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha})\simeq\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}

respects the normal complex structures.

(4)

Under the same assumptions as the previous item, the map

\widehat{\theta}_{\beta\alpha}:\pi^{*}_{\beta\alpha}{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}\oplus\pi^{*}_{\beta\alpha}{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}U_{\beta}}

intertwines with the normal complex structures.

(5)

The splitting of orbifold vector bundles (
3.9
) over
{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}
, the bundle isomorphism (
3.10
), and the splitting (
3.11
) respect the normal complex structures.

(6)

These (normal) complex structures admit a strict
\Pi
-action in the obvious sense.

Definition 3.37
.

Let
T^{\mathcal{P}}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
, and
{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}}
be as in Definition
3.36
. Suppose we are given a normal complex structure on the pair
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}}
. Then a collar structure compatibile with
{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}}
(see Definition
3.34
) is said to be compatible with the normal complex structure if the collar maps intertwine with the normal complex structures.

Remark 3.38
.

Note that the complex structure on
{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}
and the normal complex structure on
{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}
induce a normal complex structure on
{\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}}({\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha})
. Moreover, due to the behavior of orbifold charts near the boundary and corners,
\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}
indeed has a normal complex structure.

Remark 3.39
.

We could define the notion of normal complex structure in a more intrinsic way which does not rely on the choice of scaffoldings.

Remark 3.40
.

In our applications, the notion of normal complex structure is mostly useful for considering the space
{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}
, as the orbifold vector bundles
{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}
would have fiberwise complex structures in practice.

The normal complex structure is concerned with the tangential structure on the normal directions to strata specified by the isomorphism classes of the isotropy groups on an orbifold. In particular, an unorientable manifold has a (trivial) normal complex structure when being viewed as an orbifold. To define algebraic counts, we need to define orientation structures on the flow categories and flow bimodules, which is an extra structure beyond the normal complex structure.

Definition 3.41
.

Let
V
be a vector space over
{\mathbb{R}}
. The
orientation line

\mathfrak{o}_{V}
of
V
is defined to be the free
{\mathbb{Z}}
-module of rank
1
given by
H_{\dim V}(V,V\setminus\{0\};{\mathbb{Z}})
with
{\mathbb{Z}}/2
-grading the mod
2
reduction of
\dim V
. More generally, if
(V^{+},V^{-})
is a virtual vector space, its orientation line
\mathfrak{o}_{(V^{+},V^{-})}
is defined to be the tensor product of orientation lines
\mathfrak{o}_{V^{+}}\otimes\mathfrak{o}_{V^{-}}^{\vee}
.

Definition 3.42
.

An
{\mathbb{A}}
-stratified derived orbifold chart
C=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
is called
orientable
if both
{\mathcal{U}}
and
{\mathcal{E}}
are orientable. Given a connected component of an orientable derived orbifold chart, the
orientation line

\mathfrak{o}_{C}
of
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
is defined to be the orientation line of the virtual vector space

(T_{x}{\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}}_{x})

for arbitrary
x\in{\mathcal{U}}
lying in the top stratum of
{\mathcal{U}}
.

If
C=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
is an orientable
{\mathbb{A}}
-stratified derived orbifold chart, given an index
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}
, the
\partial^{\alpha}{\mathbb{A}}
-stratified derived orbifold chart
\partial^{\alpha}C=(\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}},{\mathcal{S}}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}})
is also orientable. Moreover, it is easy to see that there is a natural isomorphism

\mathfrak{o}_{\partial^{\alpha}C}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{o}_{C}\otimes(\mathfrak{o}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\vee})^{\otimes{\rm depth}(\alpha)}.

Definition 3.43
.

Suppose
T^{\mathcal{P}}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}}
are the same in Definition
3.36
An
orientation
of such a normally complex derived orbifold lift is given by:

(1)

A virtual vector space
(V_{p}^{+},V_{p}^{-})
for each
p\in{\mathcal{P}}
. Define
\mathfrak{o}_{p}:=\mathfrak{o}_{(V_{p}^{+},V_{p}^{-})}
.

(2)

An isomorphism of orientation lines

\mathfrak{o}_{C_{pq}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{o}_{p}^{\vee}\otimes\mathfrak{o}_{q}^{.}

(3.15)

(3)

For
p<r<q
, an isomorphism of orientation lines

\mathfrak{o}_{C_{pr}}\otimes\mathfrak{o}_{C_{rq}}\otimes(\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{F}_{prq,pq}}\otimes\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{F}_{prq,pq}}^{\vee})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{o}_{\partial^{prq}C_{pq}}.

(3.16)

(4)

The above isomorphisms are preserved by the
\Pi
-action.

Note that if the above derived orbifold lift is given an orientation, then for any
\alpha\leq\beta
in
{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm Floer}_{pq}
, there is an isomorphism

\mathfrak{o}_{C_{\alpha}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{o}_{\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}}.

Definition 3.44
.

Given a flow category
T^{\mathcal{P}}
endowed with a derived orbifold lift
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
, a scaffolding
{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}}
, and a compatible collar structure, if they are further endowed with a normal complex structure and orientation, we call such a datum an
oriented and normally complex derived orbifold lift
and abbreviate it as
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
.

If we have a flow bimodule
M
between
T^{\mathcal{P}}
and
T^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}
such that these three objects admit a compatible derived orbifold lift, as well as a compatible scaffolding structure, one can spell out the meaning of a normal complex structure and an orientation on
M
similarly to Definition
3.36
.

Definition 3.45
.

Let
T^{\mathcal{P}}
(resp.
T^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}
) be flow categories endowed with a derived orbifold lift
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}=\{C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}\}_{p\leq q}
(resp.
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}=\{C^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}\}_{p^{\prime}\leq q^{\prime}}
) and a scaffolding

{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}}=\Big(\big({\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}^{\mathcal{P}},{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha}\big)_{\alpha\leq\beta}\Big)_{p<q}\text{ resp. }{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}=\Big(\big({\mathcal{F}}_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}},{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}\big)_{\alpha^{\prime}\leq\beta^{\prime}}\Big)_{p^{\prime}<q^{\prime}}.

Moreover, both the pair
({\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}},{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}})
and the pair
({\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}},{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}})
have a normal complex structure in the sense of Definition
3.36
. Suppose
M
is a flow bimodule from
T^{\mathcal{P}}
to
T^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}
with a derived orbifold lift extending
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}
(Definition
3.25
)

{\mathfrak{D}}^{M}=\Big(\big\{C^{M}_{pp^{\prime}}=({\mathcal{U}}^{M}_{pp^{\prime}},{\mathcal{E}}^{M}_{pp^{\prime}},{\mathcal{S}}^{M}_{pp^{\prime}},\psi^{M}_{pp^{\prime}})\big\}_{p\in{\mathcal{P}},p^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}},{\bm{\iota}}^{M}_{pp^{\prime}}\Big)

and a scaffolding

{\mathfrak{F}}^{M}=\big(\big\{{\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{\beta}\tilde{\alpha}}^{M},{\bm{\theta}}_{\tilde{\beta}\tilde{\alpha}}^{M}\big\}_{\tilde{\alpha}\leq\tilde{\beta}}\big)_{\tilde{\alpha},\tilde{\beta}\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pp^{\prime}}^{M}}

compatible with
{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}
(Definition
3.30
). Then a
normal complex structure
(or normally complex lift) on this datum extending or compatible with the given ones on
({\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}},{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}})
and
({\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}},{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}})
consists of the following data.

(1)

A normal complex structure on each of the derived orbifold chart
({\mathcal{U}}^{M}_{pp^{\prime}},{\mathcal{E}}^{M}_{pp^{\prime}},{\mathcal{S}}^{M}_{pp^{\prime}})
.

(2)

A complex structure on the each of the orbifold vector bundles
{\mathcal{F}}_{\tilde{\beta}\tilde{\alpha}}^{M}\to{\mathcal{U}}^{M}_{\alpha}
.

(3)

The germ equivalence
\theta^{M}_{\tilde{\alpha},\tilde{\beta}}
and the bundle map covering it
\widehat{\theta}^{M}_{\tilde{\alpha},\tilde{\beta}}
all intertwine with the normal complex structures.

(4)

Splittings or isomorphism of the form (
3.9
), (
3.10
), and (
3.11
) all respect the (normal) complex structures.

(5)

The respective (normal) complex structures are identified under the
\Pi
-action.

Definition 3.46
.

Under the same setting as in Definition
3.45
, suppose we are given a compatible normal complex structure on the pair
{\mathfrak{D}}^{M}
and
{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}}
. Then a collar structure compatibile with
{\mathfrak{F}}^{M}
(see Definition
3.34
) is said to be compatible with the normal complex structure if the collar maps intertwine with the normal complex structures.

Definition 3.47
.

Suppose
(T^{\mathcal{P}},{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}},{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}})
,
(T^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}},{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}},{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}})
, and
(M,{\mathfrak{D}}^{M},{\mathfrak{F}}^{M})
be the same as in Definition
3.45
. Moreover, assume that
({\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}},{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}})
and
({\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}},{\mathfrak{F}}^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}})
are equipped with an orientation in the sense of Definition
3.43
. Then an
orientation
on the given normally complex lift of
{\mathfrak{D}}^{M}
and
{\mathfrak{F}}^{M}
is given by

(1)

An isomorphism of orientation lines

\mathfrak{o}_{C_{pp^{\prime}}^{M}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{o}_{p}^{\vee}\otimes\mathfrak{o}_{p^{\prime}}.

(3.17)

(2)

For
p<q
and
p^{\prime}
, an isomorphism of orientation lines

\mathfrak{o}_{C^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}}\otimes\mathfrak{o}_{C^{M}_{qp^{\prime}}}\otimes(\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{F}^{M}_{pqp^{\prime},pq}}\otimes\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{F}^{M}_{pqp^{\prime},pq}}^{\vee})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{o}_{\partial^{pqp^{\prime}}C_{pp^{\prime}}^{M}}.

(3.18)

(3)

For
p
and
q^{\prime}<p^{\prime}
, an isomorphism of orientation lines

\mathfrak{o}_{C^{M}_{pq^{\prime}}}\otimes\mathfrak{o}_{C^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}_{q^{\prime}p^{\prime}}}\otimes(\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{F}^{M}_{pq^{\prime}p^{\prime},pq}}\otimes\mathfrak{o}_{\mathcal{F}^{M}_{pq^{\prime}p^{\prime},pq}}^{\vee})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{o}_{\partial^{pq^{\prime}p^{\prime}}C_{pp^{\prime}}^{M}}.

(3.19)

(4)

The above isomorphisms are preserved by the
\Pi
-action.

For a flow bimodule
M
from
T^{\mathcal{P}}
to

as in Definition

(with

,
