and a compatible collar structure), if it is further endowed with a orientation structure, we say it has an
oriented and normally complex derived orbifold lift
and abbreviate as
{\mathfrak{D}}^{M}
.

3.7.
Constructing FOP perturbations

We extend the notion of FOP perturbations from the case of a single derived orbifold chart to the case of a derived orbifold lift of a flow category. The case of a flow bimodule is similar.

Definition 3.48

(Perturbations on a derived orbifold lift)
.

Given a derived orbifold lift
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
of a flow category
T^{\mathcal{P}}
(see Definition
3.24
).

(1)

A
perturbation
on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
consists of a system of smooth perturbations

{\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime}=\Big({\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}:{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}\Big)_{p<q}

of the compact derived orbifold chart
({\mathcal{U}}_{pq},{\mathcal{E}}_{pq},{\mathcal{S}}_{pq})
(see Definition
2.1
) such that the diagram (
3.6
) still commutes if we replace all
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}
by
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}
.

(2)

Suppose the derived orbifold lift is equipped with a scaffolding (see Definition
3.28
). Then we say that a perturbation
{\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime}

respects the scaffolding
if the following is true. For each pair of strata
\alpha\leq\beta
of
{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}
, the datum
{\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime}
induces a perturbation
{\mathcal{S}}_{\alpha}^{\prime}
of
C_{\alpha}
and a perturbation
{\mathcal{S}}_{\beta}^{\prime}
of
C_{\beta}
by taking the product. Then we require

{\mathcal{S}}_{\beta}^{\prime}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}}={\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}}({\mathcal{S}}_{\alpha}^{\prime}).

(3)

Suppose the derived orbifold lift is equipped with a collar structure. Then we say that a perturbation
{\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime}

respects the collar structure
if for each
pq
and each stratum
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}
, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{(\pi_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm collar})^{*}({\mathcal{E}}_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\vartheta_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm collar}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}}}
\textstyle{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\pi_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm collar})^{*}({\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}})}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm collar}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}}

(4)

Suppose the derived orbifold lift is normally complex and is equipped with a package of compatible structures (scaffolding, collar structure, and straightening). Then a perturbation
{\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime}
is called an
FOP perturbation
if respects the scaffolding, respects the collar structure, and for each
pq
, with respect to the straightening, the restriction of
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}
to the interior of each stratum
\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
is an FOP section of
{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}
.
8
8

8

Indeed being an FOP perturbation only needs to refer to the straightening and the normal complex structure.

(5)

Under the assumptions of the last item, an FOP perturbation is called
strongly transverse
if the restriction of each
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}
to the interior of each stratum of
{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
is strongly transverse (see Definition
2.7
).

Theorem 3.49
.

Given a flow category
T^{\mathcal{P}}
, a derived orbifold lift
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
with a normally complex structure, together with a package of compatible extra structures (scaffolding, collaring, and straightening), there exists a strongly transverse FOP perturbation. Moreover, we can make the perturbation
\Pi
-invariant, i.e.,

{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}={\mathcal{S}}_{a\cdot p\ a\cdot q}^{\prime}\ \forall a\in\Pi\ p,q\in{\mathcal{P}}

after identifying
C_{pq}
with
C_{a\cdot p\ a\cdot q}
.

Proof.

The construction is based on the same induction strategy as defining the Hamiltonian Floer homology (as well as continuation maps etc.) using abstract perturbations as in
[
FO99
]
and
[
LT98b
]
. The package of additional structures is necessary because the FOP perturbation scheme is more rigid than the traditional smooth or continuous multivalued perturbation scheme. We start with a pair
p<q
with minimal (nonzero) energy
{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}(q)-{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}(p)
. Then by definition,
{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}
is a poset with a single element and hence the chart
C_{pq}=({\mathcal{U}}_{pq},{\mathcal{E}}_{pq},{\mathcal{S}}_{pq})
is a derived orbifold chart with no boundary or corners. By the absolute version of Proposition
2.10
, with respect to the normal complex structure and the straightening, there exists a strongly transverse FOP perturbation
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}:{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}
which can be arbitrarily close (measured in
C^{0}
) to
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}
. In particular,
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}
has a compact zero locus. We can also make such perturbations
\Pi
-invariant.

Now for a given pair
p<q
, we state our induction hypothesis: for pair
r<s
with
p\leq r<s\leq q
and
(p,q)\neq(r,s)
, we have constructed a strongly transverse FOP section with respect to the straightening and normal complex structure

{\mathcal{S}}_{rs}^{\prime}:{\mathcal{U}}_{rs}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{rs}

satisfying the compatibility condition for a perturbation (with respect to the collar structure and scaffolding, see Definition
3.48
). We would like to construct a strongly transverse FOP perturbation
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}
which extends the existing ones and which still satisfies the compatibility conditions.

For each proper stratum
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
of
pq
, the product of
{\mathcal{S}}_{pr_{1}}^{\prime}
,
\ldots
,
{\mathcal{S}}_{r_{l}q}^{\prime}
provides a section

{\mathcal{S}}_{\alpha}^{\prime}:={\mathcal{S}}_{pr_{1}}^{\prime}\times\cdots\times{\mathcal{S}}_{r_{l}q}^{\prime}:{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha}.

Then via the stabilization map

{\bm{\theta}}_{pq,\alpha}:{\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{pq,\alpha}}(C_{\alpha})\simeq\partial^{\alpha}C_{pq}

one obtains a section

{\mathcal{S}}_{pq,\alpha}^{\prime}:\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}}

which is the stabilization of
{\mathcal{S}}_{\alpha}^{\prime}
. We check the following conditions.

(1)

The collection of
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq,\alpha}^{\prime}
agree on overlaps. Indeed, for each pair of stratum
\alpha\leq\beta
, by the compatibility condition (see item (2) of Definition
3.48
) satisfied by the existing perturbations (which is assumed as induction hypothesis), one has

{\mathcal{S}}_{\beta}^{\prime}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}}={\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}}({\mathcal{S}}_{\alpha}^{\prime}).

Then by the definition of scaffolding (see Definition
3.28
and (
3.13
)), one has

{\mathcal{S}}_{pq,\beta}^{\prime}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}}={\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{pq,\beta}}({\mathcal{S}}_{\beta}^{\prime}|_{\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{\beta}})={\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{pq,\beta}}({\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}}({\mathcal{S}}_{\alpha}^{\prime}))\\
={\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{pq,\alpha}}({\mathcal{S}}_{\alpha}^{\prime})={\mathcal{S}}_{pq,\alpha}^{\prime}.

(2)

The collection of
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq,\alpha}^{\prime}
respect the collar structure. This is a consequence of the induction hypothesis and the fact that the scaffolding respects the collar structure (see Definition
3.34
).

(3)

Each
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq,\alpha}^{\prime}
is an FOP perturbation. This is a consequence of the compatibility condition on the additional structures (see Definition
3.35
) which guarantees that the stabilization of an FOP section is still an FOP section.

(4)

Each
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq,\alpha}^{\prime}
is strongly transverse within the interior of
\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
. Notice that

{\rm Int}{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}={\rm Int}{\mathcal{U}}_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times{\rm Int}{\mathcal{U}}_{r_{l}q}.

Corollary
2.14
implies that the restriction of
{\mathcal{S}}_{\alpha}^{\prime}
to the interior of
{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}
is an FOP section. Moreover, as strong transversality is preserved under stabilization, we know that
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq,\alpha}^{\prime}
is strongly transverse within
{\rm Int}\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}={\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{pq,\alpha}}({\rm Int}{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha})
.

Therefore, we can extend the collection of
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq,\alpha}^{\prime}
to a neighborhood of
\partial{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
using the collar structure, simply by pulling back the existing perturbations using the projection maps. Hence we have obtained a section
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}
of
{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}
defined in an open neighborhood of
\partial{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
. The conditions on the collar structure imply that the extension is well-defined. The compatibility between straightenings and the collar structure implies that the extension is still an FOP section. As we can extend strongly transverse FOP sections in the standard “CUDV” fashion (see the relative version of Proposition
2.10
), one can construct a strongly FOP perturbation
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}
which extends the existing ones near the boundary. The compactness assumption on
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{-1}(0)
implies that one can make the perturbed zero locus
({\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime})^{-1}(0)
compact. The inductive construction can then be carried on. Moreover, the
\Pi
-equivariance of the perturbation can be maintained in the induction process.
∎

3.8.
Chain complexes and maps over the integers

We recall the notion of Novikov coefficient ring.

Definition 3.50
.

(1)

The integral
Novikov ring
is the ring of formal Laurent series in a single variable
T
with integer coefficients, i.e.

\Lambda:={\mathbb{Z}}[[T]][T^{-1}]=\Big\{{\mathfrak{x}}=\sum_{i=-m}^{\infty}a_{i}T^{i},\ {\rm where}\ m\in{\mathbb{Z}}\ {\rm and}\ a_{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}}\Big\}.

(2)

The
valuation
on
\Lambda
is the map

{\rm val}:\Lambda\to{\mathbb{Z}},\ {\rm val}(\sum a_{i}T^{i})={\rm min}\left\{i\ |\ a_{i}\neq 0\right\}.

Denote

\Lambda_{0}:=\{{\mathfrak{x}}\in\Lambda\ |\ {\rm val}({\mathfrak{x}})=0\}.

and

\Lambda_{+}:=\{{\mathfrak{x}}\in\Lambda\ |\ {\rm val}({\mathfrak{x}})>0\}.

3.8.1.
The chain complexes

Now we start to build the chain complexes and chain maps. First, associated to a poset
{\mathcal{P}}
as in Setup
3.5
, one can define a
{\mathbb{Z}}/2N
-graded free
\Lambda
-module as follows. Define

C_{*}^{\mathcal{P}}:=\left\{\sum_{p\in{\mathcal{P}}}m_{p}p\ |\ m_{p}\in{\mathbb{Z}},\ \forall c>0,\#\{p\ |\ m_{p}\neq 0,{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}(p)<c\}<\infty\right\}.

This is a free abelian group graded by the index function
{\rm ind}^{\mathcal{P}}:{\mathcal{P}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}/2N
. Define a
\Lambda
-module structure on
C_{*}
by

T^{a}\Big(\sum_{p\in{\mathcal{P}}}m_{p}p\Big):=\sum_{p\in{\mathcal{P}}}m_{p}(a\cdot p).

Conditions of Setup
3.5
implies that
C_{*}
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}/2N
-graded free
\Lambda
-module whose rank is equal to the cardinality of
{\mathcal{P}}/\Pi
.

Isolated zeroes in the free locus of each oriented derived orbifold chart induce integer counts. More precisely, fix a strongly transverse FOP perturbation

{\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime}:=\Big({\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}:{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}\Big)_{p<q}.

For each pair
p<q
, we can write

{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}=\bigsqcup_{i=-\infty}^{+\infty}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}^{[i]}

where
{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}^{[i]}\subset{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
is the open and closed subset of points whose local virtual dimension is
i
. It follows from (
3.5
) that

{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}^{[i]}\neq\emptyset\Longrightarrow i\equiv{\rm ind}^{\mathcal{P}}(p)-{\rm ind}^{\mathcal{P}}(q)-1\ {\rm mod}\ 2N.

Then let
n_{pq}
be the count (with signs) of zeroes of
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}
in the free locus of the zero-dimensional component
{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}^{[0]}
. The sign of an isolated zero point of
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime}
is uniquely determined by the sign read off from the isomorphism (
3.15
). The compactness assumption (see item (3) of Definition
3.7
) implies that
n_{pq}
is finite. Moreover, by the finiteness of
{\mathcal{P}}/\Pi
and the compactness condition, these counts define a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear map

d^{\mathcal{P}}:C_{*}^{\mathcal{P}}\to C_{*-1}^{\mathcal{P}}.

The
\Pi
-equivariance of the perturbation implies that

n_{pq}=n_{a\cdot p\ a\cdot q},\ \forall a\in{\mathbb{Z}}\ and\ p,q\in{\mathcal{P}}

which further implies that
d^{\mathcal{P}}
is
\Lambda
-linear. By looking at 1-dimensional components of all derived orbifold charts, using the coherence of the orientations (
3.16
), one can see that
d^{\mathcal{P}}
is a differential map, i.e.,
d^{\mathcal{P}}\circ d^{\mathcal{P}}=0
. Therefore, one obtains a chain complex

(C_{*}^{\mathcal{P}},d^{\mathcal{P}})

of
\Lambda
-modules. One hence obtains the homology

H_{*}^{\mathcal{P}}=\bigoplus_{i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/2N}H_{i}^{\mathcal{P}}

which has a natural
\Lambda
-module structure. One can show that the chain homotopy equivalence class of the chain complex does not depend on the choice of the perturbations. We omit the details because such a fact is not needed in our application.

3.8.2.
The chain maps

Now suppose we have two topological flow categories,
T^{\mathcal{P}}
over
{\mathcal{P}}
and
T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
over
{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
and a flow bimodule
M
from
T^{\mathcal{P}}
to
T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
.

Theorem 3.51
.

Assume the following conditions.

(1)

There is an oriented and normally complex derived orbifold lift
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
resp.
{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
of
T^{\mathcal{P}}
resp.
T^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
.

(2)

There is an oriented and normally complex derived orbifold lift
{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
of
M
which extends
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
.

(3)

On these derived orbifold lifts there exist compatible collar structures, scaffoldings, and straightenings.

Suppose we are given a strongly transverse FOP perturbation on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\mathcal{P}}
and a strongly transverse FOP perturbation on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
. Then there exists a strongly transverse FOP perturbation on
{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
which extends the existing ones which is
\Pi
-invariant.

Proof.

This theorem is essentially a relative version of Theorem
3.49
and the proof is the same as we can always extend strongly transverse FOP perturbations from local to global using the standard “CUDV” fashion (see the relative version of Proposition
2.10
).
∎

Now under the assumptions of Theorem
3.51
, the existing strongly transverse FOP perturbations induce chain complexes

\displaystyle\ (C_{*}^{\mathcal{P}},d^{\mathcal{P}}),\

\displaystyle\ (C_{*}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}},d^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}).

We would like to define a chain map using the extended FOP perturbations on
{\mathfrak{D}}_{{\mathcal{P}}{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
. Indeed, for
p\in{\mathcal{P}}
and
p^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
, consider the perturbation

{\mathcal{S}}_{pp^{\prime}}^{\prime}:{\mathcal{U}}_{pp^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{pp^{\prime}}.

Let
n_{pp^{\prime}}
be the count (with signs read off from the isomorphism between orientation lines (
3.17
)) of zeros of
{\mathcal{S}}_{pp^{\prime}}^{\prime}
in the free locus of all components of
{\mathcal{U}}_{pp^{\prime}}
with local virtual dimension zero. The compactness condition on
M_{pp^{\prime}}
(see Definition
3.10
) implies that
n_{pp^{\prime}}
is finite. Moreover, (
3.7
) implies that

n_{pp^{\prime}}\neq 0\Longrightarrow{\rm ind}^{\mathcal{P}}(p)\equiv{\rm ind}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}(p^{\prime})\ {\rm mod}\ 2N.

Then we formally define

\Psi^{M}:C_{*}^{\mathcal{P}}\to C_{*}^{{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}}

by linearly extending

\Psi^{M}(p)=\sum_{p^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}n_{pp^{\prime}}p^{\prime}.

We claim that the above is a legitimate element of
C_{*}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}
. Indeed, this is a consequence of the condition (
3.4
) of Definition
3.10
and the finiteness of
{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}/\Pi
. Hence
\Psi^{M}
is a well-defined morphisms of graded abelian group. Moreover, by the
\Pi
-equivariance of all structures, one has

n_{pp^{\prime}}=n_{a\cdot p\ n\cdot p^{\prime}},\ \forall a\in\Pi.

Hence
\Psi^{M}
is
\Lambda
-linear. Lastly, by the description of codimension one stratum of all bimodule moduli spaces and the coherence of orientations (
3.18
) and (
3.19
), similar to the case of
d^{\mathcal{P}}\circ d^{\mathcal{P}}=0
, one has that
\Psi^{M}
is a chain map, i.e.,

\Psi^{M}\circ d^{\mathcal{P}}=d^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}}\circ\Psi^{M}.

Remark 3.52
.

We would like to remark that the definitions of
d^{\mathcal{P}}
and
\Psi^{M}
from counting points in moduli spaces of virtual dimension
0
, and the usual proof for
(d^{\mathcal{P}})^{2}=0
and
d^{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}\circ\Psi^{M}=\Psi^{M}\circ d^{\mathcal{P}}
from counting the boundary points of moduli spaces of virtual dimension
1
works in our setting, because the pseudocycle condition on the free locus of
({\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime})^{-1}(0)
guarantees that the topological boundary
\partial(({\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{\prime})^{-1}(0))^{\rm free}
has codimension at least
2
so the desired compactness property, therefore the finiteness of algebraic counts holds.

4.
Proof of the integral Arnold conjecture

In this section, we define the topological flow category associated with a non-degenerate Hamiltonian and the topological flow category constructed from a Morse–Smale function. Then we define two bimodules between these two flow categories, which are respectively the space-level lifts of the well-known Piunikhin–Salamon–Schwarz map (the PSS map) and its inverse (the SSP map). We state in Theorem
4.7
the most important technical result in this paper, namely, the existence of derived orbifold lifts of the aforementioned objects, whose proof is contained in later sections. Finally, we explain how to use the perturbation scheme developed in Section
2
and Section
3
to prove Theorem
A
.

The following is the standing assumption of our discussions later on.

Hypothesis 4.1
.

(1)

The symplectic manifold
(M,\omega)
is integral, i.e., the de Rham cohomology class
[\omega]\in H_{dR}^{2}(M)\cong H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}})
lies in the image of
H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{Z}})\to H^{2}(M;{\mathbb{R}})
.

(2)

H
is a nondegenerate Hamiltonian on
(M,\omega)
.

(3)

All 1-periodic orbits of
H
are embedded and any two of them are disjoint.

(4)

The symplectic actions (
4.1
) of all capped 1-periodic orbits are integral.

(5)

J
is a time-independent
\omega
-compatible almost complex structure such that for all Floer trajectories with smooth domains (i.e., no sphere bubbles are attached and the trajectories are not broken)
u:\Theta\to X
, the linearized operator is surjective.

We explain why it suffices to prove the Arnold conjecture under these assumptions in order to establish it in full generality.

(1)

As we explained in the introduction of this paper, the case of Arnold conjecture for which
[\omega]
is rational implies the general case, and this is equivalent to the case when
[\omega]\in H^{2}(M;\mathbb{Z})
by suitably rescaling
\omega
and
H
using a common integer-valued factor.

(2)

Because any
C^{2}
-small perturbation of a nondegenerate Hamiltonian
H
will not change the number of periodic orbits, we can freely perturb
H
to guarantee that all
2\pi
-periodic orbits are nondegenerate.

(3)

As the 2-dimensional case of the integral Arnold conjecture is known, we can restrict ourselves to the case when
{\rm dim}M\geq 4
. Then one can slightly perturb
H
so that distinct periodic orbits do not intersect and all periodic orbits are embedded.

(4)

Given a nondegenerate Hamiltonian
H
(whose capped 1-periodic orbits are all discrete), by adding a
C^{2}
-small,
t
-independent function
f:M\to{\mathbb{R}}
whose restriction to a neighborhood of the image of each periodic orbit is a constant does not change the set of capped 1-periodic orbits but will shift their symplectic actions by constants. Then we can add a sufficiently small function
f
which makes the symplectic actions of all capped periodic orbits rational. Then the symplectic actions can be made integral by a further rescaling.

(5)

For any given
J
, one can slightly perturb
H
to achieve transversality for all smooth Floer trajectories. Moreover, the perturbed Hamiltonian can be chosen to agree with
H
up to second order on each 1-periodic orbits of
H
(see
[
FHS95
, Theorem 5.1]
).

4.1.
Hamiltonian Floer flow categories

4.1.1.
The moduli spaces of Floer trajectories

We first review the basics about the moduli spaces of stable Floer trajectories. Let
(M,\omega)
be a compact symplectic manifold and
H=(H_{t})_{t\in S^{1}}
be a smooth 1-periodic Hamiltonian on
M
. A
capped 1-periodic orbit
is a pair
p=([u],\underline{\smash{p}})
where
\underline{\smash{p}}:S^{1}\to M
is a 1-periodic orbit of the Hamiltonian vector field
X_{H_{t}}
and
[u]
is an equivalence class of maps
u:{\mathbb{D}}\to M
such that (we view
{\mathbb{D}}
as the unit disk in
{\mathbb{C}}
)

u(e^{2\pi{\bm{i}}t})=\underline{\smash{p}}(t);

two such maps
u_{1},u_{2}:{\mathbb{D}}\to M
are equivalent if

\int_{\mathbb{D}}u_{1}^{*}\omega=\int_{{\mathbb{D}}}u_{2}^{*}\omega.

Define the
symplectic action
of a capped orbit
p=([u],\underline{\smash{p}})
to be

{\mathcal{A}}_{H}(p)=\int_{\mathbb{D}}u^{*}\omega+\int_{S^{1}}H_{t}(\underline{\smash{p}}(t))dt.

(4.1)

We denote by
{\rm Per}(H)
the set of contractible 1-periodic orbits of
H
, whose elements are denoted by
\underline{\smash{p}},\underline{\smash{q}}
, etc. Denote by
\widetilde{{\rm Per}}(H)
the set of capped 1-periodic orbits of
H
, whose elements are denoted by
p,q
, etc. It follows from the integrality assumption on the symplectic class that
\widetilde{{\rm Per}}(H)
is a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-covering of
{\rm Per}(H)
.

Choose an
\omega
-compatible almost complex structure
J
on
M
. Let
\Theta={\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}
be the infinite cylinder with standard coordinates
z=s+{\bm{i}}t
. The Floer equation is the first-order equation for smooth maps
u:\Theta\to M

\frac{\partial u}{\partial s}+J\left(\frac{\partial u}{\partial t}-X_{H_{t}}(u)\right)=0.

(4.2)

The associated energy for a map
u:\Theta\to M
is defined to be

E_{H}(u):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Theta}\left(\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial s}\right|^{2}+\left|\frac{\partial u}{\partial t}-X_{H_{t}}(u)\right|^{2}\right)dsdt.

Any solution to (
4.2
) with finite energy necessarily converges as
s\to\pm\infty
to periodic orbits of
H
. Then one can use a pair of (capped) orbits to label solutions. Let
{\mathcal{M}}_{\underline{\smash{p}}\underline{\smash{q}}}^{\rm Floer}
be the set of solutions to (
4.2
) which converge to
\underline{\smash{p}}
resp.
\underline{\smash{q}}
as
s\to-\infty
resp.
s\to+\infty
, modulo the obvious time translation. Denote by

{\mathcal{M}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{\underline{\smash{p}}\underline{\smash{q}}}^{\rm Floer}

the subset of equivalence classes of solutions
u
such that the concatenation of
p
and
u
is equivalent to
q
as capped 1-periodic orbits. Then for any solution
u
to (
4.2
) representing an element of
{\mathcal{M}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
, it is standard that

E_{H}(u)={\mathcal{A}}_{H}(q)-{\mathcal{A}}_{H}(p).

(4.3)

As a result the Floer differential
increases
the symplectic action.

The moduli spaces in general are not compact with respect to the
C^{\infty}_{\rm loc}
-topology. Indeed, the space
{\mathcal{M}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
admits a natural compactification called the Gromov–Floer compactification, denoted by
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
, incorporating both bubbling of holomorphic spheres and breaking of Floer trajectories, see e.g.
[
FO99
, Section 18]
. It is standard knowledge that
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
is a compact Hausdorff topological space.
9
9

9

It is possible that
{\mathcal{M}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}=\emptyset
while
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}\neq\emptyset
.

4.1.2.
The flow category

We now package the collection of Floer moduli spaces into a flow category. First we see how this system of moduli spaces fits into Setup
3.5
. Let
N\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
be the minimal Chern number of
(M,\omega)
. The integral symplectic form
\omega
defines a homomorphism

\omega:\pi_{2}(M)\to{\mathbb{Z}}.

Define

\Pi:=\pi_{2}(M)/{\rm ker}\omega.

Then
\Pi
is an infinite cyclic group and
\omega
induces an injection

\omega:\Pi\to{\mathbb{Z}}

Then we define

{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}:=\widetilde{\rm Per}(H).

Define the partial order by the existence of nonempty Floer moduli spaces, i.e.,

p\leq q\Longrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}\neq\emptyset.

It has a free
\Pi
-action defined by taking the connected sum between a representative of a capped 1-periodic orbit and a representative of an element
a\in\Pi
. Define

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{A}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}}:={\mathcal{A}}_{H},\

\displaystyle\ {\rm ind}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}}:=\text{ Conley--Zehnder\ index}.

Then
{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}
satisfies conditions of Setup
3.5
.

Notation 4.2
.

As a convention, objects labelled by
{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}
are often also labelled by
{\rm Floer}
. For example, we abbreviate

\displaystyle\ {\mathcal{A}}^{\rm Floer}:={\mathcal{A}}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}},\

\displaystyle\ {\rm ind}^{\rm Floer}:={\rm ind}^{{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}}.

One can then describe the stratifications on Floer moduli spaces. As in Notation
3.6
, for each pair
p,q\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}
, one has the homogeneous poset

{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}:=\big\{\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\ |\ p<r_{1}<\cdots<r_{l}<q\big\}.

Then the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
an
{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
-stratified topological space. Indeed, for each
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
, one has

\partial^{\alpha}\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}\cong\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pr_{1}}^{\rm Floer}\times\cdots\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{r_{l}q}^{\rm Floer}

as the
\alpha
-stratum is the subset of “broken trajectories” of a type described by
\alpha
.

Definition 4.3
.

The
Hamiltonian Floer flow category
(associated to
H
and
J
), denoted by
T^{\rm Floer}
, is the topological flow category over
{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}
whose morphism space between
p,q\in\widetilde{{\rm Per}}(H)
is the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
, and whose composition maps are the natural inclusions

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pr}^{\rm Floer}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{rq}^{\rm Floer}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer},\ \forall p\leq r\leq q.

4.2.
Morse flow category

We describe the definition of the more classical Morse flow category and explain how it fits into the general framework of this paper. We declare that in this paper Morse flows are always the ascending flow, i.e., the flow generated by the gradient vector field. Suppose
(f,g)
is a Morse–Smale pair on
M
, namely,
f
is a Morse function and
g
is a Riemannian metric such that the unstable manifold of any critical point intersects transversely with any stable manifold. We use
\underline{\smash{x}},\underline{\smash{y}}
to denote the critical points of
f
. To ensure the moduli spaces of (unparametrized) gradient flow lines to have smooth structures, we assume that near each
\underline{\smash{x}}\in{\rm crit}(f)
, there exists a coordinate chart
(x_{1},\dots,x_{2n})
such that
f=\pm x_{1}^{2}\pm\cdots\pm x_{2n}^{2}
and
g=dx_{1}\otimes dx_{1}+\cdots dx_{2n}\otimes dx_{2n}
. Then by
[
Weh12
]
, for any pair of cricial points
\underline{\smash{x}},\underline{\smash{y}}\in{\rm crit}(f)
, the moduli space

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}^{\rm Morse}_{\underline{\smash{x}}\underline{\smash{y}}}

of unparametrized broken flow lines connecting
x
and
y
is a smooth manifold with faces. It is standard knowledge that upon choosing orientations on the unstable manifolds of all critical points, one can count rigid Morse flow lines (with signs) and define a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded chain complex over
{\mathbb{Z}}
and its homology coincides with
H_{*}(M;{\mathbb{Z}})
.

We want to fit the Morse flow category into the abstract framework. Define

{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse}:=\Pi\times{\rm crit}(f)

with a partial order defined by

(a,\underline{\smash{x}})\leq(b,\underline{\smash{y}})\Longleftrightarrow a=b\ {\rm and}\ \overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\underline{\smash{x}}\underline{\smash{y}}}^{\rm Morse}\neq\emptyset.

\Pi
naturally acts freely on
{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse}
. The action function is defined by

{\mathcal{A}}^{\rm Morse}(a,\underline{\smash{x}})=\omega(a)+\epsilon f(\underline{\smash{x}})

where
\epsilon
is a sufficiently small positive number. The index function is defined by

{\rm ind}^{\rm Morse}(a,\underline{\smash{x}})\equiv\frac{1}{2}{\rm dim}M-{\rm Morse\ index\ of\ }\underline{\smash{x}}\ {\rm mod}\ 2N.

One can easily check that the triple
({\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse},{\mathcal{A}}^{\rm Morse},{\rm ind}^{\rm Morse})
satisfies conditions of Setup
3.5
. Then following Notation
3.6
one obtains a collection of homogeneous posets
{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm Morse}
for all
x,y\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse}
. Define

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy}^{\rm Morse}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\underline{\smash{x}}\underline{\smash{y}}}^{\rm Morse},&\ {\rm if}\ x=(a,\underline{\smash{x}})\ {\rm and}\ y=(a,\underline{\smash{y}}),\\
\emptyset,&\ {\rm otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Then each
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy}^{\rm Morse}
is stratified by the poset
{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm Morse}
. We can then define the flow category
T^{\rm Morse}:=T^{\rm Morse}(f,g)
associated with
(f,g)
as follows.

Definition 4.4
.

The
Morse flow category

T^{\rm Morse}
is the topological flow category over
{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse}
whose morphism spaces are
T_{xy}^{\rm Morse}=\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy}^{\rm Morse}
and whose composition maps are the natural inclusions

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xz}^{\rm Morse}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{zy}^{\rm Morse}\cong\partial^{xzy}\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy}^{\rm Morse}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy}^{\rm Morse}.

It is obvious that
T^{\rm Morse}
is a strict
\Pi
-equivariant flow category.

As transversality is already achieved for the Morse flow category and there is no orbifold behavior, one does not need to use derived orbifold lift nor normal complex structures to define the resulting chain complex. However we would like to formally put this case into the general framework of the previous section as it will be necessary when we connect the Morse flow category to objects where transversality fails and orbifold behavior appears.

First, following the abstract outer-collaring recipe, one can construct an outer-collaring of the Morse flow category. Fix the width
r=1
. The outer-collared Morse flow category
(T^{{\rm Morse}})^{+}
has morphism spaces being
(\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy}^{{\rm Morse}})^{+}
. A priori this is only a topological flow category. However, one can equip the morphism spaces and composition maps with smooth structure. Indeed, by
[
Weh12
, Theorem 1.4]
which proves the associativity of gluing maps for the special kind of Morse–Smale pair
(f,g)
(see also
[
Qin18
]
), the original Morse flow category has compatible “interior” collars. Then the outer-collaring construction does not alter the feature and put a smooth structure on each space
(\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy}^{\rm Morse})^{+}
such that the composition maps are smooth.

Second, as transversality is already achieved and the moduli spaces are manifolds but not orbifolds, the collection of outer-collared moduli spaces together with the zero obstruction bundle and the zero Kuranishi map form a derived orbifold lift of
(T^{\rm Morse})^{+}
, denoted by
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Morse}
. The outer-collaring provides a collar structure on this lift. There is also the trivial scaffolding and the trivial straightening, which are obviously compatible with the collar structure. In the manifold case, there is only the trivial normal complex structure. Lastly, it is a classical knowledge that upon choosing orientations on all unstable manifolds the Morse moduli spaces inherit coherent orientations. Here the orientation line
{\mathfrak{o}}_{x}
associated with a capped critical point
x=(a,\underline{\smash{x}})
is defined to be the orientation line of the tangent space of the stable submanifold of
\nabla_{g}f
at
\underline{\smash{x}}
. We summarize these observations as follows.

Lemma 4.5
.

A choice of orientations on all unstable manifolds of
\nabla^{g}f
makes
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Morse}
an oriented and normally complex drived orbifold lift of
(T^{\rm Morse})^{+}
equipped with a compatible package of additional structures. Moreover, the
0
-perturbation is a strongly transverse FOP perturbation on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Morse}
which defines the
{\mathbb{Z}}/2N
-graded chain complex
(C_{*}(f;\Lambda),d^{\rm Morse})
of
\Lambda
-modules, whose homology is isomorphic to the
\Lambda
-module

H^{(2N)}_{n-*}(M;\Lambda)=\bigoplus_{i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/2N}H^{(2N)}_{n-i}(M;{\mathbb{Z}})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Lambda.

Here

H_{i}^{(2N)}(M;{\mathbb{Z}}):=\bigoplus_{j\equiv i\ {\rm mod}\ 2N}H_{j}(M;{\mathbb{Z}}).

4.3.
PSS and SSP bimodules

4.3.1.
Moduli spaces

Now we describe the moduli spaces which allow us to interpolate between Floer theory and Morse theory. We first set up a convention: PSS moduli spaces are defined by objects with input from Morse critical points and output from 1-periodic (capped) Hamiltonian orbits and SSP moduli spaces parametrizes objects in the reversed direction.

We only describe PSS moduli spaces in detail. The case of SSP moduli spaces is similar. To define the equation and hence the moduli space, we make the following choices.

(1)

An integer
C^{\rm PSS}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
satisfying

\inf_{(t,x)\in S^{1}\times M}H_{t}(x)>C^{\rm PSS}.

(4.4)

(2)

A smooth,
non-decreasing
cut-off function
\rho:{\mathbb{R}}\to[0,1]
such that

\rho(s)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}0,&\ s\leq 0,\\
1,&\ s\geq 1.\end{array}\right.

(4.5)

Define a
2
-parameter family of functions

H_{s,t}^{{\rm PSS}}(x)=(1-\rho(s))C^{\rm PSS}+\rho(s)H_{t}(x).

Then over the cylinder
\Theta
one has the vector-field-valued
1
-form

X_{H}^{\rm PSS}\otimes dt

which is defined by the Hamiltonian vector field of
H_{s,t}^{{\rm PSS}}(x)
.
Note that it vanishes for
s\leq 0
. The PSS equation is

\frac{\partial u}{\partial s}+J\left(\frac{\partial u}{\partial t}-X_{H}^{\rm PSS}(u)\right)=0,\ {\rm where}\ u\in C^{\infty}(\Theta,M).

(4.6)

The energy of a solution is defined to be and is computed as

\begin{split}E^{\rm PSS}(u):=&\ \int_{\Theta}\|\partial_{s}u\|^{2}dsdt\\
=&\ \int_{\Theta}\omega(\partial_{s}u,\partial_{t}u-X_{H}^{\rm PSS}(u))dsdt\\
=&\ \int_{\Theta}u^{*}\omega+\int_{\Theta}dH_{s,t}^{\rm PSS}(\partial_{s}u)dsdt\\
=&\ \int_{\Theta}u^{*}\omega+\int_{\Theta}\frac{\partial}{\partial s}(H_{s,t}^{\rm PSS}(u))dsdt-\int_{\Theta}\frac{\partial H^{\rm PSS}_{s,t}}{\partial s}dsdt.\end{split}

If the energy is finite, then by Gromov’s removal of singularity theorem,
u
extends to a
J
-holomorphic map near
-\infty
and hence automatically determines a cap for the periodic orbit at
+\infty
. Hence one can use a capped 1-periodic orbit
p\in\widetilde{{\rm Per}}(H)
to label the moduli space of PSS solutions. It is similar to the case of Floer trajectories that
{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\bullet p}^{\rm PSS}
admits a natural compactification consisting of “stable PSS trajectories,” i.e. configurations with both cylindrical and spherical components. Denote the compactification by

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\bullet p}^{\rm PSS}.

(4.7)

For each map
u
representing an element of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\bullet p}^{\rm PSS}
, one has the following energy identity

E^{\rm PSS}(u)={\mathcal{A}}_{H}(p)-C^{\rm PSS}-\int_{\Theta}\rho^{\prime}(s)(H(u)-C^{\rm PSS})dsdt.

An important consequence of (
4.4
) is that

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\bullet p}^{\rm PSS}\neq\emptyset\Longrightarrow{\mathcal{A}}_{H}(p)-C^{\rm PSS}>0.

(4.8)

We introduce

d_{\bullet p}:={\mathcal{A}}_{H}(p)-C^{\rm PSS}

and call it the
topological energy
of a solution
u
.

Now we bring in the Morse theory of a Morse–Smale pair
(f,g)
. Given
\underline{\smash{x}}\in{\rm crit}(f)
, consider the unstable manifold
W^{u}(\underline{\smash{x}})\subset M
of the flow of
\nabla^{g}f
and its compactification
\overline{W^{u}(\underline{\smash{x}})}
. Then define

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\underline{\smash{x}}p}^{\rm PSS}:={\rm ev}_{\bullet}^{-1}(\overline{W^{u}(\underline{\smash{x}})}),

where
{\rm ev}_{\bullet}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\bullet p}^{\rm PSS}\to M
is the evaluation map at
-\infty
.
Moreover, if
x=(a,\underline{\smash{x}})\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse}
and
p\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}
, then define

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xp}^{\rm PSS}:=\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\underline{\smash{x}}\ (-a)\cdot p}^{\rm PSS}.

The stratifications on the PSS moduli spaces fit into the general framework of bimodules described in the previous section. Recall that to
x\in{\mathcal{P}}={\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse}
and
p\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}
one associates a homogeneous poset

{\mathbb{A}}_{xp}:={\mathbb{A}}_{xp}^{\rm PSS}:=\{xy_{1}\cdots y_{k}q_{l}\cdots q_{1}p\ |\ x<y_{1}<\cdots<y_{k},q_{l}<\cdots<q_{1}<p\}

which has the natural partial order induced from inclusion and the depth function

{\rm depth}(xy_{1}\cdots y_{k}q_{l}\cdots q_{1}p)=k+l.

The PSS moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xp}^{\rm PSS}
is stratified by
{\mathbb{A}}_{xp}^{\rm PSS}
with corresponding strata

\left(\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xp}^{\rm PSS}\right)_{xy_{1}\cdots y_{k}q_{l}\cdots q_{1}}\cong\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy_{1}}^{\rm Morse}\times\cdots\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{y_{k-1}y_{k}}^{\rm Morse}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{y_{k}q_{l}}^{\rm PSS}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{q_{l}q_{l-1}}^{\rm Floer}\times\cdots\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{q_{1}p}^{\rm Floer}

i.e., the subset of configurations with breakings described by the word
xy_{1}\cdots y_{k}q_{l}\cdots q_{1}p
. Gromov compactness shows that there are at most finitely many nonempty stratum.

To define the SSP moduli spaces, let us choose an integer
C^{\rm SSP}\in{\mathbb{Z}}
such that

\sup_{(t,x)\in S^{1}\times M}H_{t}(x)<C^{\rm SSP}.

(4.9)

Using the same cut-off function as in (
4.5
), we can write down another
2
-parameter family of functions

H_{s,t}^{{\rm SSP}}(x)=(1-\rho(s))H_{t}(x)+\rho(s)C^{{\rm SSP}}.

(4.10)

Denote the associated vector-field-valued
1
-form on
\Theta
by
X_{H}^{{\rm SSP}}\otimes dt
, then the SSP equation is written as

\frac{\partial u}{\partial s}+J\left(\frac{\partial u}{\partial t}-X_{H}^{\rm SSP}(u)\right)=0,\ {\rm where}\ u\in C^{\infty}(\Theta,M).

Because
X_{H}^{{\rm SSP}}dt
vanishes for
s\geq 0
, any solution
u
extends to a
J
-holomorphic map near
+\infty
, which determines a cap for the periodic orbit at
-\infty
. Given
p\in\widetilde{{\rm Per}}(H)
, we cam similarly consider “stable SSP trajectories” which constitute a compact moduli space

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{p\bullet}^{{\rm SSP}}.

For any representative
u
of a point in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{p\bullet}^{{\rm SSP}}
, its topological energy is defined to be

d_{p\bullet}:=C^{\rm SSP}-\mathcal{A}_{H}(p).

Due to the choice (
4.9
), we see that
d_{p\bullet}>0
as long as
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{p\bullet}^{{\rm SSP}}\neq\emptyset
. Now suppose
\underline{\smash{x}}\in{\rm crit}(f)
, let
W^{s}(\underline{\smash{x}})\subset M
be the stable submanifold of
\nabla^{g}f
and denote by
\overline{W^{s}(\underline{\smash{x}})}
its compactification. Introduce the moduli space

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{{\rm SSP}}_{p\underline{\smash{x}}}:={\rm ev}_{\bullet}^{-1}(\overline{W^{s}(\underline{\smash{x}})}),

where
{\rm ev}_{\bullet}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{p\bullet}^{{\rm SSP}}\to M
is the evaluation map at
+\infty
. If
x=(a,\underline{\smash{x}})\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse}
and
p\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}
, then define

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{px}^{\rm SSP}:=\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{a\cdot p\ \underline{\smash{x}}}^{\rm SSP}.

Similar to the PSS case, the homogeneous posets associated with SSP spaces are denoted by

{\mathbb{A}}_{px}^{\rm SSP}:=\{pq_{1}\cdots q_{l}y_{k}\cdots y_{1}x|\ p<q_{1}<\cdots<q_{l},y_{k}<\cdots<y_{1}<x\}

for
p\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}
and
x\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse}
, endowed with depth function

{\rm depth}(pq_{1}\cdots q_{l}y_{k}\cdots y_{1}x)=k+l.

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{px}^{\rm SSP}
is stratified by
{\mathbb{A}}_{px}^{\rm SSP}
with corresponding strata

\left(\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{px}^{\rm SSP}\right)_{pq_{1}\cdots q_{l}y_{k}\cdots y_{1}x}\cong\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq_{1}}^{\rm Floer}\times\cdots\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{q_{l-1}q_{l}}^{\rm Floer}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{q_{l}y_{k}}^{\rm SSP}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{y_{k}y_{k-1}}^{\rm Morse}\times\cdots\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{y_{1}x}^{\rm Morse}.

4.3.2.
The bimodule structure

The PSS and SSP moduli spaces can be packaged into two flow bimodules (see Definition
3.10
). Recall that one has the Floer flow category
T^{\rm Floer}
and the Morse flow category
T^{\rm Morse}
. Essentially by the way we compactify the PSS resp. SSP moduli spaces we see that one can define a flow bimodule
M^{\rm PSS}
from
T^{\rm Morse}
to
T^{\rm Floer}
and a flow bimodule
M^{\rm SSP}
from
T^{\rm Floer}
to
T^{\rm Morse}
. We explain the specific terms for the PSS bimodule; the SSP case is completely symmetric.

Proposition 4.6
.

For
x\in{\mathcal{P}}^{{\rm Morse}}
and
p\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}
, define
M^{{\rm PSS}}_{xp}:=\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xp}^{\rm PSS}
. Then together with the natural inclusion of the boundary strata

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}^{{\rm Morse}}_{xy}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{yp}^{\rm PSS}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xp}^{\rm PSS},

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xq}^{\rm PSS}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{qp}^{\rm Floer}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xp}^{\rm PSS},

the spaces
M^{{\rm PSS}}_{xp}
define a flow bimodule from
T^{\rm Morse}
to
T^{\rm Floer}
.

Proof.

For any
x\in{\mathcal{P}}^{{\rm Morse}}
and
p\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse}
the moduli space
M^{{\rm PSS}}_{xp}
is compact due to Gromov compactness and the bound on the topological energy. For such
x
and
p
, indeed the difference of energies
{\mathcal{A}}^{\rm Morse}(x)-{\mathcal{A}}^{\rm Floer}(p)
is uniformly bounded. The associaticity of inclusions of boundary strata follows from the construction, so is the strict
\Pi
-equivariance property.
∎

We refer to such a bimodule as the
PSS bimodule
, and the version for the SSP spaces as
SSP bimodule
, denoted by
M^{\rm PSS}
and
M^{\rm SSP}
respectively.

4.4.
The pearly bimodule

We consider the moduli space of (parametrized)
J
-holomorphic maps
u:\mathbb{CP}^{1}\to M
with two marked points
z_{-}=0
and
z_{+}=\infty
. Given a homology class
A\in H_{2}(M;{\mathbb{Z}})
, let
{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}^{\rm pearl}(M,J;A)
be the moduli space of (parametrized)
J
-holomorphic maps representing the class
A
whose domains are smooth. It has a Gromov compactification
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2}^{\rm pearl}(M,J;A)
. Each element of this compactification is represented by a stable map whose domain has a distinguished component whose parametrization is fixed. Moreover, by intersecting with the unstable manifold of
\underline{\smash{x}}\in{\rm crit}f
at
z_{-}
and with the stable manifold of
\underline{\smash{y}}\in{\rm crit}f
at
z_{+}
, we have a moduli space

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\underline{\smash{x}}\underline{\smash{y}}}^{\rm pearl}(M,J;A)

For
a\in\Pi
, define

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\underline{\smash{x}}\underline{\smash{y}}}^{\rm pearl}(a):=\bigcup_{\omega(A)=\omega(a)}\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\underline{\smash{x}}\underline{\smash{y}}}^{\rm pearl}(M,J;A).

Then given
x,y\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse}
which can be written as
(a,\underline{\smash{x}})
and
(b,\underline{\smash{y}})
where
a,b\in\Pi
and
\underline{\smash{x}},\underline{\smash{y}}\in{\rm crit}f
, define

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy}^{\rm pearl}:=\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\underline{\smash{x}}\underline{\smash{y}}}^{\rm pearl}(b-a).

The pearly moduli spaces are stratified by configurations which have broken Morse trajectories either on the incoming edge or on the out-going edge. Indeed, for
{\mathcal{P}}={\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse}
, as in Notation
3.6
there is a system of homogeneous posets indexed by pairs of
x,y\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse}
. More explicitly, given
x,y\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse}
, define

{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm pearl}:=\left\{xx_{1}\cdots x_{k}y_{l}\cdots y_{1}y\ \left|\ \begin{array}[]{c}x_{i}=(a,\underline{\smash{x}}_{i}),\ y_{j}=(b,\underline{\smash{y}}_{j}),\\
\underline{\smash{x}}<\underline{\smash{x}}_{1}<\cdots<\underline{\smash{x}}_{k},\ \underline{\smash{y}}_{l}<\cdots<\underline{\smash{y}}_{1}<\underline{\smash{y}}\end{array}\right.\right\}.

Note that all the
x_{i}
’s resp.
y_{j}
’s are marked with the same class
a\in\Pi
resp.
b\in\Pi
, because otherwise, the morphism space
T^{\rm Morse}_{x_{i}x_{i+1}}
or
T^{\rm Morse}_{y_{j}y_{j+1}}
is empty by the construction in Section
4.2
. Given
\alpha=xx_{1}\cdots x_{k}y_{l}\cdots y_{1}y\in{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm pearl}
, the corresponding stratum in the pearly moduli space is

\Big(\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy}^{\rm pearl}\Big)_{\alpha}\cong\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xx_{1}}^{\rm Morse}\times\cdots\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{x_{k-1}x_{k}}^{\rm Morse}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{x_{k}y_{l}}^{\rm pearl}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{y_{l}y_{l-1}}^{\rm Morse}\times\cdots\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{y_{1}y}^{\rm Morse}.

There are again only finitely many nonempty strata, thanks to Gromov compactness. Using the formulation of flow bimodules, we can see that the collection of pearly moduli spaces and the product structures of various strata provide a flow bimodule from
T^{\rm Morse}
to
T^{\rm Morse}
, which we call the
pearly bimodule
and denote it by
M^{\rm pearl}
. The strict
\Pi
-action on
M^{\rm pearl}
follows from the construction. The outer-collaring (of width 1) of
M^{\rm pearl}
provides a bimodule
(M^{\rm pearl})^{+}
from
(T^{\rm Morse})^{+}
to
(T^{\rm Morse})^{+}
.

4.5.
Main Theorems

Using the notations introduced above, we can state the output of Sections
5
,
6
, and
7
.

Theorem 4.7
.

Denote by
(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}
the outer-collaring (see Section
3.3.2
) of the Hamiltonian Floer flow category
T^{\rm Floer}
. Then
(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}
admits a derived orbifold lift (Definition
3.24
)

{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Floer}=\Big(\big\{C^{\rm Floer}_{pq}=({\mathcal{U}}^{\rm Floer}_{pq},{\mathcal{E}}^{\rm Floer}_{pq},{\mathcal{S}}^{\rm Floer}_{pq},\psi^{\rm Floer}_{pq})\big\}_{p<q},\big\{{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}^{\rm Floer}\big\}_{\alpha\leq\beta}\Big),

with a collar structure (Definition
3.26
)

\{\widehat{\theta}_{\beta\alpha}^{{\rm collar},{\rm Floer}}\}_{\alpha\leq\beta},

and a scaffolding (Definition
3.28
)

\big({\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}^{\rm Floer},{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha}^{\rm Floer}\big)_{\alpha\leq\beta}

such that they are compatible (Definition
3.34
). Moreover, such a lift can be upgraded to an oriented and normally complex derived orbifold lift (Definition
3.44
). Further, one can equip the D-chart lift a straightening which is compatible with the collar and scaffoldings.

The proof is completed in Theorem
6.31
.

Proof of Theorem
B
.

From Theorem
4.7

this is a corollary of Theorem
3.49
and the discussion in Section
3.8.1
.
∎

Theorem 4.8
.

Let
(M^{\rm PSS})^{+}
be the outer-collaring of the flow bimodule
M^{\rm PSS}
from
(T^{\rm Morse})^{+}
to
(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}
. Then
(M^{\rm PSS})^{+}
has an oriented and normally complex derived orbifold lift

{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm PSS}=\Big(\big\{C^{\rm PSS}_{xp}=({\mathcal{U}}^{\rm PSS}_{xp},{\mathcal{E}}^{\rm PSS}_{xp},{\mathcal{S}}^{\rm PSS}_{xp},\psi^{\rm PSS}_{xp})\big\}_{x,p},\big\{{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}^{\rm PSS}\big\}_{\alpha\leq\beta}\Big),

with a collar structure

\{\widehat{\theta}_{\beta\alpha}^{{\rm collar},{\rm PSS}}\}_{\alpha\leq\beta},

and a scaffolding (Definition
3.28
)

\big({\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}^{\rm PSS},{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha}^{\rm PSS}\big)_{\alpha\leq\beta}

such that they are compatible, and they extend the given structures on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Floer}
and
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Morse}
. The same statement holds by reversing the role of
(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}
to
(T^{\rm Morse})^{+}
and replace
{\rm PSS}
by
{\rm SSP}
.

The proof is provided in Section
7
. The following assertion is proved similarly as the arguments in Section
6.3.1
.

Lemma 4.9
.

Both
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm PSS}
and
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm SSP}
have a straightening extending the straightening on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Floer}
, and they are compatible with the respective collar structure and scaffolding. ∎

As a consequence, Theorem
3.51
and the discussion in Section
3.8.1
define the
\Lambda
-linear chain maps

\Psi^{\rm PSS}:CM_{*}(f;\Lambda)\to CF_{*}(H;\Lambda),

\Psi^{\rm SSP}:CF_{*}(H;\Lambda)\to CM_{*}(f;\Lambda).

Theorem 4.10
.

Let
(M^{\rm pearl})^{+}
be the flow bimodule from
(T^{\rm Morse})^{+}
to
(T^{\rm Morse})^{+}
obtained from the outer-collaring of the pearly bimodule
M^{\rm pearl}
. Then
(M^{\rm pearl})^{+}
has an oriented and normally complex derived orbifold lift

{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm pearl}=\Big(\big\{C^{\rm pearl}_{xy}=({\mathcal{U}}^{\rm pearl}_{xy},{\mathcal{E}}^{\rm pearl}_{xy},{\mathcal{S}}^{\rm pearl}_{xy},\psi^{\rm pearl}_{xy})\big\}_{x,y},\big\{{\bm{\iota}}_{\beta\alpha}^{\rm pearl}\big\}_{\alpha\leq\beta}\Big),

with a collar structure

\{\widehat{\theta}_{\beta\alpha}^{{\rm collar},{\rm pearl}}\}_{\alpha\leq\beta},

such that the induced derived orbifold presentation on each
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}^{\rm pearl}_{xy}
is single-layered (Definition
3.23
), i.e.,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm pearl}
has a trivial scaffolding. Moreover, the orientation structure, normally complex structure, and the collar structure extend the existing ones on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Morse}
.

The proof is provided in Section
7
. By the argument in Section
6.3.1
(but simpler because the scaffolding is absent in this case), we can find a straightening of
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm pearl}
which is compatible with the collar structure. We fix it once for all.

Corollary 4.11
.

The FOP countings associated to the oriented and normally complex derived orbifold lift
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm pearl}
and the chosen straightening thereon defines a chain map

\Psi^{\rm pearl}:CM_{*}(f;\Lambda)\to CM_{*}(f;\Lambda)

which is unitriangular, i.e.,

\Psi^{\rm pearl}-{\rm Id}\in({\rm End}_{\Lambda}(CM_{*}(f;\Lambda)))_{+}.

In particular, the induced map on homology

\Psi^{\rm pearl}:H_{*}(M;\Lambda)\to H_{*}(M;\Lambda)

is invertible.

Proof.

The construction of
\Psi^{\rm pearl}
follows from Theorem
3.51
and discussions in Section
3.8
. To prove that
\Psi^{\rm pearl}
is unitriangular, observe that for a pair of capped orbits
x=(\underline{\smash{x}},a)
and
y=(\underline{\smash{y}},b)
, the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\rm pearl}_{xy}
is nonempty only if
\omega(b-a)\geq 0
. Moreover, when
\omega(b-a)=0
, the only nonempty moduli space contributing to the counting in
\Psi^{\rm pearl}(x)
is the moduli space of parametrized gradient flow lines from
x
to itself, which is a single point. It implies that the incidence coefficient
n^{\rm pearl}_{xx}=1
. As a consequence, the statement is proved.
∎

4.6.
The homotopy

In this subsection, we describe how to interpolate between the chain maps
\Psi^{\rm SSP}\circ\Psi^{\rm PSS}
and
\Psi^{\rm pearl}
and prove Theorem
C
.

We choose a 3-parameter family of Hamiltonians parametrized by
t\in S^{1}
,
s\in{\mathbb{R}}
, and
\tau\in(-1,1]
which satisfy the following conditions.

(1)

For each
\tau
,
H_{\tau,s,t}^{\rm hmtp}
is equal to
C^{\rm PSS}
for
s
near
-\infty
and is equal to
C^{\rm SSP}
for
s
near
+\infty
.

(2)

For all
\tau,s,t,x
there holds

\frac{\partial H_{\tau,s,t}^{\rm hmtp}}{\partial s}(x)\geq 0.

(3)

As
\tau\to-1
,
H_{\tau,s,t}^{\rm hmtp}
converges to the concatenation of
H_{s,t}^{\rm PSS}
and
H_{s,t}^{\rm SSP}
.

(4)

For
\tau
near
+1
,
H_{\tau,s,t}^{\rm hmtp}
does not depend on
t\in S^{1}
and
x\in M
. In particular, the associated Hamiltonian vector field is zero.

Then consider pairs
(\tau,u)
where
\tau\in(-1,1]
and
u:\Theta\to M
solving the equation

\frac{\partial u}{\partial s}+J\left(\frac{\partial u}{\partial t}-X_{H_{\tau,s,t}^{\rm hmtp}}(u)\right)=0.

(4.11)

The energy of a solution is defined by

E^{\rm hmtp}(\tau,u):=\left\|\partial_{s}u\right\|_{L^{2}(\Theta)}^{2}.

As the Hamiltonian perturbation vanishes near
s=\pm\infty
, any finite energy solution converges at the infinities. Hence one can use an element of
\Pi
to label solutions. Given
a\in\Pi
, let

{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm hmtp}(a)

to be the set of solutions to (
4.11
) whose
\Pi
-class is
a
. Moreover, by intersecting with (un)stable manifolds of Morse critical points, we can define

{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\underline{\smash{x}}\underline{\smash{y}}}^{\rm hmtp}(a)

to be the set of such solutions whose limit at
-\infty
resp.
+\infty
lies in the unstable resp. stable manifold of
\underline{\smash{x}}
resp.
\underline{\smash{y}}
. Lastly, given
x=(a,\underline{\smash{x}}),y=(b,\underline{\smash{y}})\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse}
, define

{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}:={\mathcal{M}}{}_{\underline{\smash{x}}\underline{\smash{y}}}^{\rm hmtp}(b-a).

We compactify this space by adding configurations with sphere bubbles, broken Morse trajectories, and (when
\tau\to-1
) breakings at 1-periodic orbits of
H
. Denote the compactification by

\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}.

We call such moduli spaces the
homotopy moduli spaces
.

4.6.1.
Stratifications on homotopy moduli spaces

The system of the homotopy moduli spaces can be put in a more abstract narrative to include
bimodule compositions
and
bimodule homotopies
. However, as its role in the proof of the Arnold conjecture is technical rather than conceptual, we refrain from introducing such frameworks. Here we give a more concrete description of the stratifications on these moduli spaces. Fix
x,y\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse}
. We define a poset

{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}:={\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{{\rm PSS}+{\rm SSP}}\sqcup\mathring{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}\sqcup{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm pearl}.

Here as sets

\mathring{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}={\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm pearl}

while as sets

{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{{\rm PSS}+{\rm SSP}}:=\Big\{xx_{1}\cdots x_{k}p_{1}\cdots p_{s}y_{l}\cdots y_{1}y\ |\ xx_{1}\cdots x_{k}p_{1}\cdots p_{s}\in{\mathbb{A}}_{xp_{s}}^{\rm PSS},\ p_{s}y_{l}\cdots y_{1}y\in{\mathbb{A}}_{p_{s}y}^{\rm SSP}\Big\}.

Then one can see that the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}
has a natural stratification whose strata are indexed by the set
{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}
. This then naturally induces a partial order on
{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}
according to the breaking of Morse or Floer trajectories as well as whether the parameter
\tau
hits
-1
or
1
. We use
\kappa,\nu
etc. instead of
\alpha,\beta
to denote elements of
{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}
. Then it is straightforward to check that
{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}
is a homogeneous poset with a unique maximal element and depth function being

{\rm depth}(\kappa)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\rm number\ of\ breakings},&\ \kappa\in\mathring{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp},\\
{\rm number\ of\ breakings}+1,&\ \kappa\in{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm pearl},\\
{\rm number\ of\ breakings},&\ \kappa\in{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{{\rm PSS}+{\rm SSP}}.\end{array}\right.

The types of codimension one (i.e., strata on which the depth function takes value
1
) degenerations of configurations in the homotopy moduli spaces can be described by the following types of stratified embeddings.

(1)

When the parameter
\tau
hits
+1
, there is a stratified embedding

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern (4.15)

The above maps satisfy a list of obvious associativity properties, which we do not describe explicitly here.

4.6.2.
Outer collaring

We can also apply the general outer-collaring construction on the homotopy moduli spaces so that it naturally extends the outer-collaring of the Floer, Morse, PSS, and SPP moduli spaces. The same type of structure maps as listed in (
4.12
)—(
4.15
) are still present and satisfy the same associativity properties.

4.6.3.
Derived orbifold lift

Definition 4.12
.

Assume the following objects are given.

(1)

A derived orbifold lift
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Floer}
of the outer-collared Hamiltonian Floer flow category
(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}
. (Remember there is also the trivial derived orbifold lift
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Morse}
of the outer-collared Morse flow category
(T^{\rm Morse})^{+}
).

(2)

A derived orbifold lift
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm PSS}
resp.
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm SSP}
of the outer-collared PSS resp. SSP bimodule which extends
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Floer}
and
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Morse}
.

(3)

A derived orbifold lift
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm pearl}
of the outer-collared pearly bimodule
(M^{\rm pearl})^{+}
.

Then a
derived orbifold lift
of the system of the outer-collared homotopy moduli spaces
(\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp})^{+}
consists of the following objects.

(1)

For each
x,y
, an
{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}
-stratified derived orbifold chart
C_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}
of
(\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp})^{+}
.

(2)

Stratified chart embeddings

(4.19)

These objects need to satisfy the following conditions.

(1)

The obvious associativity relations.

(2)

For each
\kappa\in{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}
, there is a product chart
C_{\kappa}^{\rm hmtp}
obtained from taking corresponding product of certain Morse, Floer, PSS, SSP, pearly, and/or homotopy derived orbifold charts. The above associativity relations induce chart embeddings

{\bm{\iota}}_{\nu\kappa}^{\rm hmtp}:C_{\kappa}^{\rm hmtp}\to\partial^{\kappa}C_{\nu}^{\rm hmtp}\ \forall\kappa\leq\nu.

Then
((C_{\kappa}^{\rm hmtp})_{\kappa\in{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}},({\bm{\iota}}_{\nu\kappa})_{\kappa\leq\nu})
form a derived orbifold presentation of the space
(\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp})^{+}
.

(3)

When
\kappa\notin\partial^{-}{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}
, the chart embedding
{\bm{\iota}}_{\nu\kappa}^{\rm hmtp}
is an open embedding.

(4)

The obvious strict
\Pi
-equivariance condition.

We also need to discuss the additional structures (collar, scaffolding, and straightening) on the derived orbifold lift of the system of homotopy moduli spaces.

Definition 4.13
.

In addition to the assumption of Definition
4.12
, assume the following objects are given.

(1)

A compatible package of additional structures on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Floer}
. Remember that there is also a package of additional structures on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Morse}
which essentially contains only the collar structure.

(2)

A compatible package of additional structures on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm PSS}
resp.
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm SSP}
which extend the ones on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Floer}
and
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Morse}
.

(3)

A compatible package of additional structures on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm pearl}
which extends the package on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Morse}
.

Let
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm hmtp}
be a derived orbifold lift of the system of homotopy moduli spaces which extends all given derived orbifold lifts.

(1)

A
collar structure
resp.
straightening
on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm hmtp}
is a collection of collar structures resp. straightenings on the derived orbifold charts
C_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}
satisfying the following condition. Fix
x,y
. For each
\kappa\in{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}
, the product of collar structures resp. straightenings on all derived orbifold chart factors defines a collar structure resp. straightening on the product chart
C_{\kappa}^{\rm hmtp}
. Then the collection of collar structures resp. straightenings for all
\kappa\in{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}
is a collar structure resp. straightening of the derived orbifold presentation of
(\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp})^{+}
.

(2)

A
scaffolding
on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm hmtp}
consists of a collection of scaffoldings

\Big(\big({\mathcal{F}}_{\nu\kappa},{\bm{\theta}}_{\nu\kappa}\big)_{\kappa\leq\nu}\Big)_{x,y\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse}}

on the collection of derived orbifold presentations
((C_{\kappa}^{\rm hmtp})_{\kappa\in{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}},({\bm{\iota}}_{\kappa\leq\nu}))
which satisfy the following conditions.

(a)

If
\nu\in\partial^{-}{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}
(i.e. a stratum in the
\tau=-1
slice of the moduli, i.e., a stratum of configurations with breakings at 1-periodic orbits of
H
), then
({\mathcal{F}}_{\nu\kappa},{\bm{\theta}}_{\nu\kappa})
is the product from corresponding difference bundles and stabilization maps on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Floer}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm PSS}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm SSP}
, and/or the trivial one on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Morse}
.

(b)

If
\kappa\notin\partial^{-}{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}
, then
{\mathcal{F}}_{\nu\kappa}=0
.

Lastly we discuss orientations and normal complex structures on a system of derived orbifold lifts on homotopy moduli spaces.

Definition 4.14
.

Given a derived orbifold lift
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm hmtp}
, and a collar structure compatible with a scaffolding
\Big(\big({\mathcal{F}}_{\nu\kappa},{\bm{\theta}}_{\nu\kappa}\big)_{\kappa\leq\nu}\Big)_{x,y\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Morse}}
, assume that
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Floer}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm PSS}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm SSP}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Morse}
and their scaffoldings and outer-collarings are endowed with a
normal complex structure
. Then a normal complex structure on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm hmtp}
with the given scaffolding and outer-collaring consists of

(1)

A normal complex structure on all the underlying derived orbifold charts of
(C_{\kappa}^{\rm hmtp})_{\kappa\in{\mathbb{A}}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}}
such that the embeddings
({\bm{\iota}}_{\kappa\leq\nu})
all intertwine with the normal complex structures.

(2)

A complex structure on the vector bundles
{\mathcal{F}}_{\nu\kappa}
such that the embeddings underlying
{\bm{\theta}}_{\nu\kappa}
all respects the induced normal complex structures.

(3)

The structural maps from the outer-collaring and the compatibility equations with the scaffolding intertwine with the normal complex structure as in Definition
3.36
.

(4)

The strict
\Pi
-action preserves the normal complex structures.

Definition 4.15
.

Let
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Floer}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm PSS}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm SSP}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Morse},{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm hmtp}
be as in Definition
4.14
with the respective collared structure, scaffolding, and normal complex structure. Moreover, assume that each of
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Floer}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm PSS}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm SSP}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Morse}
is equipped with an orientation in the sense of Definition
3.43
and Definition
3.47
. Then an
orientation
on the given normally complex lift of
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm hmtp}
consists of

(1)

For all
x,y
, an isomorphism of orientation lines

\mathfrak{o}_{C_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{o}_{x}^{\vee}\otimes\mathfrak{o}_{y}.

(4.20)

(2)

Isomorphisms of orientation lines

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{o}}_{C_{xy}^{\rm pearl}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathfrak{o}}_{\partial^{+}C^{\rm hmtp}_{xy}},

(4.21)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{o}}_{C_{xp}^{\rm PSS}}\otimes{\mathfrak{o}}_{C_{py}^{\rm SSP}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathfrak{o}}_{\partial^{xpy}C_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{o}}_{C_{xx^{\prime}}^{\rm Morse}}\otimes{\mathfrak{o}}_{C_{x^{\prime}y}^{\rm hmtp}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathfrak{o}}_{\partial^{xx^{\prime}y}C_{xy}^{\rm hmtp}},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{o}}_{C_{xy^{\prime}}^{\rm hmtp}}\otimes{\mathfrak{o}}_{y^{\prime}y}^{\rm Morse}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathfrak{o}}_{\partial^{xy^{\prime}y}C^{\rm hmtp}_{xy}}.

Here we omit the cancellation of the orientation lines of the complex scaffolding vector bundles and its dual.

(3)

The above isomorphisms are preserved by the
\Pi
-action.

Theorem 4.16
.

There is a
\Lambda
-linear chain homotopy

\Psi^{\rm hmtp}:CM_{*}(f;\Lambda)\to CM_{*-1}(f;\Lambda)

such that

\Psi^{\rm pearl}-\Psi^{\rm SSP}\circ\Psi^{\rm PSS}=d^{\rm Morse}\circ\Psi^{\rm hmtp}-\Psi^{\rm hmtp}\circ d^{\rm Morse}.

(4.22)

In particular, there holds

\Psi^{\rm SSP}\circ\Psi^{\rm PSS}=\Psi^{\rm pearl}

(4.23)

as graded linear maps on
H_{*}(M;\Lambda)
.

Proof.

Using the description presented in Section
7
, there exists a compatible oriented and normally complex derived orbifold lift
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm hmtp}
of the outer-collared homotopy moduli spaces
(\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{xy}^{\rm hmtp})^{+}
, with collared structure and scaffolding. Then the relative version of the arguments in Section
6.3.1
can be used to equip
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm hmtp}
with a straightening compatible with the given straightenings on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Floer}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm PSS}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm SSP}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Morse}
. Therefore, a relative version of Theorem
3.49
allows us to find a compatible system of strongly transverse FOP perturbations of the derived orbifold charts in
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm hmtp}
extending the FOP perturbations on
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Floer}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm PSS}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm SSP}
,
{\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm Morse}
. Using the sign read off from (
4.20
), the map
\Psi^{\rm hmtp}
is defined by the algebraic count of zeroes of the FOP sections over moduli spaces of virtual dimension
0
. The algebraic relation (
4.22
) is again read off from the boundary of moduli spaces of virtural dimension
1
, using the coherence of orientations (
4.21
). The
\Lambda
-linearity is a result of the strict
\Pi
-action as before.
∎

Proof of Theorem
C
.

This is a direct consequence of Corollary
4.11
and Equation (
4.23
).
∎

4.7.
Proof of the integral Arnold conjecture

Lemma 4.17
.

(cf.
[
HM06
, Appendix]
)
The Novikov ring
\Lambda
is a principal ideal domain (PID).

Proof.

By definition, we need to show that each ideal is a principal ideal, i.e., generated by a single element. Let
I\subseteq\Lambda
be an ideal. Denote by
a_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{+}
the greatest common divisor of the leading coefficients of all elements of
I
. Then there exists
{\mathfrak{x}}_{0}\in I
of the form
{\mathfrak{x}}_{0}=a_{0}+{\mathfrak{y}}_{0}
with
{\mathfrak{y}}_{0}\in\Lambda_{+}
. We claim that

I=({\mathfrak{x}}_{0}).

Indeed, given any
{\mathfrak{x}}\in I
,
a_{0}
divides the leading order term of
{\mathfrak{x}}
. We can inductively find an element
{\mathfrak{q}}\in\Lambda
such that
{\mathfrak{x}}={\mathfrak{q}}{\mathfrak{x}}_{0}
. Hence
I=({\mathfrak{x}}_{0})
and
\Lambda
is a PID.
∎

Recall the standard structure results about finitely-generated modules over principal ideal domains. If
R
is a principal ideal domain and
M
is a finitely-generated
R
-module, then

M\cong F\oplus T

where
F
is a free
R
-module of finite rank and
T
is a torsion
R
-module. Moreover,

(1)

There exist primes
p_{1},\ldots,p_{k}\in R
and positive integers
m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}
such that

T\cong R/(p_{1}^{m_{1}})\oplus\cdots\oplus R/(p_{k}^{m_{k}}).

The prime powers
p_{i}^{m_{i}}
are called the
elementary divisors
.

(2)

There exist nonzero nonunit elements
r_{1},\ldots,r_{l}
of
R
such that
r_{1}|r_{2}|\cdots|r_{l}
and

T\cong R/(r_{1})\oplus\cdots\oplus R/(r_{l}).

The elements
r_{i}
are called the
invariant factors
.

Lemma 4.18
.

Let
R
be a PID. Let
Z
be a free
R
-module of finite rank and
S\subseteq Z
be a submodule. Suppose

Z/S\cong F\oplus R/(a_{1})\oplus\cdots\oplus R/(a_{k})

where
F
is a free
R
-module and
a_{1}|a_{2}|\cdots|a_{k}
are invariant factors. Then

{\rm rank}Z\geq{\rm rank}F+k

and

{\rm rank}S\geq k.

Proof.

By tensoring with the quotient field of
R
, we can kill all torsions. Then it follows that
{\rm rank}Z-{\rm rank}S={\rm rank}F
. Hence it suffices to prove the first lower bound. Choose a prime divisor
p
of
a_{1}
. By tensoring with
R_{p}:=R/(p)
, we can see that

Z\otimes_{R}R_{p}/S\otimes_{R}R_{p}\cong R_{p}^{{\rm rank}F+k}.

Therefore,

{\rm rank}Z={\rm dim}_{R_{p}}Z\otimes_{R}R_{p}\geq{\rm rank}F+k.\qed

Lemma 4.19
.

Let
M
be a finitely-generated
R
-module and
N\subseteq M
be a submodule. Then

(1)

{\rm rank}N\leq{\rm rank}M
.

(2)

For each prime
p\in R
, the number of elementary divisors of
N
which are powers of
p
is less than or equal to the number of elementary divisors of
M
which are powers of
p
.

(3)

The number of invariant factors of
N
is not greater than the number of invariant factors of
M
.

Proof.

By the structure theorem of finitely-generated modules over PID, we can write

\displaystyle\ M\cong F\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}R/(p_{i}^{m_{i}}),\

\displaystyle\ N\cong F^{\prime}\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{l}R/(q_{j}^{n_{j}})

where
p_{i}
,
q_{j}
are primes of
R
and
m_{i},n_{j}\geq 1
. By tensoring the quotient field of
R
, we can see that
{\rm rank}F^{\prime}\leq{\rm rank}F
. On the other hand, let
M(p)\subseteq M
be the direct sum of
p
-torsions. Then the inclusion
N\hookrightarrow M
induces injections
N(p)\hookrightarrow M(p)
for each prime
p
. Therefore, the collection of
q_{j}
’s is a subset of the collection of
p_{i}
’s. Then for each prime
p
, denote

M[p]:=\{x\in M\ |\ px=0\}

which is a submodule. Then the inclusion
N\hookrightarrow M
induces an injection
N[p]\hookrightarrow M[p]
. Notice that
R/(p)
is a field and
M[p]
is a
R/(p)
-vector space. Clearly
N[p]
is a subspace. Moreover, recall that

(R/(p^{m}))[p]\cong R/(p).

Hence it follows that the number of
p
-elementary divisors of
N
is no greater than the number of
p
-elementary divisors of
M
. The relation between their numbers of invariant factors follows immediately.
∎

Proof of Theorem
A
.

Now consider the two
{\mathbb{Z}}/2N
-graded
\Lambda
-modules,
HF_{*}(H;\Lambda)
and
H_{*}(M;\Lambda)
. Because
\Psi^{\rm SSP}\circ\Psi^{\rm PSS}=\Psi^{\rm pearl}=Id+O(T)
, so for each
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/{2N}
, the PSS map embeds
H_{i}(M;\Lambda)
as a submodule of
HF_{i}(H;\Lambda)
. Therefore, for the free part, one has the rank inequality

{\rm rank}HF_{i}(H;\Lambda)\geq{\rm rank}H_{*}(M;\Lambda),\ i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/2N.

As of torsion, Lemma
4.19
implies the inequality between number of invariant factors

\tau_{i}^{\rm Floer}\geq\tau_{i}^{\rm Morse},\ i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/2N

On the other hand, we know that

HF_{i}(H;\Lambda)=\frac{{\rm ker}d_{i}^{\rm Floer}}{{\rm image}d_{i+1}^{\rm Floer}}

where both the numerator and denominator are free
\Lambda
-modules. Hence by Lemma
4.18
, one has

{\rm rank}({\rm ker}d_{i}^{\rm Floer})\geq{\rm rank}H_{i}(M;\Lambda)+\tau_{i}^{\rm Morse}

and

{\rm rank}({\rm image}d_{i+1}^{\rm Floer})\geq\tau_{i}^{\rm Morse}.

Therefore, let
\#{\rm Per}H
be the number of
1
-periodic orbits of
H
, we have

\#{\rm Per}H={\rm rank}CF_{*}(H;\Lambda)=\sum_{i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/{2N}}{\rm rank}CF_{i}(H;\Lambda)\\
=\sum_{i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/{2N}}\big({\rm rank}({\rm ker}d_{i}^{\rm Floer})+{\rm rank}({\rm image}d_{i}^{\rm Floer})\big)\\
\geq\sum_{i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/{2N}}{\rm rank}H_{i}(M;\Lambda)+2\sum_{i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/{2N}}\tau_{i}^{\rm Morse}\\
=\text{rank}H_{*}(M;{\mathbb{Q}})+2\sum_{i\in{\mathbb{Z}}/2N}\tau_{i}^{(2N)}(M).

∎

5.
Global Kuranishi charts on Floer moduli spaces

In this section we provide the details for the global Kuranishi chart construction of moduli spaces of Floer trajectories. The construction is primarily inspired by the construction by Abouzaid–McLean–Smith
[
AMS21
]
of the global chart on a single moduli space of genus zero stable maps. The main effort, however, is to generalize their construction to the case of the Hamiltonian Floer flow categories, where global charts must be constructed consistently for infinitely many moduli spaces. Certain care must be taken in order to have the expected properties. This and the next section serve as the construction of a derived orbifold lift of the Hamiltonian Floer flow category: this section deals with the topological construction while Section
6
deals with the smoothing.

5.1.
Basic notions and the main theorem about global charts

To state the main theorem of this section, we introduce an alternative version of charts. Because our construction of FOP perturbations is carried over orbifolds instead of smooth manifolds with an almost-free action of a compact Lie group, we refrain to give a general treatment of “global charts” beyond the concrete geometric setting in this paper.

5.1.1.
Kuranishi charts

We slightly generalize the usual notion of Kuranishi charts.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
{\mathbb{A}}
be a countable homogeneous poset and
X
be an
{\mathbb{A}}
-space.

(1)

An
{\mathbb{A}}
-stratified
(topological) Kuranishi chart
(K-chart for short) on
X
is a quintuple
(G,V,E,S,\psi)
where
G
is a compact Lie group,
V
is an
{\mathbb{A}}
-manifold with a continuous
G
-action,
E\to V
is a
G
-equivariant vector bundle,
S:V\to E
is a
G
-equivariant section, and
\psi:S^{-1}(0)/G\to X
is a homeomorphism. We require the following condition: the stabilizer of each point
x\in V
is finite, i.e., the
G
-action on
V
is almost free.

(2)

A K-chart
(G,V,E,S,\psi)
is said to be
smooth
if
V
is a smooth
{\mathbb{A}}
-manifold, the
G
-action is smooth, and
E\to V
is a smooth equivariant vector bundle (we do not impose any smoothness condition on
S
).

We often omit the map
\psi
in the notation because in the context its meaning will always be clear.

Remark 5.2
.

Historically, there are different notions of Kuranishi charts which could be defined either via orbifolds or via equivariant objects. In this paper, the orbifold version will be labelled as “derived” and the name “Kuranishi” is reserved for the equivariant version while allowing actions by general compact Lie groups. We also use the prefixes “D-” and “K-” to denote these two versions.

Definition 5.3
.

Let
K=(G,V,E,S)
be a K-chart and
\pi_{F}:F\to V
be a
G
-equivariant vector bundle. The
stabilization
of
K
by
F
, denoted by
{\rm Stab}_{F}(K)
, is the K-chart

{\rm Stab}_{F}(K)=(G,F,\pi_{F}^{*}E\oplus\pi_{F}^{*}F,\pi_{F}^{*}S\oplus\tau_{F})

where
\tau_{F}:F\to\pi_{F}^{*}F
is the tautological section.

5.1.2.
Change of groups

For K-charts, the notions of open embedding, germ equivalence, and product are almost identical to the case of derived orbifold charts after imposing the equivariance condition with respect to the Lie group action. There are some care to be taken of when the groups of symmetry change. To this end, we introduce the operation of enlarging the symmetry group.

Definition 5.4
.

Let
K=(G,V,E,S)
be a K-chart and let
G\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}
be a Lie group embedding. Define the
change of group
, or the
G^{\prime}
-equivariantization
, of
K
, to be

G^{\prime}\times_{G}K:=(G^{\prime},G^{\prime}\times_{G}V,G^{\prime}\times_{G}E,S^{\prime})

where
G^{\prime}
acts on the bundle
G^{\prime}\times_{G}E\to G^{\prime}\times_{G}V
in the obvious way and

S^{\prime}([g^{\prime},x])=[g^{\prime},S(x)].

In the rest of the paper, we also use the notations

G^{\prime}(K):=G^{\prime}\times_{G}K:=(G^{\prime},G^{\prime}(V),G^{\prime}(E),G^{\prime}(S)):=(G^{\prime},G^{\prime}\times_{G}V,G^{\prime}\times_{G}E,S^{\prime}).

Lemma 5.5
.

Suppose
G\hookrightarrow G^{\prime}
is a Lie group embedding and let
V
be a
G
-space. If
{\bm{W}}
is a
G^{\prime}
-representation and
E\to V
is the trivial
G
-bundle
V\times{\bm{W}}
where we view
{\bm{W}}
as a
G
-representation, then
G^{\prime}(E)
is isomorphic to the trivial
G^{\prime}
-bundle over
G^{\prime}(V)=G^{\prime}\times_{G}V
with fiber
{\bm{W}}
.

Proof.

Define the map
\zeta:G^{\prime}(E)=G^{\prime}\times_{G}(V\times{\bm{W}})\to(G^{\prime}\times_{G}V)\times{\bm{W}}
by

\zeta([g^{\prime},(v,w)])=([g^{\prime},v],g^{\prime}w).

Notice that

\zeta([g^{\prime}g,(v,w)])=([g^{\prime}g,v],g^{\prime}gw)=([g^{\prime},gv],g^{\prime}(gw))=\zeta([g^{\prime},(gv,gw)])=\zeta([g^{\prime},g(v,w)]).

Hence
\zeta
is well-defined. It is also straightforward to check that
\zeta
is a map of
G^{\prime}
-equivariant vector bundles over
G^{\prime}\times_{G}V
and is an isomorphism.
∎

5.1.3.
Chart embeddings

Because of the presence of compact Lie group, the definition of chart embeddings of K-charts also differs slightly from the notion of chart embeddings of derived orbifold charts.

Definition 5.6
.

Let
K_{1}=(G_{1},V_{1},E_{1},S_{1},\psi_{1})
and
K_{2}=(G_{2},V_{2},E_{2},S_{2},\psi_{2})
be two topological K-charts.

(1)

A
weak K-chart embedding
from
K_{1}
to
K_{2}
, denoted by

{\bm{\iota}}_{21}:K_{1}\rightharpoonup K_{2},

consists of a group embedding
G_{1}\hookrightarrow G_{2}
,
10
10

10

In the concrete situations of this paper, the group embeddings are always fixed by the geometric data, and the embeddings are induced from inclusions
an equivairant topological embedding
\iota_{21}:G_{2}(V_{1})\hookrightarrow V_{2}
which has a
G_{2}
-invariant neighborhood equivariantly homeomorphic to a
G_{2}
-equivariant vector bundle
11
11

11

This seemingly redundant requirement is necessary because in the topological category submanifolds do not necessarily have vector bundle neighborhoods.
, and an equivariant vector bundle embedding
\widehat{\iota}_{21}:G_{2}(E_{1})\hookrightarrow E_{2}
covering
\iota_{21}
. (Due to a small defect of the global chart construction we will use, a weak K-chart embedding does not necessarily intertwine with the Kuranishi sections. This explains the adjective “weak” in the terminology.)

(2)

A weak K-chart embedding is called a
K-chart embedding
if the following diagrams commute.

\displaystyle\ \vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 78.45969pt\raise-24.65335pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise-31.98666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 70.92497pt\raise-31.98666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 70.92497pt\raise-31.98666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}.

(3)

A (weak) K-chart embedding is called a (weak) open embedding if
\iota_{21}
is a homeomorphism onto an open subset and
\widehat{\iota}_{21}
is a bundle isomorphism.

Notice that (weak) K-chart embeddings can be composed in an obvious way.

5.1.4.
Notations

Notation 5.7
.

(1)

For each positive integer
d
, let
{\mathbb{A}}_{d}
be the poset of all ordered partitions of
d
, i.e.,

{\mathbb{A}}_{d}:=\big\{(d_{0},\ldots,d_{l})\ |\ d_{0}+\cdots+d_{l}=d,\ d_{i}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}\big\}.

The partial order is induced from refinements of partitions. It is a homogeneous poset with a unique maximal element
(d)
and depth function
{\rm depth}(d_{0},\ldots,d_{l})=l
. Moreover, there are natural inclusions

{\mathbb{A}}_{d_{0}}\times\cdots\times{\mathbb{A}}_{d_{l}}\to\partial^{(d_{0},\ldots,d_{l})}{\mathbb{A}}_{d_{0}+\cdots+d_{l}}

which satisfy the obvious associativity relation.

(2)

For each pair
p,q\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}
, define

d_{pq}:={\mathcal{A}}^{\rm Floer}(q)-{\mathcal{A}}^{\rm Floer}(p).

(5.1)

By the integrality assumption of the symplectic action (see Hypothesis
4.1
),
d_{pq}
is an integer. In practice we only consider the situation when
d_{pq}\geq 0
. Moreover, there is a natural poset map
