implies that the positivity of the curvature of the induced connection on
\overline{\rm Hom}(\iota_{F}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d},u^{*}TM)\otimes\iota_{F}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k)
is everywhere positive for
k
sufficiently large, because such a claim is true for the corresponding vector bundles on the target
X\times\mathbb{CP}^{d}
.
∎

5.3.3.
Multi-layered thickening

In Floer theory we need to deal with many moduli spaces simultaneously, and it is important to make sure that various structures on different moduli spaces fit together in a coherent way. The expected relations between different moduli spaces then require us to consider a more complicated thickening procedure which is referred to as the
multi-layered thickening
. Namely, instead of just considering the perturbation induced from one single relatively ample bundle
\text{ev}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k)\to{\mathcal{C}}
, we need to look at direct sum of such line bundles. This slight generalization of Abouzaid–McLean–Smith’s perturbation scheme will be used in the construction of the K-chart lift.

Fix a positive integer
d
and abbreviate
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{C}}_{0,2}(d)
. Suppose we have a sequence of positive integers
k_{1}<k_{2}<\cdots<k_{s}
. We are going to define complex vector bundles inductively

E_{1}^{\prime},E_{1},\ldots,E_{s}^{\prime},E_{s}\to M\times{\mathcal{C}}=M\times{\mathcal{C}}_{0,2}(d_{pq}).

First, define

E_{1}^{\prime}:=\overline{\rm Hom}(({\rm ev}\circ p_{\mathcal{C}})^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d},p_{M}^{*}TM)\otimes({\rm ev}\circ p_{\mathcal{C}})^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k_{1}).

and

E_{1}:=E_{1}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{{\rm univ}^{*}({\rm univ}_{*}{\rm ev}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k_{1}))_{0}}.

Inductively, suppose we have defined
E_{1}^{\prime},E_{1},\ldots,E_{i-1}^{\prime},E_{i-1}
. Then define

E_{i}^{\prime}:=\overline{\rm Hom}\Big(({\rm ev}\circ p_{\mathcal{C}})^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d},p_{M}^{*}TM\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{i-1}E_{i}\Big)\otimes({\rm ev}\circ p_{\mathcal{C}})^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k_{i})

and

E_{i}:=E_{i}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{{\rm univ}^{*}({\rm univ}_{*}{\rm ev}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k_{i}))_{0}}.

(5.31)

Notice that once
TM
is equipped with a Hermitian connection (and hence a Cauchy–Riemann operator). Together with the holomorphic structure of the involved bundles over
{\mathcal{C}}
, the connection on
TM
induces a Cauchy–Riemann operator on
E_{i}
.

Remark 5.44
.

We explain the above construction for the simplest nontrivial example
E_{2}
. The total space of
E_{1}\to M\times{\mathcal{C}}
is an almost complex manifold which has a projection
p_{\mathcal{C}}:E_{1}\to{\mathcal{C}}
. The goal is to construct certain perturbations of a (perturbed)
J
-holomorphic map

u:\Sigma\to E_{1}

such that
p_{\mathcal{C}}\circ u:\Sigma\to{\mathcal{C}}
is a domain map, which in particular is holomorphic. Note that

(du)^{0,1}_{J}\in\overline{\rm Hom}(T\Sigma,u^{*}TE_{1})=\overline{\rm Hom}(T\Sigma,u^{*}TM\oplus u^{*}T{\mathcal{C}}\oplus u^{*}E_{1}),

where the equality is induced from a Hermitian connection on
E_{1}
. Therefore, to keep the map
p_{\mathcal{C}}\circ u
being a domain map, the perturbation should take value in
\overline{\rm Hom}(T\Sigma,u^{*}TM\oplus u^{*}E_{1})
. The vector bundle
E_{2}\to M\times{\mathcal{C}}
is constructed just as the construction of
E_{1}\to M\times{\mathcal{C}}
in order to host an equation of the form (
5.24
), with the assistance of the relatively ample line bundle
{\rm ev}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k_{2})\to{\mathcal{C}}
.

Definition 5.45

(Multi-layered thickening)
.

For any pair
p,q
of capped periodic orbits, denote
d=d_{pq}
. Let
V_{pq}^{d}=V_{pq}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})
be the moduli space of tuples

(\Sigma,u,F,\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{d})

where
\Sigma
is a prestable cylinder,
u:\Sigma\to M
is a smooth map with topological energy
d
converging to
p
and
q
at the two marked points,
F
is a frame of the line bundle
L_{u,\Omega,H}\to\Sigma
inducing a holomorphic map
\iota_{F}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d}
such that the framing
F=(f_{0},\dots,f_{d})
satisfies
[f_{0}(z_{-}):\cdots:f_{d}(z_{-})]=[1:0:\cdots:0]
, and the corresponding domain map
\tilde{\iota}_{F}:\Sigma\to{\mathcal{C}}
, and (denoting by
\tilde{u}=(u,\tilde{\iota}_{F}):\Sigma\to M\times{\mathcal{C}}
)

\eta_{i}\in\tilde{u}^{*}E_{i}=\Gamma\left(\overline{\rm Hom}\big(\iota_{F}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d},u^{*}TM\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{i-1}\tilde{u}^{*}E_{j}\big)\otimes\iota_{F}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k_{i})\right)\otimes\overline{H^{0}(\iota_{F}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k_{i}))_{0}}

satisfying the following equations

\left\{\begin{aligned} \overline{\partial}_{J,H}u+\pi_{u^{*}TM}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\langle\eta_{i}\rangle\circ d\iota_{F}\right)&\ =0,\\
\overline{\partial}\eta_{i}+\pi_{E_{i}}\left(\sum_{j=i+1}^{d}\langle\eta_{j}\rangle\circ d\iota_{F}\right)&\ =0,\ i=1,\ldots,d.\end{aligned}\right.

(5.32)

Remark 5.46
.

Continuing Remark
5.44
, we explain the origin of the above equations for
d=2
. Note that the pair
(\tilde{u},\eta)
defines a smooth map
\tilde{u}_{1}:\Sigma\to E_{1}
. The purpose is to write down a perturbation of certain
\overline{\partial}
-equation for
\tilde{u}_{1}
using a section of
\eta_{2}\in\tilde{u}^{*}E_{2}
, which could be schematically written as

\overline{\partial}_{\tilde{J}_{\Psi},H}u_{1}+\langle\eta_{2}\rangle\circ d\iota_{F}=0,

where
\tilde{J}_{\Psi}
is the sheared almost complex structure constructed using (
5.28
) and
u_{1}
is obtained from
\tilde{u}_{1}
by forgetting the domain map. Then Equation (
5.32
) is obtained by projecting the above equation using
\pi_{u^{*}TM}
and
\pi_{E_{1}}
. For general
d\geq 2
, one just need to carry out this construction iteratively.

Similar to the case with a single thickening bundle, we have a regularity result for the above defined multi-layered thickened moduli space. The details are given in Subsection
5.3.4
. We remark that the Fredholm model for this thickened moduli space depends on certain choices which may not be canonical. However, being regular or not is a condition independent of these choices. As in Definition
5.33
,
V_{pq}^{d}
is part of the data of a K-chart.

Obstruction bundle
The first part of the obstruction bundle has its fiber over a point represented by
(\Sigma,u,F,\eta)
the vector space

\left\{\zeta=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\zeta_{1}\\
\vdots\\
\zeta_{d}\end{array}\right)\left|\begin{array}[]{c}\zeta_{i}\in\Gamma\left(\overline{\rm Hom}(\iota_{F}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d},u^{*}TM\oplus\displaystyle\bigoplus_{j=1}^{i-1}\tilde{u}^{*}E_{j}\right)\otimes\iota_{F}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k_{i})\otimes\overline{H^{0}(\iota_{F}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k_{i}))_{0}},\\
\overline{\partial}\zeta_{i}+\pi_{E_{i}}\left(\displaystyle\sum_{j=i+1}^{d}\langle\zeta_{j}\rangle\circ d\iota_{F}\right)=0\end{array}\right.\right\}.

(5.33)

It is standard that it is finite-dimensional. We denote this bundle by

O_{pq}=O_{pq}^{(d)}\to V_{pq}.

On the other hand, as before, the second factor of the obstruction bundle is the trivial bundle
Q_{pq}:=Q_{d}
whose fiber
{\bm{Q}}_{d}
is defined by (
5.12
). Let
Q_{pq}\to V_{pq}
be the trivial bundle. Note that from its definition,
Q_{pq}
is only a fiber bundle with a canonical smooth section, where the section corresponds to the element in
Q_{d}
represented by the identity matrix. However, using the vector space
{\bm{Q}}^{*}_{d}
defined by (
5.13
), the bundle
Q_{pq}
can be endowed with a vector bundle structure. We switch between these two viewpoints in different contexts. We define

E_{pq}=O_{pq}\oplus Q_{pq}\to V_{pq}.

Moreover, similar to the single-layered case, there is a
G_{pq}
-action on
V_{pq}
as well as on the bundles
O_{pq}
and
Q_{pq}
making
E_{pq}
an equivariant vector bundle.

Properties of obstruction bundle.
There are many structural facts about the obstruction bundles which will play important roles in the construction of the K-chart lift of the Hamiltonian Floer flow category. We summarize them here. First, recall that the thickening depends on the choice of the sequence
k_{1}<\cdots<k_{d}
. We (will) fix an increasing sequence
\{k_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
such that for all
pq
, the thickening
V_{pq}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d_{pq}})
is regular (see Proposition
5.49
). However, we can have higher obstruction spaces. Given
d^{\prime}>d\geq d_{pq}
, one can construct thickenings
V_{pq}(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})
and
V_{pq}(k_{1},\dots,k_{d^{\prime}})
, over which live the two vector bundles

\displaystyle\ O_{pq}^{(d)}\to V_{pq}(k_{1},\dots,k_{d}),\

\displaystyle\ O_{pq}^{(d^{\prime})}\to V_{pq}(k_{1},\dots,k_{d^{\prime}}).

As we use sections with higher
k_{i}
labels to perturb sections with lower
k_{i}
labels, there is a natural inclusion

O_{pq}^{(d)}\hookrightarrow O_{pq}^{(d^{\prime})}

(5.34)

covering the natural inclusion
V_{pq}(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\hookrightarrow V_{pq}(k_{1},\dots,k_{d^{\prime}})

such that when
d<d^{\prime}<d^{\prime\prime}
, the following diagram commutes

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 12.40923pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.40923pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{O_{pq}^{(d)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.40923pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 87.93935pt\raise-5.93481pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 36.40923pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{O_{pq}^{(d^{\prime})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 87.93254pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 87.93254pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{O_{pq}^{(d^{\prime\prime})}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}.

Then we can define the direct limit of the obstruction bundles as

O_{pq}^{\infty}:=\lim_{d\to\infty}O_{pq}^{(d)}.

(5.35)

The obstruction bundle also splits over each boundary stratum. Let
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
and let
\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}\subset V_{pq}
be the subspace of the thickened moduli space which consists of elements such that the underlying map
(u,\Sigma)
is of type
\alpha
(see Definition
5.31
). Then one has a direct sum decomposition

O_{pq}^{(d)}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}\cong O_{pq;pr_{1}}^{(d)}\oplus\cdots\oplus O_{pq;r_{l}q}^{(d)}

(5.36)

where the fiber of
O_{pq;r_{i}r_{i+1}}^{(d)}
over the point
[\Sigma,u,F,\eta]
is

\left\{\zeta=(\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{d})^{T}\ |\ {\rm supp}\zeta\subset\Sigma_{r_{i}r_{i+1}}\subset\Sigma\right\},

where we use the convention that
r_{0}=p
and
r_{l+1}=q
.
(
5.36
) is a direct sum because elements of
O_{pq}^{(d)}
necessarily vanish at nodes connecting cylindrical components.

Kuranishi section.
Define

\begin{split}S^{d}_{pq}:V_{pq}&\ \to E_{pq}\\
[\Sigma,u,F,\eta]&\ \mapsto(\eta,H_{F})\end{split}

where
H_{F}\in Q_{d}
is represented by the Hermitian matrix whose entries are

\int_{\Sigma}\langle f_{i},f_{j}\rangle_{L_{u,\Omega,H}}\Omega_{u},

where
f_{0},\dots,f_{d}
is a basis representing the frame
F
.
It is also the same as the single-layered case that
S^{d}_{pq}
is
G_{pq}
-equivariant,

Therefore, one obtains a quadruple

K_{pq}=(G_{pq},V_{pq},E_{pq},S_{pq}):=(G_{pq},V^{d}_{pq},E^{d}_{pq},S^{d}_{pq})

(5.37)

which is a candidate for a global Kuranishi chart on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{pq}
. Still, there is a canonical map

S_{pq}^{-1}(0)/G_{pq}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{pq}

which defines a homeomorphism onto its image.

5.3.4.
Regularity for the multi-layered thickening

Now we prove the regularity of the multi-layered thickening. First we transform the description of the thickened moduli space to a moduli space where one can use Gromov’s graph trick. Recall that one has the bundles
E_{1},\ldots,E_{d}\to M\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d}
. Then one can define the tower of bundles

{\mathcal{E}}_{d}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{d-1}\to\cdots\to{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\to M\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d}

where each
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
is the total space of the pullback of
E_{i}\to M\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d}
onto the previous
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}\to M\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d}
. Then
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
is still a complex vector bundle over
M\times{\mathcal{C}}_{d}
. To proceed we would like to equip each
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
with a Hermitian metric and a Hermitian connection. From the construction one can see that all the ingredients in constructing these bundles have a natural Hermitian structure except for the bundle
({\rm univ}_{*}{\rm ev}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k_{i}))_{0}
, which is the bundle of fiberwise global sections of the holomorphic line bundle
{\rm ev}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k_{i})
that vanish at the markings
z_{-}
and
z_{+}
. We choose an arbitrary smooth Hermitian metric on
{\rm univ}_{*}{\rm ev}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k_{i})
. Then each
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
has an induced Hermitian metric. Moreover, together with the Chern connection on all involved holomorphic vector bundles and the chosen Hermitian metric on
TM
,
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
is equipped with a Hermitian connection.

Now we inductively define a sheared almost complex structure on the total space of
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
similar to the constructions in Section
5.3.2
. The details are almost identical and omitted. We denote the resulting almost complex structure on
{\mathcal{E}}_{d}
by
J_{\Psi}
.

Theorem 5.47
.

For a given list
k_{1}<\cdots<k_{d-1}
of positive integers. There exists
k^{*}>0
such that for all
k_{d}\geq k^{*}
, for all pairs
pq
with
d_{pq}\leq d
, all elements of
S_{pq}^{-1}(0)
are regular elements of
{\mathcal{M}}_{J_{\tilde{\Psi}},H}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})
.

Proof.

The proof is essentially the same as the single-layered case. Indeed, we can replace the almost complex manifold
(M,J)
by the pair
({\mathcal{E}}_{d-1},J_{d-1})
where
J_{d-1}
is the sheared almost complex structure on the total space
{\mathcal{E}}_{d-1}
lifted via the chosen connections. Then we can proceed by writing the linearized Cauchy–Riemann operator in the block form as in (
5.30
). As long as
k_{d}
is sufficiently positive, we can make the linearization of the first equation in (
5.32
) surjective by the peak section argument from
[
AMS21
, Proposition 6.26]
. For the surjectivity of the linearization of the second part in (
5.32
), apply Lemma
5.42
and Lemma
5.43
.
∎

Lemma 5.48
.

Each fiber of the natural forgetful map

\pi_{pq}:V_{pq}\to B_{pq}

(5.38)

which takes a representative
(\Sigma,u,F,\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{d})
to the equivalence class of the holomorphic map

\iota_{F}:\Sigma\to{\mathbb{C}P}^{d}

has a canonical structure of smooth manifold and the restriction of the obstruction bundle
O_{pq}^{(d)}
to each fiber is canonically a smooth vector bundle. Moreover, each
g\in G_{pq}
induces diffeomorphisms between fibers and smooth isomorphisms between the fiberwise restrictions of the obstruction bundle.

Proof.

As the domain curve of elements in each fiber of
\pi_{pq}
is fixed, a fiber of
\pi_{pq}
is the zero locus of a smooth Fredholm section of a Banach vector bundle. The regularity along the vertical direction implies that the fibers are smooth because the complex structure on the domain is unchanged. For the same reason the restriction of
O_{pq}^{(d)}
to each fiber is smooth. Lastly, if
g\in G_{pq}
, the
g
induces a smooth identification between two Banach vector bundles which intertwines with the Fredholm sections.
∎

In fact, the forgetful map
\pi_{pq}:V_{pq}\to B_{pq}
is a
G_{pq}
-equivariant map between stratified spaces, where the underlying map between posets is the same as (
5.2
). Lemma
5.48
says that this map further has a fiberwise smooth structure. However, Lemma
5.48

does not
assert that the locally closed stratum
\partial^{\alpha}\mathring{V}_{pq}
has a smooth structure. Indeed, the
{\mathbb{A}}_{d}
-stratification on
B_{d}
ignores the appearance of sphere bubbles. More refined structures of
\pi_{pq}
will be explored in Section
6
.

5.4.
Global chart construction III

5.4.1.
Inductive construction of the thickened moduli spaces

Proposition 5.49
.

There exists an increasing sequence of positive integers

k_{1}<k_{2}<\cdots<k_{d}<\cdots

which satisfies the following condition: for each
d\geq 1
, each
pq
with
d_{pq}\leq d
, the thickened moduli space
V_{pq}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})
is regular near the zero locus of the Kuranishi map.

Proof.

This statement can be proved by induction on
d_{pq}
, combining the proof for the case of a single multi-layered thickening in Theorem
5.47
.
∎

Remark 5.50
.

One can choose the sequence
k_{1},k_{2},\ldots
such that the corresponding thickening of
{\rm PSS}
,
{\rm SSP}
,
{\rm pearl}
, and the homotopy moduli spaces are all regular (see Section
7
).

From now on we fix the sequence
k_{1}<k_{2}<\cdots
which satisfies the conditions of Proposition
5.49
. By shrinking to an open neighborhood of
S_{pq}^{-1}(0)
inside
V_{pq}
, one obtains a global Kuranishi chart. Just as Lemma
5.48
, there are also the natural forgetful maps

\pi_{pq}:V_{pq}\to B_{pq}.

These maps should be thought of as the natural forgetful map which takes a
J
-holomorphic curve to its moduli parameter in the Deligne–Mumford space.

5.4.2.
Product construction: the case of two factors

To obtain a K-chart lifting, one needs to define embeddings of products of charts into corresponding boundary strata of a bigger chart. We call the definition of such embeddings the product construction. This construction needs to be compatible with the product construction of the auxiliary moduli spaces
B_{d}
.

We first consider the case of two factors. Consider a nonempty codimension one stratum
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{prq}\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{pq}
. We would like to define an equivariant map

\vartheta_{prq}:V_{pr}\times V_{rq}\to\partial^{prq}V_{pq}

which lifts the map
\zeta_{prq}
(see (
5.6
) and (
5.10
)). Suppose a point
\tilde{x}_{pr}\in V_{pr}
is represented by a quadruple
(\Sigma_{pr},u_{pr},F_{pr},\eta_{pr})
and a point
\tilde{x}_{rq}\in V_{rq}
is represented by a quadruple
(\Sigma_{rq},u_{rq},F_{rq},\eta_{rq})
. Let
x_{pr}\in B_{pr}
,
x_{rq}\in B_{rq}
be the underlying stable maps into projective spaces. Then using the map
\zeta_{prq}:B_{pr}\times B_{rq}\to\partial^{prq}B_{pq}
one can define a point

x_{prq}:=\zeta_{prq}(x_{pr},x_{rq})

which can be represented by a stable cylinder
\underline{\smash{\mu}}_{prq}:\Sigma_{prq}\to\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}}
. Moreover, there is a natural framing

F_{prq}=(f_{0},\ldots,f_{d_{pq}})

which lifts
u_{prq}
defined as follows. Let
L_{prq}\to\Sigma_{prq}
be the pullback of
{\mathcal{O}}(1)\to\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}}
by
u_{prq}
which carries the pullback of the Fubini–Study metric. Then the map
u_{prq}
can be represented by a basis

f_{0},\ldots,f_{d_{pq}}

of
H^{0}(L_{prq})
which is well-defined up to a
{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
-factor. Moreover, the
M
-factors of
u_{pr}
and
u_{rq}
naturally define a map

u_{prq}:\Sigma_{prq}\to M

which converges to the correct periodic orbits at
z_{\pm}
. Lastly, one needs to define the combined vector
\eta_{prq}
.

We first describe the single-layered case. The map
u_{prq}:\Sigma_{prq}\to\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}}
induces linear embeddings

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{CP}^{d_{pr}}\cong Y_{pr}\subset\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}},\

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{CP}^{d_{rq}}\cong Y_{rq}\subset\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}}.

(5.39)

Indeed, using the notations similar to (
5.2.5
), the first embedding is

[z_{0},\ldots,z_{d_{pr}}]\mapsto[z_{0},\ldots,z_{d_{pr}},\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{d_{rq}}]

and the second embedding is

[z_{0},\ldots,z_{d_{rq}}]\mapsto[a_{0}z_{0},\ldots,a_{d_{pr}}z_{0},z_{1},\ldots,z_{d_{rq}}].

Here
(a_{0},\ldots,a_{d_{pr}})\in\mathbb{C}^{d_{pr}+1}
is the normalized evaluation of the first component at
z_{+}
. Over these embeddings there are the natural bundle maps

\displaystyle\ \vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 16.20967pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.53822pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{O}}(k)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.85304pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.50499pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 42.85304pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 40.20967pt\raise-32.49501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 40.20967pt\raise-32.49501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}},\

\displaystyle\ \vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 16.09738pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.53822pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 42.74075pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 56.27898pt\raise-23.50499pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-16.09738pt\raise-32.49501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{CP}^{d_{rq}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.09738pt\raise-32.49501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 40.09738pt\raise-32.49501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}.

By definition,

\eta_{pr}\in H^{0}\left(\overline{\rm Hom}(\iota_{F_{pr}}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pr}},u^{*}TM)\otimes\iota_{F_{pr}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k)\right)\otimes\overline{H^{0}(\iota_{F_{pr}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k))_{0}}.

Notice that there are natural linear inclusions

\displaystyle\ H^{0}(\iota_{F_{pr}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k))_{0}\hookrightarrow H^{0}(\iota_{F_{prq}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k))_{0},\

\displaystyle\ H^{0}(\iota_{F_{rq}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k))_{0}\hookrightarrow H^{0}(\iota_{F_{prq}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k))_{0}

defined by extending by zero. Moreover, the linear embeddings (
5.39
) induce bundle maps

\overline{\rm Hom}(\iota_{F_{pr}}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pr}},u_{pr}^{*}TM)\otimes\iota_{F_{pr}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k)\to\overline{\rm Hom}(\iota_{F_{prq}}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}},u_{prq}^{*}TM)\otimes\iota_{F_{prq}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k)

and

\overline{\rm Hom}(\iota_{F_{rq}}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d_{rq}},u_{rq}^{*}TM)\otimes\iota_{F_{rq}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k)\to\overline{\rm Hom}(\iota_{F_{prq}}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}},u_{prq}^{*}TM)\otimes\iota_{F_{prq}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k)

covering the inclusion maps between domains.
These are defined by extending elements in
Hom
by zero in the normal directions to
Y_{pr}
resp.
Y_{rq}
. As elements of
H^{0}(\overline{\rm Hom}(\iota_{F_{pr}}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pr}},u^{*}TM)\otimes\iota_{F_{pr}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k))
vanish at nodes (because of the holonomy of
\nabla^{TM}
), one naturally has the linear inclusions

H^{0}\left(\overline{\rm Hom}(\iota_{F_{pr}}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pr}},u_{pr}^{*}TM)\otimes\iota_{F_{pr}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k)\right)\to H^{0}\left(\overline{\rm Hom}(\iota_{F_{prq}}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}},u_{prq}^{*}TM)\otimes\iota_{F_{prq}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k)\right)

and

H^{0}\left(\overline{\rm Hom}(\iota_{F_{rq}}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d_{rq}},u_{rq}^{*}TM)\otimes\iota_{F_{rq}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k)\right)\to H^{0}\left(\overline{\rm Hom}(\iota_{F_{prq}}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}},u_{prq}^{*}TM)\otimes\iota_{F_{prq}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k)\right).

Therefore, by taking the sum of the images of
\eta_{pr}
and
\eta_{rq}
, one obtains an element

\eta_{prq}\in H^{0}\left(\overline{\rm Hom}(\iota_{F_{prq}}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}},u_{prq}^{*}TM)\otimes\iota_{F_{prq}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k)\right)\otimes\overline{H^{0}(\iota_{F_{prq}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k))_{0}}.

Consider the quadruple
(\Sigma_{prq},u_{prq},F_{prq},\eta_{prq})
. One can check that this is a framed cylinder of type
prq
which represents an element
\tilde{x}_{prq}
in the thickened moduli space
V_{prq}
. It is also straightforward to see that we just defined a continuous embedding

\vartheta_{prq}:V_{pr}\times V_{rq}\to\partial^{prq}V_{pq}

which is equivariant with respect to the group inclusion
G_{pr}\times G_{rq}\hookrightarrow G_{pq}
. Denote the image of
\vartheta_{prq}
by
V_{prq}
. The equivariance implies that one can extend
\vartheta_{prq}
to

\vartheta_{prq}^{\sim}:G_{pq}\times_{G_{prq}}(V_{pr}\times V_{rq})\to\partial^{prq}V_{pq}

(5.40)

whose image is denoted (in the same pattern as
B_{(d_{0},d_{1})}^{\sim}
) by

V_{prq}^{\sim}\subset\partial^{prq}V_{pq}\subset V_{pq}.

Remark 5.51
.

However, the restriction of the Kuranishi map
S_{pq}:V_{pq}\to E_{pq}
to the stratum
\partial^{prq}V_{pq}
does not match with the Kuranishi map of the product of
S_{pr}\times S_{rq}
. For example, if
x_{pr}\in V_{pr}
and
x_{rq}\in V_{rq}
have orthonormal framings, the corresponding framing on
\vartheta_{prq}(x_{pr},x_{rq})
is not necessarily orthonormal (with respect to the
L^{2}
-pairing). Nonetheless, the Hermitian matrix part of the Kuranishi maps are given by two different kinds of normalization scheme, which can be interpolated once we have an outer-collaring (see Subsection
5.7
).

Just as the space
B_{d}
, for
p<r<s<q
, the embeddings of the thickened moduli spaces
V_{pq}
fit in to the following commutative diagram

(5.41)

where the arrows are constructed from (
5.40
). Indeed, the associativity on the gluing of the underlying map
(u,\Sigma)
and the superposition of perturbation sections
\eta
is straightforward from definition, while the associativity of the framing component
F
follows from Proposition
5.22
.

Embedding obstruction bundles.
We now describe how the obstruction bundles
E_{pr}\to V_{pr}
and
E_{rq}\to V_{rq}
can be embedded into
E_{pq}
restricted to
V_{prq}
. Recall that

\displaystyle\ E_{pr}=O_{pr}\oplus Q_{pr},\

\displaystyle\ E_{rq}=O_{rq}\oplus Q_{rq}

where the
O
-factors are the spaces of the sections
\eta
and the
Q
-factors are the trivial bundle which are use to normalize the framings. The normalization strategy is to use the
L^{2}
-metric on the domain curves and to require the framings are orthonormal.

Indeed, the embeddings
\vartheta_{{prq}}
and
\vartheta_{{prq}}^{\sim}
described above illustrate how to embed the
O
-factors. Hence there are natural bundle maps

G_{pr}\times G_{rq}\hookrightarrow G_{pq}
, the above diagram lifts to

\textstyle{G_{pq}(E_{prq})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vartheta}_{prq}^{\sim}}
\textstyle{E_{pq}|_{\partial^{prq}V_{pq}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G_{pq}(V_{prq})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\vartheta_{prq}^{\sim}}
\textstyle{\partial^{prq}V_{pq}}

and we denote the image of
G_{pq}(E_{prq})
under the map
\widehat{\vartheta}_{prq}^{\sim}
by
E^{\sim}_{prq}
. For a quadruple of elements
p<r<s<q
, the following diagram lives over (
5.41
).

\textstyle{G_{pq}(E_{pr}\times E_{rs}\times E_{sq})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G_{pq}\big(E_{pr}\times\partial^{rsq}E_{rq}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G_{pq}\big(\partial^{prs}E_{ps}\times E_{sq}\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial^{prsq}E_{pq}}

(5.42)

Beyond the ingredients of showing the commutativity of (
5.41
), Proposition
5.26
covers the associativity of the superposition of the
Q
-component of the obstruction bundle.

5.4.3.
The general case

Now we describe the product construction in the general situation, i.e. with multi-layered thickenings and multiple breakings. Consider a stratum
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm Floer}_{pq}
. We can form the product chart

K_{\alpha}:=K_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times K_{r_{k}q}

whose domain is
V_{\alpha}:=V_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times V_{r_{l}q}
and whose obstruction bundle is

E_{\alpha}:=E_{pr_{1}}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus E_{r_{l}q}.

We would like to construct an equivariant embedding
V_{\alpha}
into
\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
and embed the obstruction bundle equivariantly as well.

To embed the thickened moduli space, the first step is to unify the number of layers. Recall that in the multi-layered thickening strategy, each moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{rs}
is thickened to
V_{rs}^{d_{rs}}
which depends on the sequence of integers
k_{1}<\cdots<k_{d_{rs}}
.

Lemma 5.52
.

There exists a natural
G_{rs}
-equivariant inclusion

V_{rs}^{d}\hookrightarrow V_{rs}^{d^{\prime}}\ {\rm if}\ d<d^{\prime}

defined by

[\Sigma,u,F,\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{d}]\mapsto[\Sigma,u,F,\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{d},\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{d^{\prime}-d}]

where the last
d^{\prime}-d
zeroes are the zero vectors of the bundles of
(u,\iota_{F})^{*}E_{k_{d+1}}
,
\ldots
,
(u,\iota_{F})^{*}E_{k_{d^{\prime}}}
.

Proof.

Indeed, as we use
\eta_{j}
to perturb the equation for
\eta_{i}
if
j>i
, the degree
d
version of (
5.32
) implies that the representative
(\Sigma,u,F,\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{d},\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{d^{\prime}-d})
satisfies the degree
d^{\prime}
version of (
5.32
). As the
G_{rs}
-action changes the framing
F
, such a map is indeed
G_{rs}
-equivariant.
∎

Now abbreviate
d=d_{pq}
. For
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
, one then obtains a
G_{\alpha}
-equivariant inclusion between the thickened moduli spaces

V_{\alpha}=V^{d_{pr_{1}}}_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times V^{d_{r_{l}q}}_{r_{l}q}\hookrightarrow V^{d}_{\alpha}:=V^{d}_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times V^{d}_{r_{l}q}.

The space
V^{d}_{\alpha}
on the right hand side can be embedded into
\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}^{d_{pq}}
. Indeed, it is straightforward to generalize the construction in the previous single-layered case with only two horizontal levels and the associativity can be verified as before.

Embedding obstruction bundles

Now for each stratum
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
, define the bundles

O_{\alpha}:=O_{pr_{1}\to pq}\oplus\cdots\oplus O_{r_{l}q\to pq}:=O_{pr_{1}}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus O_{r_{l}q}\to V_{\alpha}=V_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times V_{r_{l}q}

and

Q_{\alpha}:=Q_{pr_{1}\to pq}\oplus\cdots\oplus Q_{r_{l}q\to pq}:=Q_{pr_{1}}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus Q_{r_{l}q}\to V_{\alpha}=V_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times V_{r_{l}q}.

The construction of the two-factor case and the corresponding associativity induce
G_{\alpha}
-equivariant bundle embeddings

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 35.09947pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-35.09947pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E_{\alpha}:=O_{\alpha}\oplus Q_{\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.12718pt\raise 7.36111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.86111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\vartheta}_{\alpha}=\widehat{\vartheta}_{\alpha}^{O}\oplus\widehat{\vartheta}_{\alpha}^{Q}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 89.09947pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 89.09947pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{V_{\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.44724pt\raise-37.65276pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.93056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\vartheta_{\alpha}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 96.91287pt\raise-31.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 59.09947pt\raise-31.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 96.91287pt\raise-31.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Notation 5.53
.

The
G_{pq}
-equivariantization of the embedding
\vartheta_{\alpha}
is denoted by

\vartheta_{\alpha}^{\sim}:G_{pq}\times_{G_{\alpha}}V_{\alpha}\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}

and its image is denoted by

V_{\alpha}^{\sim}\subset\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}.

Points in
V_{\alpha}^{\sim}
are those maps whose underlying map into
\mathbb{CP}^{d}
are in
\delta(\alpha)
-normal position and whose obstruction vector are coming from factors of
\alpha
.

5.4.4.
More on obstruction bundles

Here we single out a particular pattern of the obstruction bundles which will be used several times in the rest of the paper. We fix
p,q\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}
and
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
. For each
d\geq d_{pq}
, one has part of the obstruction bundles

O_{pr_{1}}^{(d)}\to V_{pr_{1}},\ldots,O_{r_{l}q}^{(d)}\to V_{r_{l}q}.

Via the above product construction, denote their images by

O_{pr_{1}\to pq}^{(d)},\ldots,O_{r_{l}q\to pq}^{(d)}\to V_{\alpha}=V_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times V_{r_{l}q}.

They are embedded into the bundle
O_{pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{\alpha}}
. Denote their (direct) sum by

O_{\alpha}^{(d)}\to V_{\alpha}.

Notice that inside
O_{pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{\alpha}}
there are also subbundles which are embedded images of
O_{\beta}^{(d)}|_{V_{\alpha}}
for all intermediate strata
\beta
.

In many inductive constructions, we wouldl like to choose structures (such as inner products, connections, maps, etc.) on
O_{pq}^{(d)}
inductively. When we make this choice over a particular stratum
V_{\alpha}\subset V_{pq}
, we would like to extend the existing structures on those subbundles coming from product construction. These subbundles have overlaps and the existing structures agree on overlaps by the correct inductive hypothesis. However, this does not guarantee the existence of an extension; indeed some extra properties of these subbundles
O_{\beta}^{(d)}
is needed.

Lemma 5.54
.

Fix
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
. Consider the two subbundles

O_{pr_{l}\to pq}^{(d)}\to V_{pr_{l}q}\subset O_{pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{pr_{l}q}},O_{r_{1}q\to pq}^{(d)}\to V_{pr_{1}q}\subset O_{pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{pr_{1}q}}.

(1)

One has

O_{pr_{l}\to pq}^{(d)}\cap O_{r_{1}q\to pq}^{(d)}=O_{r_{1}r_{l}\to pq}^{(d)}.

(5.43)

(2)

For each
\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
with
\alpha\leq\beta
, there one has

O_{\beta}^{(d)}|_{V_{\alpha}}\subset O_{pr_{l}\to pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{\alpha}}+O_{r_{1}q\to pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{\alpha}}.

(5.44)

Proof.

We first consider the single-layered case. Choose a point
x\in V_{pr_{1}r_{l}q}
with underlying curve
\Sigma
. Recall that vectors in
O_{pq}
are
\eta\in H^{0}(Y)\otimes\overline{H^{0}(L)_{0}}
where
Y\to\Sigma
is a Hermitian vector bundle and
L\to\Sigma
is a holomorphic line bundle. Let
\Sigma_{r_{1}r_{l}}\subset\Sigma
be the union of components “between”
r_{1}
and
r_{l}
. Then a vector in the intersection (
5.43
) is
\eta\in H^{0}(Y)\otimes\overline{H^{0}(L)_{r_{1}r_{l}}}
where
H^{0}(L)_{r_{1}r_{l}}\subset H^{0}(L)_{0}
is the subspace of holomorphic sections supported in
\Sigma_{r_{1}r_{l}}
which vanish at the corresponding two nodes. On the other hand, recall
Y=\overline{\rm Hom}(\iota_{F}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d},u^{*}TM)\otimes\iota_{F}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k)
. As the underlying curve inside
\mathbb{CP}^{d}
is in normal position, there is a corresponding fan
W_{x,pr_{1}},W_{x,r_{1}r_{l}},W_{x,r_{l}q}
in
\mathbb{C}^{d+1}
in the normal position. The condition
\eta\in O_{pr_{l}\to pq}
implies that
\eta
vanishes on the normal direction of
W_{x,pr_{1}}+W_{x,r_{1}r_{l}}
; the condition
\eta\in O_{r_{1}\to pq}
implies that
\eta
vanishes on the normal direction of
W_{x,r_{1}r_{l}}+W_{x,r_{l}q}
. Therefore,
\eta
vanshes on the normal direction of
W_{x,r_{1}r_{l}}
. By the way we define the inclusion
O_{r_{1}r_{l}\to pq}\hookrightarrow O_{pq}
, we see that
\eta\in O_{r_{1}r_{l}\to pq}
. This proves (
5.43
) in the single layered case. The multi-layered case, more generally, can be proved by induction on the number of layers.

For (
5.44
), suppose
\beta=ps_{1},\ldots,s_{m}q
. Then
s_{1}\geq r_{1}
and
s_{m}\leq r_{l}
. Hence each summand of
O_{\beta}^{(d)}
i.e., one of
O_{ps_{1}\to pq}^{(d)}
,
\ldots
,
O_{s_{m}q\to pq}^{(d)}
, is either contained in
O_{pr_{l}\to pq}^{(d)}
or in
O_{r_{1}q\to pq}^{(d)}
.
∎

5.5.
Stabilization maps

Now we state our proposition regarding the stabilization maps.

Proposition 5.55
.

Fix
p<q
in
{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}
. There exist the following objects.

(1)

For each
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
, a
G_{\alpha}
-equivariant subbundle
F_{pq,\alpha}\subset E_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}}
which is complementary to
E_{\alpha}
and carries a stratification by
G_{\alpha}
-invariant subbundles

F_{\beta\alpha}\ \forall\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer},\alpha\leq\beta

such that

E_{\alpha}\oplus F_{\beta\alpha}=E_{\beta}|_{V_{\alpha}}.

(2)

A germ of
G_{\alpha}
-equivariant embedding

\theta_{pq,\alpha}:{\rm Stab}_{F_{pq,\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}

satisfying the following conditions.

(a)

Its
G_{pq}
-equivariantization is a germ of open embedding.

(b)

Its restriction to
V_{\alpha}
(the zero section) coincides with the embedding
\iota_{pq,\alpha}:V_{\alpha}\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
.

(c)

For each stratum
\beta
between
\alpha
and
pq
, one has

\theta_{pq,\alpha}\left({\rm Stab}_{F_{\beta\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\right)\subset\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}.

(5.45)

In fact we would like the maps constructed to have better properties. The discussion here is to showcase the analytical part of the construction. The above proposition is enough to establish the construction at this moment, i.e., Theorem
5.62
.

5.5.1.
The difference bundle

We first construct the bundle appeared in the stabilization map. Recall that
E_{pq}\to V_{pq}
is naturally decomposed into
O_{pq}
and
Q_{pq}
where the latter is a trivial bundle. The embedding
\iota_{pq,\alpha}:V_{\alpha}\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
is covered by a bundle embedding
O_{\alpha}\to O_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}
. We can take a
G_{pq}
-invariant inner product on
O_{pq}
and consider the orthogonal complement
O_{pq,\alpha}:=(O_{\alpha})^{\bot}
. On the other hand, we have already chosen a good complement of
Q_{\alpha}\subset Q_{pq}
, denoted by
Q_{pq,\alpha}
(which is trivial). Define

F_{pq,\alpha}:=O_{pq,\alpha}\oplus Q_{pq,\alpha}.

5.5.2.
Analytical setup

As expected, the stabilization map is constructed using the infinite-dimensional implicit function theorem in a global fashion. We need a global analytical setup for this operation.

Fix
pq
and abbreviate
d=d_{pq}
. For each point
\underline{\smash{x}}\in B_{d}
, its fiber in the universal curve is a well-defined prestable cylinder
\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}
over which there is a holomorphic map
\underline{\smash{\mu}}:\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}\to\mathbb{CP}^{d}
. Then there is a fiber bundle over
\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}\times M
denoted by

E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}\to\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}\times M.

More explicitly, in the single-layered case,

E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}=\overline{\rm Hom}(\underline{\smash{\mu}}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d},TM)\otimes\underline{\smash{\mu}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k)\otimes\overline{H^{0}(\underline{\smash{\mu}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k))_{0}}.

(5.46)

If we fix the point
\underline{\smash{x}}
, then the fiber
\pi_{pq}^{-1}(\underline{\smash{x}})
is a subset of the space of sections
(u,\eta):\Sigma\to E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}
where
u:\Sigma\to M
is a map and
\eta
is a section of
E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}|_{{\rm graph}(u)}
.

Now fix a positive integer
l
. Let
{\mathcal{C}}^{l}(x):=C^{l}(\Sigma,E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)})
be the space of
C^{l}
-sections
(u,\eta):\Sigma\to E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}
. Here we impose the asymptotic constraints at nodes and marked points to periodic orbits. Take the union over
B_{d}
, we obtained a well-defined topological space, denoted by
{\mathcal{C}}^{l}_{pq}
. It is also stratified by
{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
. Now notice that one has a
G_{pq}
-action on
{\mathcal{C}}^{l}_{pq}
and an invariant subspace

V_{pq}=V_{pq}^{(d)}\subset{\mathcal{C}}^{l}_{pq}.

In fact, the complexification
G_{pq}^{\mathbb{C}}
acts on
{\mathcal{C}}_{l}^{pq}
by transforming the underlying points
\underline{\smash{x}}
and the maps
(u,\eta)
, although
V_{pq}
is not
G_{pq}^{\mathbb{C}}
-invariant.

Now we consider the difference between
V_{\alpha}
and
\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
. We regard both as subspaces of the infinite-dimensional object, i.e.

V_{\alpha}\subset\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}\subset\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{l}.

We define an
approximate solution map

\theta_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm app}:{\rm Stab}_{F_{pq,\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{l}.

(5.47)

by

[\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta_{\alpha},\eta_{pq,\alpha},h_{pq,\alpha}]\mapsto({\rm Id}+\rho_{h_{pq,\alpha}})[\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta_{\alpha}+\eta_{pq,\alpha}].

Here
\rho_{h_{pq,\alpha}}
is the upper-triangular matrix defined in the proof of Proposition
5.24
. The matrix
{\rm Id}+\rho_{h_{pq,\alpha}}
acts on the framing map
\underline{\smash{\mu}}:\Sigma\to{\mathbb{C}P}^{d}
and the obstruction sections. This is a
G_{\alpha}
-equivariant map and satisfies

\theta_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm app}\left({\rm Stab}_{F_{\beta\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\right)\subset\partial^{\beta}{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{l}.

However, it is not necessarily an exact solution, because the extra
\eta_{pq,\alpha}
can perturb the equation and the equation is not invariant under the complexification of
G_{pq}
. We need to use right inverses to correct the solution.

Remark 5.56
.

When we construct stabilization maps inductively, the approximate solution map may not be just the linear addition in the fiber direction. In fact we may also need to deform the map
u
.

We first discuss the notion of linearizations of the defining equation of the thickened moduli space more carefully. Fix a real number
a>2
. Fix an element
x\in V_{pq}
.

Definition 5.57
.

Given
\underline{\smash{x}}\in B_{d}
, one has the Banach manifold
{\mathcal{B}}_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}}
of
W^{1,a}
-sections
(u,\eta):\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}\to E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}
which are asymptotic to prescribed periodic orbits and a Banach bundle
{\mathcal{E}}_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}}\to{\mathcal{B}}_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}}
of corresponding inhomogeneous terms of class
L^{a}
. Here the Banach norms are defined with respect to the cylindrical metric on cylindrical components and arbitrary smooth metric on spherical components.

For each point
x\in\pi_{pq}^{-1}(\underline{\smash{x}})
, the linearization of the defining equation at
x
is a Fredholm operator

D_{pq,x}:T_{x}{\mathcal{B}}_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}}|_{x}.

(5.48)

By the regularity statement Proposition
5.49
, the linearizations are always surjective in our setting. Then we can talk about bounded right inverses. A right inverse of
D_{pq,x}
is denoted by
T_{pq,x}
. When
pq
is understood, we use
D_{x}
and
T_{x}
instead.

To obtain global stabilization maps using the implicit function theorem, we need to choose a family of right inverses to the linearization. Obviously, we need a continuity condition. We define the notion of continuity by comparing with the local families obtained from the standard gluing procedure. Let us briefly recall the gluing construction in the context of pseudoholomorphic curves which should be most familiar to the reader. Given a nodal
J
-holomorphic curve
(\Sigma,u)
with a stable domain
\Sigma
therefore finite automorphism group, there exists a local universal unfolding of the domain
\Sigma
. There is also a way to identify any compact subset of the nodal
\Sigma
away from the nodes with a compact subset of nearby fibers. Choose a right inverse
T
at the point represented by
(\Sigma,u)
. Then using such identifications and certain cut-and-paste procedure, one can obtain a family of approximate right inverses for nearby solutions. For details, the reader could consult
[
Par16
, Equation (C.7.7)]
for instance. These approximate right inverses determine exact right inverses which have the same images, as one could read off from
[
Par16
, Equation (C.7.30)]
. If
\zeta
is a parameter in a neighborhood of the point represented by
(\Sigma,u)
, then we denote by
T_{\zeta}^{\rm glue}
. The construction further depends on some auxiliary data including Riemannian metrics which define the parallel transport to compare the deformation space between the central curve and nearby curves, and a cut-off function which is needed for the construction of the pre-gluing map
[
Par16
, Equation (C.7.9)]
. These data are referred to as a
gluing profile
.

Similar construction applies to our current situation for the thickened moduli spaces
V_{pq}
. Indeed, as the spaces
B_{d}
are locally isomorphic to the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,d^{\prime}+2}^{\mathbb{R}}
for some
d^{\prime}>0
,the space of stable marked cylinders we can compare nearby fibers of the fiberwise Banach manifolds and identify compact subsets away from nodes with subsets of nearby fibers. The corresponding gluing construction can be performed in a similar way. The reader could consult Proposition
6.22
for more arguments along this line.

Definition 5.58

(Continuous family of right inverses)
.

A family of right inverses
\{T_{x}\}_{x\in V_{pq}}
is said to be
continuous
at
x
if the following is true. On one hand, for each
x\in V_{pq}
and any representative
\tilde{x}=(\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta)
, the restriction of
T_{x}
to each irreducible component of the domain defines a right inverse for the restriction of the linearized Cauchy–Riemann operator. Then given a gluing profile for
\tilde{x}
, these right inverses induce a right inverse
T_{\zeta}^{\rm glue}
for any
\zeta
near
\tilde{x}
. On the other hand,
T_{x}
induces a family of right inverses
T_{\zeta}
over this open neighborhood. Then for each compact set
Z\subset\Sigma\setminus\{{\rm nodes}\}
there holds

\lim_{\zeta\to 0}\|T_{\zeta}^{\rm glue}-T_{\zeta}\|_{Z}=0.

Here we use the operator norm on the domain
Z
.

Remark 5.59
.

We left to the reader to check that the above notion of continuity is intrinsic, i.e., independent of the choice of the gluing profile. In fact one only needs to check that the family of right inverse obtained from one gluing profile is continuous with respect to another gluing profile.

Lemma 5.60
.

For each pair
p<q
in
{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}
, there exists a
G_{pq}
-equivariant continuous family of right inverses
T_{x}
for
x\in V_{pq}
.

Proof.

It suffices to construct a continuous family of right inverses without the equivariance condition as we can average over the symmetry group
G_{pq}
. Moreover, as any convex linear combination of two right inverses is still a right inverse, we only needs to construct continuous families locally and patch together using a continuous partition of unity. From the definition we know the local family of right inverses given by a gluing construction is locally continuous.
∎

Let us fix a choice of a
G_{pq}
-equivariant continuous family of right inverses
T_{x}
for
x\in V_{pq}
. Now using the product construction, for
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
, one obtains a fiberwise Banach manifold
{\mathcal{B}}_{\alpha,\underline{\smash{x}}}
for each
\underline{\smash{x}}\in B_{\delta(\alpha)}
and corresponding linearized Cauchy–Riemann operator
D_{\alpha,x}
at each
x\in\pi_{\alpha}^{-1}(\underline{\smash{x}})
. A chosen family of right inverses
T_{pr_{1}},\ldots,T_{r_{l}q}
provides a family of right inverse
T_{\alpha,x}
for all
x\in V_{\alpha}
. Now as we have another layer of thickening, and we would like to solve the corresponding equation for this “thicker” problem, we need to stabilize the right inverse.

First, notice that we have the natural inclusions

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 13.02301pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.88412pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{\alpha,\underline{\smash{x}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.16191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.16191pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.46599pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-13.02301pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{B}}_{\alpha,\underline{\smash{x}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.02301pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.02301pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{B}}_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

The differences are given by deformations/obstructions in the difference between the two bundles over
M
,
E_{\alpha,\underline{\smash{x}}}
and
E_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}}
. By looking at the thickened equation (
5.32
), we can see that the linearization at
x\in V_{\alpha}\subset\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
is block upper-triangular

D_{pq,x}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}D_{\alpha,x}&*\\
0&D_{pq,\alpha,x}\end{array}\right]

where
D_{pq,\alpha,x}
is a surjective linear Cauchy–Riemann operator. Hence we can extend
T_{\alpha,x}
to a block-upper triangular right inverse

T_{pq,x}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}T_{\alpha,x}&*\\
0&T_{pq,\alpha,x}\end{array}\right].

In fact this only depends on the choice of the right inverse
T_{pq,\alpha,x}
and we can make this choice continuously depending on
x
.

The last ingredient we need for applying the implicit function theorem is a choice of local charts in the Banach manifold and local frames of the Banach vector bundle. In fact there is a natural one coming from the Riemannian metric on
M
. For each
\underline{\smash{x}}\in B_{d}
, the fiberwise Banach manifold is a space of maps into the total space of a vector bundle
E_{pq,\underline{\smash{x}}}\to M
which is essentially a tensor bundle over
M
. Hence the parallel transport using the Levi–Civita connection associated to the metric
\omega(\cdot,J)
induces local charts of the fiberwise Banach manifold.

We would like to say that so far the right inverse and the local charts/frames are chosen for points which are in normal position. If we go slightly away from the locus of normal position, the parallel transport along shortest geodesics automatically produce a family of local charts/frames as well as right inverses.

5.5.3.
Definition of the stabilization map

Now we can describe the stabilization map. Let us temporarily denote by
{\mathcal{F}}_{pq}
the nonlinear Cauchy–Riemann operator (
5.32
). First, apply the approximate solution map (
5.47
)

\theta_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm app}:{\rm Stab}_{F_{pq,\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{l}.

(which is in fact only defined in a small disk bundle in
F_{pq,\alpha}
). Then there exists
\epsilon>0
such that for all
x\in V_{\alpha}
and
(\eta_{pq,\alpha},h_{pq,\alpha})\in F_{pq,\alpha}|_{x}
with
\|\eta_{pq,\alpha}\|+\|h_{pq,\alpha}\|<\epsilon
,
{\mathcal{F}}_{pq}(\theta^{\rm app}_{pq,\alpha}(x,\eta_{pq,\alpha},h_{pq,\alpha}))
is very small. Then using the local charts on the fiberwise Banach manifold induced from the Levi–Civita connection centered at
x
and the corresponding right inverse of the linear operator, one obtains an exact solution to
{\mathcal{F}}_{pq}(x)=0
which is sufficiently close to the approximate solution and its difference with the approximate solution is contained in the image of the right inverse.

There are several things to check. First, we need to show that the map is continuous. In fact, this is due to the continuity of the family of right inverses and approximate solutions and how Gromov topology on
V_{pq}
is defined. Second, we show that the map is injective. This is a consequence of the implicit function theorem and the fact that (on each fiber over
B_{pq}
), the approximate solution is transverse to the image of the right inverse we use. Third, we show that the map is onto an open subset. Indeed, from the construction we know that the stabilization map is onto an open subset on each fiber over
B_{d}
. As the image also covers a neighborhood of
B_{\delta(\alpha)}^{\sim}
inside
\partial^{\delta(\alpha)}B_{d}
, the stabilization map is onto an open subset. In fact, this is exactly the content of the gluing theorem presented in
[
Par16
, Appendix C]
. Again, the reader could refer to Proposition
6.22
to see how to implement the aforementioned theorem into our setting. Lastly, it satisfies the stratum-preserving property (
5.45
) which can be seen from the fact that the right inverses we used are all block upper-triangular.

Remark 5.61
.

It is natural to require the stabilization map to be compatible with compositions of chart embeddings. In fact to achieve this goal one needs to make a number of compatible choices, including showing that such compatible choices exist. The choices include a system of compatible inner products on the obstruction bundles, a system of stabilization maps, and a system of bundle identifications. The details are given in Appendix
C
.

5.6.
Weak K-chart lift

We summarize the construction we have done. The following theorem is a more comprehensive version of Theorem
5.11
.

Theorem 5.62
.

There exist the following objects.

(1)

A weak K-chart lift of
T^{\rm Floer}
(see Definition
5.9
), consisting of a collection of K-charts
K_{pq}=(G_{pq},V_{pq},E_{pq},S_{pq},\psi_{pq})
and a collection of weak K-chart embeddings

{\bm{\iota}}_{prq}:K_{pr}\times K_{rq}\rightharpoonup\partial^{prq}K_{pq}

(which satisfy the required conditions).

(2)

A collection of
G_{pq}
-equivariant stratified maps

Moreover, the collection of maps
\pi_{pq}
satisfy the following conditions.

Every fiber of
\pi_{pq}
has a canonical smooth structure and the restriction of

to each fiber of
\pi_{pq}
has a canonical smooth bundle structure. Moreover, each

induces diffeomorphisms between fibers and smooth bundle isomorphisms between the fiberwise restrictions of the obstruction bundles.

(2)

The collection of maps
\pi_{pq}
are compatible in the following sense. For each
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
, the products of the projections induces a map

\pi_{\alpha}:V_{\alpha}\to B_{\delta(\alpha)}.

Then the following diagram commutes.

5.7.
Outer collaring

We need to apply the outer-collaring construction in order to improve the weak K-chart lift constructed above to a K-chart lift and set space for the inductive smoothing procedure. Recall that the outer-collaring construction has been reviewed in Subsection
3.3
in the topological category, including both the case of a single stratified space or a flow category, bimodule, etc. There is no more complexity to generalize to the case of the K-chart lift
{\mathfrak{K}}^{\rm Floer}
. Indeed, for each pair
p<q
, if we apply the outer-collaring construction to the thickened moduli space
V_{pq}
(with a fixed width, say
1
), then one obtains a new
{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
-manifold, denoted by
V_{pq}^{+}
. The
G_{pq}
-action naturally extends to a
G_{pq}
-action on
V_{pq}^{+}
. The obstruction bundle
E_{pq}
and the Kuranishi section
S_{pq}
are also naturally extended to a bundle
E_{pq}^{+}\to V_{pq}^{+}
and a section
S_{pq}^{+}:V_{pq}^{+}\to E_{pq}^{+}
. The footprint map
\psi_{pq}:S_{pq}^{-1}(0)/G_{pq}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
is also canonically extended to a map

\psi_{pq}^{+}:(S_{pq}^{+})^{-1}(0)/G_{pq}\to(\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer})^{+}

where (recall that)
(\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer})^{+}
is the outer-collaring of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
. Notice that the structure maps required for a K-chart lift of a flow category are also obtained among the new charts via outer-collaring and their properties are still satisfied. In summary, one obtains a K-chart lift
({\mathfrak{K}}^{\rm Floer})^{+}
of the outer-collared flow category
(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}
.

In a similar way, one can apply the outer-collaring construction to the system of auxiliary moduli spaces. Each space
B_{d}
becomes a new space
B_{d}^{+}
and the projection maps
\pi_{pq}:V_{pq}\to B_{d_{pq}}
are extended to
\pi_{pq}^{+}:V_{pq}^{+}\to B_{d_{pq}}^{+}
with listed properties in Theorem
5.62
remain valid.

What we need to take care of is the smoothness of the outer-collared auxiliary moduli spaces. Though
B_{d}
is a smooth manifold with faces, it is not
a priori
clear whether
B_{d}^{+}
has an automatically induced smooth structure or if the outer-collaring of the maps
\zeta_{(d_{0},d_{1})}:B_{d_{0}}\times B_{d_{1}}\to\partial^{(d_{0},d_{1})}B_{d_{0}+d_{1}}
are still smooth. What we learned from the work of Fukaya–Oh–Ohta–Ono
[
FOOO
]
is that moduli spaces of pseudoholomorphic curves have a stronger structure near corners which comes from their “exponential decay estimates” associated to the gluing construction. This kind of structure is formalized in
[
FOOO20
, Chapter 25]
under the name “admissible smooth structure.” In short, an admissible structure on a smooth manifold (or orbifold) with faces is an atlas of smooth charts such that the coordinate changes among them satisfy certain exponential decay estimates near the boundary and corner; moreover, the outer-collaring of an admissible smooth manifold or orbifolds automatically carries an admissible smooth structure. This argument applies to our situation of
B_{d}
. Indeed,
B_{d}
comes from the moduli space of stable maps into
\mathbb{CP}^{d}
which is an algebraic object. The exponential decay estimate holds automatically, for both the original moduli and the real blowup
B_{d}
. Therefore, the outer-collaring
B_{d}^{+}
is still smooth and the embeddings

\zeta_{(d_{0},d_{1})}^{+}:B_{d_{0}}^{+}\times B_{d_{1}}^{+}\to B_{d_{0}+d_{1}}^{+}

are still smooth.

5.7.1.
Matching the Kuranishi sections

The collection of global Kuranishi charts constructed in the previous section does not have matching Kuranishi maps over boundary or corners. Using the room constructed by the outer-collaring operation, we can connect these Kuranishi maps.

Proposition 5.63
.

There exist a collection of
G_{pq}
-equivariant sections

\check{S}_{pq}^{+}:V_{pq}^{+}\to E_{pq}^{+}

satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

The restriction of
\check{S}_{pq}^{+}
to
V_{pq}\subset V_{pq}^{+}
coincides with
S_{pq}:V_{pq}\to E_{pq}
.

(2)

With respect to the direct sum decomposition
E_{pq}^{+}=O_{pq}^{+}\oplus Q_{pq}^{+}
, if we write
\check{S}_{pq}^{+}
as
\check{S}_{pq}^{O,+}\oplus\check{S}_{pq}^{Q,+}
, then
\check{S}_{pq}^{O,+}=S_{pq}^{O,+}
.

(3)

There is a homeomorphism

\psi_{pq}^{+}:(\check{S}_{pq}^{+})^{-1}(0)/G_{pq}\cong\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}.

(4)

Let
\check{\psi}_{pq}
be the corresponding extension of the footprint map. If we replace
S_{pq}^{+}
by
\check{S}_{pq}^{+}
and replace
\psi_{pq}^{+}
by
\check{\psi}_{pq}^{+}
for all
pq
, then the collection

({\mathfrak{K}}^{\rm Floer})^{+}:=\Big((K_{pq}^{+})_{p<q},({\bm{\iota}}_{prq}^{+})_{p<r<q}\Big)

is a K-chart lift of the flow category
(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}
.

Proof.

The failure of the naively obtained outer-collaring to be a K-chart lift is that the normalization condition (i.e. the section
S_{pq}^{Q,+}:V_{pq}^{+}\to Q_{pq}^{+}
) does not match with the products on the boundary. We need to interpolate between
S_{pq}^{Q,+}
and the products on the collar parts. Here we only describe the situation near a codimension one stratum
prq
. Let
(x,t)\in V_{pq}^{+}
be a point in the collar region
\partial^{prq}V_{pq}\times[-1,0]
. Geometrically,
x
is represented by a framed cylinder
(\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta)
where
\Sigma=\Sigma_{pr}\cup\Sigma_{rq}
and the map
\mu:\Sigma\to{\mathcal{C}}_{0,2}(d_{pq})
is represented by a frame

F=(f_{0},\ldots,f_{d_{pq}})\in(H^{0}(L_{u},\Sigma))^{d_{pq}+1}.

Proposition
5.24
tells us that the underlying map
\underline{\smash{\mu}}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}}
can be transformed canonically to a map
\underline{\smash{\mu}}^{\rm normal}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}}
via an invertible complex matirx of the form
I_{d_{pq}+1}+\rho_{Q}
. Choose a smooth cut-off function
\chi(t)
which is equal to
1
for
t
near
-1
and euqal to
0
for
t
near
0
. Then define

\underline{\smash{\mu}}^{t}=(I_{d_{pq}+1}+\chi(t)\rho_{Q})\underline{\smash{\mu}}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}}.

Then
\underline{\smash{\mu}}^{t}
is in normal position for
t
near
-1
and corresponding to a frame

F^{t}=(f_{0}^{t},\ldots,f_{d_{pq}}^{t})\in(H^{0}(L_{u},\Sigma))^{d_{pq}+1}

(which is well-defined up to
{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
). For those
t
near
-1
, the frame
F^{t}
can be reconstructed from a frame on the component
\Sigma_{pr}
and a frame on
\Sigma_{rq}
and one can interpolate between the normalization using from the
L^{2}
-inner product on
\Sigma
and the normalization using the product construction. The situation of the interpolation near deeper strata can be iterated by a routine induction argument.
∎

5.7.2.
Collar structure

The outer-collaring provides an analogous collar structure as in the case of derived orbifold liftings. To save notations we would rather view the collar structure as being given by “interior” collars than “exterior” ones. Therefore, we have obtained a K-chart lift of a flow category over
{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}
such that each chart
K_{pq}=(G_{pq},V_{pq},E_{pq},S_{pq})
has a system of collars

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 45.06264pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-45.06264pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 57.76045pt\raise 7.84723pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.37502pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm collar}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 99.11127pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 69.06264pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 99.11127pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E_{pq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 110.35976pt\raise-25.57895pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-37.24924pt\raise-32.91226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.70273pt\raise-40.14337pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.25891pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\theta_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm collar}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 99.06264pt\raise-32.91226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 69.06264pt\raise-32.91226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 99.06264pt\raise-32.91226pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{V_{pq}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}},\ \forall\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}

(5.49)

which are compatible in a sense similar to the requirements of collar structures of a derived orbifold lift stated in Definition
3.26
, where one needs to replace the notions for derived orbifold charts by the ones for K-charts, with extra care taken on the equivariance with respect to the Lie group actions. The readers are invited to fill in the details.

5.8.
Constructing scaffolding

The stabilization map constructed previously describes the relation between a product chart and the corresponding stratum of a larger thickened moduli space. However, to inductively construct the FOP perturbations, we need to specify such stabilization maps in all such situations and we require that the stabilization maps are compatible in some natural way. Such a requirement was packaged in terms of the notion “scaffolding” for derived orbifold lifts of a flow category and in particular, in the smooth category (see Definition
3.28
and Definition
3.29
). In our construction of the derived orbifold lift of
(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}
, we need a scaffolding-like structure to do smoothing. Hence, we first need such structures in the topological category, also in the equivariant setting but not in the orbifold setting. Below we state what we can construct in this setting; its proof is given in Appendix
C
. Just as (
5.49
), we simplify the notations by pretending that the K-charts are equipped with interior collars. The reader should keep in mind that the following proposition holds only after taking the outer-collaring. In the sequel, the resulting structure from the following proposition will be referred to as a
collared scaffolding
.

Proposition 5.64
.

There exist the following objects.

(1)

For each
pq
and each integer
d\geq d_{pq}
, a
G_{pq}
-invariant inner product on the bundle
O_{pq}^{(d)}\to V_{pq}
. To state the next class of objects, introduce the following notions. Let
O_{pq,\alpha}\subset O_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}}
be the orthogonal complement of
O_{\alpha}\to V_{\alpha}
and let
Q_{pq,\alpha}\subset Q_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}}
be the orthogonal complement of
Q_{\alpha}\to V_{\alpha}
(with resepct to the trivial inner product on
Q_{pq}
). Denote

F_{pq,\alpha}:=O_{pq,\alpha}\oplus Q_{pq,\alpha}\subset E_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}}

and its equivariantization

F_{pq,\alpha}^{\sim}:=G_{pq}\times_{G_{\alpha}}F_{pq,\alpha}\subset E_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}^{\sim}}.

(2)

For each stratum
\alpha
, a stratified topological embedding over the poset
\partial^{\alpha}{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm Floer}_{pq}

\theta_{pq,\alpha}:{\rm Stab}_{F_{pq,\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\hookrightarrow\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}

(5.50)

whose restriction to
V_{\alpha}
coincides with the embedding
\iota_{pq,\alpha}:V_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
such that its equivariantization

\theta_{pq,\alpha}^{\sim}:{\rm Stab}_{F_{pq,\alpha}^{\sim}}(V_{\alpha}^{\sim})\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}

is an open embedding. This embedding induces a projection map

\pi_{pq,\alpha}:\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}\to V_{\alpha}^{\sim}.

(5.51)

(3)

For each
d\geq d_{pq}
, an equivariant bundle isomorphism

\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{(d),\sim}:\pi_{pq,\alpha}^{*}\Big(O_{pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{\alpha}^{\sim}}\Big)\cong O_{pq}^{(d)}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}.

(5.52)

These objects need to satisfy the following list of conditions.

(A1)

For all
pq
and
d^{\prime}>d\geq d_{pq}
, the inclusion
O_{pq}^{(d)}\hookrightarrow O_{pq}^{(d^{\prime})}
(
5.34
) is isometric.

(A2)

For each stratum
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
of
pq
, the bundle embedding

\widehat{\iota}_{pq,\alpha}^{(d),\sim}:O_{pq,\alpha}^{(d)}\hookrightarrow O_{pq}^{(d)}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}

(5.53)

is isometric. By Lemma
5.65
below, it follows that, for any intermediate stratum
\beta=ps_{1}\cdots s_{m}q
between
\alpha
and
pq
, the following is true. Abbreviate
\alpha_{0}=pr_{1}\cdots s_{1}
,
\ldots
,
\alpha_{m}=s_{m}\cdots r_{l}q
as strata of
ps_{1}
,
\cdots
,
s_{m}q
respectively. Then

O_{pq,\alpha}\cong O_{pq,\beta}|_{V_{\alpha}}\oplus\left(O_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus O_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}\right).

(We explain the notations here. The bundles
O_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}
etc. in the second summand above are subbundles of
O_{ps_{1}}|_{V_{\alpha_{0}}}
etc. Their product is a subbundle of
O_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}}
.) It implies that the bundle
O_{pq,\alpha}
is stratified (linearly) by all strata between
\alpha
and the top stratum, where the stratum corresponding to the intermediate stratum
\beta
is the subbundle

O_{\beta\alpha}:=O_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus O_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}.

Similarly, the bundle
Q_{pq,\alpha}
is also stratified by subbundles
Q_{\beta\alpha}
where

Q_{\beta\alpha}=Q_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus Q_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}.

Denote

F_{\beta\alpha}:=O_{\beta\alpha}\oplus Q_{\beta\alpha}\subset F_{pq,\alpha}.

Then there is a splitting

F_{pq,\alpha}=F_{pq,\beta}|_{V_{\alpha}}\oplus F_{\beta\alpha}.

(5.54)

(B1)

The stabilization maps are stratum-preserving, i.e., for any intermediate stratum
\beta
between
\alpha
and the top stratum, there holds

\theta_{pq,\alpha}\Big({\rm Stab}_{F_{\beta\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\Big)\subset\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta},

where
F_{\beta\alpha}
is viewed as a subbundle of
F_{pq,\alpha}
under the splitting (
5.54
), and the space
V_{\beta}
is identified with its image in
\partial^{\beta}V_{pq}
under
\iota_{pq,\beta}
.

(B2)

The above restriction of the stabilization map coincides with the product of stabilization maps. More precisely this means the following. Abbreviate
\alpha_{0}=pr_{1}\cdots s_{1}
,
\ldots
,
\alpha_{m}=s_{m}\cdots r_{l}q
as strata of
ps_{1}
,
\cdots
,
s_{m}q
respectively. The stabilization maps
\theta_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}
,
\cdots
,
\theta_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}
are germs of homeomorphisms

\theta_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}:{\rm Stab}_{F_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}}(V_{\alpha_{0}})\to\partial^{\alpha_{0}}V_{ps_{1}},\ \cdots,\ \theta_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}:{\rm Stab}_{F_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}}(V_{\alpha_{m}})\to\partial^{\alpha_{m}}V_{s_{m}q}.

Their product gives a germ of homeomorphisms

{\rm Stab}_{F_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus F_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}}(V_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha_{0}}V_{ps_{1}}\times\cdots\times\partial^{\alpha_{m}}V_{s_{m}q}\hookrightarrow\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}.

Notice that the left hand side is just
{\rm Stab}_{F_{\beta\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})
. We require that

\theta_{pr_{1},\alpha_{0}}\times\cdots\times\theta_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}=\theta_{pq,\alpha}|_{{\rm Stab}_{F_{\beta\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})}.

(Compare this conditions with the conditions in Definition
3.29
).

(C1)

The bundle isomorphisms satisfy the following commutative diagram for all
d<d^{\prime}
.

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 83.87561pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-34.96843pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.32086pt\raise-33.59616pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{O_{pq}^{(d^{\prime})}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Hence they induce an isomorphism

\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O}:\pi_{pq,\alpha}^{*}\left(O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{V_{\alpha}^{\sim}}\right)\cong O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}.

(C2)

The bundle isomorphism
\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O}
preserves the stratification. Namely, for any intermediate stratum
\beta
between
\alpha
and
pq
, one has

\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O}\left(\pi_{pq,\alpha}^{*}(O_{\beta}|_{V_{\alpha}^{\sim}})\right)=O_{\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}.

(D)

Let
\zeta_{pq,\alpha}=\zeta_{pq,\beta}+\zeta_{\beta\alpha}
be a point of
F_{pq,\alpha}
at a point
x_{\alpha}\in V_{\alpha}
with respect to the splitting (
5.54
). Then the above condition (C3) implies that the bundle isomorphism
\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O}
identifies
\zeta_{pq,\beta}
with a vector of
F_{pq,\beta}
at
x_{\beta}=\theta_{pq,\alpha}(x_{\alpha},\zeta_{\beta\alpha})\in\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}
. Then we require that

\theta_{pq,\beta}(x_{\beta},\zeta_{pq,\beta})=\theta_{pq,\alpha}(x_{\alpha},\zeta_{pq,\alpha}).

This implies that

\pi_{pq,\alpha}\circ\pi_{pq,\beta}=\pi_{pq,\alpha}.

(5.55)

(E)

The above implies that

\pi_{pq,\alpha}^{*}(O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{V_{\alpha}^{\sim}})=\pi_{pq,\beta}^{*}\left(\pi_{pq,\alpha}^{*}(O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{V_{\alpha}^{\sim}})|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}^{\sim}}\right).

We require that the bundle isomorphism satisfies

\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O}=\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\beta}^{O}\circ\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O}.

(5.56)

More precisely, using notations in condition (D) above, the following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{x_{\alpha}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O}}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O}}
\textstyle{O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{x_{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\beta}^{O}}
\textstyle{O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{\theta_{pq,\alpha}(x_{\alpha},\zeta_{pq,\alpha})}}

(F)

Given any intermediate stratum
\beta=ps_{1}\cdots s_{m}q
, using the notations used in (B2), one has

\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}}=\widehat{\theta}_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}^{O}\times\cdots\times\widehat{\theta}_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}^{O}.

(G)

The metric on
O_{pq}^{(d)}
, the stabilization map
\theta_{pq,\alpha}
, and the bundle isomorphism
\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{O}
respect collars. More precisely,

(a)

The bundle isomorphism associated to the collar structure
\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm collar}
is isometric. It follows that following diagram commutes

\textstyle{F_{pq,\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm collar}}
\textstyle{F_{pq,\beta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}^{\rm collar}}
\textstyle{E_{pq}|_{V_{\beta}}}

(b)

The following diagram commutes.

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 71.35017pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-71.35017pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise The following diagram commutes.

\textstyle{(\pi_{pq,\beta}^{*}O_{pq}^{\infty}|_{V_{\beta}^{\sim}})|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

.

Lemma 5.65
.

Suppose the bundles
O_{pq}^{(d)}\to V_{pq}
are equipped with
G_{pq}
-invariant inner products which satisfy conditions (A1) and (A3) of Proposition
5.64
. Then the following is true. Consider a pair of strata
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
where
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
and
\beta=ps_{1}\cdots s_{m}q
. Denote
\alpha_{0}=pr_{1}\cdots s_{1}
,
\ldots
,
\alpha_{m}=s_{m}\cdots r_{l}q
. Consider the bundles

O_{ps_{1},\alpha_{0}}\to V_{\alpha_{0}},\ \cdots,\ O_{s_{m}q,\alpha_{m}}\to V_{\alpha_{m}}

whose product is a subbundle

O_{\beta\alpha}\subset O_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}}.

Then as subbundles of
O_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}}
, one has

O_{pq,\alpha}=O_{pq,\beta}|_{V_{\alpha}}\oplus O_{\beta\alpha}.

Proof.

Linear algebra.
∎

6.
Smoothing
