In this section, we use equivariant (relative) smoothing theory to equip the K-chart presentation of various moduli spaces constructed in Section
5
with smooth structures after stabilization. The proof is based on induction, which is quite similar to the construction of straightening and coherent FOP perturbations discussed in Section
3
and Section
4
. After taking the associated quotient orbifold of the smooth K-chart presentation, we obtain a derived orbifold lift of the Hamiltonian Floer flow category
T^{\rm Floer}
. Moreover, we explain how to construct a normal complex structure on the resulting derived orbifold lift.

6.1.
Preliminaries

We list out some basic notions relevant for the smoothing theory in this subsection. Then we state the relative smoothing statement Theorem
6.10
which will proved in Appendix
B
. Following that, some elementary technical results in the smoothing construction will be proved.

6.1.1.
Microbundles

We start by discussing some notions and facts about microbundles and their roles in smoothing theory.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
X
be a topological space.

(1)

A
microbundle
(of rank
n
) over
X
is a diagram

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{X}

where
{\mathscr{E}}
is a topological space,
p
and
s
are continuous maps satisfying

(a)

p\circ s={\rm Id}_{X}
.

(b)

for each
x\in X
there exist a neighborhood
U_{x}\subset X
of
x
, a neighborhood
{\mathscr{V}}_{x}\subset{\mathscr{E}}
of
s(x)
, and a homeomorphism

h_{x}:U_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathscr{V}}_{x}

for which
p\circ h_{x}={\rm pr}_{U_{x}}
and
h_{x}|_{U_{x}\times\{0\}}=s
. Here
{\rm pr}_{U_{x}}
is the natural projection onto the factor
U_{x}
.

Often we use a single symbol
{\mathscr{E}}
to denote a microbundle.

(2)

Given two microbundles over
X
(not necessarily of the same rank)

\displaystyle\ \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.53471pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.2517pt\raise 5.00694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{s_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 53.25824pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 72.8264pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 72.8264pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,\

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s_{2}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{2}}
\textstyle{X}

a
morphism
from
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
to
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
is a germ of continuous maps
\phi:U_{1}\to E_{2}
defined over a neighborhood
{\mathscr{U}}_{1}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
of
s_{1}(X)
which commutes with the maps
s_{1},s_{2}
and
p_{1},p_{2}
. Two morphisms
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
from
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
to
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
, defined over
{\mathscr{U}}_{1}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
and
{\mathscr{U}}_{2}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
respectively, are called
equivalent
if there exists a neighborhood of
s_{1}(X)
in
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
contained in
{\mathscr{U}}_{1}\cap{\mathscr{U}}_{2}
on which
\phi_{1}=\phi_{2}
.

(3)

Using the notations as above, a morphism between microbundles

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{E}}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{E}}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}

is said to be an
isomorphism
if
\phi
maps
{\mathscr{U}}_{1}
homeomorphically onto an open subset
{\mathscr{U}}_{2}\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
containing
s_{2}(X)
.

(4)

An
isotopy
between two isomorphisms
\phi_{0},\phi_{1}:{\mathscr{E}}\to{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}
of microbundles over
X
is a family of microbundle isomorphisms
\tilde{\phi}_{t}:{\mathscr{E}}\to{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}
,
t\in[0,1]
which defines a continuous map from
{\mathscr{E}}\times[0,1]\to{\mathscr{E}}^{\prime}
, such that
\tilde{\phi}_{0}
is equivalent to
\phi_{0}
and
\tilde{\phi}_{1}
is equivalent to
\phi_{1}
.

Example 6.2
.

(1)

If
p:E\to X
is an ordinary real vector bundle, then together with the zero section
E
defines a microbundle over
X
. We denote this microbundle by
E_{\mu}
.

(2)

Let
X
be a topological manifold. The
tangent microbundle
is the diagram

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta}
\textstyle{X\times X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\textstyle{X}

where
p_{1}
is the projection onto the first factor. We denote the tangent microbundle of
X
by
T_{\mu}X
.

If a topological manifold admits a smooth structure, then its tangent microbundle is isomorphic to the associated tangent bundle. In general, a microbundle
{\mathscr{E}}
over a space
X
may or may not come from a vector bundle. We recall the following definition.

Definition 6.3
.

Let
{\mathscr{E}}\to X
be a microbundle. A
vector bundle lift
(or
vector bundle reduction
) of
{\mathscr{E}}
is a vector bundle
E\to X
together with a microbundle isomorphism
E_{\mu}\to{\mathscr{E}}
.

Microbundles have many properties similar to vector bundles. One can construct microbundles by taking certain operations.

Definition 6.4

(Direct sums of microbundles)
.

Let
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s_{i}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{i}}
\textstyle{X}
,
i=1,2
be two microbundles and let
\Delta:X\to X\times X
be the diagonal inclusion map. Let

{\mathscr{E}}_{12}:=(p_{1}\times p_{2})^{-1}(\Delta(X))\subset{\mathscr{E}}_{1}\times{\mathscr{E}}_{2}.

Then the
direct sum
of
{\mathscr{E}}_{1}
and
{\mathscr{E}}_{2}
is the microbundle

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.53471pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.27419pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(s_{1}\times s_{2})\circ\Delta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 61.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}_{12}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 95.66237pt\raise 5.84723pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.48613pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p_{1}\times p_{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 136.81255pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 106.81255pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 136.81255pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Delta(X)\cong X}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

On the other hand, the notions of pullbacks and restrictions of microbundles can be defined in the same way as for the case of vector bundles. The correspondence
E\mapsto E_{\mu}
commutes with these operations.

For the purpose of discussing the uniqueness of stable (equivariant) smoothings later, we need to compare different vector bundle lifts.

Definition 6.5
.

(
[
Las79
]
)
Let
\phi_{i}:(E_{i})_{\mu}\to{\mathscr{E}}
,
i=0,1
be two vector bundle lifts of a microbundle
{\mathscr{E}}
over
X
. We say that
\phi_{0}
and
\phi_{1}
are

(1)

equivalent
if there exists a vector bundle isomorphism
\psi:E_{0}\to E_{1}
(which induces a microbundle isomorphism
\psi:(E_{0})_{\mu}\to(E_{1})_{\mu}
such that
\phi_{0}
is equivalent to
\phi_{1}\circ\psi
,

(2)

isotopic
if there exist a vector bundle
E\to X\times[0,1]
and a continuous family of vector bundle lifts
\phi_{t}:(E|_{X\times\{t\}})_{\mu}\to{\mathscr{E}}
which restricts to
\phi_{0}
and
\phi_{1}
when
t=0,1
.

Example 6.6
.

Suppose
M
is a smooth manifold. Then there is a canonical isotopy class of vector bundle reductions of
T_{\mu}M
. Choose a Riemannian metric
g
on
M
and let
\exp
be the associated exponential map. Then there is an open neighborhood
U(TM)
of the zero section of
TM
such that the exponential map is defined over
U(TM)
. Then define

\varphi_{g}:U(TM)\to M\times M,\ (x,\xi)\mapsto(x,\exp_{x}\xi)

which is a vector bundle reduction of
T_{\mu}M
. Moreover, the vector bundle reduction defined by any two Riemannian metrics are isotopic as microbundle isomorphisms.

Now we discuss the equivariant situation.

Definition 6.7
.

Let
G
be a topological group and
X
be a
G
-space. A
G
-microbundle
is a microbundle
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{X}
where
{\mathscr{E}}
is a
G
-space such that
p
and
s
are both equivariant.

All previous notions for non-equivariant microbundles can be easily extended to the equivariant situation. We just emphasize that if
X
is a
G
-topological manifold, then its tangent microbundle
T_{\mu}X
is a
G
-microbundle; if
G
is a compact Lie group and
X
is a smooth
G
-manifold, then its tangent microbundle
T_{\mu}X
admits a
G
-equivariant vector bundle lift (by its smooth tangent bundle); a concrete lift can be given via the exponential map associated to a
G
-invariant Riemannian metric; moreover, the equivariant isotopy class of such a lift is unique.

6.1.2.
Stable equivariant smoothings

Let
M
be a topological manifold (without boundary). A
smoothing
of
M
is by definition a pair
(N,\phi)
where
N
is a smooth manifold and
\phi:N\to M
is a homeomorphism. A smoothing is also equivalent to a smooth structure
\alpha
on
M
, i.e., a maximal atlas of
C^{\infty}
compatible charts. Two smooth structures
\alpha_{0}
,
\alpha_{1}
on
M
are called
isotopic
if there is an isotopy (through homeomorphisms) between
{\rm Id}_{M}
and a diffeomorphism
\phi:M_{\alpha_{0}}\to M_{\alpha_{1}}
, where
M_{\alpha_{i}}
is the smooth manifold given by equipping
M
the smooth structure
\alpha_{i}
.

If
G
is a compact Lie group acting on
M
, then a
G
-smoothing
is a smooth structure
\alpha
on
M
such that the
G
-action is smooth. Two
G
-smoothings
\alpha_{0}
and
\alpha_{1}
are
G
-isotopic if there is an isotopy through
G
-equivariant homeomorphisms between
{\rm Id}_{M}
and a diffeomorphism from
M_{\alpha_{0}}
to
M_{\alpha_{1}}
.

For the non-equivariant case, one can see that a necessary condition for a topological manifold being smoothable is that its tangent microbundle admits a vector bundle lift. In fact, this is almost also a sufficient condition. Once the tangent microbundle of a topological manifold
M
admits a vector bundle lift, then
M
is
stably smoothable
, i.e., there is a smoothing
(M\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k})_{\alpha}
on
M\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
for some
k\geq 0

[
Mil64
, Section 5]
. Two stable smoothings
(M\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{i}})_{\alpha_{i}}
,
i=0,1
, are
stably isotopic
if there exist
k_{0}^{\prime},k_{1}^{\prime}\geq 0
with
k_{0}+k_{0}^{\prime}=k=k_{1}+k_{1}^{\prime}
and an isotopy between
(M\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{0}})_{\alpha_{0}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{0}^{\prime}}
and
(M\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{1}})_{\alpha_{1}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{1}^{\prime}}
as smoothings on
M\times{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
, where the Euclidean spaces are endowed with the standard smooth structure. The work of Lashof
[
Las79
]
basically shows that this correspondence extends to the
G
-equivariant case. Let us introduce the notion of stable
G
-smoothings.

Definition 6.8
.

[
Las79
]

Let
G
be a compact Lie group and
M
be a topological
G
-manifold.

(1)

A
stable
G
-smoothing
of
M
is a
G
-smoothing on the product
M\times{\bm{R}}
where
{\bm{R}}
is a finite-dimensional orthogonal representation of
G
.

(2)

Two stable
G
-smoothings,
(M\times{\bm{R}}_{i})_{\alpha_{i}}
,
i=0,1
, of
M
, are
stably
G
-isotopic
, if there exist orthogonal representations
{\bm{R}}_{0}^{\prime}
,
{\bm{R}}_{1}^{\prime}
of
G
such that
{\bm{R}}_{0}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{0}^{\prime}\cong{\bm{R}}\cong{\bm{R}}_{1}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{1}^{\prime}
as
G
-representations and
(M\times{\bm{R}}_{0})_{\alpha_{0}}\times{\bm{R}}_{0}^{\prime}
is isotopic to
(M\times{\bm{R}}_{1})_{\alpha_{1}}\times{\bm{R}}_{1}^{\prime}
as
G
-smoothings on
M\times{\bm{R}}
.

(3)

A
stable
G
-vector bundle lift
of
T_{\mu}M
is a
G
-vector bundle lift of
T_{\mu}M\oplus R
, where
R\to M
is the trivial bundle
M\times{\bm{R}}
with
{\bm{R}}
being an orthogonal representation of
G
.

(4)

Two stable
G
-vector bundle lifts,
\phi_{i}:(E_{i})_{\mu}\cong T_{\mu}M\oplus R_{i}
,
i=0,1
, are
stably
G
-isotopic
, if there are orthogonal
G
-representations
{\bm{R}}_{0}^{\prime}
,
{\bm{R}}_{1}^{\prime}
, with
{\bm{R}}_{0}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{0}^{\prime}\cong{\bm{R}}\cong{\bm{R}}_{1}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{1}^{\prime}
such that the induced
G
-vector bundle lifts

\phi_{i}\oplus{\rm Id}_{R_{i}^{\prime}}:(E_{i}\oplus R_{i}^{\prime})_{\mu}\cong T_{\mu}M\oplus R_{i}\oplus R_{i}^{\prime}\cong T_{\mu}M\oplus R,\ i=0,1

are
G
-isotopic.

Any stable
G
-smoothing
(M\times{\bm{R}})_{\alpha}
of
M
induces a stable
G
-vector bundle lift

T(M\times{\bm{R}})_{\alpha}\to T_{\mu}M\oplus R.

However it is not obvious that the stable
G
-isotopy class of the lift only depends on the stable
G
-isotopy class of the smoothing. By using an intermediate notion of stable sliced concordance among stable
G
-smoothings, Lashof
[
Las79
]
showed that there is indeed such a correspondence sending stable
G
-isotopy classes of stable
G
-smoothings to stable
G
-isotopy classes of stable
G
-vector bundle reductions of
T_{\mu}M
. In fact, this correspondence is bijective.

Theorem 6.9
.

[
Las79
]
Let
G
be a compact Lie group and
M
be a topological
G
-manifold which only has finitely many orbit types. Suppose
E\to M
is a
G
-equivariant vector bundle and
\varphi:E_{\mu}\to T_{\mu}M
is a
G
-equivariant vector bundle reduction of
T_{\mu}M
, then there exists an orthogonal
G
-representation
{\bm{R}}
and a
G
-smoothing
\alpha
on
M\times{\bm{R}}
satisfying

(1)

There is a
G
-equivariant vector bundle isomorphism
\rho:p_{M}^{*}E\oplus R\cong T(M\times{\bm{R}})_{\alpha}
where
p_{M}:M\times{\bm{R}}\to M
is the natural projection.

(2)

Denote by
\rho_{\mu}
the induced map between microbundles from
\rho
. Then the vector bundle reduction
\exp_{M\times R}\circ\rho_{\mu}:p_{M}^{*}E_{\mu}\oplus R\to T_{\mu}(M\times{\bm{R}})
is isotopic to the stabilization
p_{M}^{*}\varphi\oplus{\rm Id}_{R}
.
17
17

17

This claim is not explicitly stated in
[
Las79
]
but can be observed from Lashof’s construction.

Moreover, the correspondence
\varphi\mapsto\alpha
induces a bijection between stable isotopy classes of stable
G
-smoothings of
M
and stable isotopy classes of stable
G
-vector bundle reductions of
T_{\mu}M
.

Our construction relies on the following relative version of Lashof’s theorem.

Theorem 6.10
.

Let
G
,
M
,
E
, and
\varphi
be as in Theorem
6.9
. Let
C\subset M
be a
G
-invariant closed set and
U\subset M
be a
G
-invariant open neighborhood of
C
. Suppose

(1)

U
is equipped with a
G
-smoothing
\alpha_{0}
and
E|_{U}
is equipped with a smooth
G
-equivariant vector bundle structure.

(2)

\varphi|_{U}:(E|_{U})_{\mu}\to T_{\mu}U
is a smooth microbundle reduction and is isotopic to the microbundle reduction
TU_{\alpha_{0}}\to T_{\mu}U
.

Then there exists an orthogonal
G
-representation
{\bm{R}}
and a
G
-smoothing
\alpha
on
M\times{\bm{R}}
such that over a
G
-invariant open neighborhood
U^{\prime}\subset U
of
C
,
(U^{\prime}\times{\bm{R}})_{\alpha}
is diffeomorphic to the product
(U^{\prime})_{\alpha_{0}}\times{\bm{R}}
.

Proof.

See Appendix
B
.
∎

6.1.3.
Topological submersions

Now we start to prepare for smoothing the K-chart lift of the Hamiltonian Floer flow category following a generalization of the strategy used in
[
AMS21
]
. First, we discuss topological submersions which are relevant for constructing lifts of tangent microbundles.

Definition 6.11
.

Let
M
be an
{\mathbb{A}}
-space and
B
be a smooth
{\mathbb{A}}^{\prime}
-manifold. Let
\pi:M\to B
be a continuous map covering a map between the posets
{\mathbb{A}}\to{\mathbb{A}}^{\prime}
.

(1)

Let
p\in M
and
b=\pi(p)\in B
. A
product neighborhood
of
p
is a pair
(W,\iota)
where
W
is an open neighborhood of
p
(denoting
W|_{b}=\pi^{-1}(b)\cap W
) and
\iota:W\to W|_{b}\times\pi(W)
is an homeomorphism satisfying

•

\pi(W)
is an open neighborhood of
b
;

•

\pi\circ\iota^{-1}:W|_{b}\times\pi(W)\to\pi(W)
is the projection to
\pi(W)
;

•

\iota|_{W|_{b}}:W|_{b}\to W|_{b}\times\{b\}
is the identity map.

(2)

The map
\pi
is called a
topological submersion
if every point
p\in M
admits a product neighborhood and each fiber is a topological manifold (of a fixed dimension) without boundary. In this case, the
vertical tangent microbundle

T_{\mu}^{\rm vt}M
is the microbundle

\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta}
\textstyle{M\times_{B}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\textstyle{M}

where
p_{1}:M\times_{B}M\to M
is the projection to the first factor, viewing the fiber product
M\times_{B}M
as a subset of
M\times M
.

Remark 6.12
.

Our primary examples of topological submersions come from the forgetful map
\pi_{pq}:V_{pq}\to B_{pq}
in (
5.38
). It is important to notice that the fibers are indeed topological manifolds without boundary, because the complex structure on the domain of the element
(\Sigma,u,F,\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{d})
has been fixed.

Now we include group actions. Let
G
be a compact Lie group. Recall that a
G
-action on an
{\mathbb{A}}
-space is a continuous
G
-action which preserve each stratum. We assume that
G
acts continuously on
M
and smoothly on
B
, and that
\pi:M\to B
is
G
-equivariant. Then for each
g\in G
,
p\in M
, and a product neighborhood
(W,\iota)
of
p
,
(g(W),g_{*}\iota)
is a product neighborhood of
g(p)
, where

g_{*}\iota(w)=g(\iota(g^{-1}(w))).

Here
g:W|_{b}\times\pi(W)\to W|_{g(b)}\times\pi(g(W))
is the diagonal action.

Definition 6.13
.

[
AMS21
, Definition 4.19]

For each
p\in M
, denote by
G_{p}\subset G
the stabilizer of
p
. A
G_{p}
-invariant product neighborhood of
p
is a product neighborhood
(W,\iota)
of
p
which is
G_{p}
-invariant, i.e.,
(g(W),g_{*}\iota)=(W,\iota)
for any
g\in G_{p}
.

We say that the
G
-equivariant map
\pi:M\to B
is a
G
-equivariant topological submersion
if every point
p\in M
admits a
G_{p}
-invariant product neighborhood. We say that the
G
-action is
fiberwise locally linear
if for each
b\in B
, the action of
G_{b}\subset G
on
M_{b}
is locally linear.

Remark 6.14
.

Any
G
-equivariant vector bundle
p:E\to B
is a fiberwise locally linear equivariant topological submersion.

Now we recall how to construct smoothings of the total space of a topological submersion with a fiberwise smooth structure.

Definition 6.15
.

[
AMS21
, Definition 4.27]

Let
G
be a compact Lie group. Let
M
be a topological manifold with a
G
-action. Let
B
be a smooth
G
-manifold and
\pi:M\to B
be a
G
-equivariant topological submersion.

(1)

A
fiberwise smooth structure
on
M
(with respect to the map
\pi
) is a choice of smooth structures (by definition a maximal
C^{\infty}
-compatible atlas) on all fibers of
\pi
.

(2)

Given a fiberwise smooth structure on
M
, two product neighborhoods
\iota_{i}:W_{i}\to W_{i}|_{b_{i}}\times\pi(W_{i})
,
i=1,2
are
C_{\rm loc}^{1}
-compatible
if for each
p\in W_{1}\cap W_{2}
there exists a product neighborhood
\iota:W\to W|_{b}\times\pi(W)
,
b=\pi(p)
satisfying the following conditions. For each
v\in\pi(W)
, the definition of product neighborhoods (see Definition
6.11
) implies that the map

\iota_{v}:W|_{v}\to\{v\}\times W|_{b}

is a homeomorphism. Consider the family of maps

\eta_{v}:W|_{b}\to W_{i}|_{b_{i}},\ w\mapsto\Pi_{i}\big(\iota_{i}(\iota_{v}^{-1}(w))\big)

where
\Pi_{i}:W_{i}|_{b_{i}}\times\pi(W_{i})\to W_{i}|_{b_{i}}
is the projection. Then for each
v
and
i=1,2
,
\eta_{v}
is a smooth map from
W|_{b}
to
W_{i}|_{b_{i}}
and varies with
v
continuously with respect to the
C_{\rm loc}^{1}
-topology.

(3)

A
fiberwise smooth
C_{\rm loc}^{1}

G
-bundle
is a
G
-equivariant topological submersion
\pi:M\to B
together with a collection
(\iota_{i})_{i\in I}
of
C_{\rm loc}^{1}
-compatible
G_{p}
-invariant product neighborhoods around a collection of points
(p_{i})_{i\in I}
whose domains cover
M
.

Remark 6.16
.

Despite our previous discussions work under the setting that
M
is an
{\mathbb{A}}
-manifold, the above notions is defined for
M
being a manifold without boundary. In practice, when we perform smoothing inductively, we apply Theorem
6.10
to the top stratum of a topological
{\mathbb{A}}
-manifold
M
. Therefore, it suffices to set up certain parts of the theory for manifolds without boundary or corners.

6.1.4.
Fiberwise submersions

A fiberwise smooth
C_{\rm loc}^{1}
-bundle
\pi:M\to B
has a well-defined
vertical tangent bundle
, denoted by
T^{\rm vt}M
, by patching up the tangent bundles of the fibers, and an isotopy class of
G
-vector bundle lift

T^{\rm vt}M\to T_{\mu}^{\rm vt}M

by using a
G
-invariant fiberwise Riemannian metric (see
[
AMS21
, Lemma 4.29]
).

Now suppose
\pi:M\to B
is a fiberwise smooth
C_{\rm loc}^{1}

G
-bundle. There is a well-defined
G
-microbundle

\pi^{*}T_{\mu}B\oplus T^{\rm vt}_{\mu}M,

a well-defined
G
-vector bundle

\pi^{*}TB\oplus T^{\rm vt}M,

and a well-defined
G
-isotopy class of vector bundle lifts

(\pi^{*}TB\oplus T^{\rm vt}M)_{\mu}\to\pi^{*}T_{\mu}B\oplus T^{\rm vt}_{\mu}M

(6.3)

constructed using the exponential map after further choosing a Riemannian metric on
B
. Note that both the space of fiberwise Riemannian metrics on
M
varying continuously over
B
, and the space of Riemannian metrics are contractible. Therefore, all these choices induce the same isotopy class of the vector bundle reduction for the direct sum
\pi^{*}T_{\mu}B\oplus T^{\rm vt}_{\mu}M
.
To qualify the condition for stable
G
-smoothing (see Theorem
6.9
), one only needs to identify the right hand side with the tangent microbundle of the total space
M
. However this identification is not canonical even up to isotopy of microbundle isomorphisms. This is what we need to construct during the smoothing process.

Remark 6.17
.

Different data for constructing the splitting (
6.3
) from
[
AMS21
, Lemma 4.24, Proposition 4.26]
can in fact be interpolated with each other, so the splittings (
6.3
) arising from this way should be isotopic to each other. Keeping track of these choices are important for showing that the “invariants” constructed in this paper are indeed independent of all the auxiliary choices, but we do not need such invariance results for our applications.

Definition 6.18
.

[
AMS21
, Definition 4.22]

Suppose
\pi:M\to B
is a
G
-equivariant topological submersion. Let
W\subset M
be a
G
-invariant open subset. A
G
-equivariant fiberwise submersion along
W
is a continuous map
\phi:\widetilde{W}\to M
where
\widetilde{W}\subset W\times W
is an open neighborhood of the diagonal satisfying the following properties.

(1)

\phi_{q,b}:=\phi|_{\widetilde{W}\cap(\{q\}\times M_{b})}
is a homeomorphism onto an open subset of
M_{\pi(q)}
for all
b\in\pi(W)
and
q\in W
.

(2)

\phi_{q,\pi(q)}
sends each point
(q,q^{\prime})\in\widetilde{W}\cap(\{q\}\times M_{\pi(q)})
to
q^{\prime}
.

(3)

\widetilde{W}
is a
G
-invariant set with respect to the diagonal
G
-action on
W\times W
and
\phi
is a
G
-equivariant map.

Remark 6.19
.

Suppose
M
is smooth and
\pi:M\to B
is a smooth submersion. Then one can construct a fiberwise submersion using a
G
-invariant Riemannian metric and the exponential map in the horizontal direction.

Remark 6.20
.

A
G
-equivariant fiberwise submersion along
W=M
provides a
G
-microbundle morphism

\tau:T_{\mu}M\to T_{\mu}^{\rm vt}M

which is defined as

M\times M\supseteq\widetilde{M}\ni(p,p^{\prime})\mapsto(p,\phi(p,p^{\prime}))

which is the identity morphism on the sub-microbundle
T_{\mu}^{\rm vt}M\subset T_{\mu}M
. On the other hand, there is a canonical
G
-microbundle morphism

P:T_{\mu}M\to\pi^{*}T_{\mu}B,\ (p,p^{\prime})\mapsto(p,(\pi(p),\pi(p^{\prime}))).

Hence the
G
-equivariant fiberwise submersion induces an isomorphism of microbundles

P\oplus\tau:T_{\mu}M\to\pi^{*}T_{\mu}B\oplus T^{\rm vt}_{\mu}M.

If
\pi
is a fiberwise smooth
C_{\rm loc}^{1}

G
-bundle, then a
G
-equivariant fiberwise submersion along
M
induces a well-defined
G
-isotopy class of vector bundle lift

(\pi^{*}TB\oplus T^{\rm vt}M)_{\mu}\cong T_{\mu}M.

Lemma 6.21
.

Let
C\subset M
be a
G
-invariant closed set and
U\subset M
be a
G
-invariant open neighborhood of
C
. Let
D\subset M
be another
G
-invariant closed set. Suppose
\phi:\widetilde{U}\to M
is a
G
-equivariant fiberwise submersion along
U
. Then there exists a
G
-invariant open neighborhood
W
of
C\cup D
and a
G
-equivariant fiberwise submersion
\psi:\widetilde{W}\to M
along
W
which coincides with
\phi
in a small neighborhood of the diagonal of
C\times C
in
U\times U
.

Proof.

This is a restatement of the extension lemma
[
AMS21
, Lemma 4.24]
and a relative version of
[
AMS21
, Proposition 4.25]
.
∎

6.2.
Pre-smoothing operations and stable complex structures

This subsection can be viewed as a continuation of Section
5
, in which we explore the more refined information of the K-charts
K_{pq}=(V_{pq},E_{pq},G_{pq},S_{pq})
. These properties will be used in the construction of smoothing. Furthermore, we also present materials relevant for building the normal complex structure.

6.2.1.
Topological submersions

We have the following analogue of
[
AMS21
, Corollary 6.28, 6.29]
.

Proposition 6.22
.

The natural forgetful map

\pi_{pq}:V_{pq}\to B_{d_{pq}}

is a
G_{pq}
-equivariant topological submersion and has the structure of a
C_{\rm loc}^{1}
fiberwise smooth
G_{pq}
-bundle.

Proof.

This statement essentially a reformulation of the standard gluing construction in Floer theory which is presented for instance, in
[
Par16
, Appendix C]
. The slight difference is, here we are in a Morse–Bott situation because the asymptotic operator of elements in the moduli space
{\mathcal{M}}_{J_{\tilde{\Psi}},H}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})
has nontrivial kernel along the
{\mathcal{C}}
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}
direction. However, this does not introduce any problem to the argument.

As a first step, let us show that
\pi_{pq}
is a
G_{pq}
-equivariant topological submersion. Consider a point
x\in V_{pq}
represented by
(u,\Sigma,F,0)
. Using the sheared almost complex structure and Gromov’s graph trick, we can view
(u,\Sigma,F,\eta_{1},\dots,\eta_{d})
as a pseudo-holomorphic stable cylinder contained in the
0
-section of
{\mathcal{E}}_{d}\to M\times{\mathcal{C}}
. Recall that the framing
F
defines a holomorphic map
\iota_{F}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d}
of degree
d
. Let us choose
d^{\prime}=(d+2)d
generic hyperplanes
H_{1},\ldots,H_{d^{\prime}}\subset\mathbb{CP}^{d}
which intersect the image of
\underline{\smash{\mu}}
transversely at points different from nodes and markings, and choose

z_{i}\in v(\Sigma)\cap H_{i},\ i=1,\ldots,d^{\prime}.

Then the map
[v]\mapsto(z_{1},\ldots,z_{d^{\prime}})
is a local diffeomorphism from
{\mathcal{F}}{}_{0,2}^{\mathbb{R}}(d)
to an open subset
j:U\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{R}}
. These intersection points play the role as local stabilizing divisors. Namely, we choose the preimage of the divisors
H_{1},\dots,H_{d^{\prime}}
under the projection map
{\mathcal{E}}_{d}\to M\times{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathbb{C}P}^{d}
to be the local stabilizing divisors. By keeping track of the positions of the marked points obtained from intersecting the framing map with these divisors, we obtain map

\mu_{H}:V_{pq}\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{R}}

defined over a neighborhood of
x\in V_{pq}
.

Let
K_{\rm map}
be the kernel of the linearized Cauchy–Riemann operator at
(u,\iota_{F},0)
(without deforming the underlying map to
\mathbb{CP}^{d}
). The gluing map

g:U\times K\to V_{pq}

defined by
[
Par16
, Equation (C.10.3)]
fit into the following diagram:

\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 19.3063pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.67424pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{U\times K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.44661pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.74854pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.3063pt\raise-16.06139pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}_{U}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.78947pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 47.74854pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{V_{pq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.04565pt\raise-16.06139pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mu_{H}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.04565pt\raise-23.21053pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-6.95901pt\raise-32.12279pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{U\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.66605pt\raise-38.1193pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.63542pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{j}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.67424pt\raise-32.12279pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.67424pt\raise-32.12279pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{0,2+d^{\prime}}^{\mathbb{R}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}.

According to
[
Par16
, Section C.12]
,
g
is a homeomorphism onto its image. The commutativity of the above diagram follows from the definition
[
Par16
, Equation (C.10.4)]
. Furthermore, the restriction of the gluing map
g
to a fiber
\{a\}\times K
is smooth, see
[
Par16
, Section C.9]
(this has already been explored in Section
5
). Note that the topological submersion property holds over a
G_{pq}
-invariant open neighborhood of
S_{pq}^{-1}(0)
in
V_{pq}
by the transversality assumption. Up to restricting to such an open subset, we see that
\pi_{pq}:V_{pq}\to B_{pq}
is indeed a
G_{pq}
-equivariant topological submersion.

It remains to see that
\pi_{pq}
actually has a
C^{1}_{\rm loc}

G_{pq}
-bundle structure. Note that the gluing construction of
J
-holomorphic curves is based on applying a Newton–Picard iteration scheme to a pre-glued curve. By
[
Par16
, Proposition B.11.1, C.11.1]
, the
L^{2}
-norm of the honest solution near the ends of the neck region controls all
C^{k}
-norms through the region. With this in mind, the rest of the argument follows from
[
AMS21
, Corollary 6.29]
.
∎

As a consequence, we see that the tangent microbundle of the interior of
V_{pq}
admits a vector bundle lift by (
6.3
) and Lemma
6.21
. This suffices for us to construct a
G_{pq}
-equivariant smoothing on the interior of
V_{pq}
, but it falls short of providing a smoothing of
V_{pq}
as a manifold with corners. This problem will be solved on the outer-collaring
V_{pq}^{+}
by performing an inductive argument.

6.2.2.
Vertical stable complex structures

Following
[
AB21
, Section 11]
, we describe how to construct a stabilization of the vertical tangent bundle
T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}
of the
C^{1}_{\rm loc}

G_{pq}
-bundle
\pi_{pq}:V_{pq}\to B_{pq}
such that the resulting vector bundle admits a complex structure. We also explain how to use these stable complex structures to construct normal complex structures on derived orbifold charts.

For any
1
-periodic orbit
\underline{\smash{p}}
of
H
, fix a unitary trivialization of the complex vector bundle
\underline{\smash{p}}^{*}TM\to S^{1}
. Moreover, we choose a complex linear connection
\nabla
defined on the pullback of
TM
under the projection map
S^{1}\times M\to M
, such that its restriction to the graph of any
1
-periodic orbit is induced by the chosen trivializations. For a
1
-periodic orbit
\underline{\smash{p}}
, using the projection
{\rm pr}_{S^{1}}:{\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}\to S^{1}
, we consider the complex vector bundle

(\underline{\smash{p}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM\to{\mathbb{R}}\times S^{1}.

Denote by
B_{H,\underline{\smash{p}}}(t):S^{1}\to\text{End}(\underline{\smash{p}}^{*}TM)
the section obtained by differentiating the flow of
X_{H_{t}}
, i.e.,

B_{H,\underline{\smash{p}}}(t)(W)=\nabla_{W}X_{H_{t}},\ \forall W\in T_{\underline{\smash{p}}(t)}M.

Using a smooth cut-off function, we can consider the Cauchy–Riemann operator

\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{p}}}:\Omega^{0}((\underline{\smash{p}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM)\to\Omega^{0,1}((\underline{\smash{p}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM)

such that

\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{p}}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{rr}(\nabla-B_{H,\underline{\smash{p}}}\otimes dt)_{J}^{0,1}\text{ near }-\infty\\
\nabla^{0,1}\text{ near }+\infty.\end{array}\right.

It is a standard fact that
\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{p}}}
defines an elliptic operator. Therefore, we can find a finite dimensional
complex
vector space
V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-}
and a linear map
\lambda_{\underline{\smash{p}}}:V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-}\to\Omega^{0,1}((\underline{\smash{p}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM)
such that the map

\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{p}}}\oplus\lambda_{\underline{\smash{p}}}:\Omega^{0}((\underline{\smash{p}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM)\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-}\to\Omega^{0,1}((\underline{\smash{p}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM)

is surjective. This map is called the asymptotic operator of
\underline{\smash{p}}
. We fix a choice of
V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-}
and
\lambda_{\underline{\smash{p}}}
, and denote the kernel of the above map by
V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{+}
. The virtual vector space
(V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{+},V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-})
will be used to construct a stable complex structure on
T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}
.

Lemma 6.23
.

For any
p,q\in T^{\rm Floer}
such that
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}^{\rm Floer}_{pq}\neq\emptyset
, there exists a complex vector bundle
I^{\rm vt}_{pq}\to V_{pq}
such that there exists an homotopy between the vector bundles

V_{\underline{\smash{q}}}^{+}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-}\cong V_{\underline{\smash{q}}}^{-}\oplus I^{\rm vt}_{pq}\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{+}.

(6.4)

Proof.

We explain the construction in the case of a single-layered thickening. The vertical tangent bundle of
V_{pq}
can be identified with the kernel of the linearized operator associated with (
5.24
) with the framing
F
being kept fixed. To be more precise, using the model as in the proof of Theorem
5.41
, given an element in
V_{pq}
with representative
(\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta)
, the deformation operator over each component
\Sigma_{\alpha}
is of the form

\displaystyle W^{1,p}(\Sigma_{\alpha},u^{*}TM)\oplus W^{1,p}(\Sigma_{\alpha},(u,\mu)^{*}E(k))\to L^{p}(\Lambda^{0,1}(\Sigma_{\alpha},u^{*}TM))\oplus L^{p}(\Lambda^{0,1}(\Sigma_{\alpha},u^{*}TM))

\displaystyle(\xi,\eta^{\prime})\mapsto(D_{u}\xi+P(\eta^{\prime}),D_{E}\eta^{\prime}).

By taking the direct sum of these linear operators ranging over all the components of
\Sigma
, and impose the usual matching condition in the case of nodal curves, we indeed identify
T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}
over
(\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta)
with the kernel of a Fredholm operator which is schematically written as

D^{\rm vt}_{u,\mu,\eta}:{\mathcal{E}}_{u,\mu,\eta}\to{\mathcal{F}}_{u,\mu,\eta}.

Now we consider the concatenation of the three operators
D^{\rm vt}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{q}}}\oplus\lambda_{\underline{\smash{q}}}\oplus 0

{\mathcal{E}}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus(\Omega^{0}((\underline{\smash{q}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM)\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{q}}}^{-})\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-}\to{\mathcal{F}}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus\Omega^{0,1}((\underline{\smash{q}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM).

This operator is Fredholm and surjective, with kernel identified with

T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}\oplus V^{+}_{\underline{\smash{q}}}\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-}.

We will construct a homotopy of this operator following the proof of
[
AB21
, Proposition 11.30]
. To this end, introduce an auxialiary moduli space
\tilde{V}_{pq}\to V_{pq}
. It is described by adding an additional marked point on the compactified lateral line
{\mathbb{R}}\cup\{\pm\infty\}\cong[0,1]
of the cylindrical components and the map
\tilde{V}_{pq}\to V_{pq}
is the associated forgetful map. The fiber of the universal family over
\tilde{V}_{pq}
is the same as the fiber of
V_{pq}
when the newly-added marked point
\neq\pm\infty
; when it is given by
\pm\infty
, we insert a cylinder between the consecutive two cylindrical components which is mapped to the common asymptotic orbit. In particular,
\tilde{V}_{pq}\to V_{pq}
is a fiber bundle with fiber
\cong[0,1]
. For an element
(\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta)\in V_{pq}
, the domain of the universal family over
(\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta)\times\{1\}
coincides with the domain of
D^{\rm vt}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{q}}}\oplus\lambda_{\underline{\smash{q}}}\oplus 0
.

Write

D^{\rm vt}_{u,\mu,\eta}=D^{{\rm vt},{\mathbb{C}}}_{u,\mu,\eta}+Y^{\rm vt}_{u,\mu,\eta}

where
D^{{\rm vt},{\mathbb{C}}}_{u,\mu,\eta}
is a complex linear Cauchy–Riemann operator and
Y^{\rm vt}_{u,\mu,\eta}
is a
0
-th order differential operator which is complex anti-linear. Then we can consider another operator
D^{{\rm vt},{\mathbb{C}}}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{p}}}\oplus\lambda_{\underline{\smash{p}}}\oplus 0

{\mathcal{E}}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus(\Omega^{0}((\underline{\smash{p}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM)\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-})\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{q}}}^{-}\to{\mathcal{F}}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus\Omega^{0,1}((\underline{\smash{p}}\circ{\rm pr}_{S^{1}})^{*}TM).

If this Fredholm operator is surjective, then there exists a finite dimensional complex vector space
(I^{\rm vt}_{pq})_{u,\mu,\eta}
such that the kernel of this operator is

(I^{\rm vt}_{pq})_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{+}\oplus V_{\underline{\smash{q}}}^{-}.

Note that using the position of the additional marked point on the lateral line
\tilde{V}_{pq}\to V_{pq}
and a cut-off function defined on the domains of the universal family over
\tilde{V}_{pq}
whose differential has support on the horizontal component where the marked point lives on, we can construct a homotopy between two Fredholm operators

D^{\rm vt}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{q}}}\oplus\lambda_{\underline{\smash{q}}}\oplus 0\text{ and }D^{{\rm vt},{\mathbb{C}}}_{u,\mu,\eta}\oplus\nabla^{0,1}_{\underline{\smash{p}}}\oplus\lambda_{\underline{\smash{p}}}\oplus 0

which varies smoothly as we vary
(\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta)
along the fiber of
V_{pq}\to B_{pq}
.

Moreover,
D^{{\rm vt},{\mathbb{C}}}_{u,\mu,\eta}
can be made surjective if the
k
as from
E(k)
is chosen to be large enough. This fact can be derived from exactly the same proof of
[
AMS21
, Proposition 6.26]
. Therefore the statement is proved by defining
I^{\rm vt}_{pq}
to be the vector bundle over
V_{pq}
by assembling
(I^{\rm vt}_{pq})_{u,\mu,\eta}
.
∎

Corollary 6.24
.

If the almost free action of
G_{pq}
on
V_{pq}
is smooth, then the induced orbifold vector bundle
T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}
on the quotient orbifold
V_{pq}/G_{pq}
has a normal complex structure.

Proof.

This follows from Lemma
6.23
and the follwing fact. For
p\in{\mathcal{P}}
(also for
q\in{\mathcal{P}}
), the trivial vector bundles
V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{+}\times V_{pq}\to V_{pq}
and
V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-}\times V_{pq}\to V_{pq}
can be viewed as
G_{pq}
-equivariant vectors for which
G_{pq}
acts trivially on the factor
V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{\pm}
. In particular, we can use
V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{\pm}
to stabilize the space
V_{pq}
. Because the induced
G_{pq}
-action on
V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{\pm}\times V_{pq}\to V_{pq}
is trivial on the first factor, they do not affect the
normal
directions to the union of
G_{pq}
-orbits with the same isotropy group of both the tangent bundle of
V_{pq}
. Therefore the statement follows from taking the nontrivial part of the representations of (
6.4
) after taking the
G_{pq}
-quotient.
∎

6.2.3.
Stabilizing the K-chart lift

In the equivariant smoothing theorem of Lashof
[
Las79
]
a smooth structure can only be obtained after stabilizing the manifold. Given a
G
-manifold
M
, a stabilization of
M
is
M\times{\bm{R}}
where
{\bm{R}}
is an orthogonal
G
-space.
18
18

18

We could try to extend the theory to the case of nontrivial bundles but that is not completely necessary.
This operation fits into the stabilization of Kuranishi charts. As we are dealing with infinitely many Kuranishi charts associated to a K-chart lift of the Floer category
T^{\rm Floer}
(or its outer-collaring), once we stabilize certain charts (and their products) and equipped them with smooth structures, a “larger” chart needs to be stabilized by a larger representation in order to include the stabilization of products corresponding to its strata. Thus we need to introduce the following concept which describes how a system of stabilizations should fit together.

Definition 6.25
.

Let
T^{\mathcal{P}}
be a flow category over
{\mathcal{P}}
and suppose the collection
\{K_{pq}=(G_{pq},V_{pq},E_{pq},S_{pq})\}_{p<q}
of Kuranishi charts with the collection
\{{\bm{\iota}}_{prq}\}_{p<r<q}
of K-chart embeddings define a K-chart lift of
T^{\mathcal{P}}
(see Definition
5.9
). A
stabilization
of consists of the following objects.

(1)

For each
T_{pq}
a finite-dimensional orthogonal
G_{pq}
-representation
{\bm{R}}_{pq}
. Denote by
R_{pq}
the trivial
G_{pq}
-equivariant vector bundle
V_{pq}\times{\bm{R}}_{pq}
. Then for each stratum
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
, define the
G_{\alpha}
-equivariant vector bundle
R_{\alpha}
over
V_{\alpha}=V_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots V_{r_{l}q}
to be

R_{pr_{1}}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus R_{r_{l}q}.

(6.5)

(2)

For any pair of strata
\alpha\leq\beta
, an equivariant bundle embedding

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.87715pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.68445pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R_{\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.57878pt\raise 6.4472pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.4139pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\phi_{\beta\alpha}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 33.87715pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.3889pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 33.87715pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R_{\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.98567pt\raise-24.27777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-9.87715pt\raise-31.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{V_{\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.9368pt\raise-31.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.9368pt\raise-31.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Assume that these data satisfy the following compatibility condition.

(A)

For each triple of strata
\alpha\leq\beta\leq\gamma
one has
\phi_{\gamma\beta}\circ\phi_{\beta\alpha}=\phi_{\gamma\alpha}
.

(B)

The bundle embeddings are induced from products. Namely, given a stratum
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathcal{P}}_{pq}
, there exist equivariant isometric linear embeddings

\phi_{r_{i}r_{i+1}}:R_{r_{i}r_{i+1}}\hookrightarrow R_{pq}\text{ for }i=0,\dots,l

with respect to the group embeddings
G_{r_{i}r_{i+1}}\hookrightarrow G_{pq}
such that

\phi_{pq,\alpha}=\phi_{pq,pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times\phi_{pq,r_{l}q},

and
\phi_{\beta\alpha}
for a general pair
\beta\leq\alpha
is constructed similarly using the corresponding factorization.

Given a stabilization containing
\{{\bm{R}}_{pq}\}
and
\{\phi_{\beta\alpha}\}
we can define another K-chart lift for
T^{\mathcal{P}}
. Its collection of Kuranishi charts are

\widehat{K}_{pq}:=(G_{pq},\widehat{V}_{pq},\widehat{E}_{pq},\widehat{S}_{pq}):={\rm Stab}_{R_{pq}}(K_{pq});

its collection of chart embeddings

\widehat{\bm{\iota}}_{prq}:\widehat{K}_{pr}\times\widehat{K}_{rq}\to\partial^{prq}\widehat{K}_{pq}

are defined as follows. Given
(x_{pr},e_{pr})\in\widehat{V}_{pr}
,
(x_{rq},e_{rq})\in\widehat{V}_{rq}
, define

\widehat{\iota}_{prq}((x_{pr},e_{pr}),(x_{rq},e_{rq}))=(\iota_{prq}(x_{pr},x_{rq}),\phi_{pq,prq}(e_{pr},e_{rq}))

which is clearly equivariant with respect to the group map
G_{pr}\times G_{rq}\to G_{pq}
.

Lemma 6.26
.

The data
\{\widehat{K}_{pq}\}_{p<q}
with
\{\widehat{\bm{\iota}}_{prq}\}_{p<r<q}
is a K-chart lift of
T^{\mathcal{P}}
.

Proof.

This follows directly from the definition.
∎

We need to equip a stabilization certain extra structures.

Definition 6.27
.

Suppose the K-chart lift
{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathcal{P}}
is equipped with a collar structure. Then a stabilization is called
collared
if there are equivariant bundle isomorphisms

\textstyle{R_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R_{pq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V_{pq}}

such that the bundle embeddings are collared, i.e.,

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 49.48822pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-49.48822pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R_{\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.48822pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-25.12282pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 73.48822pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R_{\gamma}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{\gamma}}\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}\setminus{\mathbb{F}}_{\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 122.70016pt\raise-24.45613pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-9.35228pt\raise-32.45613pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R_{\beta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 102.17462pt\raise-32.45613pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 102.17462pt\raise-32.45613pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R_{\gamma}|_{\partial^{\beta}V_{\gamma}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Note that although our stabilization is constructed using product bundles, the stabilization maps are not necessarily induced from the linear maps between the representations. Therefore, the above definition is not entirely superfluous.

We will also use not-necessarily-trivial inner products on those trivial bundles
R_{pq}
in the construction.

Definition 6.28
.

Let
{\mathfrak{K}}^{\mathcal{P}}
be a K-chart lift of
T^{\mathcal{P}}
equipped with a collar structure and a collared stabilization
{\mathfrak{R}}
. A
collared system of inner products
on
{\mathfrak{R}}
is a collection of
G_{pq}
-equivariant inner products on the bundle
R_{pq}
such that

(1)

All the embeddings
\phi_{\beta\alpha}:R_{\alpha}\to R_{\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}}
are isometric.

(2)

The collar isomorphisms are isometric.

Suppose
{\mathfrak{R}}
is equipped with a collared system of inner products. Then define

R_{\beta\alpha}\subset R_{\beta}|_{V_{\alpha}}

to be the orthogonal complement of
R_{\alpha}
in
R_{\beta}|_{V_{\alpha}}
. Then by basic linear algebra one can see that whenever
\alpha\leq\beta\leq\gamma
, as subbundles of
R_{\gamma}|_{V_{\alpha}}
, one has

R_{\gamma\alpha}=R_{\gamma\beta}|_{V_{\alpha}}\oplus R_{\beta\alpha}.

Then the original scaffolding can be extended to include the extra pieces
R_{\beta\alpha}
. Indeed, define

\widehat{F}_{\beta\alpha}:=\pi^{*}F_{\beta\alpha}\oplus\pi^{*}R_{\beta\alpha}

where
\pi
denotes (temporarily) the projection
V_{pq}\times{\bm{R}}_{pq}\to V_{pq}
and the induced projection
V_{\alpha}\times{\bm{R}}_{\alpha}\to V_{\alpha}
. The stabilization described in Proposition
5.64
is also extended to a map

{\rm Stab}_{\widehat{F}_{pq,\alpha}}(\widehat{V}_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}.

After taking the equivariantization, we obtain a map

G_{pq}\times_{G_{\alpha}}({\rm Stab}_{\widehat{F}_{pq,\alpha}}(\widehat{V}_{\alpha}))\to\widehat{V}_{pq}

which defines an equivariant open embedding. We would like to remark that the orthogonal complement of the
G_{pq}
-equivariantization of
R_{pq,\alpha}\to V_{\alpha}
in
R_{pq}|_{G_{pq}(V_{\alpha})}
is not necessarily a product bundle. Therefore, some extra care needs to be taken in the smoothing process.

6.2.4.
An induction scheme of stabilization

In the smoothing process, the construction of successive stabilizations is elementary but complicated. In general, the stabilizations are constructed inductively, similar to many other inductive constructions. We would like to describe how the induction can be carried out. We first look at the simple case when a moduli space has strata of codimension at most 2. Assume
p<q
in
{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}
and let
V_{pq}
be the domain of a global Kuranishi chart of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
. In the following discussion, we assume that the tangent microbundle of the underlying thickened moduli space of the relevant K-charts admits a vector bundle lift, which is the necessary condition for us to apply the smoothing theory. Moreover, in the discussion of extending the smoothing relatively, we assume that the lifts are compatible in a suitable sense. The construction of vector bundle lifts and the precise meaning of compatibility will be detailed in Section
6.4
.

Note that we also need to endow the vector bundle
E_{pq}\to V_{pq}
with a smooth structure after suitable stabilization. However, this is rather straightforward after smoothing
V_{pq}
: we can simply approximate the classifying map of
E_{pq}
by a smooth map which stays in the same homotopy class.

The case with no boundary or corner.
When
V_{pq}
has no boundary or corner, then it is the initial step of the induction. One can find a trivial
G_{pq}
-bundle
V_{pq}\times{\bm{R}}_{pq}
induced by a suitable representation
R_{pq}
needed for stable smoothing.

The case with only codimension one boundary strata.
When
V_{pq}
has only codimension one boundary strata, without loss of generality, suppose there is only one boundary stratum
V_{prq}
. Using the induction hypothesis, suppose
{\bm{R}}_{pr}
and
{\bm{R}}_{rq}
have been given. Consider the representation
{\bm{R}}_{pr}
resp.
{\bm{R}}_{rq}
of
G_{pr}
resp.
G_{rq}
. By Frobenius reciprocity (see
[
Sep07
, Theorem 7.47]
), there exist orthogonal representations
{\bm{R}}_{pr}^{pq}
resp.
{\bm{R}}_{rq}^{pq}
of
G_{pq}
which contains
G_{pr}
resp.
G_{rq}
as subrepresentations of
G_{pr}
resp.
G_{rq}
. Then define

{\bm{R}}_{pq}={\bm{R}}_{pr}^{pq}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{rq}^{pq}

which is an orthogonal
G_{pq}
-representation.

Now we define the bundle embeddings required for stabilization. We introduce more notations. Over the product
V_{prq}=V_{pr}\times V_{rq}
, define

\displaystyle\ R_{pr\to pq}:=R_{pr}\boxplus\{0\},\

\displaystyle\ R_{rq\to pq}:=\{0\}\boxplus R_{rq}.

Then

R_{prq}=R_{pr\to pq}\oplus R_{rq\to pq}.

We would like the bundle embedding
\phi_{pq,prq}
to be the sum of

\displaystyle\ \phi_{pr\to pq}:R_{pr\to pq}\to R_{pq}|_{\partial^{prq}V_{pq}},\

\displaystyle\ \phi_{rq\to pq}:R_{rq\to pq}\to R_{pq}|_{\partial^{prq}V_{pq}}.

Indeed,
\phi_{pr\to pq}
and
\phi_{rq\to pq}
are the ones canonically induced from the linear inclusions

\displaystyle\ {\bm{R}}_{pr}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pr}^{pq}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pq},\

\displaystyle\ {\bm{R}}_{rq}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{rq}^{pq}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pq}.

Then all the requirements of Definition
6.25
are automatically satisfied. In addition, for the purpose of stable smoothing, one can also take an additional direct sum to
{\bm{R}}_{pq}
by another orthogonal
G_{pq}
-space
{\bm{R}}_{pq,0}
.

Note that in this final step, we need to apply the relative smoothing result Theorem
6.10
. Indeed, the space
R_{prq}
admits a smooth structure by taking the product of smooth structures on
R_{pr}
and
R_{rq}
, whose existence is based on the induction hypothesis. Then the equivariantization
G_{pq}(R_{prq})
has a smooth structure. Denote by
G_{pq}(R_{prq}^{\perp})
the equivariantization of the orthogonal complement of
R_{prq}
in
R_{pq}|_{V_{prq}}
. Using the projection map
R_{prq}\to V_{prq}
, the vector bundle
G_{pq}(R_{prq}^{\perp})
can be pulled back to
G_{pq}(R_{prq})
and the total space of this vector bundle can be endowed with a smooth structure, by approximating the classifying map. This in turn equips
R_{pq}|_{\partial^{prq}V_{pq}}
with a smooth structure. Using the collar structure

\partial^{prq}V_{pq}\times[0,\epsilon)\to V_{pq}

which extends to a collar structure of the stabilized charts, we see that an open neighborhood of
R_{pq}|_{\partial^{prq}V_{pq}}
has a smooth structure using the product decomposition

R_{pq}|_{\partial^{prq}V_{pq}}\times[0,\epsilon).

By replacing the open interval
[0,\epsilon)
by a closed interval of the form
[0,\frac{\epsilon}{2}]
, a suitable compatibility between the vector bundle lifts guarantees that we can apply Theorem
6.10
to obtain the
G_{pq}
orthogonal representation
{\bm{R}}_{pq,0}
which induces a stabilized smoothing for
V_{pq}
extending the previously constructed stabilized smoothings on
V_{pr}
and
V_{rq}
.

Remark 6.29
.

We hope that the above arguments showcase the importance of applying Frobenius reciprocity, approximations of classifying maps of equivariant vector bundles, and the relative smoothing result in our inductive construction. Moreover, as we can see from above, outer-collaring conveniently provide us with an automatic smoothing near the boundary stratum. Additionally, it is important to keep in mind that the compatibility between the vector bundle lifts of tangent microbundles of the total space and boundary stratum is crucial for the application of Theorem
6.10
.

The case with only codimension one or two strata.
Now we consider the case when
{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\mathcal{P}}=\{pq,prq,psq,prsq\}
. Suppose the representations

{\bm{R}}_{pr},\ {\bm{R}}_{rs},\ {\bm{R}}_{sq},{\bm{R}}_{ps},{\bm{R}}_{rq}

are chosen so that they induce compatible stabilized smoothings except the top stratum
V_{pq}
. Define the bundles
R_{pr\to pq}
etc. in a way similar to the previous case. Suppose we have also defined bundle embeddings

\phi_{pr\to ps},\ \phi_{rs\to ps},\ \phi_{rs\to rq},\ \phi_{sq\to rq}.

In addition, we make the following assumptions, which basically says that for
ps
and
rq
, the stabilization data are constructed as in the previous case.

(1)

{\bm{R}}_{ps}
is the direct sum

{\bm{R}}_{ps}={\bm{R}}_{pr}^{ps}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{rs}^{ps}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{ps,0}

of orthogonal
G_{ps}
-spaces such that
{\bm{R}}_{pr}^{ps}
resp.
{\bm{R}}_{rs}^{ps}
contains
{\bm{R}}_{pr}
resp.
{\bm{R}}_{rs}
as subrepresentations of
G_{pr}
resp.
G_{rs}
. Similarly, there is a direct sum

{\bm{R}}_{rq}={\bm{R}}_{rs}^{rq}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{sq}^{rq}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{rq,0}

of orthogonal
G_{rq}
-spaces such that
{\bm{R}}_{rs}^{rq}
resp.
{\bm{R}}_{sq}^{rq}
contains
{\bm{R}}_{rs}
resp.
{\bm{R}}_{sq}
as subrepresentations of
G_{rs}
resp.
G_{sq}
.

(2)

The bundle embedding

\phi_{pr\to ps}:R_{pr\to ps}\to R_{ps}|_{\partial^{prs}V_{ps}}

is induced from the linear inclusion
{\bm{R}}_{pr}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pr}^{ps}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{ps}
. Similar requirement applies to other bundle embeddings.

Now we define an orthogonal
G_{pq}
-space
{\bm{R}}_{pq}
. There are two codimension one strata,
prq
and
psq
. For the
G_{pr}
-space
{\bm{R}}_{pr}
, by Frobenius reciprocity, there is a
G_{pq}
-orthogonal space
{\bm{R}}_{pr}^{pq}
which contains
{\bm{R}}_{pr}
as a subrepresentation of
G_{pr}
. We choose similarly
{\bm{R}}_{rq}^{pq}
,
{\bm{R}}_{ps}^{pq}
,
{\bm{R}}_{sq}^{pq}
. For the purpose of stable smoothing, we choose another
G_{pq}
-orthogonal space
{\bm{R}}_{pq,0}
, which is constructed similarly as the case of one boundary stratum by applying relative smoothing. Then define

{\bm{R}}_{pq}:=\Big({\bm{R}}_{pr}^{pq}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{rq}^{pq}\Big)\oplus\Big({\bm{R}}_{ps}^{pq}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{sq}^{pq}\Big)\oplus{\bm{R}}_{pq,0}.

We rewrite this decomposition as

{\bm{R}}_{pq}={\bm{R}}_{pq,r}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{pq,s}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{pq,0}.

(6.6)

Then we want to define the bundle embeddings into

R_{pq}:=R_{pq,r}\oplus R_{pq,s}\oplus R_{pq,0}.

This time the bundle embeddings are not purely induced from linear maps between the representations.

(1)

We define
\phi_{pr\to pq}
and
\phi_{sq\to pq}
. We only describe
\phi_{pr\to pq}
in detail. With respect to (
6.6
), we write

\phi_{pr\to pq}=\phi_{pr,r}\oplus\phi_{pr,s}\oplus 0.

We define
\phi_{pr,r}:R_{pr\to pq}\to R_{pq,r}
to be the one induced from the linear inclusion

{\bm{R}}_{pr}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pr}^{pq}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pq,r}.

For
\phi_{pr,s}:R_{pr\to pq}\to R_{pq,s}
, notice that it is defined over
V_{prq}
. We first define it over
\partial^{prsq}V_{prq}
, which is induced from the existing bundle map

\phi_{pr\to ps}:R_{pr\to ps}\to R_{ps}

and its equivariantization and stabilization (by the bundle associated to the scaffolding), composed with the linear inclusion

R_{ps}\hookrightarrow R_{ps}^{pq}\hookrightarrow R_{pq,s}.

To extend to the whole
V_{prq}
, we use the collar structure and a cut-off function on
V_{prq}
which is supported in the collar region near
\partial^{prsq}V_{prq}
and which only depends on the collar coordinate.

(2)

We define
\phi_{rs\to pq}
. Again, we write
\phi_{rs\to pq}=\phi_{rs,r}\oplus\phi_{rs,s}\oplus 0
. The first component
\phi_{rs,r}
is induced from the bundle map

\phi_{rs\to rq}:R_{rs\to rq}\to R_{rq}

composed with the linear inclusion
{\bm{R}}_{rq}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{rq}^{pq}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pq,r}
; the second component
\phi_{rs,s}
is induced from the bundle map

\phi_{rs\to ps}:R_{rs\to ps}\to R_{ps}

composed with the linear inclusion
{\bm{R}}_{ps}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{ps}^{pq}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pq,s}
.

(3)

The definitions of
\phi_{ps\to pq}
and
\phi_{rq\to pq}
is similar to the case of
\phi_{pr\to pq}
and
\phi_{sq\to pq}
. We only describe in detail the bundle map
\phi_{ps\to pq}
(which should be defined over
V_{psq}
). We write
\phi_{ps\to pq}
as
\phi_{ps,r}\oplus\phi_{ps,s}\oplus 0
. The second component
\phi_{ps,s}
is naturally induced from the linear inclusion
{\bm{R}}_{ps}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{ps}^{pq}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{pq,s}
, For the first component
\phi_{ps,r}
, we decompose it further as

\phi_{ps,r}=\phi_{ps,pr}\oplus\phi_{ps,rs}.

We first define it over the boundary stratum
\partial^{prsq}V_{psq}
and then use a cut-off function to turn it off as we leave this stratum towards the interior of
V_{psq}
. Consider the composition of orthogonal projections

{\bm{R}}_{ps}\to{\bm{R}}_{pr}^{ps}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{rs}^{ps}\to{\bm{R}}_{pr}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{rs}

which induces a bundle map

R_{ps\to pq}|_{V_{prsq}}\to R_{pr\to pq}|_{V_{prsq}}\oplus R_{rs\to pq}.

Then using the linear inclusions
{\bm{R}}_{pr}\to{\bm{R}}_{pr}^{pq}
and the map
R_{rs\to rq}\to R_{rq}\to R_{rq}^{pq}
, one defines the map
\phi_{ps,r}
over the closed set
V_{prsq}
. One can then equivariantize, stabilize to define it over
\partial^{prsq}V_{psq}
. Lastly, use a cut-off function to extend this component to a neighborhood.

Remark 6.30
.

We put effort on defining the embeddings maps between charts in the above discussions, which contains certain distinct features than the case with fewer strata because the bundle maps are no longer linear. We would like to remark that the compatibility between the stabilized smoothings of
V_{prq}
and
V_{psq}
near their common stratum
V_{prsq}
is already guaranteed by the inductive nature of our construction.

The above inductive strategy, especially the case with codimension two strata, indicates that it seems difficult to construct a stabilization of K-chart lifts of a flow category using purely linear maps between representations. This brings in an extra layer of complexity in the following smoothing process because we would like the bundle embeddings also to be smooth.

6.3.
The main theorem about smoothing

We state the main theorem on smoothing the outer-collared K-chart lift of
(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}
. In the following, a smoothing on a
{\mathbb{A}}
-manifold
V
equipped with a collar structure (Definition
3.26
) is called a
collared smoothing
if the smoothing is equal to the product of the standard smooth structure on
[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}
and a smoothing on
\partial^{\alpha}V
over the collar region
\partial^{\alpha}V\times[0,\epsilon)^{{\mathbb{F}}_{\alpha}}
. The same notion is also used for a smoothing of a vector bundle.

Theorem 6.31
.

Given the outer-collared K-chart lift
{\mathfrak{K}}^{+}
of the outer-collared flow category
(T^{{\rm Floer}})^{+}
equipped with a collared scaffolding (see Proposition
5.64
), there exist the following objects. We revome the “
+
” to make the notations more succinct.

(1)

A stabilization of
{\mathfrak{K}}
(see Definition
6.25
), given by a collection of orthogonal
G_{pq}
-spaces
{\bm{R}}_{pq}
and a collection of bundle embeddings.

(2)

A collared
G_{pq}
-smoothing on
\widehat{V}_{pq}:=V_{pq}\times{\bm{R}}_{pq}
.

(3)

A collared bundle
G_{pq}
-smoothing on the
O(n)
-bundle
\widehat{O}_{pq}\to\widehat{V}_{pq}
, where
\widehat{O}_{pq}
is the pullback of
O_{pq}
under the natural projection
\widehat{V}_{pq}\to V_{pq}
.

(4)

A collared
G_{pq}
-invariant inner product on the trivial bundle
R_{pq}\to V_{pq}
inducing an inner product on the vector bundle
\widehat{R}_{pq}\to\widehat{V}_{pq}
, where
\widehat{R}_{pq}
is the pullback of
R_{pq}
under the natural projection
\widehat{V}_{pq}\to V_{pq}
.

(5)

A collared
G_{pq}
-smoothing on the
O(n)
-bundles
\widehat{R}_{pq}
.

These objects satisfy the following conditions.

(A)

The smoothing of
\widehat{V}_{pq}
induces a smooth bundle structure
\widehat{Q}_{pq}
as it is induced from the
G_{pq}
-representation
{\bm{Q}}_{pq}
. Then the smooth structures on
\widehat{O}_{pq}
and
\widehat{R}_{pq}
induce a smooth structure on the obstruction bundle

\widehat{E}_{pq}=\widehat{O}_{pq}\oplus\widehat{Q}_{pq}\oplus\widehat{R}_{pq}.

(B)

Boundary smoothings are given by products and stabilizations. More precisely, this means the following. For any stratum
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
of
{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm Floer}_{pq}
, the smoothings on
\widehat{V}_{pr_{1}}
,
\ldots
,
\widehat{V}_{r_{l}q}
induce a smooth structure on the prodcut
\widehat{V}_{\alpha}=\widehat{V}_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times\widehat{V}_{r_{l}q}
and its equivariantization
\widehat{V}_{\alpha}^{\sim}
; the smooth bundle structures on
\widehat{O}_{pr_{1}}
,
\ldots
,
\widehat{O}_{r_{l}q}
induce a smooth structure on the bundle
\widehat{O}_{\alpha}^{\sim}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}^{\sim}
; there is also a canonical smooth bundle structure on
R_{\alpha}^{\sim}
as it is induced from trivial bundles. Then

(a)

The embedding
\widehat{V}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}
is smooth.

(b)

The bundle embedding
\widehat{O}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}}
and the bundle embedding
\widehat{R}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\widehat{R}_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}}
are smooth bundle embeddings. It follows that the orthogonal complement
\widehat{O}_{pq,\alpha}
and the orthogonal complement
\widehat{R}_{pq,\alpha}
are smooth bundles over
\widehat{V}_{\alpha}
. It follows that the bundle

\widehat{F}_{pq,\alpha}=\widehat{O}_{pq,\alpha}\oplus\widehat{Q}_{pq,\alpha}\oplus\widehat{R}_{pq,\alpha}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}

is smooth, where the smooth structure on
\widehat{Q}_{pq,\alpha}
is induced from the orthogonal complement of the
G_{pq}
-subrepresentation
{\bm{Q}}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow{\bm{Q}}_{pq}
.

(c)

The stabilization map

\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}:{\rm Stab}_{\widehat{F}_{pq,\alpha}}\Big(\widehat{V}_{\alpha}\Big)\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}

(which is the
\theta_{pq,\alpha}
in (
5.50
) lifted) is a smooth embedding.

(d)

Let
\widehat{\pi}_{pq,\alpha}:\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}^{\sim}
be the projection map induced from the stabilization map above, which is smooth. Then bundle isomorphism

\widehat{\vartheta}_{pq,\alpha}:\widehat{\pi}_{pq,\alpha}^{*}\Big(\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}\Big)\to\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}}

(included in the scaffolding) is smooth.

Assuming this theorem, it is straightforward to construct a derived orbifold lift of the flow category
(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}
by taking group quotients. Remove the
\widehat{\cdot}
from the notation. Define

C_{pq}=({\mathcal{U}}_{pq},{\mathcal{E}}_{pq},{\mathcal{S}}_{pq},\psi_{pq})

where
{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}=V_{pq}/G_{pq}
,
{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}=E_{pq}/G_{pq}
,
{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}:{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}
is the induced orbibundle section, and
\psi_{pq}:{\mathcal{S}}_{pq}^{-1}(0)\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{+}
is the induced footprint map. Moreover, for
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
, one has the natural identification

K_{\alpha}/G_{\alpha}\cong C_{\alpha}:=C_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times C_{r_{l}q}.

The scaffolding in the K-chart sense also descends to a scaffolding in the D-chart sense. Indeed, for
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
with
\alpha\leq\beta
, the bundle

{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}:=F_{\beta\alpha}/G_{\alpha}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}

is a smooth orbifold vector bundle over
{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}
. Then the stabilization map
{\rm Stab}_{F_{\beta}\alpha}(V_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}
induces

{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha}:{\rm Stab}_{{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}}(C_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}C_{\beta}.

One checkes directly that the data
({\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha},{\bm{\theta}}_{\beta\alpha})_{\alpha\leq\beta}
form a scaffolding in the D-presentation of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{pq}^{+}
. It should be pointed out that these derived orbifold charts are effective due to the geometric construction.

Theorem 6.32
.

The D-chart lift of
(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}
induced from
\{C_{pq}\}
equipped with the compatible collar structure and scaffolding can be upgraded to an oriented and normally complex derived orbifold lift by doing a further stabilization of the corresponding K-chart lift.

Proof.

In the case of a single chart
({\mathcal{U}}_{pq},{\mathcal{E}}_{pq},{\mathcal{S}}_{pq},\psi_{pq})
, recall that
{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
is obtained from a quotient
\widehat{V}_{pq}/G_{pq}
, such that

(1)

The space
\widehat{V}_{pq}
is equal to the product
V_{pq}\times{\bm{R}}_{pq}
.

(2)

There is a
C^{1}_{\rm loc}

G_{pq}
-bundle structure
\pi_{pq}:V_{pq}\to B_{pq}
which ensures that the tangent space of the smoothing of
\widehat{V}_{pq}
is isotopic to the direct sum

R_{pq}\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}\oplus\pi^{*}TB_{pq},

(6.7)

where we abuse the notation to denote the corresponding bundles obtained by the pullback under the natural projection map
\widehat{V}_{pq}\to V_{pq}
.

The first simple obervation is that the stabilizing factors
{\bm{R}}_{pq}
can be taken to be unitary representations. We can simply use
{\bm{R}}_{pq}\oplus{\bm{i}}{\bm{R}}_{pq}
instead of a possibly real representation
{\bm{R}}_{pq}
. Moreover, the bundle inclusions
R_{\alpha}\oplus{\bm{i}}R_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow R_{pq}\oplus{\bm{i}}R_{pq}
are then complex linear and the inner products on the trivial bundles
R_{pq}\oplus{\bm{i}}R_{pq}
become Hermitian.

We would like to do a further stabilization to the (already smoothed) K-chart lift. Indeed, we stabilize by the
G_{pq}
-representation
{\bm{Q}}_{d_{pq}}
. Let
\widehat{Q}_{pq}^{\prime}\to\widehat{V}_{pq}
be the trivial bundle (to be distinguished from the original
Q
-bundle). Notice that the bundle inclusions
Q_{\delta}\hookrightarrow Q_{d}
over
B_{\delta}\subset B_{d}
for all
\delta\in{\mathbb{A}}_{d}
is defined via a linear inclusion
{\bm{Q}}_{\delta}\to{\bm{Q}}_{d}
the corresponding bundle. Then there are natural bundle inclusions

\widehat{Q}_{\alpha}^{\prime}\to\widehat{Q}_{pq}^{\prime}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}.

As this bundle map is induced from linear map between representation spaces, it is automatically smooth. Hence we obtained another smooth K-chart lift whose K-charts are

\widehat{\widehat{K}}_{pq}={\rm Stab}_{\widehat{Q}_{pq}^{\prime}}\widehat{K}_{pq}.

It induces a new D-chart lift.

Now we consider the normal complex structures on this new D-chart lift. Indeed, the obstruction bundles are now all complex. Indeed,

\widehat{\widehat{E}}_{pq}=\widehat{\widehat{O}}_{pq}\oplus\widehat{\widehat{R}}_{pq}\oplus\widehat{\widehat{Q}}_{pq}\oplus\widehat{\widehat{Q}}{}_{pq}^{\prime}

and we give the last two summands the natural complex structure.

Now the quotient orbifold

\widehat{\widehat{V}}_{pq}/G_{pq}

is normally complex. Indeed, (
6.7
) becomes

T\widehat{\widehat{V}}_{pq}\cong\big(R_{pq}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\big)\oplus T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}\oplus(\pi_{pq}^{*}TB_{d_{pq}}\oplus\widehat{\widehat{Q}}{}_{d_{pq}}^{\prime}).

As shown in Proposition
5.27
, the quotient of the last summand above is normally complex. Moreover, by Lemma
6.23
and Corollary
6.24
, the quotient of
T^{\rm vt}V_{pq}
is also normally complex.

To obtain a normal complex structure on the D-chart lift, we further check:

(1)

A complex structure on the scaffolding. Indeed, the original difference bundles

\widehat{F}_{pq,\alpha}=\widehat{O}_{pq,\alpha}\oplus\widehat{Q}_{pq,\alpha}\oplus\widehat{R}_{pq,\alpha}

is already complex as explained below. First, the bundle
\widehat{O}_{pq,\alpha}
is complex because we can choose the metric on the
O
-bundles to be Hermitian. The factor
Q_{pq,\alpha}
is complex because it consists of “off-diagonal” Hermitian matrices. As mentioned above, the stabilization in the smoothing can be chosen to be complex representations, therefore
\widehat{R}_{pq,\alpha}
also has a complex structure. Then the further stabilization by
{\bm{Q}}_{d_{pq}}
gives an extra copy of
\widehat{Q}_{pq,\alpha}
which is complex as well.

(2)

Compatibility between the information on the product boundary charts and the information on the restriction of the chart to the boundary. This follows from the constructions
[
AB21
, Section 11]
by incorporating the geometry of the broken trajectories from the thick-and-thin decomposition of the domains.

(3)

The compatibility with collars follows from the construction.

By examing conditions of Definition
3.36
we obtained a normal complex structure on this (further stabilized) D-chart lift of
(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}
.

For any capped orbit
p\in T^{\rm Floer}
, we define its associated virtual vector space to be

(V_{p}^{+},V_{p}^{-}):=(V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{+},V_{\underline{\smash{p}}}^{-}),

where the right hand side is constructed before Lemma
6.23
. Then the existence of orientation (Definition
3.43
) is a classical result in Floer theory
[
FH93
]
. For a modern exposition, the reader could refer to
[
Par16
, Section C.13]
.
∎

6.3.1.
Constructing straightening

We sketch how to construct a straightening on the D-chart lift which is compatible with the existing collar and scaffolding structures. This construction relies on the concrete geometric feature of the obstruction bundles. We do not know if instead the straightening can be constructed in a more abstract level.

We start with moduli spaces without boundary or corners. Let
C_{pq}
be the D-chart. Then by the method of
[
BX22
, Lemma 3.15, Lemma 3.20]
, one can construct a straightening on the pair
({\mathcal{U}}_{pq},{\mathcal{E}}_{pq})
. Indeed, notice that there are a sequence of obstruction bundles
{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{(d)}
for
d\geq d_{pq}
. We can actually construct, not just a connection on
{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{(d_{0})}
for the lowest
d_{0}=d_{pq}
(which is required for straightening), but actually a sequence of metrics on all
{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{(d)}
and metric connections such that the natural inclusion
{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{(d)}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{(d^{\prime})}
(for
d<d^{\prime}
) preserves the metric and connection.

Inductively, suppose we have constructed such compatible structures for all
C_{rs}
for
d_{rs}<d_{pq}
. These structures induce metrics and metric connections on the difference bundles
{\mathcal{F}}_{\beta\alpha}
. Now consider the chart
C_{pq}
. Over a lowest stratum
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
, the existing structures induce metrics and metric connections on
{\mathcal{E}}_{\beta}^{(d)}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}}
for all
d\geq d_{pq}
and proper strata between
\alpha
and
pq
. We would like to extend the metric and connection to
{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{(d)}
over
{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}
for all
d\geq d_{pq}
. Starting from the lowest
d_{0}=d_{pq}
. Properties proved in Lemma
5.54
shows that we can first extend the metric and metric connection to the sum
{\mathcal{E}}_{pr_{l}\to pq}^{(d_{0})}+{\mathcal{E}}_{r_{1}q\to pq}^{(d_{0})}
. This will be compatible with all existing metrics and connections. Then we extend (arbitrarily but satisfying the requirement for straightening) to
{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{(d_{0})}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}}
. Then it induces a metric and connection on the difference bundle
{\mathcal{F}}_{pq,\alpha}
. Via the stabilization map from
{\mathcal{U}}_{\alpha}
to
\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
, one obtains a metric on
\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{U}}_{pq}
which is straightened. Next, we can inductively construct metrics and connections on
{\mathcal{E}}_{pq}^{(d)}
for higher
d
. Once we finished for the lowest stratum
\alpha
, we can use the collar structure to extend to all nearby higher strata. For a next stratum
\beta
, we carry out the induction from
d_{0}
above again. We omit the details.

6.4.
Proof of Theorem
6.31

To save notations, we remove all the superscript “
+
