” which indicates the outer-collaring. Instead, we keep in mind that all objects and structures have corresponding collar structures or respect the collar structure.

6.4.1.
Smoothing charts without boundary

Consider a moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{pq}
of Floer trajectories which has no codimension one strata. Let
K_{pq}=(G_{pq},V_{pq},E_{pq},S_{pq})
be the global Kuranishi chart constructed. Then
V_{pq}
has no boundary or corner and one can apply Lashof’s smoothing theory directly. In this case there is no outer-collaring to the chart. The projection
\pi_{pq}:V_{pq}\to B_{pq}
is a
G_{pq}
-equivariant
C^{1}_{\rm loc}
fiberwise smooth topological submersion. Then by the existence of
G
-equivariant fiberwise submersions (Lemma
6.21
), one can choose a
G
-equivariant fiberwise submersion of
\pi_{pq}
which induces an isotopy class of
G_{pq}
-vector bundle reductions of
T_{\mu}V_{pq}
. By Lashof’s theorem (Theorem
6.9
) on stable
G
-smoothings, there exists a stable
G_{pq}
-smoothing on
V_{pq}
, i.e., a finite-dimensional orthogonal representation
{\bm{R}}_{pq}
of
G_{pq}
and a
G_{pq}
-invariant smooth structure on the product

\widehat{V}_{pq}:=V_{pq}\times{\bm{R}}_{pq}.

Let
\widehat{\pi}_{pq}:\widehat{V}_{pq}\to B_{pq}
be composition
\widehat{V}_{pq}\to V_{pq}\to B_{pq}
. Then there is an isomorphism of
G_{pq}
-equivariant vector bundles

T\widehat{V}_{pq}\cong T^{\rm vt}\widehat{V}_{pq}\oplus\widehat{\pi}_{pq}^{*}TB_{pq}.

(However, the projection
\widehat{\pi}_{pq}
may not be smooth and the fibers may not be smooth submanifolds.)

Moreover one can give smooth structures on the obstruction bundles. Indeed, one can inductively construct a structure of smooth equivariant
O(n)
-bundles on
\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)}\to\widehat{V}_{pq}
for all
d\geq d_{pq}
such that
\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)}
is a smooth subbundle of
\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d+1)}
by smoothing the relevant equivariant classifying map. The bundle
\widehat{R}_{pq}\to\widehat{V}_{pq}
, which is the trivial bundle with fiber
{\bm{R}}_{pq}
automatically has a smooth structure. We also use the inner product on
{\bm{R}}_{pq}
to equip the bundle
\widehat{R}_{pq}
with an inner product structure, which is a smooth inner product.

6.4.2.
Smoothing charts with boundary and corners

Now consider a chart
K_{pq}=(G_{pq},V_{pq},E_{pq},S_{pq})
. We have assumed that
K_{pq}
has a collar structure which was actually constructed via outer-collaring. We need to construct a stable smoothing which is of “product type” near the boundary and which extends the existing stable smoothings. We first state the induction hypothesis. As an initial remark, we induct on the energy of the moduli spaces
d_{pq}
.

Induction Hypothesis I. Stabilization.
Suppose we have the following data.

(1)

An orthogonal
G_{rs}
-representation
{\bm{R}}_{rs}
for each
rs
with
d_{rs}<d_{pq}
. We define a bundle

R_{uv\to rs}^{\sim}\to\partial^{ruvs}V_{rs}\ {\rm whenever}\ ruvs\in{\mathbb{A}}_{rs}^{\rm Floer}

as follows. Consider the product
V_{ruvs}=V_{ru}\times V_{uv}\times V_{vs}
. Then the trivial bundle
R_{uv}=V_{uv}\times{\bm{R}}_{uv}\to V_{uv}
is pulled back to
V_{ruvs}
. Via the stabilization map
{\rm Stab}_{F_{rs,ruvs}}(V_{ruvs})\hookrightarrow\partial^{ruvs}V_{rs}
this trivial bundle is pulled back to a trivial bundle. Then using the
G_{rs}
-equivariantization we obtain a not-necessarily-trivial vector bundle

R_{uv\to rs}^{\sim}\to\partial^{ruvs}V_{rs}\cong G_{rs}\times_{G_{ruvs}}({\rm Stab}_{F_{rs,ruvs}}(V_{ruvs})).

(2)

A
G_{rs}
-equivariant bundle embedding

\phi_{uv\to rs}:R_{uv\to rs}\to R_{rs}|_{\partial^{ruvs}V_{rs}}.

(3)

For each
\alpha=ru_{1}\cdots u_{l}s\in{\mathbb{A}}_{rs}^{\rm Floer}
, define a bundle map

\phi_{rs,\alpha}:R_{\alpha}:=R_{ru_{1}}\boxplus\cdots\boxplus R_{u_{l}s}\to R_{rs}

to be the sum of the maps
\phi_{ru_{1}\to rs},\ldots,\phi_{u_{l}s\to rs}
. We require that the bundle map is actually a bundle embedding. This induces bundle embeddings

\phi_{\beta\alpha}:R_{\alpha}\to R_{\beta}\ \alpha\leq\beta,\ \alpha,\beta\in{\mathbb{A}}_{rs}^{\rm Floer}.

We require that the collection
\{R_{rs}\}
and the collection
\{\phi_{\beta\alpha}\}
satisfy the requirements for stabilization of a K-chart lift (see Definition
6.25
) up to level
d_{pq}
, i.e., the K-charts for moduli spaces
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{rs}^{\rm Floer}
with
d_{rs}<d_{pq}
are equipped with stabilizations induced from
R_{rs}
with compatibility conditions satisfied.

(4)

We also assume we have a
G_{rs}
-invariant inner product on the trivial bundle
R_{rs}\to V_{rs}
for all
d_{rs}<d_{pq}
such that the bundle maps
\phi_{rs,\alpha}
are all isometric.

Moreover, we assume that the representations
{\bm{R}}_{rs}
and bundle embeddings
\phi_{uv\to rs}
are of the following particular forms. (One can see the discussion in Section
6.2.4
for why we impose the following requirement.)

(1)

We have a
G_{rs}
-equivariant orthogonal decomposition

{\bm{R}}_{rs}={\bm{R}}_{rs,-}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{rs,0}

and a
G_{rs}
-equivariant orthogonal decomposition

{\bm{R}}_{rs,-}=\bigoplus_{r<w<s}{\bm{R}}_{rs,w}=\bigoplus_{r<w<s}{\bm{R}}_{rw}^{rs}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{ws}^{rs}.

Intuitively,
{\bm{R}}_{rs,-}
comes from lower stratum via Frobenius reciprocity, and
{\bm{R}}_{rs,0}
is chosen so that a relative smoothing exists. Then one has the (trivial) bundles
R_{rs,-}
,
R_{rs,0}
,
R_{rw}^{rs}
,
R_{ws}^{rs}
over
V_{rs}
.

(2)

Whenever
r<w<s
, the representations
{\bm{R}}_{rw}^{rs}
resp.
{\bm{R}}_{ws}^{rs}
contains
{\bm{R}}_{rw}
resp.
{\bm{R}}_{ws}
as orthogonal subrepresentations of
G_{rw}
resp.
G_{ws}
.

(3)

For all
uv\prec rs
(which implies
r\leq u<v\leq s
), abbreviate
\phi_{uv}=\phi_{uv\to rs}
. Then the range of
\phi_{uv}
is contained in
R_{rs,-}
and can be written as

\phi_{uv}=\bigoplus_{r<w<s}\phi_{uv,w},\ \phi_{uv,w}:R_{uv\to rs}\to R_{rs,w}.

Moreover, for each
w
, the map
\phi_{uv,w}
satisfies the following conditions. We write
\phi_{uv,w}:R_{uv\to rs}\to R_{rs,w}=R_{rw}^{rs}\oplus R_{ws}^{rs}
as the sum
\phi_{uv,rw}\oplus\phi_{uv,ws}
.

(a)

If
w\leq u
, then
\phi_{uv,rw}=0
. Moreover, over the stratum
V_{rwuvs}\subset V_{ruvs}
,
\phi_{uv,ws}
is induced from the bundle map

\textstyle{R_{uv\to ws}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{uv\to ws}}
\textstyle{R_{ws}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V_{wuvs}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V_{ws}}

and the linear inclusion
{\bm{R}}_{ws}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{ws}^{rs}
.

(b)

If
w\geq v
, then
\phi_{uv,ws}=0
. Moreover, over the stratum
V_{ruvws}\subset V_{ruvs}
,
\phi_{uv,rw}
is induced from the bundle map

\textstyle{R_{uv\to rw}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{uv\to rw}}
\textstyle{R_{rw}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V_{ruvw}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V_{rw}}

and the linear inclusion
{\bm{R}}_{rw}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{rw}^{rs}
.

(c)

If
u<w<v
, then the following is true. Consider the inclusion

{\bm{R}}_{uw}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{wv}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{uw}^{uv}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{wv}^{uv}\hookrightarrow{\bm{R}}_{uv}.

The orthogonal projection

{\bm{R}}_{uv}\to{\bm{R}}_{uw}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{wv}

is
G_{uwv}
-equivariant, hence induces a bundle map

R_{uv}|_{V_{uwv}}\to R_{uw}\boxplus R_{wv}.

On the other hand, there are bundle embeddings

\displaystyle\ R_{uw\to rw}\hookrightarrow R_{rw}\hookrightarrow R_{rw}^{rs},\

\displaystyle\ R_{wv\to ws}\hookrightarrow R_{ws}\hookrightarrow R_{ws}^{rs}.

Then over the stratum
V_{ruwvs}\subset V_{ruvs}
, the map
\phi_{uv,w}
is the composition

R_{\underline{\smash{uv\to rs}}}|_{V_{\underline{\smash{ruwvs}}}}\to R_{\underline{\smash{uw\to rs}}}\oplus R_{\underline{\smash{wv\to rs}}}\to R_{rw}^{rs}\oplus R_{ws}^{rs}.

(d)

If none of the above happends, then
\phi_{uv,w}=0
.

Moreover, the bundles and bundle maps will be added with
\widehat{\cdot}
when lifted to the stabilizations
\widehat{V}_{rs}
. Such lifts are canonical.

Induction Hypothesis II. Fiberwise submersion.
Whenever
d_{rs}<d_{pq}
, we have chosen a
G_{rs}
-equivariant fiberwise submersion (Definition
6.18
) of
\widehat{\pi}_{rs}:\widehat{V}_{rs}\to B_{rs}
, which is a microbundle isomorphism

T_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{rs}\cong T^{\rm vt}_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{rs}\oplus\widehat{\pi}_{rs}^{*}T_{\mu}B_{d_{rs}}

which is the canonical projection map on the second component. Together with the structure of
C_{\rm loc}^{1}
fiberwise smooth
G_{rs}
-bundle on
\widehat{\pi}_{rs}
, this provides an isotopy class of vector bundle lifts

T^{\rm vt}\widehat{V}_{rs}\oplus\widehat{\pi}_{rs}^{*}TB_{d_{rs}}\to T_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{rs},

(6.8)

where the isotopy class is fixed by Equation (
6.3
).

Induction Hypothesis III. Stable smoothings and bundle smoothings.
Whenever
d_{rs}<d_{pq}
, we have chosen a
G_{rs}
-invariant smoothing on
\widehat{V}_{rs}:=V_{rs}\times{\bm{R}}_{rs}
in the
G_{rs}
-isotopy class corresponding to stable isotopy class of the vector bundle lift (
6.8
). Moreover, we have chosen a structure of smooth
G_{rs}
-equivariant
O(n)
-bundle on
\widehat{O}_{rs}^{(d)}\to\widehat{V}_{rs}
for all
d\geq d_{rs}
and a structure of smooth
G_{rs}
-equivariant
O(n)
-bundle on
\widehat{R}_{rs}
.

Before we state the conditions about these structures, we summarize a few consequences of these induction hypotheses. First, the stable smoothings induce, for each stratum
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
, a
G_{\alpha}
-equivariant smooth structure on the product

\widehat{V}_{\alpha}=\widehat{V}_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times\widehat{V}_{r_{k}q}

and hence a
G_{pq}
-equivariant smooth structure on
\widehat{V}_{\alpha}^{\sim}
by taking the equivariantization. Second, the smooth structures on the vector bundles induce smooth structures on the product bundles
\widehat{O}_{\alpha}
and
\widehat{R}_{\alpha}
. Third, as the bundle
Q_{rs}\to V_{rs}
is trivial, the pullback
\widehat{Q}_{rs}\to\widehat{V}_{rs}
is automatically smooth. Similarly, the bundle

\widehat{Q}_{rs,\alpha}=(\widehat{Q}_{\alpha})^{\bot}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}

is smooth because it comes from a product bundle.

Now we state the conditions.

(1)

The bundle embedding

\widehat{\phi}_{\beta\alpha}:\widehat{R}_{\alpha}\to\widehat{R}_{\beta}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{\beta}}

is smooth.
19
19

19

This is not automatic as this bundle embedding is not purely induced from linear maps between representation spaces.
This implies that the orthogonal complement

\widehat{R}_{rs,\alpha}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}

is a smooth bundle.

(2)

Whenever
d^{\prime}>d\geq d_{rs}
, the natural bundle embedding
\widehat{O}_{rs}^{(d)}\to\widehat{O}_{rs}^{(d^{\prime})}
induced from (
5.34
) is smooth.

(3)

For all
d\geq d_{rs}
, the bundle embedding

\widehat{\phi}_{uv\to rs}:\widehat{O}_{uv\to rs}^{(d)}\to\widehat{O}_{rs}^{(d)}|_{\partial^{ruvs}V_{rs}}

is smooth. This implies that (when
d=d_{rs}
) the orthogonal complement

\widehat{O}_{rs,\alpha}\to V_{\alpha}

is a smooth bundle.

(4)

It follows that

\widehat{F}_{rs,\alpha}:=\widehat{O}_{rs,\alpha}\oplus\widehat{Q}_{rs,\alpha}\oplus\widehat{R}_{rs,\alpha}

over the smooth manifold with corners
\widehat{V}_{\alpha}
is smooth. Then we require that the stabilization map

\widehat{\theta}_{rs,\alpha}:{\rm Stab}_{\widehat{F}_{rs,\alpha}}(\widehat{V}_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{rs}

is a germ of diffeomorphisms onto an open set. These structures ensure that the projection map

\widehat{\pi}_{rs,\alpha}:\partial^{\alpha}V_{rs}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}^{\sim}

is smooth.

(5)

For all
d\geq d_{rs}
, the bundle isomorphism

\widehat{\psi}_{rs,\alpha}:\widehat{\pi}_{rs,\alpha}^{*}\Big(\widehat{O}_{rs}^{(d)}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}^{\sim}}\Big)\to\widehat{O}_{rs}^{(d)}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{rs}}

is smooth.

(6)

The existing smoothings (on both domains and bundles) respect the corner structures. Namely, for all
rs
with
d_{rs}<d_{pq}
, item (B) of Theorem
6.31
is satisfied.

Now we start to construct the stabilized smoothings corresponding to the Kuranishi chart
K_{pq}
.

Inductive Construction I. We construct an orthogonal
G_{pq}
-space
{\bm{R}}_{pq}
(which defines the trivial bundle
R_{pq}\to V_{pq}
) and bundle embeddings

\phi_{rs\to pq}:R_{rs\to pq}\to R_{pq}|_{\partial^{prsq}V_{pq}}

which, together with the existing stabilizations and bundle embeddings, satisfy the conditions required for stabilizations.

(1)

Firstly, we need to find an orthogonal
G_{pq}
-representation
{\bm{R}}_{pq}
such that the stabilization
R_{pq}=V_{pq}\times{\bm{R}}_{pq}
receives embeddings from
R_{\alpha}
for all
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm Floer}_{pq}
.

To start, for each
p<w<q
, consider the
G_{pw}
-representation
{\bm{R}}_{pw}
and the
G_{wq}
-representation
{\bm{R}}_{wq}
granted by the induction hypothesis. By Frobenius reciprocity, there exist an orthogonal
G_{pq}
-representation
{\bm{R}}_{pw}^{pq}
which contains
{\bm{R}}_{pw}
as a subrepresentation of
G_{pw}
, and an orthogonal
G_{pq}
-representation
{\bm{R}}_{wq}^{pq}
which contains
{\bm{R}}_{wq}
as a subrepresentation of
G_{wq}
. Then define

{\bm{R}}_{pq}:=\bigoplus_{p<w<q}{\bm{R}}_{pw}^{pq}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{wq}^{pq}

(6.9)

which is an orthogonal
G_{pq}
-space. Now we define the bundle embeddings

\phi_{rs\to pq}:R_{rs\to pq}\to R_{pq}|_{\partial^{prsq}V_{pq}}.

We abbreviate the decomposition
6.9
as

{\bm{R}}_{pq}=\bigoplus_{w}{\bm{R}}_{pq,w}=\bigoplus_{p<w<q}{\bm{R}}_{pw}^{pq}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{wq}^{pq}.

Abbreviate the bundle embedding to be defined by
\phi_{rs}
. Then we define it to be the direct sum

\phi_{rs}=\bigoplus_{w}\phi_{rs,w}=\bigoplus_{w}\phi_{rs,pw}\oplus\phi_{rs,wq}\ {\rm where}\ \phi_{rs,w}:R_{rs\to pq}\to R_{pq,w}|_{\partial^{prsq}V_{pq}}.

In fact, for each
w
, one can define the restriction of
\phi_{rs,w}
to the corresponding stratum involving
w
, such as
pwrsq
,
prswq
, or
prwsq
in the form described in
Induction Hypothesis I
. These are all codimension-1 boundary strata of
prsq
. Then using the collar structure of existing objects and a cut-off function which only depends on the collar coordinate, one can turn off the corresponding maps
\phi_{rs,w}
once we go away from this corner. On the other hand, if
w=r
or
w=s
, we just have a bundle embedding coming from the induction hypothesis. One can check that the newly constructed objects still satisfy the requirement for a stabilization up to level
d_{pq}
(see Definition
6.25
).

(2)

Secondly, we would like to construct an inner product on
R_{pq}
.

We start from a minimal
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
. Notice that inside
R_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}
, there are a collection of embedded subbundles
R_{\beta\to pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}
for all
\beta
with
\alpha\leq\beta<pq
with an inner product equipped. We first check that, for any pair
\beta,\beta^{\prime}
of such strata, their inner products agree on the overlap

R_{\beta\to pq}\cap R_{\beta^{\prime}\to pq}\subset R_{pq}.

In fact the intersection
\partial^{\beta}V_{pq}\cap\partial^{\beta^{\prime}}V_{pq}
is a deeper stratum
\partial^{\beta\#\beta^{\prime}}V_{pq}
. Here
\beta\#\beta^{\prime}
stands for the stratum described by the word which includes all the intermediate capped orbits in
\beta
and
\beta^{\prime}
between
p
and
q
. We can in fact check from the explicit construction that

R_{\beta\to pq}\cap R_{\beta^{\prime}\to pq}=R_{\beta\#\beta^{\prime}\to pq}

Hence by induction hypothesis, the inner products agrees on overlaps.

Now there are two special codimension one strata,
\beta_{1}=pr_{1}q
and
\beta_{l}=pr_{l}q
. We can check that

R_{\beta\to pq}\subset R_{\beta_{1}\to pq}+R_{\beta_{l}\to pq}.

Hence we can define an inner product on the sum
R_{\beta_{1}\to pq}+R_{\beta_{l}\to pq}
which extends the existing ones. Then extend arbitrarily to
R_{pq}
over this stratum.

Inductively, we can use the collar structure near
\alpha
to extend the inner product on
\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
to a neighborhood. Then the same argument above can be applied to construct an inner product inductively on
R_{pq}
.

Inductive Construction II. We construct smooth bundle structures on the obstruction bundles
\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)}\to\widehat{V}_{pq}
for all
d\geq d_{pq}
over
\partial\widehat{V}_{pq}
.
The construction is inductive on strata
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
of
pq
. We start with a minimal
\alpha
. Then there are the vector bundles

O_{\alpha}^{(d)}\to V_{\alpha}=V_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times V_{r_{l}q},\ \forall d\geq d_{pq}.

Denote by its pullback to
\widehat{V}_{\alpha}
by

\widehat{O}_{\alpha}^{(d)}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}.

The induction hypothesis granted a smooth
O(n)
-bundle structure on it which is the product of each individual factor. Notice that this is a subbundle of
\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}
. We would like to extend this smooth structure to a smooth structure on
\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}
.

Start with
d=d_{0}=d_{pq}
and remember
\widehat{O}_{pq}=\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d_{0})}
. Consider any intermediate stratum
\beta=ps_{1}\cdots s_{m}q
. Then one has the inclusion induced by (
5.53
)

\widehat{O}_{\alpha}^{(d_{0})}\subset\widehat{O}_{\beta}^{(d_{0})}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}\subset\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}.

Notice that by the induction hypothesis, the smooth structure on
\widehat{O}_{\beta}^{(d_{0})}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}
extends the smooth structure on
\widehat{O}_{\alpha}^{(d_{0})}
. This requires that the smooth structure on
\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}
needs to be an extension of all such smooth structures. Indeed, consider two codimension one strata,
pr_{1}q
and
pr_{l}q
. Consider the two bundles

\displaystyle\ \widehat{O}_{pr_{l}\to pq}^{(d_{0})}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}},\

\displaystyle\ \widehat{O}_{r_{1}q\to pq}^{(d_{0})}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}.

Abbreviate them temporarily by
\widehat{O}_{r_{l}}^{(d_{0})}
and
\widehat{O}_{r_{1}}^{(d_{0})}
respectively. Then it is easy to see that for any intermediate stratum
\beta
, one has

\widehat{O}_{\beta}^{(d_{0})}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}\subset\widehat{O}_{r_{l}}^{(d_{0})}+\widehat{O}_{r_{1}}^{(d_{0})}.

(6.10)

Then Lemma
6.33
below provides a smooth structure on the sum
\widehat{O}_{r_{l}}^{(d_{0})}+\widehat{O}_{r_{1}}^{(d_{0})}
. Then we extend this extension to
\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}
by choosing an arbitrary smooth structure on its orthogonal complement.

Lemma 6.33
.

Let
M
be a smooth manifold and let
E\to M
be a topological vector bundle equipped with an inner product. Let
E_{1},E_{2}\subset E
be subbundles such that
E=E_{1}+E_{2}
. Moreover, suppose
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
are equipped with structures of smooth vector bundles with respect to which the restriction of the inner product is smooth, such that the intersection
E_{1}\cap E_{2}
is both a smooth subbundle of
E_{1}
and a smooth subbundle of
E_{2}
. Then there exists a unique smooth bundle structure of
E
which extends the smooth bundle structures on
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
such that the inner product is smooth.

Proof.

Define a smooth vector bundle
E^{\prime}\to M
from
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
as

E^{\prime}:=E_{1}^{\prime}\oplus E_{2}^{\prime}\oplus(E_{1}\cap E_{2})

where
E_{1}^{\prime}
resp.
E_{2}^{\prime}
is the orthogonal complement of
E_{1}\cap E_{2}
in
E_{1}
resp.
E_{2}
. Then there is an canonical isometric bundle isomorphism
E^{\prime}\to E
.
∎

As
\widehat{O}_{pq,\alpha}
is the orthogonal complement of
\widehat{O}_{\alpha}^{(d_{0})}
, the above smooth structure induces a smooth structure on
\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}
, hence one on
\widehat{O}_{pq,\alpha}\to\widehat{V}_{\alpha}
. On the other hand, run the same argument as above, one can cook up a smooth
O(n)
-bundle structure on
\widehat{R}_{pq}|_{\widehat{V}_{\alpha}}
which extends existing ones. Hence the orthogonal complement
\widehat{R}_{pq,\alpha}
is a smooth
O(n)
-bundle. The bundle
\widehat{Q}_{pq,\alpha}
, which is essentially trivial, automatically carries a smooth structure. Hence the bundle

\widehat{F}_{pq,\alpha}=\widehat{O}_{pq,\alpha}\oplus\widehat{Q}_{pq,\alpha}\oplus\widehat{R}_{pq,\alpha}

(6.11)

is smooth. Then the stabilization map (see (
5.50
))

\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}:{\rm Stab}_{\widehat{F}_{pq,\alpha}}(\widehat{V}_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}

and its equivariantization induce a smooth structure on the stratum
\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}
.

Next we can inductively construct smooth structures on the bundle
\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{pq,\underline{\smash{\alpha}}}}
for all
d\geq d_{0}=d_{pq}
. This is similar to the construction of inner products. We omit the details. Using equivariantizations and the bundle isomorphisms coming from the stabilization map associated to
\widehat{F}_{pq,\alpha}
, one obtains smooth structures on the bundle
\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}}
for all
d\geq d_{pq}
. Using the collar structure, we extend slightly to a neighborhood of
\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}
inside
\widehat{V}_{pq}
.

We can carry on the induction for all strata
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
. Suppose we have constructed a smooth structure near the boundary of
\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}
and smooth bundle structures on
\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)}
over this neighborhood. Now start with
d=d_{0}=d_{pq}
and we would like to construct the smooth structure of
\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}
and the smooth bundle structure on
\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d_{0})}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}}
. First, the induction hypothesis granted a smooth structure on the product
\widehat{V}_{\alpha}
. This is compatible with all the smooth structures near the boundary of
\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}
. Then similar to above, one can have a smooth structure on the sum (the right hand side of (
6.10
)). Then extend to a smooth structure on
\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}}
. The stabilization map then grants a smooth structure on
\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}
which extends the existing one near the boundary. The bundle isomorphism provides a smooth bundle structure of
\widehat{O}_{pq}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}}
. Inductively, one extends the smooth bundle structure to
\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)}|_{\partial^{\alpha}\widehat{V}_{pq}}
for all
d\geq d_{0}
. Then use the collar structure we extend the structures slightly into the interior of
\widehat{V}_{pq}
.

Inductive Construction III. We construct a
G_{pq}
-equivariant vector bundle reduction of
T_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{pq}
which is smooth near the boundary and is in the the isotopy class of the vector bundle reduction induced from the existing smoothings.
The previous construction induces a
G_{pq}
-smoothing on a neighborhood
N_{\epsilon}(\partial\widehat{V}_{pq})
of the boundary, using the smooth structures on bundles of the form (
6.11
) and equivariantization. Hence there is a corresponding (isotopy class) of vector bundle lift of the tangent microbundle
T_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{pq}
restricted to this region. From the construction we can see that

T\left(N_{\epsilon}(\partial\widehat{V}_{pq})\right)\cong\left(T^{\rm vt}\widehat{V}_{pq}\oplus\widehat{\pi}_{pq}^{*}TB_{d_{pq}}\right)|_{N_{\epsilon}(\partial\widehat{V}_{pq})}.

On the other hand, we know that via a fiberwise submersion on
\widehat{V}_{pq}
one can obtain another vector bundle lift of
T_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{pq}
by the same vector bundle.

We claim that these two vector bundle lifts, when restricted to their common domains, are in the same stable
G_{pq}
-isotopy class of vector bundle lifts. We show why this is the case if
\alpha
is a codimension 1 stratum. In this case, we know that

\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}={\rm Stab}_{F_{pq,\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})

i.e., the boundary
\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
is a disk bundle over
V_{\alpha}
. We can choose an (invariant) connection on
F_{pq,\alpha}
to induce a smooth splitting

TF_{pq,\alpha}\cong T^{\rm vt}F_{pq,\alpha}\oplus\pi_{F_{pq,\alpha}}^{*}TV_{\alpha}.

It also induces a corresponding splitting on the microbundle level. Although the fibers of the projection onto
B_{\delta(\alpha)}
are no longer smooth, the microbundle version of the above splitting can still be restricted to each fiber. Then combining with the microbundle splitting of
T_{\mu}V_{\alpha}
one obtains

T_{\mu}(\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq})\cong\pi_{F_{pq,\alpha}}^{*}(F_{pq,\alpha})_{\mu}\oplus\pi_{F_{pq,\alpha}}^{*}T_{\mu}V_{\alpha}\cong T^{\rm vt}_{\mu}(\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq})\oplus\pi^{*}T_{\mu}B_{\delta(\alpha)}.

Notice that this is an equivariant fiberwise submersion of
\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}\to\partial^{\delta(\alpha)}B_{d}
(see Definition
6.18
). The collar structure can be used to extend this fiberwise submersion trivially into the collar region. Using Lemma
6.21
one can extend it globally to
V_{pq}
. Then using the fiberwise smooth structure we obtained a vector bundle lift of
T_{\mu}V_{pq}
.

On the other hand, the vector bundle lift on
V_{\alpha}
induced from smoothing is in the same stable isotopy class of vector bundle lifts as the one from fiberwise smooth structure and microbundle splitting. Therefore, one can find another orthogonal
G_{pq}
-space
{\bm{R}}_{pq}^{\prime}
such that the
{\bm{R}}_{pq}^{\prime}
-stabilization of the interior vector bundle lift of
T_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{pq}
is isotopic to the one from the boundary smooth structure. Also notice that one can choose a
G_{pq}
-invariant continuous cut-off function on the overlap of the two domains of the vector bundle lifts to interpolate this two vector bundle lifts using the isotopy. Note that this is one salient feature of the existence of collar structures: we are free to interpolate between different data over the collar regions. Therefore, we have obtained a vector bundle lift of
T_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{pq}
which is smooth near the boundary.

In general, when
\alpha
is not a codimension 1 stratum, there are many intermediate stratum. However, one can choose connections on all the intermediate difference bundles
F_{\beta\alpha}
compatibly, utilizing Lemma
5.54
. This still allows us to build the interior microbundle splitting which extends the boundary ones. We omit the details.

Inductive Construction IV. Smoothing for
pq
.
In the previous steps, we have obtained a smooth structure on a neighborhood of
\partial\widehat{V}_{pq}
, a smooth
O(n)
-bundle structure of
\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)}
over this neighborhood, and a
G_{pq}
-equivariant vector bundle reduction

\rho_{pq}:(T^{\rm vt}\widehat{V}_{pq}\oplus\widehat{\pi}_{pq}^{*}B_{pq})_{\mu}\to T_{\mu}\widehat{V}_{pq}

which is smooth near
\partial\widehat{V}_{pq}
. Then one can apply the relative version of Lashof’s
G
-smoothing theorem (see Appendix
B
and Theorem
B.3
) to obtain another
G_{pq}
-orthogonal space
{\bm{R}}_{pq,0}
and a
G_{pq}
-smoothing on
{\rm Int}\widehat{V_{pq}}\times{\bm{R}}_{pq,0}
which coincides with the stabilization of the existing smoothing. Redefine
{\bm{R}}_{pq}
by taking direct sum with
{\bm{R}}_{pq,0}
and redefine
\widehat{V}_{pq}
,
\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)}
etc. Moreover, one can extend the smooth structures on
\widehat{O}_{pq}^{(d)}
inductively to the interior of
\widehat{V}_{pq}
. Furthermore, we extend the existing inner product on the trivial bundle
R_{pq}
to the whole
\widehat{V}_{pq}
smoothly.

Finally, we see that we have constructed a smoothing for any K-chart
K_{pq}
with energy
d_{pq}
. Furthermore, they satisfy the induction hypotheses listed at the beginning. Therefore, Theorem
6.31
holds. ∎

7.
Constructions for PSS, SSP, and the homotopy

In this subsection, we discuss K-charts for PSS, SSP, and continuation type moduli spaces. The constructions here are carried out almost verbatim as the case of moduli spaces of Floer trajectories. Therefore, our main purpose here is to fix the notations, and only the key modifications are presented in detail.

7.1.
PSS bimodule and SSP bimodule

7.1.1.
Thimbles and auxiliary moduli spaces

Definition 7.1
.

(1)

A
prestable PSS thimble
is a triple
(\Sigma,\Sigma_{\text{PSS}},{\bf L})
where
\Sigma
is a genus
0
prestable curve with two marked points
z_{-},z_{+}
, with
\Sigma_{\text{PSS}}
being a distinguished horizontal irreducible component of
\Sigma
, and
{\bf L}=({\rm L}_{i})
where
{\rm L}_{i}
is a lateral line on each horizontal component lying between
\Sigma_{\text{PSS}}
(included!) and the marked point
z_{+}
(see Figure
2
).

Figure 2.
An example of prestable PSS thimble. The red curves are the lateral lines and the gray sphere is
\Sigma_{\rm PSS}
on which we interpolate between the Hamiltonian
H
and a constant function.

(2)

A
prestable SSP thimble
is a triple
(\Sigma,\Sigma_{\text{SSP}},{\bf L})
where
\Sigma
is a genus
0
prestable curve with two marked points
z_{-},z_{+}
, with
\Sigma_{\text{SSP}}
being a distinguished horizontal irreducible component of
\Sigma
, and
{\bf L}=({\rm L}_{i})
where
{\rm L}_{i}
is a lateral line on each horizontal component lying between the marked point
z_{-}
and
\Sigma_{\text{SSP}}
.

By imposing extra marked points and stability conditions, one can define the notion of stable marked PSS/SSP thimble as in Definition
5.12
. Given an integer
d>0
, the moduli space of stable PSS resp. SSP thimbles in
\mathbb{CP}^{d}
is the set of equivalence classes of objects

(\Sigma,\Sigma_{\rm PSS},{\bf L},u),\ \ \ \ \ {\rm resp.}\ \ \ \ \ (\Sigma,\Sigma_{\rm SSP},{\bf L},u)

where
(\Sigma,\Sigma_{\text{PSS}}/\Sigma_{\text{SSP}},{\bf L})
is a prestable PSS/SSP thimble and
u:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d}
is a stable map. We define
{\mathcal{F}}^{\text{PSS}}_{0,2}(d)
to be the subset of moduli spaces of stable PSS thimbles such that the underlying map
u:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d}
is not contained in any hyperplane, and
{\mathcal{F}}^{\text{SSP}}_{0,2}(d)
can be defined similarly. These two moduli spaces are smooth manifolds with corners. Just as Lemma
5.15
, the following statements hold.

(1)

The natural forgetful map
{\mathcal{F}}^{\text{PSS}}_{0,2}(d),{\mathcal{F}}^{\text{SSP}}_{0,2}(d)\to{\mathcal{F}}_{0,2}(d)
is smooth.

(2)

The evaluation maps
\text{ev}_{\pm}
at the marked points
z_{\pm}
are smooth and stratified submersive onto
\mathbb{CP}^{d}
.

(3)

By intersecting with
d(d+2)
generic hyperplanes in
\mathbb{CP}^{d}
, the moduli spaces
{\mathcal{F}}^{\text{PSS}}_{0,2}(d),{\mathcal{F}}^{\text{SSP}}_{0,2}(d)
are locally diffeomorphic to a real blowup of the Deligne–Mumford space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,d(d+2)}
.

Note that we do not necessarily equip all the horizontal components of
(\Sigma,{\bf L})
with a lateral line, so the real blowup in (3) is different from the one in Lemma
5.15
.

The spaces
{\mathcal{F}}_{0,2}^{\rm PSS}(d)
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{0,2}^{\rm SSP}(d)
are stratified by the same poset
{\mathbb{A}}_{d}
of ordered partitions of
d
(with the same depth function, see Notation
5.7
). However, the geometric meaning of an element
\delta=(d_{0},\ldots,d_{l})\in{\mathbb{A}}_{d}
is different. For example, in the PSS case, the integer
d_{0}
represents the total degree of the components between
z_{-}
and
\Sigma_{\rm PSS}
(included).

Notation 7.2
.

(1)

Let
p,q
be capped 1-periodic capped Hamiltonian orbits. Introduce the integers

\displaystyle\ d_{\bullet q}:={\mathcal{A}}_{H}(q)-C^{\rm PSS},\

\displaystyle\ d_{p\bullet}:=C^{\rm SSP}-{\mathcal{A}}_{H}(p)

where
C^{\rm PSS}
resp.
C^{\rm SSP}
is the constant chosen to define the
{\rm PSS}
resp.
{\rm SSP}
moduli spaces (see Subsection
4.3
).

(2)

Extra symmetry groups

G^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q}:=\{g\in PU(d_{\bullet q}+1)\ |\ g([1,0,\ldots,0])=[1,0,\ldots,0]\in\mathbb{CP}^{d_{\bullet p}}\}\cong U(d_{\bullet p})

G_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}:=\{g\in PU(d_{p\bullet}+1)\ |\ g([1,0,\ldots,0])=[1,0,\ldots,0]\in\mathbb{CP}^{d_{p\bullet}}\}\cong U(d_{p\bullet})

where the isomorphisms are induced from (
5.3
).

(3)

Homogeneous posets with partial order induced from refinement of words

{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}:=\{\bullet r_{1}\cdots r_{l}q|r_{1}<\cdots<r_{l}<p\text{ as objects in }T^{\rm Floer}\}

{\mathbb{A}}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}:=\{pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}\bullet|p<r_{1}<\cdots<r_{l}\text{ as objects in }T^{\rm Floer}\},

with depth functions

{\rm depth}(\bullet r_{1}\cdots r_{l}q)=l,\ {\rm depth}(pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}\bullet)=l.

The concatenation of words induces maps between homogeneous posets

{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet p}^{\rm PSS}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm Floer}_{pq}\to{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}

{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm Floer}_{pq}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{q\bullet}^{\rm SSP}\times\to{\mathbb{A}}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}.

There are natural maps of posets

\delta^{\rm PSS}:{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\to{\mathbb{A}}_{d_{\bullet q}},\ \delta^{\rm SSP}:{\mathbb{A}}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}\to{\mathbb{A}}_{d_{p\bullet}}

defined by

\delta^{\rm PSS}(\bullet r_{1}\cdots r_{l}q):=(d_{\bullet r_{1}},\dots,d_{r_{l}q}),\ \delta^{\rm SSP}(pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}\bullet):=(d_{pr_{1}},\dots,d_{r_{l}\bullet}),

such that the following diagrams commute.

\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet p}^{\rm PSS}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm Floer}_{pq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{\rm PSS}\times\delta}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{\rm PSS}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{d_{\bullet p}}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{d_{pq}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{d_{\bullet q}}}

\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm Floer}_{pq}\times{\mathbb{A}}_{q\bullet}^{\rm SSP}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta\times\delta^{\rm SSP}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{\rm PSS}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{d_{pq}}\times{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm SSP}_{d_{q\bullet}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{A}}_{d_{p\bullet}}}

(4)

For an index
\alpha=\bullet r_{1}\cdots r_{l}p\in{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q}
, there is an embedding of Lie groups

G^{\text{PSS}}_{\alpha}:=G^{\text{PSS}}_{\bullet r_{1}}\times\cdots\times G_{r_{l}q}\hookrightarrow G^{\text{PSS}}_{\bullet q}=G^{\text{PSS}}_{\bullet q}

as from Notation
5.7
, which further induces an embedding
G^{\text{PSS}}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow G^{\text{PSS}}_{\beta}
for any pair
\alpha\leq\beta
in
{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet p}^{\rm PSS}
. Analogously, there are embeddings of Lie groups
G^{\text{SSP}}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow G^{\text{SSP}}_{\beta}
where
\alpha\leq\beta
are in
{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm SSP}_{p\bullet}
.

(5)

The system of auxiliary moduli spaces

B^{\text{PSS}}_{d}:=\big\{x=[\Sigma,\Sigma_{\text{PSS}},{\bf L},u]\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\text{PSS}}_{0,2}(d)\ |\ {\rm ev}_{-}(x)=[1,0,\ldots,0]\in\mathbb{CP}^{d}\big\},

B^{\text{SSP}}_{d}:=\big\{x=[\Sigma,\Sigma_{\text{SSP}},{\bf L},u]\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\text{SSP}}_{0,2}(d)\ |\ {\rm ev}_{-}(x)=[1,0,\ldots,0]\in\mathbb{CP}^{d}\big\},

where
{\rm ev}_{-}(x)
is the image of the evaluation map of
u
at the marked point
z_{-}
.
Each
B^{\text{PSS}}_{d}
resp.
B^{\text{SSP}}_{d}
is a smooth
{\mathbb{A}}_{d}
-manifold and has a
G_{d}
-action.

(6)

For
\alpha=\bullet r_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q}
(resp.
pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}\bullet\in{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm SSP}_{p\bullet}
), define

B^{\rm PSS}_{\alpha}:=B^{\rm PSS}_{d_{\bullet r_{1}}}\times\cdots\times B_{d_{r_{l}q}}

\text{resp. }B^{\rm SSP}_{\alpha}:=B_{d_{pr_{1}}}\times\cdots\times B^{\rm SSP}_{d_{r_{l}\bullet}}.

Then
B^{\rm PSS}_{\alpha}
(resp.
B^{\rm SSP}_{\alpha}
) has a smooth
G_{\alpha}^{\rm PSS}
(resp.
G_{\alpha}^{\rm SSP}
) action.

(7)

Just as Equation (
5.11
), under the group embedding
G^{\rm PSS}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow G_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
, there is an equivariant embedding

B^{\rm PSS}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow B^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q},

which is part of the data of the embeddings of the form

B^{\rm PSS}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow B^{\rm PSS}_{\beta}

for any
\alpha\leq\beta
under the group embedding
G_{\alpha}^{\rm PSS}\hookrightarrow G_{\beta}^{\rm PSS}
. This fact is also true after replacing PSS by SSP.

7.1.2.
Description of global charts

The following statement allows to construct framings from solutions to the PSS/SSP equations. The proof is the same as the proof of Corollary
5.30
using the integrality assumptions.

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
(\Sigma,\Sigma_{\rm PSS},{\bm{L}})
be a prestable PSS thimble, and suppose that

u:\Sigma\to M

is a
stable PSS thimble
, i.e. the restriction of
u
to the irreducible component
\Sigma_{\rm PSS}
solves the PSS equation (
4.6
), its restriction to the cylindrical components on the right of
\Sigma_{\rm PSS}
solves the Floer equation (
4.2
), while its restriction to other components solves the genuine
J
-holomorphic curve equation, and
u
has finite automorphism group. Consider the
2
-form
\Omega_{u,{\rm PSS}}
defined by

\Omega_{u,{\rm PSS}}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\displaystyle u^{*}\omega-d(H_{s,t}^{\rm PSS}(u)dt)&\text{ on the distinguished component $\Sigma_{\rm PSS}$}\\
\displaystyle u^{*}\omega-d(H_{t}(u)dt)&\text{ on cylindrical components on the right of $\Sigma_{\rm PSS}$}\\
\displaystyle u^{*}\omega&\text{ on other irreducible components.}\end{array}\right.

(7.1)

Then there exists a holomorphic Hermitian line bundle on
\Sigma
whose curvature form is
-2\pi{\bm{i}}\Omega_{u,{\rm PSS}}
. Moreover, this line bundle is unique up to isomorphism.

Similarly, if
(\Sigma,\Sigma_{\rm SSP},{\bm{L}})
is a prestable SSP thimble and
u:\Sigma\to M
is a
stable SSP thimble
which solves the SSP equation (
4.10
) on
\Sigma_{{\rm SSP}}
, there exists a Hermitian line bundle over
\Sigma
, unique up to isomorphism, such that the curvature of its Chern connection is equal to
-2\pi{\bm{i}}\Omega_{u,{\rm PSS}}
, where

\Omega_{u,{\rm SSP}}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\displaystyle u^{*}\omega-d(H_{s,t}^{\rm SSP}(u)dt)&\text{ on the distinguished component $\Sigma_{\rm SSP}$}\\
\displaystyle u^{*}\omega-d(H_{t}(u)dt)&\text{ on cylindrical components on the left of $\Sigma_{\rm SSP}$}\\
\displaystyle u^{*}\omega&\text{ on other irreducible components.}\qed\end{array}\right.

Given a stable PSS (resp. SSP) thimble, let us denote the line bundle constructed as above by
L_{u,{\rm PSS}}
(resp.
L_{u,{\rm SSP}}
). Now we can introduce the concept of framed curves in the context of PSS/SSP map.

Definition 7.4
.

Given a moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q}
of stable PSS thimbles and a stratum
\alpha=\bullet r_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
which corresponds to thimbles breaking at the orbits
r_{1},\dots,r_{l}
, denoting
d=d_{\bullet q}
, a
framed PSS thimble
(of type
\alpha
) is a tuple
(u,\Sigma,F)
where

(1)

\Sigma
is a prestable PSS thimble with
l
horizontal levels on the right of
\Sigma_{\rm PSS}
. (see Definition
7.1
).

(2)

u:\Sigma\to M
is a smooth map whose restriction to each cylindrical component on the right of
\Sigma_{\rm PSS}
(included) converges to periodic orbits prescribed by the capped orbits
p,r_{1},\dots,r_{l},q
at
\pm\infty
in an exponential rate, and the topological energy of these levels are prescribed accordingly. Moreover, the
2
-form
\Omega_{u,{\rm PSS}}
defined in Equation (
7.1
) is non-negative and is strictly positive on each unstable component of
u
.

(3)

F=(f_{0},f_{1},\ldots,f_{d})
is basis of global sections of the line bundle
L_{u,{\rm PSS}}
constructed using the
2
-form (
7.1
). Moreover, the induced holomorphic map

\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d},\ w\mapsto[f_{0}(w),\ldots,f_{d}(w)]

(7.2)

is a stable map, which represents a point
[\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}]\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\rm PSS}_{0,2}(d)
. Denote by

\tilde{\iota}_{F,{\rm PSS}}:\Sigma\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\rm PSS}={\mathcal{C}}^{\rm PSS}_{0,2}(d)

to be the identification between
\Sigma
and the fiber of the universal curve
{\mathcal{C}}^{\rm PSS}_{0,2}(d)\to{\mathcal{F}}^{\rm PSS}_{0,2}(d)
over
[\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}]
. This will also be called the domain map.

By replacing PSS with SSP, we obtain the definition of
framed PSS thimble
. Isomorphisms of framed PSS/SSP thimbles are defined in the same way as Definition
5.32
. The compact Lie group
PU(d+1)
acts on the space of framed PSS/SSP thimbles by changing the framing. The stabilizer of this
PU(d+1)
action at
(u,\Sigma,F)
agrees with the automorphism group of the map
u:\Sigma\to M
if
u
is a stable PSS/SSP thimble.

Now we can repeat the global thickening construction in the setting of PSS/SSP maps as we did in the case for Floer trajectories. Let
p
be a capped
1
-periodic orbit. Fix
d=d_{\bullet q}
. Let

k_{1}<\cdots<k_{d}

be a sequence of positive integers. Define
V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}:=V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})
be the moduli space of tuples

(\Sigma,u,F,\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{d})

where
\Sigma
is a prestable PSS thimble,
u:\Sigma\to M
is a smooth map with topological energy
d
converging to
q
at the marked point
z_{+}
,
F
is a frame of the line bundle
L_{u,{\rm PSS}}\to\Sigma
inducing a holomorphic map
\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d}
such that the framing
F=(f_{0},\dots,f_{d})
satisfies
[f_{0}(z_{-}):\cdots:f_{d}(z_{-})]=[1:0:\cdots:0]
, and the corresponding domain map
\tilde{\iota}_{F,{\rm PSS}}:\Sigma\to{\mathcal{C}}^{\rm PSS}
, and (denoting by
\tilde{u}=(u,\tilde{\iota}_{F,{\rm PSS}}):\Sigma\to M\times{\mathcal{C}}^{\rm PSS}
)

\eta_{i}\in\tilde{u}^{*}E_{i}=\Gamma\left(\overline{\rm Hom}\big(\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}^{*}T\mathbb{CP}^{d},u^{*}TM\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{i-1}\tilde{u}^{*}E_{j}\big)\otimes\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k_{i})\right)\otimes\overline{H^{0}(\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(k_{i}))_{0}}

satisfying the following equations

\left\{\begin{aligned} \overline{\partial}_{J,H^{\rm PSS}}u+\pi_{u^{*}TM}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\langle\eta_{i}\rangle\circ d\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}\right)&\ =0,\\
\overline{\partial}\eta_{i}+\pi_{E_{i}}\left(\sum_{j=i+1}^{d}\langle\eta_{j}\rangle\circ d\iota_{F,{\rm PSS}}\right)&\ =0,\ i=1,\ldots,d,\end{aligned}\right.

(7.3)

where
E_{i}
is defined in the same way as (
5.31
), and the
\overline{\partial}
-operator
\overline{\partial}_{J,H^{\rm PSS}}
is read off from the PSS equation
4.6
.

By replacing PSS with SSP, we can similarly define the thickened moduli space
V_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}=V_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})
for a capped orbit
p
.

The space
V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
(resp.
V_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}
) admits a
G_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}=G_{d_{\bullet q}}
(resp.
G_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}=G_{d_{p\bullet}}
) action extending the action on the framing. The obstruction bundle over
E_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\to V_{\bullet q}^{\bullet}
also has a direct sum decomposition

E_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}=O_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\oplus Q_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}

where
O_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
is specified by (
5.33
), and
Q_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
is the trivial vector bundle from
{\bm{Q}}_{d_{\bullet q}}
as in (
5.12
). This is a
G^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q}
-equivariant vector bundle. As for the Kuranishi section, it is defined by

\begin{split}S_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}:V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}&\ \to E_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\\
[\Sigma,u,F,\eta]&\ \mapsto(\eta,H_{F})\end{split}

where
H_{F}\in Q_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
is represented by the Hermitian matrix whose entries are

\int_{\Sigma}\langle f_{i},f_{j}\rangle_{L_{u,{\rm PSS}}}\Omega_{u,{\rm PSS}},

where
f_{0},\dots,f_{d}
is a basis representing the frame
F
. It is obvious that there exists a homeomorphism

\psi_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}:(S_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS})^{-1}(0)/G^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q}\xrightarrow{\sim}\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}.

This finishes the description of the K-chart

K_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}:=K_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d}):=(G^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q},V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS},E^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q},S_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS},\psi_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}).

The same description applies to the SSP moduli space and we obtain the K-chart

K_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}:=K_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}=(G^{\rm SSP}_{p\bullet},V_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP},E_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP},S_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP},\psi_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP})

for the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}
.

The following statement follows from the proof of Theorem
5.47
.

Proposition 7.5
.

For fixed
k_{1},\dots,k_{d-1}
, as long as
k_{d}
is sufficiently large, the thickened moduli spaces
V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}=V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})
and
V_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}=V_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})
is regular near the zero locus of the Kuranishi section and
E_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\to V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
,
E_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}\to V_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}
are indeed vector bundles. ∎

7.1.3.
The K-chart lift of the flow bimodule

We would like the system of K-charts obtained above provide a K-chart lift of the flow bimodule
M^{\rm PSS}
resp.
M^{\rm SSP}
. Still we describe the PSS case with more details. Recall that the moduli space (
4.7
) is a
{\mathbb{A}}^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q}
-space. Given a stratum
\alpha=\bullet r_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
, the moduli space

\partial^{\alpha}\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}=\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\bullet r_{1}}^{\rm PSS}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{r_{1}r_{2}}^{\rm Floer}\times\cdots\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}^{{\rm Floer}}_{r_{l}q}

has a product K-chart

K_{\alpha}^{\rm PSS}:=K_{\bullet r_{1}}^{\rm PSS}\times K_{r_{1}r_{2}}\times\cdots\times K_{r_{l}q}

where
K_{r_{i}r_{i+1}}
is the K-chart constructed in (
5.37
) for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{r_{i}r_{i+1}}^{\rm Floer}
. Similarly, for
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}\bullet
, the moduli space

\partial^{\alpha}\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}=\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}^{\rm Floer}_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{r_{l-1}r_{l}}^{\rm Floer}\times\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}^{\rm SSP}_{r_{l}\bullet}

has a product K-chart

K_{\alpha}^{\rm SSP}:=K_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times K_{r_{l-1}r_{l}}\times K_{r_{l}\bullet}^{\rm SSP}.

Recall that the flow category
T^{\rm Floer}
is endowed with a weak K-chart lift as from Theorem
5.11
of the form

\big\{K_{pq}=(G_{pq},V_{pq},E_{pq},S_{pq},\psi_{pq})\big\}_{p<q},

together with the collection of weak K-chart embeddings

\big\{{\bm{\iota}}_{prq}:K_{pr}\times K_{rq}\rightharpoonup\partial^{prq}K_{pq}\big\}_{p<r<q}.

Theorem 7.6
.

For a capped
1
-periodic orbits
p
resp.
q
, there exist a collection of weak K-chart embeddings

\big\{{\bm{\iota}}_{\bullet rq}:K_{\bullet r}^{\rm PSS}\times K_{rq}\rightharpoonup\partial^{\bullet rq}K_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\big\}_{r<q}

\text{resp. }\big\{{\bm{\iota}}_{pr\bullet}:K_{pr}\times K^{\rm SSP}_{r\bullet}\rightharpoonup\partial^{pr\bullet}K_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}\big\}_{p<r},

such that the following statements are true.

(1)

The following diagrams are commutative under the chart embeddings:

\textstyle{K^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet r}\times K_{rs}\times K_{sq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial^{\bullet rs}K_{\bullet s}^{\rm PSS}\times K_{sq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K_{\bullet r}^{\rm PSS}\times\partial^{rsq}K_{rq}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial^{\bullet rsq}K^{\rm PSS}_{\bullet q},}

\textstyle{K_{rs}\times K_{sp}\times K^{\rm SSP}_{p\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial^{rsp}K_{rp}\times K^{\rm SSP}_{p\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K_{rs}\times\partial^{sp\bullet}K^{\rm SSP}_{s\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\partial^{\bullet rsp}K^{\rm SSP}_{\bullet p}.}

(2)

For each stratum
\alpha=\bullet r_{1}\cdots r_{l}q\in{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
resp.
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}\bullet\in{\mathbb{A}}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}
, define the product chart

K_{\alpha}^{\rm PSS}:=K_{\bullet r_{1}}^{\rm PSS}\times\cdots\times K_{r_{l}q}

{\rm resp.}K^{\rm SSP}_{\alpha}:=K_{pr_{1}}\times\cdots\times K^{\rm SSP}_{r_{l}\bullet}.

Then for any pair
\alpha\leq\beta
in
{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
resp.
{\mathbb{A}}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}
, there are induced product weak embeddings

{\bm{\iota}}^{\rm PSS}_{\beta\alpha}:K^{\rm PSS}_{\alpha}\rightharpoonup\partial^{\alpha}K^{\rm PSS}_{\beta}\text{\ resp. }{\bm{\iota}}^{\rm SSP}_{\beta\alpha}:K^{\rm SSP}_{\alpha}\rightharpoonup\partial^{\alpha}K^{\rm SSP}_{\beta}.

Then the system

\{K^{\rm PSS}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}}\text{ resp. }\{K^{\rm SSP}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}}

defines a weak K-chart presentation of the
{\mathbb{A}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
-space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
resp. the
{\mathbb{A}}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}
-space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}
.

(3)

After outer-collaring, one can match up the Kuranishi sections so that the weak K-chart presentation is a genuine K-chart presentation.

(4)

The natural projection
\pi_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}:V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\to B_{d_{\bullet q}}^{\rm PSS}
resp.
\pi_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}:V_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}\to B_{d_{p\bullet}}^{\rm SSP}
has a
C_{\rm loc}^{1}
equivariant fiberwise smooth bundle structure.

Sketch of proof.

The embeddings
{\bm{\iota}}_{\bullet rq}^{\rm PSS}
and
{\bm{\iota}}_{pr\bullet}^{\rm SSP}
are defined in a similar way as in Section
5.4
when we embed a product of K-charts of a list of Floer moduli spaces. It is routine to check that these embeddings satisfy the listed commutative diagrams and give weak K-chart presentations of the corresponding PSS and SSP moduli spaces. Notice that for the same reason, the product Kuranishi section only matches with the boundary restriction of the larger Kuranishi chart in the
O
-summand. We can use outer-collaring and an interpolation to match their
Q
-summand. Lastly, the projection
\pi_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
and
\pi_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}
have the structure of
C_{\rm loc}^{1}
equivariant fiberwise smooth bundles for the same reason as in the case of Floer trajectories.
∎

7.1.4.
Scaffolding

We could state and prove a proposition similar to Proposition
5.64
. However, instead of doing the tedious thing, we only describe how the existing scaffoldings for the K-chart lift of
(T^{\rm Floer})^{+}
can be extended. Notice that we will not use any object or structure we chose in Section
6
. Essentially (for the PSS case), we would like to choose subbundles

F_{\bullet q,\alpha}^{\rm PSS}\subset O_{\bullet q}|_{V_{\alpha}^{\rm PSS}}

and define stabilization maps

{\rm Stab}_{F_{\bullet q,\alpha}}(V_{\alpha}^{\rm PSS})\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}

whose
G_{\bullet q}
-equivariantization is an open embedding. (We skip the discussion of the companion bundle isomorphisms.) This bundle
F_{\bullet q,\alpha}
is indeed the orthogonal complement of
E_{\alpha}^{\rm PSS}
inside
E_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
; a good choice of an inner products can be made inductively as in the proof of Proposition
5.64
which extend the inner products we have already chosen on the obstruction bundles over the thickend Floer moduli spaces. Then the stabilization map is still constructed using the implicit function theorem which depends on choosing a family of local Banach manifold charts, a family of approximate solutions, and a family of right inverses. These objects can all be extended from the existing ones we have chosen for the Floer moduli spaces. Therefore a compatible system of stabilization maps can be constructed. Of course, the collar structure is necessary for the construction.

7.1.5.
Stable smoothing

We would like to construct a system of stable equivariant smoothings of
V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
resp.
V_{p\bullet}^{\rm SSP}
. Notice that we have chosen stabilizations by
G_{pq}
-representations
{\bm{R}}_{pq}
for all
p<q
in
{\mathcal{P}}^{\rm Floer}
. The method we used in Subsection
6.4
can be used again here to construct stabilizations
{\bm{R}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
and to construct smoothings on
\widehat{V}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}:=V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\times{\bm{R}}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
whose boundary is diffeomorphic to products of stabilized thickenings.

We can also require that the stabilizations satisfy the following property. Namely, there exist smooth submersive evaluation maps

{\rm ev}_{\bullet}:\widehat{V}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}\to M

which coincides with the original evaluation map on
V_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
. Indeed, by observation of Abouzaid–McLean–Smith
[
AMS21
, Lemma 4.5]
, for a single smooth Kuranishi chart, on a further stabilization there exists a smooth submersive extension of the evaluation map. Then when we inductively construct stable smoothings, we can always achieve this extra condition.

It follows that by intersecting with the unstable manifolds of the given Morse function
f:M\to{\mathbb{R}}
, one obtains smooth Kuranishi charts

\widehat{K}_{xq}^{\rm PSS}=(G_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS},\widehat{V}_{xq}^{\rm PSS},\widehat{E}_{xq}^{\rm PSS},\widehat{S}_{xq}^{\rm PSS})

where
\widehat{V}_{xq}^{\rm PSS}\subset\widehat{V}_{\bullet q}^{\rm PSS}
is the intersection with the (outer-collared) compactified unstable manifold
\overline{W^{u}(\underline{\smash{x}})}\subset M
. It is straightforward to check that we obtained a smooth K-chart lift of the flow bimodule
(M^{\rm PSS})^{+}
.

Then after taking quotient, we obtained a D-chart lift of
(M^{\rm PSS})^{+}
equipped with a collar structure and a scaffolding. The straightening that is compatible with the collar structure and the scaffolding can be constructed in a way similar to the case of Floer trajectories.

7.1.6.
Normal complex structures and orientaions

The construction of normal complex structure and orientation on
\widehat{K}_{xq}^{\rm PSS}
essentially follows from the proof of Theorem
6.32
. and the relevant discussions in
[
AB21
, Section 11]
. Two remarks should be pointed out. First, the stabilization from
[
AMS21
, Lemma 4.5]
and the fiber product with unstable submanifolds does not affect the
normal
complex structure, because the changes on the tangent bundles are all included in the trivial summand of the corresponding representation of the isotropy group. Second, when constructing orientation structures, the virtual vector bundle associated with an object
x\in T^{\rm Morse}
is defined by
(TW^{u}(\underline{\smash{x}}),0)
.

7.2.
Pearly bimodule

Consider the pearly bimodule defined in Section
4
. Here we describe a K-chart lift of it and show how to use the same argument to construct a stable smoothing. First, for each
d
, consider an auxiliary moduli space which is similar to
B_{d}
,
B_{d}^{\rm PSS}
, and
B_{d}^{\rm SSP}
defined before. Indeed, let
{\mathcal{F}}_{0,2}^{\rm pearl}(d)
be the moduli space of parametrized 2-marked holomorphic spheres into
\mathbb{CP}^{d}
which have degree
d
and whose images are not entirely contained in a hyperplane. In particular, each point of
{\mathcal{F}}_{0,2}^{\rm pearl}(d)
is represented by a stable map with 2 marked points that have a distinguished component whose parametrization is fixed.
{\mathcal{F}}_{0,2}^{\rm pearl}(d)
is a smooth manifold (with no boundary or corners). Moreover there are holomorphic submersions

{\rm ev}_{\pm}:{\mathcal{F}}_{0,2}^{\rm pearl}(d)\to\mathbb{CP}^{d}.

Let

B_{d}^{\rm pearl}\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{0,2}^{\rm pearl}(d)

be the subset of elements whose evaluation at
z_{-}
is
[1,0,\ldots,0]\in\mathbb{CP}^{d}
. Then
B_{d}^{\rm pearl}
is a smooth manifold. There is a
G_{d}\cong U(d)
-action on
B_{d}^{\rm pearl}
.

Now consider the corresponding space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}{}_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}(d)
of stable parametrized holomorphic spheres in
M
whose degree is
d
. Using multi-layered thickening associated to a sequence of integers
k_{1}<k_{2}<\cdots<k_{d}<\cdots
one can produce a similar thickened moduli space

K_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}(d)=(G_{d},V_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}(d),E_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}(d),S_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}).

The domain
V_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}(d)
admits a
G_{d}
-action and an equivariant projection map

\pi_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}:V_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}(d)\to B_{d}^{\rm pearl}.

Moreover, by taking intersections with stable and unstable submanifolds of the Morse function
f:M\to{\mathbb{R}}
, one obtains stratified charts

K_{xy}^{\rm pearl}=(G_{xy},V_{xy}^{\rm pearl},E_{xy}^{\rm pearl},S_{xy}^{\rm pearl})

This collection of charts obviously defines a K-chart lift of the pearly bimodule
M^{\rm pearl}
.

Theorem 7.7
.

There exists a “single-layered” normally complex derived orbifold lift of the outer-collared pearly bimodule
(M^{\rm pearl})^{+}
.

Sketch of Proof.

We can construct stable smoothings of
K_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}(d)
individualy for each
d\geq 1
to obtain smooth Kuranishi charts. To save notations, still denote them by
K_{\bullet\bullet}^{\rm pearl}(d)
. We can do a further stabilization such that the evaluations at
z_{\pm}
are smooth submersions onto
M
. Hence each
K_{xy}^{\rm pearl}
becomes smooth stratified Kuranishi charts. Its outer-collaring is again smooth Kuranishi charts. The scaffoldings are trivial as the Morse theory part is already regular. Then taking quotient by
G_{xy}
one obtains a D-chart lift of
(M^{\rm pearl})^{+}
, denoted by
({\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm pearl})^{+}
. As the scaffolding is trivial, this is a single-layered lift. Lastly, there is a normal complex structure and an orientation on
({\mathfrak{D}}^{\rm pearl})^{+}
for the same reason as the case of
[
AMS21
]
.
∎

7.3.
The homotopy

Lastly, we explain the collection of D-chart lifts of the system of moduli spaces referred to as the “homotopy moduli spaces.” Although this
ad hoc
collection lacks a formal package, the constructions are almost identical to previous cases. The global chart construction, including the multi-layered strategy, is the same as the case of Floer trajectories or PSS/SSP cases. Then one obtains a weak K-chart lift of the collection of moduli spaces. By taking outer-collaring and interpolating between the product Kuranishi section and the boundary restriction of Kuranishi sections, one obtains a K-chart lift of the outer-collared system. The construction of scaffolding (see Proposition
5.64
) has completely the same induction procedure. We can also run the smoothing procedure without essential differences.

Appendix A
Product of canonical Whitney stratifications

A.1.
Basics of Whitney stratifications

We review the basic notions of (Whitney) prestratifications and stratification. Following Mather
[
Mat73
]
, our convention is the same as our previous work
[
BX22
]
.

Definition A.1
.

Let
S
be a topological space. A
prestratification
on
S
is a decomposition

S=\bigsqcup_{\alpha\in{\mathfrak{A}}}S_{\alpha}

of
S
into the disjoint union of locally closed subsets satisfying the following condition

(1)

The decomposition is locally finite.

(2)

(Axiom of frontier) If
\alpha,\beta\in{\mathfrak{A}}
,
S_{\alpha}\cap\overline{S_{\beta}}\neq\emptyset
, then
S_{\alpha}\subset\overline{S_{\beta}}
.

Each
S_{\alpha}
is called a
stratum
of the prestratification. The axiom of frontier induces a partial order among strata:
S_{\alpha}\leq S_{\beta}
if
S_{\alpha}\subset\overline{S_{\beta}}
. We use the symbol
{\mathfrak{A}}
to denote the prestratification as well as the partially ordered set of strata. A space equipped with a prestratification
{\mathfrak{A}}
is called an
{\mathfrak{A}}
-stratified space.

Now we consider the notion of stratifications. Two subsets
A,B\subseteq M
are called
equivalent
at
x\in M
if there exists an open neighborhood
U_{x}
of
x
such that
A\cap U_{x}=B\cap U_{x}
. An equivalence class is called a
setgerm
at
x
. Given a prestratification
{\mathcal{A}}
on
S\subseteq M
, it assigns to each
x\in S
a setgerm represented by the unique stratum that contains
x
. A
stratification
of a subset
S\subset M
is a rule which assigns to each point
x\in S
a set-germ
S_{x}
, such that for each
x\in S
, there is an open neighborhood
U_{x}
of
x
and a prestratification of
U_{x}\cap S
such that the setgerm-valued function restricted to
U_{x}
is induced from this prestratification.

Definition A.2

(Whitney stratification)
.

Let
M
be a smooth manifold.

(1)

Given two disjoint smooth submanifolds
S
,
S^{\prime}
, we say that the pair
(S,S^{\prime})
satisfies
Whitney’s condition (b)
at
x\in S^{\prime}\cap\overline{S}
if the following is true: suppose
x_{i}\in S
,
x_{i}^{\prime}\in S^{\prime}
are two sequences converging to
x\in\overline{S}\cap S^{\prime}
. Suppose the sequence of tangent spaces
T_{x_{i}}S
converges to a subspace
H\subset T_{x}M
and the sequence of secant lines
\overline{x_{i}x_{i}^{\prime}}
converges to a line
L\subset T_{x}M
, then
L\subset H
.

(2)

Let
S\subset M
be a subset. A prestratification on
S
is called a
Whitney prestratification
if each stratum
S_{\alpha}
is a smooth submanifold and each pair
(S_{\alpha},S_{\beta})
of strata with
S_{\beta}\subsetneq\overline{S_{\alpha}}
satisfies Whitney’s condition (b) at every point of
S_{\beta}
.

(3)

A stratification on
S\subset M
is called a
Whitney stratification
if for each point
x\in S
there exists an open neighborhood
U_{x}\subset S
of
x
and a Whitney prestratification on
U_{x}
which induces this stratification inside
U_{x}
. We use the symbol
{\mathcal{S}}
to denote a Whitney stratification on
S
, which is a setgerm-valued function on
S
.

On the same set there could be many different Whitney stratifications. One can use a partial order among them to compare. Suppose
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
are two Whitney stratifications, which assign to each
x
setgerms
S_{1,x}
and
S_{2,x}
respectively. Then define

S_{i}^{(k)}=\{x\in S\ |\ {\rm dim}_{\mathbb{R}}S_{i,x}\leq k\},\ i=1,2.

Then one has descending sequence of closed sets

S_{i}=S_{i}^{(m)}\supseteq S_{i}^{(m-1)}\supseteq\cdots\supseteq S_{i}^{(0)}

where
m={\rm dim}_{\mathbb{R}}M
.

Definition A.3
.

{\mathcal{S}}_{1}<{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
if there exists an integer
k
such that

S_{1}^{(l)}=S_{2}^{(l)}\ \forall l>k\ {\rm and}\ S_{1}^{(k)}\subsetneq S_{2}^{(k)}.

If a Whitney stratification is minimal, then it is unique. Moreover, a minimal Whitney stratification on
S\subseteq M
is invariant under diffeomorphisms of
M
which preserve
S
set-wise (see
[
BX22
, Lemma A.11]
).

Theorem A.4
.

[
Whi65
]

Given a smooth complex algebraic variety and
S\subseteq M
a constructible set. There exists a minimal Whitney stratification which is induced from a Whitney prestratification whose strata are all smooth complex algebraic submanifolds.

We sketch the constructive proof following Mather
[
Mat73
]
as this construction will be needed for further discussions. Suppose the (real) dimension of
M
is
m
. Then we construct inductively a sequence of closed algebraic subsets

S=S^{(m)}\supseteq S^{(m-1)}\supset\cdots\supseteq S^{(0)}

satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

Each
S^{(l)}
is a closed algebraic subset of
M
of real dimension at most
l
.

(2)

Each
\mathring{S}^{(l)}:=S^{(l)}\setminus S^{(l-1)}
is a smooth complex algebraic submanifold of (real) dimension
l
. It can empty, for example, when
l
is odd.

(3)

For each pair
l>k
, the pair
(\mathring{S}^{(l)},\mathring{S}^{(k)})
satisfies Whitney’s condition (b) at every point of
\mathring{S}^{(k)}
.

Indeed, suppose
S^{(m)},\cdots,S^{(k)}
have been constructed. Then we define
S^{(k-1)}\subset S^{(k)}
to be the subset of points
x\in S^{(k)}
satisfying one of the following conditions.

(1)

x
is a singular point of
S^{(k)}
or a regular point of
S^{(k)}
with local (real) dimension strictly less than
k
.

(2)

x
is a regular point of
S^{(k)}
with local dimension
k
and there exists
l>k
such that the pair
(\mathring{S}^{(l)},S^{(k)}_{reg})
does not satisfy Whitney’s condition (b) at
x
. Here
S_{reg}^{(k)}\subseteq S^{(k)}
is the Zariski open subset of regular points of local dimension
k
.

Basic algebraic geometry implies that the set of points satisfying the first condition above is a closed algebraic set of real dimension strictly less than
k
. On the other hand, it is a fundamental theorem of Whitney that the set of points satisfying the second condition above is also a closed algebraic set of real dimension strictly less than
k
. Then this produces a Whitney prestratification whose strata are all connected components of all
\mathring{S}^{(k)}
. It is not difficult to prove that this Whitney stratification is the minimal one.

A.2.
Product of canonical Whitney stratifications

Now we consider if the product of canonical Whitney stratifications on a product algebraic set is the canonical one. Let
M_{1},M_{2}
be smooth complex algebraic varieties and
S_{1}\subseteq M_{1}
,
S_{2}\subseteq M_{2}
be complex algebraic subsets, equipped with the canonical Whitney stratification of with levels
S_{i}^{(k)}
. Denote

\displaystyle\ M=M_{1}\times M_{2},\

\displaystyle\ S=S_{1}\times S_{2}.

For each
k\geq 0
, denote

\mathring{S}_{\times}^{(k)}:=\bigcup_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\mathring{S}_{1}^{(k_{1})}\times\mathring{S}_{2}^{(k_{2})}.

It is easy to see that the prestratification on
S
whose strata are all connected components of all
\mathring{S}_{\times}^{(k)}
is a Whitney prestratification. Denote the associated Whitney stratification by
{\mathcal{S}}_{\times}
. It is easy to see the induced dimension filtration on
S
is given by

S_{\times}^{(k)}=\bigcup_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}S_{1}^{(k_{1})}\times S_{2}^{(k_{2})}.

Proposition A.5
.

The Whitney stratification
{\mathcal{S}}_{\times}
on
S
is the minimal (canonical) one.

Proof.

We know that the canonical Whitney stratification, denoted by
{\mathcal{S}}
, is the minimal one. Then one has
{\mathcal{S}}\leq{\mathcal{S}}_{\times}
. Suppose this is not an equality. Then by definition (see Definition
A.3
), there exists
l
such that

S^{(k)}=S_{\times}^{(k)}\ \forall k>l,\ S^{(l)}\subsetneq S_{\times}^{(l)}.

Therefore, there exists a point

x=(x_{1},x_{2})\in S^{(l+1)}=S_{\times}^{(l+1)}=\bigcup_{l_{1}+l_{2}=l+1}S_{1}^{(l_{1})}\times S_{2}^{(l_{2})},

x\in S_{\times}^{(l)}
,
x\notin S^{(l)}
. Then by the construction of the canonical Whitney stratification,
x
is a regular point of
S^{(l+1)}
of local dimension
l+1
. Then
x
is a regular point of the union of
S_{1}^{(l_{1})}\times S_{2}^{(l_{2})}
for all possible
l_{1}+l_{2}=l+1
. Then for some
l_{1},l_{2}
with
l_{1}+l_{2}=l+1
,
x=(x_{1},x_{2})
is a regular point of
S_{1}^{(l_{1})}\times S_{2}^{(l_{2})}
with local dimension
l+1
. This implies that
x_{i}
is a regular point of local dimension
l_{i}
in each factor.

However,
x\in S_{\times}^{(l)}
also implies that either
x_{1}\in S_{1}^{(l_{1}-1)}
or
x_{2}\in S_{2}^{(l_{2}-1)}
. Without loss of generality, we assume
x_{1}\in S_{1}^{(l_{1}-1)}
. Then as
x_{1}
is a regular point of
S_{1}^{(l_{1})}
, it implies that for some
k_{1}>l_{1}
, Whitney’s condition (b) for
(\mathring{S}_{1}^{(k_{1})},S_{1,reg}^{(l_{1})})
fails at
x_{1}
. Then there exists a sequence
y_{1,\nu}\in\mathring{S}_{1}^{(k_{1})}
and a sequence
x_{1,\nu}\in S_{1,reg}^{(l_{1})}
, both of which converging to
x_{1}
such that
T_{y_{1,\nu}}\mathring{S}_{1}^{(k_{1})}
converges to a subspace
H_{1}\subset T_{x_{1}}M_{1}
, the secant line
\overline{x_{1,\nu}y_{1,\nu}}
converges to a line
L_{1}\subset T_{x_{1}}M
, but
L_{1}\nsubseteq H_{1}
. Now we separate the discussion in two scenarioes

(1)

If
x_{2}\in\mathring{S}_{2}^{(l_{2})}
, then consider the two sequences
(x_{1,\nu},x_{2})\in S_{1,reg}^{(l_{1})}\times S_{2,reg}^{(l_{2})}\subset S^{(l+1)}_{reg}
,
(y_{1,\nu},x_{2})\in\mathring{S}_{1}^{(k_{1})}\times\mathring{S}_{2}^{(l_{2})}\subset\mathring{S}^{(k_{1}+l_{2})}
. Then one can see that Whitney’s condition (b) fails for
(\mathring{S}^{(k_{1}+l_{2})},S_{reg}^{(l+1)})
at the limit
x=(x_{1},x_{2})
, which is a contradiction that
x\in\mathring{S}^{(l+1)}
.

(2)

Suppose
x_{2}\notin\mathring{S}_{2}^{(l_{2})}
. Then by construction, there exist some
k_{2}>l_{2}
, a sequence
y_{2,\nu}\in\mathring{S}_{2}^{(k_{2})}
converging to
x_{2}
, a sequence
x_{2,\nu}\in S_{2,reg}^{(l_{2})}
converging to
x_{2}
, such that the sequence of tangent spaces
T_{y_{2,\nu}}\mathring{S}_{2}^{(k_{2})}
converges to
H_{2}
and the sequence of secant lines
\overline{x_{2,\nu}y_{2,\nu}}
converges to a line
L_{2}
but
L_{2}
is not contained in
H_{2}
. Then consider the sequence of points
y_{\nu}=(y_{1,\nu},y_{2,\nu})\in\mathring{S}_{1}^{(k_{1})}\times\mathring{S}_{2}^{(k_{2})}\subset\mathring{S}^{(k_{1}+k_{2})}
, the sequence of points
x_{\nu}=(x_{1,\nu},x_{2,\nu})\in S_{1,reg}^{(l_{1})}\times S_{2,reg}^{(l_{2})}\subset S_{reg}^{(l+1+l_{2})}=S_{reg}^{(l+1)}
. This breaks the hypothesis that
x\in\mathring{S}^{(l+1)}
.

∎

A.3.
Relative case

Now we consider the relative case. Let
S
be equipped with a prestratification
{\mathfrak{A}}

S=\bigsqcup_{\alpha\in{\mathfrak{A}}}S_{\alpha}

such that each stratum
S_{\alpha}
is algebraic. A Whitney stratification
{\mathcal{S}}
on
S
is said to respect the given prestratification if for each
x\in S
, the germ
S_{x}
is contained in the stratum
S_{\alpha}
which contains
x
. We call such Whitney stratifications
{\mathfrak{A}}
-Whitney stratifications. We proved in
[
BX22
]
that there exists a unique minimal
{\mathfrak{A}}
-Whitney stratification, which we call the canonical
{\mathfrak{A}}
-Whitney stratification.

For the purpose of this paper, we need to verify that such canonical Whitney stratification is natural with respect to products. Let
M,N
be two smooth complex algebraic varieties and
S\subset M
,
T\subset N
be constructible subsets. Given algebraic prestratifications

\displaystyle\ S=\bigsqcup_{\alpha\in{\mathfrak{A}}}S_{\alpha},\

\displaystyle\ T=\bigsqcup_{\beta\in{\mathfrak{B}}}T_{\beta},

one has an associated decomposition

R:=S\times T=\bigsqcup_{(\alpha,\beta)\in{\mathfrak{A}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}}T_{(\alpha,\beta)}=\bigsqcup_{(\alpha,\beta)\in{\mathfrak{A}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}}S_{\alpha}\times T_{\beta}.

We can check that this is still a prestratification with algebraic strata. Moreover, the induced partial order on the set of strata
{\mathfrak{A}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}
is the product one:

(\alpha,\beta)\leq(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})\Longleftrightarrow\alpha\leq\alpha^{\prime}\ {\rm and}\ \beta\leq\beta^{\prime}.

Let the prestratification be denoted by
{\mathfrak{A}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}
. On
S
resp.
T
there are minimal
{\mathfrak{A}}
- resp.
{\mathfrak{B}}
-Whitney stratification, whose product is also a Whitney stratification on
R=S\times T
.

Proposition A.6
.

The product Whitney stratification on
R=S\times T
is the minimal
{\mathfrak{A}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}
-Whitney stratification.

Proof.

We prove by induction. Proposition
A.5
implies that for each top stratum
(\alpha_{0},\beta_{0})\in{\mathfrak{A}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}
, the product Whitney stratification coincides with the minimal Whitney stratification on
S_{\alpha_{0}}\times T_{\beta_{0}}
. Now given a stratum
(\alpha,\beta)\in{\mathfrak{A}}\times{\mathfrak{B}}
. Suppose for any
(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})>(\alpha,\beta)
, we have proved that the product Whitney stratification on
S_{\alpha^{\prime}}\times T_{\beta^{\prime}}
coincides with the restriction of the minimal
({\mathfrak{A}}\times{\mathfrak{B}})
-Whitney stratification. We would like to show that it is still the case for the pair
(\alpha,\beta)
.

Suppose our claim is false. Then there exists
k\geq 0
such that

(S_{\alpha}\times T_{\beta})^{(l)}=\bigcup_{p+q=l}S_{\alpha}^{(p)}\times T_{\beta}^{(q)},\ \forall l\geq k+1,\ (S_{\alpha}\times T_{\beta})^{(k)}\subsetneq\bigcup_{p+q=k}S_{\alpha}^{(p)}\times T_{\beta}^{(q)}.

Then one can choose a point

x\in(S_{\alpha}\times T_{\beta})^{(k+1)}\setminus(S_{\alpha}\times T_{\beta})^{(k)}

(A.1)

such that

x\in\bigcup_{p+q=k}S_{\alpha}^{(p)}\times T_{\beta}^{(q)}.

(A.2)

By the construction of the minimal Whitney stratifications on constructible sets (sketched after Theorem
A.4
), one knows that
x=(y,z)
is a regular point of
(S_{\alpha}\times T_{\beta})^{(k+1)}
of local dimension
k+1
. This implies that for some
p+q=k+1
,
x
is a regular point of
S_{\alpha}^{(p)}\times T_{\beta}^{(q)}
of local dimension
k+1
. Then
y
resp.
z
is a regular point of
S_{\alpha}^{(p)}
resp.
T_{\beta}^{(q)}
of dimension
p
resp.
q
. Moreover, (
A.2
) implies that either
y\in S_{\alpha}^{(p-1)}
or
z\in T_{\beta}^{(q-1)}
.

We claim that
y
is not in the boundary of any
\mathring{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(p^{\prime})}
with
\alpha^{\prime}>\alpha
and
p^{\prime}\leq p
. If it is the case, assume that
z\in\mathring{T}_{\beta}^{(q^{\prime})}
for some
q^{\prime}\leq q
. Then we see that

x=(y,z)\in\partial\mathring{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(p^{\prime})}\times\mathring{T}_{\beta}^{(q^{\prime})}\subset\partial\left(\mathring{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(p^{\prime})}\times\mathring{T}_{\beta}^{(q^{\prime})}\right)\subset\partial\mathring{R}_{(\alpha^{\prime},\beta)}^{(p^{\prime}+q^{\prime})}.

Notice that
p^{\prime}+q^{\prime}\leq k+1
and
(\alpha^{\prime},\beta)>(\alpha,\beta)
. This contradicts the fact that
x\notin R_{(\alpha,\beta)}^{(k)}
. Similarly,
z
is not in the boundary of any
\mathring{T}_{\beta^{\prime}}^{(q^{\prime})}
with
\beta^{\prime}>\beta
and
q^{\prime}\leq q
.

Without loss of generality, assume we are in one of the following two scenarios.

(1)

y\in S_{\alpha}^{(p-1)}
and
z\in\mathring{T}_{\beta}^{(q)}
. Then we know that for some
\alpha^{\prime}\geq\alpha
and
p^{\prime}\geq p+1
, Whitey’s condition (b) fails for the pair
(\mathring{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(p^{\prime})},S_{\alpha,reg}^{(p)})
at
y
. Then this implies that Whitney’s condition (b) fails for the pair

\left(\mathring{R}_{(\alpha^{\prime},\beta)}^{(p^{\prime}+q)},R_{(\alpha,\beta),reg}^{(p+q)}\right)

at
x=(y,z)
. This contradicts the assumption that
x\notin R_{(\alpha,\beta)}^{(k)}
.

(2)

y\in S_{\alpha}^{(p-1)}
and
z\in T_{\beta}^{(q-1)}
. Then we know for some
\alpha^{\prime}\geq\alpha
,
\beta^{\prime}\geq\beta
,
p^{\prime}\geq p+1
,
q^{\prime}\geq q+1
, Whitney’s condition (b) fails for the pair
(\mathring{S}_{\alpha^{\prime}}^{(p^{\prime})}\times\mathring{T}_{\beta^{\prime}}^{(q^{\prime})},S_{\alpha,reg}^{(p)}\times T_{\beta,reg}^{(q)})
at
(y,z)
. It follows that Whitney’s condition (b) fails for the pair

\left(\mathring{R}_{(\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})}^{(p^{\prime}+q^{\prime})},R_{(\alpha,\beta),reg}^{(p+q)}\right).

at
x=(y,z)
. This contradicts the assumption that
x\notin R_{(\alpha,\beta)}^{(k)}
.

∎

Appendix B
Relative stable equivariant smoothing

Our construction in this paper relies on equivariant smoothings of infintely many moduli spaces. It is necessary to have an extension of the stable
G
-smoothing results of Lashof
[
Las79
]
to a relative setting. Namely, if a
G
-manifold
M
has a
G
-smoothing over an open set, then one can extend the existing smoothing to
M
once the tangent microbundle admits a
G
-vector bundle reduction which is compatible with the existing smoothing.

Lashof’s original construction relies on two technical results. The first one is Mostow’s embedding theorem
[
Mos57
]
and the second is Jaworowski’s extension theorem
[
Jaw76
]
. We need to recall these two theorems in order to obtain a generalization of Lashof’s results on stable
G
-smoothing to the relative setting.

Lemma B.1
.

[
Mos57
, Lemma 5.2]

Let
G
be a compact Lie group of transformations on a metric space
E
, and let
T_{1},T_{2}
be invariant subsets with
E=T_{1}\cup T_{2}
and
T_{2}
be closed. Assume that there exist
G
-equivariant topological embeddings
\varphi_{i}:T_{i}\to{\bm{R}}_{i}
,
i=1,2
where
{\bm{R}}_{1},{\bm{R}}_{2}
are orthogonal
G
-spaces. Then there exists a
G
-equivariant topological embedding
\varphi:E\to{\bm{R}}_{1}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{2}
such that

\varphi|_{T_{2}}=(0,\varphi_{2}).

Theorem B.2
.

[
Jaw76
, Theorem 2.2]

Let
X
be a locally compact
G
-space which can be equivariantly embedded into some finite-dimensional orthogonal
G
-vector space. Suppose
X
has only finitely many orbit types. Let
A\subset X
be a closed
G
-subspace and
f:A\to Y
be a continuous
G
-map to a locally compact separable metrizable
G
-space
Y
. If for each
x\in X\setminus A
the fixed point set
Y^{G_{x}}
is an ANR (absolute neighborhood retract), then
f
can be extended to a continuous
G
-map from a neighborhood of
A
in
X
.

Now we want to extend Lashof’s construction to a relative setting.

Theorem B.3
.

Let
M
be a topological
G
-manifold with only finitely many orbit types. Let
O\subset M
be an open subset such that its closure
\overline{O}
is compact. Suppose that

(1)

the tangent microbundle
T_{\mu}M
of
M
admits a
G
-vector bundle reduction
\varphi:E_{\mu}\to T_{\mu}M
for a vector bundle
E\to M
.

(2)

A
G
-invariant open neighborhood
U
of
\overline{O}
is equipped with a
G
-smoothing and
E|_{U}
is equipped with a smooth
G
-bundle structure such that the reduction
\varphi
is smooth over
U
. Moreover,
\varphi
is in the same isotopy class of the canonical
TU\to T_{\mu}U
.

Then there exists a finite-dimensional orthogonal representation
{\bm{R}}
of
G
and a
G
-smoothing
\alpha
on
M\times{\bm{R}}
satisfying

(1)

The induced stable isotopy class of
G
-vector bundle reductions of
T_{\mu}(M\times R)
coincides with the
{\bm{R}}
-stabilization of
\varphi
.

(2)

There exists a
G
-invariant open neighborhood
U^{\prime}
of
\overline{O}
with
\overline{U^{\prime}}\subset U
, such that the restriction of the
G
-smoothing
\alpha
on
U^{\prime}\times{\bm{R}}
is diffeomorphic to the given
G
-smoothing on
U^{\prime}\times{\bm{R}}
.

Proof.

We generalize the construction of Lashof
[
Las79
]
to the current setting. We first consider the case that
E\to M
is trivial. Assume that

E=M\times{\bm{W}}

where
{\bm{W}}
is an orthogonal
G
-space. The vector bundle lift is given by a map

\exp:M\times{\bm{W}}\to M\times M

which is smooth over
U\times{\bm{W}}
. For each
x\in M
, denote the restriction of
\exp
to
\{x\}\times{\bm{W}}
by

\exp_{x}:{\bm{W}}\to\{x\}\times M

which is a homeomorphism onto an open neighborhood of
x
.

Now we prove the following claims.

Claim A. There exist an orthogonal
G
-representation
{\bm{R}}
and a
G
-equivariant topological embedding
\iota:M\to{\bm{R}}
which is smooth near
\overline{O}
.

Proof of Claim A.
As
\overline{O}
is compact, by a theorem of Palais (see
[
Pal57
, Theorem III]
), there is a smooth
G
-equivariant embedding from an open neighborhood of
\overline{O}
into an orthogonal
G
-space
{\bm{R}}_{1}
. We may assume that this embedding is defined and smooth over
U
, denoted by
\iota_{U}:U\to{\bm{R}}_{1}
. Let
U^{\prime}\subset U
be a
G
-invariant open neighborhood of
\overline{O}
such that
\overline{U^{\prime}}\subset U
. Then
M^{\prime}:=M\setminus\overline{U^{\prime}}
is a topological
G
-manifold with only finitely many orbit types. Hence by Mostow’s embedding theorem (Theorem
B.1
), there exists a
G
-orthogonal representation
{\bm{R}}_{2}
and a
G
-equivariant topological embedding
\iota_{M^{\prime}}:M^{\prime}\to{\bm{R}}_{2}
. Then by Lemma
B.1
, writing
M=\overline{U^{\prime}}\cup M^{\prime}
, there exists a
G
-equivariant topological embedding

\iota:M\to{\bm{R}}_{1}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{2}

such that its restriction to
\overline{U^{\prime}}
coincides with
(\iota_{U},0)
. In particular, the restriction of
\iota
to
U^{\prime}
is smooth. Now define
{\bm{R}}:={\bm{R}}_{1}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{2}
.
End of the proof of Claim A.

Claim B. There is a
G
-invariant open neighborhood
N_{\iota}\subset{\bm{R}}
and a continuous
G
-equivariant retraction
r:N_{\iota}\to\iota(M)
which is smooth near
\iota(\overline{O})
.

Proof of Claim B.
Note that there exist a
G
-invariant open neighborhood
N_{U^{\prime}}\subset{\bm{R}}_{1}
of
\iota_{U}(U^{\prime})
and a smooth
G
-equivariant retraction
