r_{U^{\prime}}:\overline{N_{U^{\prime}}}\to\iota_{U}(U^{\prime}).

Then
r_{U^{\prime}}
induces a retraction from
\overline{N_{U^{\prime}}}\times{\bm{R}}_{2}
to
\iota(U^{\prime})
. Choose a smaller open neighborhood
U^{\prime\prime}
of
\overline{O}
with
\overline{U^{\prime\prime}}\subset U^{\prime}
. By applying Jaworowski’s extension theorem (Theorem
B.2
) to
X={\bm{R}}_{1}\oplus{\bm{R}}_{2}
,
A=(r_{U^{\prime}}^{-1}(\overline{U^{\prime\prime}})\times{\bm{R}}_{2})\cup\iota(M)
, and
Y=\iota(M)
, one obtains an extension, i.e., a
G
-invariant open neighborhood
N_{\iota}
of
\iota(M)
and a
G
-invariant retraction
r:N_{\iota}\to\iota(M)
which coincides with
r_{U^{\prime}}
near
\iota(\overline{U^{\prime\prime}})
. Indeed, such an extension is a retraction because it extends the identity map
Y\to Y
. In particular, it is smooth near
\iota(\overline{O})
.
End of the proof of Claim B.

Claim C. There is a
G
-equivariant continuous map

\psi:M\times{\bm{R}}\to{\bm{R}}\times{\bm{W}}

satisfying

(1)

\psi
is a homeomorphism onto an open subset.

(2)

\psi
is a diffeomorphism near
\overline{O}\times\{0\}
with respect to the given
G
-smoothing near
\overline{O}
.

Proof of Claim C.
Consider the
G
-equivariant map

\theta:M\times{\bm{R}}\to{\bm{R}},\ (x,y)\mapsto x+y.

There exists a continuous function
\delta:M\to(0,+\infty)
such that

|y|<\delta(x)\Longrightarrow\theta(x,y)\in N_{\iota}.

We can choose
\delta
such that it is smooth over
U
. Let
y\mapsto y^{\prime}
be a
G
-invariant smooth map which maps
{\bm{R}}
diffeomorphically onto the unit ball
{\bm{R}}(1)
. Then define

\begin{split}\psi:M\times{\bm{R}}\to&\ {\bm{R}}\times{\bm{W}},\\
(x,y)\mapsto&\ \Big(\theta(x,\delta(x)y^{\prime}),\exp_{x}^{-1}(r(\theta(x,\delta(x)y^{\prime})))\Big).\end{split}

This map is of the same form of the one used in
[
Las79
, Theorem 1.3]
. Hence this map is a homeomorphism onto an open subset of
{\bm{R}}\times{\bm{W}}
. Moreover, by the explicit construction one can verify that
\psi
is a smooth embedding near
\overline{O}\times\{0\}\subset\overline{O}\times{\bm{R}}
.
End of the proof of Claim C.

Therefore,
\psi
pulls back the standard
G
-invariant smooth structure on
{\bm{R}}\times{\bm{W}}
to a smooth structure on
M\times{\bm{R}}
, giving a stable
G
-smoothing on
M
. Moroever, as
\psi
is smooth near
\overline{O}\times\{0\}
, this stable
G
-smoothing is diffeomorphic to the
{\bm{R}}
-stabilization of the given one. Lastly, one can check easily that the induced stable
G
-vector bundle reduction of
T_{\mu}(M\times{\bm{R}})
is in the same isotopy class of the given one.

Now we consider the general case where
E\to M
is not necessarily trivial. We know that
E
has a stable inverse, which is a
G
-equivariant vector bundle
\pi_{F}:F\to M
such that
E\oplus F\cong M\times{\bm{W}}
for some orthogonal
G
-space
{\bm{W}}
. We may assume that both
E
and
F
are smooth
G
-equivariant vector bundles near
\overline{O}
and the isomorphism
E\oplus F\cong M\times{\bm{W}}
is smooth there. We may assume assume that the
G
-vector bundle reduction, which is equivalent to a map

\exp:E\to M\times M

is smooth near
\overline{O}
.

Consider the total space
F
. It is a topological
G
-manifold hence has a
G
-tangent microbundle
T_{\mu}F
. By
[
Las79
, Lemma 1.6]
, the
G
-vector bundle reduction

\exp:E\to M\times M

of
T_{\mu}M
induces a
G
-vector bundle reduction

\widehat{\varphi}:F\times{\bm{W}}\cong\pi_{F}^{*}(E\oplus F)\to F\times F

of
T_{\mu}F
. Notice that there is an induced
G
-smoothing in a neighborhood of
\pi_{F}^{-1}(\overline{O})
in the total space of
F
. The construction of
\widehat{\varphi}
in
[
Las79
, Lemma 1.6]
can be carried out to guarantee that with respect to the smooth structures on
E
and
F
near
\overline{O}
,
\widehat{\varphi}
is smooth near
\pi_{F}^{-1}(\overline{O})
. Then by the special case when
E
is trivial, one can construct a
G
-smoothing on
F\times{\bm{W}}
for some orthogonal
G
-space
{\bm{R}}
which coincides with the induced smooth structure near
\pi_{F}^{-1}(\overline{O})\times{\bm{R}}
. Then consider

M\times{\bm{W}}\times{\bm{R}}=(E\oplus F)\times{\bm{R}}=\pi_{F}^{*}E\times{\bm{R}}

which is the total space of a
G
-equivariant vector bundle over
F
. Hence we can choose a smooth bundle structure on this bundle making the total space
M\times{\bm{W}}\times{\bm{R}}
smooth. As
E
and
F
are already smooth near
\overline{O}
, we can guarantee that the smooth bundle structure is induced from the original smooth bundle structures on
E
and
F
near
\overline{O}
.
∎

Appendix C
A proof sketch of Proposition
5.64

C.1.
Analytical setup

We provide a systematic setup which generalizes the case of a single stabilization map in Subsection
5.5
. For each
pq
and each integer
d\geq d_{pq}
we describe a topological space
{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d)}
which is morally a stratified Banach fiber bundle over
B_{d_{pq}}
. Indeed, for each
\underline{\smash{x}}\in B_{d_{pq}}
, its fiber over the universal curve is a prestable cylinder
\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}
over which there is a stable map
\underline{\smash{\mu}}_{\underline{\smash{x}}}:\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}\to\mathbb{CP}^{d_{pq}}
representing
\underline{\smash{x}}
. Then there are bundles

E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}\to\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}\times M

whose fibers are similar to the one (
5.46
) in the single-layered case. Fixing
a>2
, there is then a space
{\mathcal{C}}^{1,a}(\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}},E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)})
of
W^{1,a}
-sections
(u,\eta):\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}\to E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}
, where
u:\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}\to M
is a map asymptotic to possible periodic orbits at cylindrical nodes or the two marked points and
\eta
is a section of
\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}|_{{\rm graph}u}
such the total map
(u,\eta)
is of class
W^{1,a}
. Define

{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d)}=\bigsqcup_{\underline{\smash{x}}\in B_{d_{pq}}}{\mathcal{C}}^{1,a}(\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}},E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}).

It is stratified by
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
. There are natural inclusions

{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d)}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d^{\prime})}\ \forall d^{\prime}>d.

There are also inclusions

\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}\subset\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d_{pq})}\subset\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d)}\ \forall d\geq d_{pq}.

Notice that there is a
G_{pq}^{\mathbb{C}}
-action on
{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d)}
.

Over each
{\mathcal{C}}^{1,a}(\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}},E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)})
there is also a Banach space bundle denoted by
Y_{\underline{\smash{x}}}
whose fiber at
(u,\eta)
is the space of
L^{a}
-sections of certain bundles along
(u,\eta)
. We omit its detailed definition. Indeed there is a section
{\mathcal{F}}_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}
of this Banach space bundle whose zero locus in the
d=d_{pq}
case is the fiber of
V_{pq}
over
\underline{\smash{x}}
.

One can consider the linearization of the (multi-layered) thickened Floer equation at any
x\in\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
. Indeed, there are sequences of linear operators

D_{x}^{(d)}:T_{x}{\mathcal{C}}^{1,a}(\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}},E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)})\to Y_{\underline{\smash{x}}}|_{x}.

In fact, by the nature of the thickening equation (perturbations with lower
d
does not deform the equation for perturbations with higher
d^{\prime}
), the linear operators are always block upper-triangular, i.e., when
d<d^{\prime}
, one has

D_{x}^{(d^{\prime})}=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}D_{x}^{(d)}&*\\
0&D_{x}^{[d,d^{\prime}]}\end{array}\right]

where the last block is a linear Cauchy–Riemann operator on some vector bundle.

Our construction guarantees that all these linearizations are surjective. Hence we can consider their bounded right inverses. By the above block upper-triangular form, we can have right inverses which are also block upper-triangular. We always assume this without further clarification in the rest of this appendix.

The construction of a compatible system of stabilization maps (as well as other structures included in Proposition
5.64
), as we showed in the case of a single stabilization map, depends on the choices of three kinds of structures. They are 1) continuous families of right inverses, 2) families of approximate solution maps, and 3) families of local Banach manifold charts and local Banach vector bundle trivializations. A crucial point of our proof presented in this appendix is that these three kinds of objects can always be obtained from local to global and can always be interpolated between each other.

We first define these notions under the current setting.

Definition C.1
.

An
approximate solution map
(for level
d
) is a germ of continuous maps

O_{pq}^{(d)}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d)}

which satisfies the following conditions.

(1)

It is level preserving, i.e., if
d^{\prime}<d
, then it sends
O_{pq}^{(d^{\prime})}\subset O_{pq}^{(d)}
into
{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d^{\prime})}
.

(2)

It is fiberwise smooth. It means the following. For each
\underline{\smash{x}}\in B_{d_{pq}}
, the fiber
\pi_{pq}^{-1}(\underline{\smash{x}})\subset V_{pq}
of the thickened moduli is smooth, the fiberwise restriction of the level-
d
obstruction bundle
O_{pq}^{(d)}
is smooth, and the Banach manifold
{\mathcal{C}}^{1,a}(\Sigma_{\underline{\smash{x}}},O_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)})
is also smooth. Then the fiberwise restriction of the approximate solution map is smooth.

(3)

Over
V_{\alpha}\subset V_{pq}
for any
\alpha\in{\mathbb{A}}_{pq}^{\rm Floer}
, the approximate solution map is equal to the product.

Remark C.2
.

(1)

An obvious choice of such approximate solution map is just to add sections of the obstruction bundle linearly in the infinite-dimensional space. This obvious option does not deform the underlying map into
M
. However in certain stages of our construction we must use more general kinds of approximate solution maps which deforms the underlying map.

(2)

To construct the stabilization map from a product
V_{\alpha}
to
\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
, we do not need to use the whole approximate solution map for all
d
, but only its restriction to some subbundle of
O_{pq}^{(d_{pq})}
restricted to
V_{\alpha}
. However, using the approximate solution map to
O_{pq}^{(d)}
for all
d
helps one to extend the map we actually need from a lower to a higher stratum.

(3)

Remember that the obstruction bundle also contains the quadratic form part
Q_{pq}
. When constructing the actual approximate solutions to the thickened equation, we also need to change the approximate solution by variables from
Q_{pq}
via the
G_{pq}^{\mathbb{C}}
-actions.

We can also define the notion of continuous family of bounded right inverses, for all levels, generalizing the case of Subsection
5.5
.

We can also define the notion of families of local charts/trivializations. The obvious choices are the one coming from parallel transport along shortest geodesics starting at any
x\in V_{pq}
. We should regard this kind of choices as “continuous” when the domain changes. However, we may also need to use the chart induced from a map
x^{\prime}
near
x
. We can also define a notion of continuity.

C.2.
The inductive proof of Proposition
5.64

We have the following induction procedure to construct the structures we need.

Step 1.
We first consider any minimal thickened moduli space
V_{pq}
which does not have boundary or corners.

(1)

We can choose
G_{pq}
-invariant inner products on
O_{pq}^{(d)}\to V_{pq}
inductively on
d\geq d_{pq}
such that the embeddings
O_{pq}^{(d)}\to O_{pq}^{(d+1)}
for all
d\geq d_{pq}
are isometric.

(2)

We can also inductively construct continuous families of vertical right inverses of all levels
d\geq d_{pq}
inductively.

(3)

We can also construct a collection of approximate solution map. For each
d\geq d_{pq}
, we can choose a germ of maps

O_{pq}^{(d)}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d)}

defined by

[\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta,\zeta]\mapsto[\Sigma,u,\mu,\eta+\zeta].

The summation is defined because
\eta
and
\zeta
are contained in the same vector bundle.

Step 2.
Suppose we have constructed the three kinds of structures up to an energy level
d_{pq}
. There are certain compatibility conditions we need to require. For example, they must respect the collars. We will describe the required conditions we we need them. Now we start to consider the structures over the thickened moduli space
V_{pq}
and the stabilizations maps from each
V_{\alpha}
to
\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
. In this step we describe how to define these structures over a minimal stratum
\alpha=pr_{1}\cdots r_{l}q
.

(1)

We first consider the metric on
O_{pq}^{(d)}
restricted to
V_{\alpha}
. Start with
d_{0}=d_{pq}
. We view this stratum as contained in
V_{pr_{1}r_{l}q}
. Denote
r_{1}=r
,
r_{l}=s
. Then there are the two subbundles

\displaystyle\ O_{ps\to pq}^{(d_{0})},\

\displaystyle\ O_{rq\to pq}^{(d_{0})}

on which the expected properties and inner products we have already constructed induce unique inner products. We verify that these two inner products on these two subbundles agree on their intersection. By the canonical decomposition (
5.36
), one has

O_{ps\to pq}^{(d_{0})}\cap O_{rq\to pq}^{(d_{0})}\subset O_{pq;rs}^{(d_{0})}.

In fact we can show that

O_{ps\to pq}^{(d_{0})}\cap O_{rq\to pq}^{(d_{0})}=O_{rs\to pq}^{(d_{0})}.

Then over this intersection the existing inner products agree. Moreover
O_{ps\to pq}^{(d_{0})}+O_{rq\to pq}^{(d_{0})}
contains all existing subbundles of
O_{pq}^{(d_{0})}|_{V_{\alpha}}
. Hence they induce an inner product on the sum
O_{ps\to pq}^{(d_{0})}+O_{rq\to pq}^{(d_{0})}
which agrees with all existing induced ones. Then extend it arbitrarily to
O_{pq}^{(d_{0})}|_{V_{\alpha}}
.

(2)

Now we obtained an orthogonal complement

O_{pq,\alpha}\subset O_{pq}|_{V_{\alpha}}.

Define

F_{pq,\alpha}:=O_{pq,\alpha}\oplus Q_{pq,\alpha}\to V_{\alpha}.

At this moment we can define the stabilization map

\theta_{pq,\alpha}:{\rm Stab}_{F_{pq,\alpha}}(V_{\alpha})\to\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}

In fact, the existing approximate solution maps can be extended to one on
O_{pq}^{(d_{0})}
in the same fashion as we extend the inner product in the above. Then we can restrict the approximate solution map to
O_{pq,\alpha}
. Then as we did in (
5.47
), we can define a map from
F_{pq,\alpha}=O_{pq,\alpha}\oplus Q_{pq,\alpha}
to
\partial^{\alpha}{\mathcal{C}}_{pq}^{(d_{0})}
. One can also extend the family of right inverse and the existing charts, in the same fashion as we extended the inner products above. Then it produces the way to correct the approximate solutions to exact solutions canonically. Then the corresponding projection map

\pi_{pq,\alpha}:\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}\to V_{\alpha}^{\sim}

is obtained. From the construction, we can see that the projections are compatible with all existing ones when restrict to
\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}
for an intermediate stratum
\beta
. The bundle isomorphism

\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\alpha}:\pi_{pq,\alpha}^{*}\left(O_{pq}^{(d)}|_{V_{\alpha}^{\sim}}\right)\to O_{pq}^{(d)}

for all
d
can be obtained.

(3)

Now we inductively construct inner products on
O_{pq}^{(d)}
over
V_{\alpha}
for all
d\geq d_{0}
. Suppose we have done this for some
d
. Then similar to the above case, one can obtain a unique inner product on the sum

O_{ps\to pq}^{(d)}+O_{rq\to pq}^{(d)}.

We can verify that this metric agrees with the one on
O_{pq}^{(d-1)}
on the intersection
O_{pq}^{(d-1)}\cap(O_{ps\to pq}^{(d)}+O_{rq\to pq}^{(d)})
. Indeed,

O_{pq}^{(d-1)}\cap(O_{ps\to pq}^{(d)}+O_{rq\to pq}^{(d)})=O_{ps\to pq}^{(d-1)}+O_{rq\to pq}^{(d-1)}.

Hence there exists a further extension to
O_{pq}^{(d)}
over the set
V_{\alpha}
.

Then using the bundle isomorphisms we can extend the inner products to
O_{pq}^{(d)}|_{\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}}
. At last, the approximate solution map, the right inverse map, and the local charts can be extended to
\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
.

Step 3.
Using the collar structure one can extend all we have constructed over
\partial^{\alpha}V_{pq}
to a small neighborhood. Now we would like to carry out the induction for a higher stratum
\beta
. Indeed, for the higher stratum one has its own construction which can be irrelevant to what we just did. What we need to do here is to connect the structures over the collar region near
\partial^{\alpha}V_{\beta}
.

In fact, the interpolation between bundle metrics can be easily done. The interpolation between right inverses can also be done using convex combinations (notice that convex combinations of block upper-triangular operators are still block upper-triangular). The more nonlinear interpolation is the one for approximate solution and the one for local charts/trivializations. For approximate solutions, we can first interpolate (arbitrarily) between the underlying maps into
M
, then interpolate over the fiber (linear) direction of
E_{\underline{\smash{x}}}^{(d)}
. For local charts/trivializations, as they are induced from parallel transport “centered” at families of maps into
M
, we only needs to interpolate the certer maps. Indeed, the maps we want to interpolate between are close enough to each other. We can do the same construction for all stratum of
V_{pq}
inductively.

Lastly, the construction of the stabilizations maps implies that

\pi_{pq,\alpha}=\pi_{\beta\alpha}\circ\pi_{pq,\beta}

The bundle isomorphisms
\widehat{\theta}_{pq,\beta}
for all stratum can be obtained as well.
